# University of Barishal BBA in Accounting & Information Systems
## Eight-semester syllabus-to-YouTube study guide

**Curriculum basis:** AIS curriculum for the 11th batch (Session 2022–2023 onward), using the course matrix and the *Detailed Syllabus* section in the supplied PDF. The PDF also contains an earlier Management Studies curriculum; it is not used for this guide.

This guide brings together the detailed topic outline for each listed course and direct YouTube lesson links mapped to those topics. A video may support several topics, and multiple videos are included when useful. Course-specific coverage audits identify what has a verified lesson and what remains uncovered. **A “NOT COVERED” marker means this guide did not verify a sufficiently specific lesson for that item; do not assume the neighboring video covers it.**

The researchers opened each recommended YouTube watch page to check the displayed title, channel, and relevance. This is page-level verification, not a guarantee that the full video was watched or that a lecture exactly follows University of Barishal exam questions. Some pages expose only a title/description or chapter list, and a few players blocked playback. Use the syllabus and class materials as the exam authority; for Bangladesh laws and tax, verify current statutory wording and distinguish Bangladesh lessons from videos teaching another jurisdiction. No video selection can guarantee a passing grade.

The roster contains **41 theory-course entries and four separately listed lab entries (45 course entries total)** across eight semesters. The semester-wise course index follows that roster. The PDF contains code/title/credit inconsistencies for a few entries; the affected fragments flag them. The internship/research/project report appears as a final capstone but has no detailed topic outline in the supplied AIS section; the PDF gives inconsistent internship-credit figures (4 credits in the semester listing versus 3 in the credit-count summary) and describes a four-month placement. Semester viva-voce examinations are also listed in the curriculum but are not separate topic courses.

## Course-by-course syllabus and videos

# Semester 1 — First Year, First Semester

# 1101 Introduction to Financial Accounting

**Semester:** First Year — First Semester (Semester 1)  
**Curriculum source:** University of Barishal, AIS 11th-batch curriculum, *DETAILED SYLLABUS*, printed PDF pages 35–37. This is the AIS section, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

### 1. Introduction to Accounting
- Definition of accounting.
- Uses and users of accounting information.
- Measurement principles and assumptions.
- Elements of financial statements.
- Basic accounting equation.
- Transaction analysis.
- Financial statements.

### 2. Accounting Regulation and Conceptual Framework
- **Regulation:** IFRS Foundation; IASB; setting of IFRS; standard-setting process in Bangladesh; Financial Reporting Council (FRC); Companies Act 1994; Securities and Exchange Rules 1987; BSEC Directives; Income Tax Act 2023; ICAB; ICMAB; GAAP; IASB vs FASB.
- **IASB Conceptual Framework:** introduction; objective of general-purpose financial reporting; assumptions and principles; qualitative characteristics of useful financial information; financial statements and the reporting entity; elements of financial statements; recognition and derecognition; measurement; presentation and disclosure; concepts of capital and capital maintenance.

### 3. The Recording Process and Accounting Cycle
- Nature of business: manufacturer, merchandizer, and service provider.
- Types of business: sole proprietorship, partnership, and companies.
- The account; chart of account; debit-credit procedures; double-entry accounting system.
- Recording process: journal, ledger, trial balance, and adjustments; need for adjustments; adjusted trial balance; preparation of financial statements; closing entries; post-closing trial balance; worksheet and reversing entries; correcting entries.

### 4. Accounting for Merchandising Concern
- Operating cycle.
- Accounting for merchandising operations under perpetual and periodic inventory systems.
- Completing the accounting cycle.

### 5. Accounting System Design
- Charts of accounts and coding of accounts.
- Control accounts and their purposes.
- Special journals and their advantages.
- Cash receipt journal; cash payment journal; sales journals; purchase journal.
- General ledgers and subsidiary ledgers.

### 6. Preparation of Financial Statements (IAS 1)
- Company statements according to IAS 1: Statement of Financial Position; Statement of Profit or Loss and Other Comprehensive Income; Statement of Changes in Equity; Statement of Cash Flows; and notes comprising significant accounting policies and other explanatory information.
- Template of a company’s financial statements.
- Financial-statement preparation using MS Excel.

### 7. Bank Reconciliation Statement
- Cash and bank transactions.
- Meaning and need for a bank reconciliation statement.
- Prepare a reconciliation statement to determine the correct bank balance.

## Topic-to-video map

Each link below was opened as a direct YouTube watch page to confirm its displayed title and channel and to check that it is an actual lesson relevant to the stated syllabus topics. Topic statements are limited to coverage indicated by the page title, description, or visible chapters; recommendations are not treated as proof of a full course match.

### 1. Introduction to Accounting

- [FA1 - Introduction to Financial Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Rpa_UAciIeU) — **Tony Bell**. **Coverage:** introductory financial-accounting orientation only; the page gives the lesson title and workbook reference but does not enumerate the syllabus’s specific definitions or principles. **Exam use:** useful as a first-pass lecture before writing concise definitions; it is not being relied on for any specific listed subtopic.
- [The ACCOUNTING EQUATION For BEGINNERS](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=56xscQ4viWE) — **Accounting Stuff**. **Coverage:** basic accounting equation and its role as the foundation of double-entry accounting. **Exam use:** the simple equation explanation helps students reason through account effects before attempting transaction questions.
- [Transaction Analysis using the Accounting Equation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eG-nFshilc0) — **Kristin Ingram**. **Coverage:** transaction analysis using the equation; assets, liabilities, and equity; 11 sample transactions; deriving balance-sheet amounts from the equation. **Exam use:** worked transactions give direct practice tracing accounting-equation effects into a balance sheet.
- **NOT COVERED by a directly verified, sufficiently specific video:** definition of accounting; uses and users of accounting information; measurement principles and assumptions; the full set of financial-statement elements; preparing financial statements as a separate introductory topic. Search YouTube for: `financial accounting definition users uses measurement principles assumptions elements lesson`; `accounting measurement principles assumptions examples financial statements`.

### 2. Accounting Regulation and Conceptual Framework

- [The Conceptual Framework for Financial Reporting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F4A9UGI9Guk) — **Kaplan UK**. **Coverage:** visible chapters cover why a framework is needed, the framework, objective/purpose of financial reporting, qualitative characteristics, elements, recognition and derecognition, measurement, and presentation and disclosure. The page does not show chapters for the course’s assumptions and principles, financial statements and reporting entity, or capital and capital maintenance. **Exam use:** its structured chapter sequence is useful for short notes and written answers on the framework’s main components.
- [GAAP Explained: FASB, SEC, IASB & Core Accounting Principles](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=R_EIWtcTm9Q) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Coverage:** GAAP meaning; FASB, SEC, and IASB roles/differences; accounting principles and assumptions. **Jurisdiction:** US-oriented coverage (including SEC); use it for the comparative concepts, not as Bangladesh regulation. **Exam use:** helps frame a comparison answer on GAAP/standard setters and distinguish IASB from FASB.
- [GAAP to IAS & IFRS under IASB/IASC | Accounting practices in Bangladesh and Global context in Bangla](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sACkaXiLZ2M) — **Md. Shafiqul Alam LL.B, FCS, FCA, FCMA**. **Coverage:** GAAP-to-IAS/IFRS under IASB/IASC and Bangladesh/global accounting-practice context, as stated on the watch page. **Exam use:** Bangladesh-context framing makes this a useful supplement when discussing the move from local practice to international standards; the page does not establish coverage of the whole Bangladesh regulatory list below.
- [CL- Business Laws- Chapter 01 (Part 1)- The Companies Act,1994 -Yasin Miah FCA](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6EmciQ9qY9w) — **CA Chamber**. **Coverage:** Bangladesh Companies Act 1994; the page identifies the lesson as ICAB Certificate Level Business Laws. **Exam use:** supports statutory recall and written answers on the named Companies Act; it is a business-law lecture, not a financial-accounting treatment lesson.
- [Income from Other Sources – Easy Understanding | Income Tax Act 2023 | Bangladesh](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Gts7UJkjoSU) — **H2B Corporate**. **Coverage:** a specific income head and tax treatment under Bangladesh’s Income Tax Act 2023. **Exam use:** a Bangladesh-specific example can help identify the named statute in context, but it does **not** teach the course’s regulation/standard-setting survey or the entire Act.
- **NOT COVERED by a directly verified suitable video:** IFRS Foundation as an institution; IFRS-setting process; Bangladesh’s standard-setting process; Financial Reporting Council (FRC); Securities and Exchange Rules 1987; BSEC Directives; ICAB and ICMAB roles; a Bangladesh-specific treatment of the complete regulation landscape. Search YouTube for: `Bangladesh FRC standard setting IFRS financial reporting council lecture`; `Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Rules 1987 BSEC directives accounting lecture`; `ICAB ICMAB roles financial reporting Bangladesh FRC`; `IFRS Foundation IFRS standard setting process IASB lesson`.
- **NOT COVERED by the verified framework video’s visible chapters:** the framework’s assumptions and principles; financial statements and the reporting entity; concepts of capital and capital maintenance. Search YouTube for: `IASB conceptual framework reporting entity capital maintenance assumptions principles lecture`.

### 3. The Recording Process and Accounting Cycle

- [The Accounting Cycle Explained: From Transaction to Financial Statements](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AMyBLQ0rnW0) — **Dr. Brian Routh | Professor | Expert Bookkeeper**. **Coverage:** the high-level accounting-cycle path from a receipt, invoice, or bank transaction toward financial information and financial statements. The watch page does not list every cycle step. **Exam use:** useful for a written overview of why the accounting process is sequential and culminates in statements.
- [FA9 - Journal Entries Explained](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MZPXC4v8NCU) — **Tony Bell**. **Coverage:** journal-entry recording. **Exam use:** supports journal-entry practice and the debit/credit recording step; pair it with equation questions to check that entries stay balanced.
- [FA14 – Adjusting Entries - Comprehensive Accounting Problem (Part 1 of 4)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=udhouxDeCKU) — **Tony Bell**. **Coverage:** adjusting entries in a comprehensive accounting problem (part 1 of a series). **Exam use:** worked-problem format is useful for practicing end-of-period adjustments and their effect on later statements.
- **NOT COVERED by the verified pages:** manufacturer/merchandizer/service-provider business forms; sole proprietorship/partnership/company forms; account and chart of account; detailed debit-credit procedures; ledger posting; trial balance; need for adjustments beyond the adjustment example; adjusted trial balance; closing entries; post-closing trial balance; worksheet; reversing entries; correcting entries. Search YouTube for: `accounting cycle journal ledger trial balance worksheet closing reversing correcting entries full example`; `adjusted trial balance closing post closing reversing correcting entries worked problem`; `manufacturer merchandiser service provider types sole proprietorship partnership company accounting lesson`.

### 4. Accounting for Merchandising Concern

- [Merchandising Operations: Operating Cycle, Inventory, Purchase Discounts - Accounting video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=col_eHVJhcg) — **Dr. Brian Routh | Professor | Expert Bookkeeper**. **Coverage:** merchandising operating cycle, inventory, and purchase-discount terms, as stated in the description. **Exam use:** connects the operating-cycle idea with transaction context and provides a lecture basis for merchandising questions.
- [Perpetual vs. Periodic Inventory System 15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HYlgVwL8a8o) — **Accounting Instruction, Help, & How To (Financial & Managerial)**. **Coverage:** comparison of perpetual and periodic inventory systems. **Exam use:** the explicit side-by-side systems topic is useful for distinguishing when inventory and cost-of-goods-sold entries are recorded.
- **NOT COVERED by these verified pages:** a complete merchandising accounting cycle under both systems, including closing/completion steps. Search YouTube for: `merchandising accounting complete accounting cycle periodic perpetual worked problem closing entries`.

### 5. Accounting System Design

- [Special Journals in Accounting Explained: Sales, Purchases, Cash & Subsidiary Ledgers](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JrvpUFJVfOw) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Coverage:** sales journal, purchases journal, cash receipts journal, cash disbursements journal, general journal, and how customer/supplier subsidiary ledgers connect to general-ledger control accounts. **Exam use:** transaction examples clarify journal selection and subsidiary-ledger/control-account relationships likely to be tested in classification or posting questions.
- **NOT COVERED by the verified page:** chart-of-accounts design; account coding; explicit advantages of special journals; general-ledger operation beyond its relationship to subsidiary-ledger control accounts; control-account purposes as a standalone theory answer. Search YouTube for: `chart of accounts coding accounting control accounts purpose special journals advantages lesson`; `control account worked example general ledger subsidiary ledger`.

### 6. Preparation of Financial Statements (IAS 1)

- [FINANCIAL STATEMENTS FOR PUBLICATION (PART 1)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mr1_jRtPcYQ) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. **Coverage:** lesson on financial statements mandatory for public-company publication and preparation of those statements. **Jurisdiction caveat:** its page labels the course context **ICAG Paper 1.1 / ACCA Paper F3**; it is not Bangladesh-specific and should be used for statement-format practice, not Bangladesh filing rules.
- [How to Create Financial Statements Using Excel](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=H5_L5WZ0RRE) — **Custom Excel Spreadsheets**. **Coverage:** step-by-step preparation in Excel of a Profit & Loss/Income Statement, Cash Flow Statement, and Balance Sheet. **Exam use:** practical spreadsheet construction reinforces the syllabus’s MS Excel skill and statement preparation.
- [How to Prepare Financial Statements with Tax, TDS & VDS Compliance | Practical Accounting Bangladesh](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mcdb6oIp2e4) — **H2B Corporate**. **Coverage:** financial-statement preparation with Bangladesh tax, tax deducted at source (TDS), and VAT deducted at source (VDS) compliance, per its description. **Exam use:** a Bangladesh-specific practical supplement for seeing statements in a local compliance setting; it is not evidence of IAS 1 coverage or a substitute for syllabus-specific statement formats.
- **NOT COVERED by the verified pages:** a proven IAS 1 company template; Statement of Changes in Equity; Other Comprehensive Income presentation; notes, significant accounting policies, and explanatory information; exact Bangladesh company statement formats. The Excel page confirms only the income statement, cash flow statement, and balance sheet. Search YouTube for: `IAS 1 statement of financial position profit or loss OCI changes in equity notes worked example`; `Bangladesh company financial statements IAS 1 template statement changes equity notes`.

### 7. Bank Reconciliation Statement

- [FA19 – Bank Reconciliation Example - Step-by-Step Accounting Walkthrough](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rh8-Ob-Din8) — **Tony Bell**. **Coverage:** a worked bank-reconciliation example; the page offers a workbook alongside the walkthrough. **Exam use:** step-by-step reconciliation practice helps students calculate the reconciled/correct bank balance from the bank and book records.
- **NOT COVERED by the verified page:** a separate Bangladesh banking-rule treatment (not specified in this syllabus) or a distinct cash-book-versus-bank-statement theory lecture. Search YouTube for: `bank reconciliation statement cash book bank statement differences need meaning worked example`.

## Coverage audit and syllabus notes

- **Topic count:** 7 numbered detailed-syllabus sections.
- **Verified videos:** 18 unique direct watch pages; titles and channel names were checked on those pages. The links above are direct `youtube.com/watch?v=...` pages, not search-result-only references or channel homepages.
- **Complete video coverage:** **No.** Every syllabus section has at least one relevant verified lesson, but many finer-grained items are explicitly marked **NOT COVERED** above with targeted YouTube search phrases. The largest gaps are the Bangladesh regulatory/institutional framework and the less commonly taught accounting-cycle steps and IAS 1 disclosure/equity-statement details.
- **Text/technical notes:** The 11th-batch AIS section correctly gives course code 1101, course title, and First Year First Semester. The PDF says “Trail balance” in the recording-process line; this is treated as the apparent typo **trial balance**. The syllabus groups the “Statement of Cash Flows” under “Preparation of Financial Statements (IAS 1)”; technically, cash-flow statement requirements are in **IAS 7**, while IAS 1 covers presentation of financial statements. This report preserves the syllabus wording above and flags the standard-reference discrepancy here.

# 1102 Introduction to Business

**Semester:** First Year, First Semester (Semester 1)

**Source and heading check:** This is taken from the Accounting & Information Systems (AIS) curriculum’s **DETAILED SYLLABUS** section, printed pp. 37–40 (PDF pp. 37–40); the semester heading is on printed p. 36. The AIS course heading is explicitly **1102: Introduction to Business**. I did not use the earlier Management Studies curriculum. No material course-code/heading conflict appears in this AIS section. The printed ILO text has minor typographic/OCR errors (“compreh7end”, “differentstakeholders”); they do not alter course identification. The syllabus prints “Bangladesh Garments Manufacturers’ Association” and “Federation of Bangladesh Chamber of Commerce and Industries”; those names are retained below as printed.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **The nature of business:** Definition of Business; Vision; Mission; Indicators of A Successful Business; Customer Needs; Quality Products and Services; The Challenges and Complexities of Today’s Business; Forms of Business Ownership; MNCs.
2. **The Evolution of Business:** Feudalism—Land, Labour and Property Rights; Money, Capital and Commerce; Mercantilism—Trade and Enterprise; The Growth of Enterprise; Industrial Revolution—First to Fourth IR; The Changing Forms of Business Organization.
3. **The Stakeholders:** Stockholders; Board of Directors (BOD); Management; Government; Community.
4. **Business Environment:**
   - **4.1 The Impact of External Forces:** Political forces; Economic Forces; Social Forces; Technological Forces; Natural Environment and Business; Legal Forces; Competition; Porter’s Five Forces Model; Opportunities and Threats.
   - **4.2 The Internal Environment:** Accounting & Finance; Marketing; Human Resource Management; Production/Operation; Management Information Systems; Research & Development; Porter’s Generic Value Chain; Strengths and Weaknesses; An Introduction to SWOT.
5. **Management:** Definition of Management; Decision making; Planning; Organizing; Leading; Motivating; Controlling.
6. **Marketing:** Definition of Marketing; Product; Pricing; Promotion; Place.
7. **Finance:** Equity vs. Debt; Financial Markets; Stock Market; Blockchain/Fintech.
8. **International Business:** Introduction and overview of international business; Environmental variables of culture, politics, and economics; Entry strategies for new markets and countries; International trade and foreign direct investment; Global outsourcing and its role in international operations.
9. **Ethics and Social Responsibility of Business:** Ethics; Business ethics; The Need for Business Ethics; Ethical Dilemma; Corporate Social Responsibility; Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs); Social Business.
10. **Entrepreneurship:** Definition of Entrepreneurs and Entrepreneurship; Types of Entrepreneurs; Advantages and Disadvantages of Being an entrepreneur; Characteristics of a Successful Entrepreneur; Start-ups; Franchising.
11. **Global, Regional and Local Business Associations:** Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC); Association of Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN); Bangladesh Garments Manufacturers’ Association (BGMEA); BRICS; Federation of Bangladesh Chamber of Commerce and Industries (FBCCI); European Union (EU); Organization for Economic Cooperation and Development (OECD); South Asian Association for Regional Cooperation (SAARC); World Trade Organization (WTO).

## Topic-to-video map

Each linked watch page below was opened to check the actual video title, channel/uploader, and that it is a relevant lesson. Coverage claims are deliberately limited to what the watch-page title/description establishes; a course-topic title is not treated as proof that every subtopic in the Barishal syllabus is taught.

### 1. The nature of business

- [**PRC-5 | Intro to Business | Chapter 1 | Nature of Business | CA | CMA | PIPFA | BCOM | MBA | ACCA |**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Z6nHB_eX64k) — **TaxKar**. **Mapped topic:** Definition/nature of business. Its watch-page description identifies it as an Intro to Business Chapter 1 lesson on nature of business, useful for framing a basic written definition; it is auto-dubbed and is not a Barishal-specific lecture.
- **NOT COVERED:** Vision; Mission; Indicators of A Successful Business; Customer Needs; Quality Products and Services; The Challenges and Complexities of Today’s Business; MNCs. Search: `introduction to business vision mission successful business customer needs quality MNC lecture`.
- **Forms of Business Ownership** is mapped in section 2 below.

### 2. The Evolution of Business

- **NOT COVERED:** Feudalism—Land, Labour and Property Rights; Money, Capital and Commerce; Mercantilism—Trade and Enterprise; The Growth of Enterprise; Industrial Revolution—First to Fourth IR; The Changing Forms of Business Organization. A verified watch page for a broad business-history lecture was found, but its page did not establish these specific syllabus subtopics; it is not presented as coverage. Search: `business history feudalism mercantilism land labour property rights industrial revolutions business organization lecture`.
- [**Introduction to Business: Business Ownership**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9BPPmdNxHdE) — **Westminster International University in Tashkent**. **Mapped topic:** Forms of Business Ownership. This university lecture is explicitly about business ownership and is a useful comparison/revision prompt; its page does not enumerate every ownership form in the Barishal syllabus.
- **NOT COVERED:** MNCs (also listed in syllabus unit 1). Search: `multinational corporations MNC introduction to business lecture`.

### 3. The Stakeholders

- [**An organisation’s stakeholders - ACCA Paper BT**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kxxboP4osmI) — **OpenTuition**. **Mapped topic:** Stakeholders as a business concept. The watch page identifies it as an ACCA BT lecture with accompanying study notes, useful for defining stakeholders and structuring a short exam answer; its page does not verify coverage of the syllabus’s named groups.
- **NOT COVERED as individually verified syllabus coverage:** Stockholders; Board of Directors (BOD); Management; Government; Community. Search: `business stakeholders stockholders board of directors management government community lecture`.

### 4. Business Environment

- [**Introduction to Business: Business Environment**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vfNGr5gCbdw) — **Westminster International University in Tashkent**. **Mapped topic:** Introduction/overview of the business environment. This is a university lecture explicitly titled for the subject and is suitable as a broad conceptual primer; the watch page does not publish a subtopic outline.
- [**PESTEL Analysis EXPLAINED | B2U | Business To You**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bYn4CyL3r5w) — **Business To You**. **Mapped topics:** Political forces; Economic Forces; Social Forces; Technological Forces; Natural Environment and Business; Legal Forces. Its description spells out PESTEL’s political, economic, social, technological, environmental and legal macro-environment factors; the clear framework helps organize written answers. This is a general, not Bangladesh-law-specific, explanation.
- [**Porter's 5 Forces EXPLAINED | B2U | Business To You**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Dfp23xSqpdk) — **Business To You**. **Mapped topics:** Competition; Porter’s Five Forces Model. The verified description explains the five-forces industry-attractiveness framework and gives airline-industry examples, useful for remembering the forces and applying them in a short case answer.
- **NOT COVERED:** Opportunities and Threats.
- **NOT COVERED—Internal Environment:** Accounting & Finance; Marketing; Human Resource Management; Production/Operation; Management Information Systems; Research & Development; Porter’s Generic Value Chain; Strengths and Weaknesses; An Introduction to SWOT. Search: `internal business environment functional areas accounting finance HR operations MIS R&D value chain SWOT lecture`.

- [**International Business Management - Global Business, Internationalization, Globalization Explained**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFr77UOL6Go) — **Academic Gain Tutorials**. **Mapped topics:** Introduction and overview of international business. Its description identifies foundations of global business, internationalization, globalization and business environment, useful for an overview answer.
- **NOT COVERED:** Environmental variables of culture, politics, and economics; Entry strategies for new markets and countries; International trade and foreign direct investment; Global outsourcing and its role in international operations. Search: `international business culture political economic environments entry modes trade FDI outsourcing lecture`.
- This is general international-business teaching, not Bangladesh-specific regulatory instruction.

### 9. Ethics and Social Responsibility of Business

- [**Business Ethics and Social Responsibility | Episode 26**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lM7hLWeIUz8) — **Alanis Business Academy**. **Mapped topics:** Ethics; Business ethics; Corporate Social Responsibility. The lesson is explicitly devoted to business ethics and social responsibility and is useful for framing definitions and a written CSR response; its watch page does not confirm the syllabus’s remaining named themes.
- [**Muhammad Yunus - The Social Business Model**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0C3XQ3BTd4o) — **FORA.tv**. **Mapped topic:** Social Business. The description identifies Muhammad Yunus explaining the social-business model as an approach to social problems, providing a concrete Bangladesh-origin example for an exam answer.
- **NOT COVERED:** The Need for Business Ethics; Ethical Dilemma; Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). Search: `need for business ethics ethical dilemma cases SDGs business sustainability lesson`.

### 10. Entrepreneurship

- [**Introduction to Business Chapter 6: Entrepreneurship and Small Business**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jZ0mF-Gvy1E) — **Luther Maddy - Helping Humans Learn**. **Mapped topics:** Entrepreneurship and small-business lesson; Franchising (advantages/disadvantages). The instructor’s description says this college/university Intro to Business lecture covers entrepreneur traits and the advantages/disadvantages of franchising, making the latter useful for a compare/contrast answer.
- **NOT COVERED:** Definition of Entrepreneurs and Entrepreneurship; Types of Entrepreneurs; Advantages and Disadvantages of Being an entrepreneur; Characteristics of a Successful Entrepreneur (the video page only says entrepreneur traits, not specifically successful-entrepreneur characteristics); Start-ups; Franchising (a general definition/classification is not verified). Search: `entrepreneur definition types advantages disadvantages successful entrepreneur characteristics startup franchising lecture`.

### 11. Global, Regional and Local Business Associations

- **NOT COVERED:** Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC); Association of Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN); Bangladesh Garments Manufacturers’ Association (BGMEA); BRICS; Federation of Bangladesh Chamber of Commerce and Industries (FBCCI); European Union (EU); Organization for Economic Cooperation and Development (OECD); South Asian Association for Regional Cooperation (SAARC); World Trade Organization (WTO). No direct lesson covering the complete listed set was verified. Search: `APEC ASEAN BGMEA BRICS FBCCI EU OECD SAARC WTO business associations lecture Bangladesh` and, for local bodies, `BGMEA FBCCI Bangladesh business associations lecture`.
- **Bangladesh-specific note:** None of the verified videos claims coverage of BGMEA/FBCCI or Bangladesh regulatory law. The course’s association names above are retained as printed; verify spellings/names against the syllabus/department before memorizing. No Bangladesh law, tax or VAT topic is listed in this course’s detailed contents.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 11 numbered detailed-syllabus units. **Verified video count:** 13 unique direct watch-page links. Every syllabus subtopic is accounted for in the map, either as mapped coverage or explicitly **NOT COVERED** with targeted search terms. **Is every topic fully covered by a verified video? No.** Some units have only partial conceptual support; evolution (unit 2) and the named global/regional/local bodies (unit 11) have no verified direct coverage. Major unresolved items include the syllabus-specific history sequence, business vision/mission and success indicators, internal functions/value chain/SWOT, detailed management functions, financial-market and fintech topics, international entry/trade/FDI/outsourcing, SDGs/ethical dilemmas and several entrepreneurship points. The PESTEL and international-business lessons are general/jurisdiction-neutral, not Bangladesh regulatory lessons.

# 1103 — Business Communication and Report Writing

**Semester:** First Year, First Semester (Semester 1), 3 credits; 11th-batch AIS curriculum (effective from Session 2022–2023).

*Source: University of Barishal, Department of Accounting and Information Systems, “Detailed Syllabus,” PDF pp. 40–41 (printed pp. 41–42), read within the 11th-batch AIS curriculum. The course-list entry is numbered **1103**, while the detailed-syllabus heading prints **103**; title and semester identify the requested course. This appears to be a code/heading omission.*

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Introduction to Communication [ILO 1, ILO 2]:** Communication; communication process/cycle; business communication; objectives and importance; communication situations; communication barriers; ethical, legal, and cultural considerations in business communication; importance of technology in today’s communication.
2. **Types of Communication [ILO 1, ILO 2]:** Downward, upward, and horizontal communication; verbal and non-verbal communication; importance of understanding non-verbal communication; grapevine; process of the grapevine.
3. **Media of Communication [ILO 3, ILO 5]:** Written communication; oral communication; non-verbal communication; audio-visual communication; technology-driven communication.
4. **Written Communication [ILO 3]:** Basic writing issues—word selection and constructing clear sentences and paragraphs; communicating good, neutral, and bad news; different types of business letters; memorandum; written emails.
5. **Report Writing [ILO 3]:** Issues in writing short and long reports; use of graphics; composing a report on a computer; preparing presentation slides for a report.
6. **Oral Communication [ILO 4, ILO 5]:** Informal talking; listening; interviews; meetings; online meetings; telephone conversation; formal speeches; team presentations; presenting reports; videoconferencing.
7. **Audio-visual Communication [ILO 1, ILO 3, ILO 4, ILO 5]:** Types of aids; making audio-visual communication effective; technology-enabled audio-visual communication.
8. **Formal Business Meetings [ILO 4]:** Invitation for a meeting; meeting preparation; memorandum; minutes writing; etiquettes; decision-making process; proposal and resolution.
9. **Getting Prepared for Job Interviews [ILO 6]:** Preparation for facing a job interview; preparation for taking a job interview; CV/Resume preparation.
10. **Citation and Referencing in Academic Writing [ILO 3]:** Introduction to referencing styles.

## Topic-to-video map

Direct watch pages below were opened to verify that each recommendation is an actual YouTube video; exact video titles and channels were checked against the YouTube watch-page metadata. Recommendations are exam aids, not claims that a single video covers the full course.

### Communication foundations, types, and media (Topics 1–3)

- [Introduction to Business Communication 2026 | Business Communication Tutorial | Simplilearn](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=10YgTqd9M9Y) — **Simplilearn**. Covers introductory business communication concepts and foundational process/barrier material; use it to organize definitions and short conceptual answers. It does not establish coverage of the syllabus’s ethics, legal, or cultural issues.
- [Class 12th – Types of Communication | Business Studies](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aHCNR5ISgGY) — **TutorialsPoint**. Covers communication categories including verbal/non-verbal forms; its lesson format helps distinguish types for definition-and-example exam answers.
- [Direction of Communication](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M4VLGlwCs2c) — **GreggU**. Covers downward/upward and lateral flow and formal/informal networks (including the grapevine); the classification is useful for concise comparison questions.
- [What is Grapevine Communication](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xaWzG8pL6Ic) — **Explearning Social Fluency with Mary Daphne**. Explains grapevine communication and its four network patterns, giving concrete material for a short note on the grapevine and its process.
- [Audio Visual Aids –Meaning, Definition & Classification](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MGobKltZtII) — **Vidya-mitra**. Covers meaning and classification of audio-visual aids, supporting the syllabus’s “types of aids” and classification questions.
- [Lecture 49: Use of Visual Aids in Oral Presentation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1yE5_59er2c) — **IIT Roorkee July 2018**. Explains prerequisites and effective use of different visual aids, useful for exam answers about making aided presentations effective.

**NOT COVERED by these verified videos:** communication situations; ethical, legal, and cultural considerations; importance of technology in communication; importance of understanding non-verbal communication; the complete list of media (written/oral/non-verbal/audio-visual/technology-driven) as a comparative lesson; and technology-enabled audio-visual communication. Search YouTube for: `business communication ethical legal cultural issues lecture`, `importance of technology in business communication lecture`, `nonverbal communication importance business lecture`, `media of communication written oral nonverbal audiovisual lesson`, `technology enabled audiovisual communication lecture`.

### Written communication and reports (Topics 4–5)

- [How to Write a Business Letter](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=egeyiUpFsaw) — **Catlin Tucker**. Demonstrates business-letter format with an example, which is useful for reproducing letter structure in a practical/written exam.
- [Types of Business Letters](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pkldlXjZH90) — **GreggU**. Reviews letter categories for classification and “write/identify the appropriate letter” questions.
- [Episode 57: Conveying Bad News in Business Writing](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F2p9L4QLQEA) — **Alanis Business Academy**. Compares written examples and explains why one is preferable, helping with tactful bad-news message framing.
- [How to write a business report |  | UPV](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GQYeDvtMydc) — **Universitat Politècnica de València - UPV**. Gives guidance on business-report composition, useful for report structure and written-answer planning.
- [Report Writing Part 1 - Headings and Table of Contents | Microsoft Word Tutorials](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VFMPLeC7h0I) — **Lisa Doe**. Demonstrates report formatting in Word, including headings and contents, helping with the syllabus’s practical “compose a report on the computer” skill.

**NOT COVERED:** neutral- and good-news messages; the full range of business-letter types beyond the introductory lesson; memorandum; written email; short-versus-long report distinctions; use of graphics in reports; presentation slides specifically for a report. Search YouTube for: `neutral and good news messages business writing examples`, `business memo format writing lesson`, `professional email writing business communication lecture`, `short and long business reports differences lecture`, `graphics in business reports charts tables lecture`, `create presentation slides for business report tutorial`.

### Oral communication, audiovisual communication, meetings, interviews, and academic referencing (Topics 6–10)

- [Active Listening Skills](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ra6L83XwLko) — **Alexander Lyon Communication Coach**. Covers listening barriers and active, comprehensive, and empathetic listening, giving useful concepts and practice for listening questions.
- [4 Types of Public Speaking](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9HMln8BAh5E) — **Alexander Lyon Communication Coach**. Distinguishes four speech types and their preparation/delivery, helping frame formal-speech answers.
- [How To Write An Awesome Meeting Agenda (Tutorial & Template)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GAJV5hyrCD4) — **The KAM Club**. Demonstrates a stepwise agenda and template, which helps answer meeting-preparation questions.
- [How To Write Meeting Minutes In English](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JJIiHeEd4ww) — **Derek Callan - English for Professionals**. Teaches common wording and phrases for minutes with examples, useful for a practical minutes-writing question.
- [FREE Job Interview Preparation Course: Interview Tips and Sample Answers](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y0gIQs40KOo) — **Greg Langstaff**. Provides interview preparation and sample answers, useful for rehearsing likely interview questions and structuring responses.
- [Stand Out in a Job Interview | The Harvard Business Review Guide](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZU9x1vFx5lI) — **Harvard Business Review**. Offers expert preparation strategies that help turn interview advice into concise, defensible written responses.
- [The Basics of Citing and Referencing in APA 7th Edition](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hKu2-tjNMok) — **Smart Student**. Demonstrates in-text citations and reference-list links in APA 7; useful for applying one concrete referencing style. APA is only one style, not a Bangladesh-specific or syllabus-prescribed exclusive style.

**NOT COVERED:** informal talking; interviews as a broad oral-communication form beyond job-interview preparation; meetings as an oral interaction; online meetings; telephone conversation; team presentations; presenting reports; videoconferencing; technology-driven/technology-enabled AV interaction; formal meeting invitation; meeting memorandum; meeting etiquette; meeting decision-making; proposal and resolution; preparation for taking/conducting an interview (interviewer side); CV/Resume preparation; and referencing styles other than APA 7. Search YouTube for: `informal communication workplace oral communication lecture`, `online meeting telephone conversation videoconferencing business communication lesson`, `team presentation and presenting reports communication skills lesson`, `formal business meeting invitation memorandum etiquette lecture`, `meeting decision making proposal resolution business meeting lesson`, `how to conduct a job interview interviewer preparation tutorial`, `CV resume writing university career center tutorial`, `MLA Chicago Harvard citation referencing styles lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**10 numbered syllabus topics. 18 unique videos mapped.** Verified watch pages were opened for all listed videos, and title/channel identity was checked. Every syllabus topic has at least one relevant verified lesson recommendation, but **not every syllabus subtopic has video coverage**: uncovered elements are explicitly marked above with targeted YouTube search phrases. No Bangladesh-specific law, tax, VAT, or regulatory syllabus content is present in this course outline.

# 1104 Mathematics for Business Decisions–I

**Semester:** Semester 1 — First Year, First Semester (11th-batch AIS curriculum; Session 2022–2023 onward)  
**Credit:** 3

*Source and scope:* University of Barishal, Department of Accounting & Information Systems, BBA 11th-batch curriculum: course list (printed p. 34) and “Detailed Course Contents” for 1104 (printed pp. 41–44). This is the AIS curriculum’s 1104 entry only, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum. Its detailed-syllabus heading and semester course list agree on code and title. Minor source typos include “f77ixes cost” (read as “fixed cost”) and “correspondingbusiness”; the course list uses a hyphen while the detailed heading uses an en dash in “Decisions–I.”

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Theory of Numbers.** Introduction to the real-number system; definitions and concepts of rational, irrational, integer, decimal, prime, composite and related numbers; basic properties; proof of irrationality of a number.

2. **Equations and Inequalities.**
   - **2.1 Equations and linear systems:** definition and concepts of equations; linear equations; systems of linear equations; sketching their graphs; solving systems by algebraic methods, substitution, cross-multiplication, determinants, graphing, etc.; existence and uniqueness of solutions.
   - **2.2 Quadratics:** definition and concepts of quadratic equations; finding solutions/roots; discriminants and nature of roots; relationship between roots and coefficients; forming quadratic and higher-order equations from given roots.
   - **2.3 Nonlinear equations and applications:** basic concepts of nonlinear equations; systems of nonlinear equations and their solutions; sketching linear and nonlinear curves; applying equations and systems to business and economics problems.
   - **2.4 Inequalities:** definition, concepts and properties; linear, nonlinear and absolute-value inequalities; solving inequalities and representing solutions graphically on the real line.

3. **Applied Linear Equations.** Different forms of linear equations; budget equation and rate of substitution; slope and intercept and their business/economic meaning; cost equation and fixed, variable, total, average and marginal cost; revenue and profit functions, break-even point and shutdown condition; forming a linear cost equation from marginal and fixed cost or from two points; demand and supply equations, equilibrium point and shifts; piecewise linear functions and business/economic applications.

4. **Theory of Sets.** Set definitions and concepts, types and representations; Venn diagrams; union, intersection, difference and complement with properties; De Morgan’s laws; number of elements in sets; Cartesian (cross) product; business/economic applications.

5. **Mathematical Induction, Series and Sequences.** Sequence and series definitions/concepts; sigma (summation) notation; introduction to mathematical induction; sum of natural numbers; nth term and sum to n terms of a finite series.

6. **Progressions.**
   - **Arithmetic progression (AP):** introduction; first term and common difference; formulas for nth term and sum to n terms; mathematical problems and business/economic applications.
   - **Geometric progression (GP):** introduction; first term and common ratio; formulas for nth term and sum to n terms; proof of annuity formulas; mathematical problems and business/economic applications.

7. **Indices and Logarithms.**
   - **Indices:** definition and laws; computation with integer, fractional, positive and negative indices; surds and operations with surds; related mathematical problems.
   - **Logarithms:** definition and properties; common and natural logarithms; logarithm laws; antilogarithms; related mathematical problems.

8. **Permutations and Combinations.**
   - **Permutations:** counting concepts, factorial notation and permutation; principles and theorems; permutation of 0 and 1; arrangements of different and repeated things; circular and restricted permutations; mathematical problems.
   - **Combinations:** definition/concept; principles and theorems; relationship between permutation and combination; combinations of different things, with repeated things and restricted combinations; mathematical problems.

## Topic-to-video map

Every link below was opened as a YouTube **watch** page. Titles and channels are transcribed from the page; descriptions/page context were used to check lesson relevance. “NOT COVERED” means no verified direct lesson is mapped here for that syllabus item.

### 1. Theory of Numbers

- [Understand & Identify Rational and Irrational Numbers](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y9ImfFdtkY8) — **Math and Science**. Covers identifying rational versus irrational numbers in the real-number system; useful for classification questions and explaining why a number belongs to one class. The opened page does not establish coverage of the complete integer/decimal/prime/composite list or all their properties. **NOT COVERED:** prime/composite and integer/decimal classification as a complete set; search `real number system integers decimals prime composite classification examples`.
- [Proof: Square Root of 2 is Irrational](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2FlMkP7jqQo) — **Wrath of Math**. Gives a contradiction proof using a fraction in lowest terms, making it useful for writing a clear exam proof of irrationality; it demonstrates √2 specifically, not every irrationality proof. For extensions, search `proof square root of prime number irrational`.

### 2. Equations and Inequalities

- [Solving Systems of Equations By Elimination & Substitution With 2 Variables](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oKqtgz2eo-Y) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Worked elimination and substitution examples help rehearse two named algebraic solution methods and present workings clearly. **NOT COVERED:** cross-multiplication method; search `simultaneous linear equations cross multiplication method worked examples`.
- [Solving Systems of Equations By Graphing](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Pd4hwS8qHms) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Shows graph intersection as the solution, useful for the graph-sketching and graphical-solution exam steps.
- [Cramer's Rule - 2x2 Linear System](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vXqlIOX2itM) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Works a two-variable system using determinants, giving practice in the syllabus’s determinant method.
- [One Solution, No Solution, or Infinitely Many Solutions - Consistent & Inconsistent Systems](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bq5gDsEdN3Q) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Distinguishes unique, absent and infinite solution cases and consistent/inconsistent systems, directly supporting existence/uniqueness written answers.
- [How To Determine The Discriminant of a Quadratic Equation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6fU9ZgwbU74) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Explains how the discriminant determines the number and type of roots, suitable for short theory answers and classifying roots. **NOT COVERED:** a full worked method for finding roots and constructing quadratic/higher-order equations from supplied roots; search `solve quadratic equation roots and form equation given roots higher order`.
- [How To Find The Sum and Product of the Roots of a Quadratic Equation - Algebra](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kBSj35rik8w) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Worked examples make the relationship of the roots to the coefficients easier to recall and apply in exam calculations.
- [Solving Non-Linear Simultaneous Equations (Line and Parabola)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FXKN8KUwEWM) — **Smart Alex Mathematics**. Demonstrates solving a nonlinear system involving a line and quadratic function, useful for showing algebraic working and checking intersection solutions. **NOT COVERED:** systematic sketching of nonlinear curves and business/economic applications of nonlinear systems; search `nonlinear curve sketching systems business economics worked problems`.
- [Solving Absolute Value Equations and Inequalities - Number Line & Interval Notation - Algebra](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3LN0IDooaIE) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Works absolute-value inequality cases and number-line/interval answers, useful for practicing solution-set presentation. **NOT COVERED:** a complete lesson focused on general linear and nonlinear inequalities and their real-line graphs beyond absolute-value cases; search `linear nonlinear inequalities solve graph number line examples`.

### 3. Applied Linear Equations

- [Business Calculus: Linear Functions & Applications, Supply & Demand (1.2) | Math with Professor V](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cXJZexUq5G0) — **Math with Professor V**. The page description explicitly covers linear functions, cost, fixed and marginal costs, supply/demand equations, revenue, profit and equilibrium; its examples help frame the business meanings of slope/intercept and apply linear functions. **NOT COVERED:** budget equation/rate of substitution, equilibrium shifts, shutdown condition and piecewise-linear functions; search `budget line rate of substitution piecewise linear function business math`, `supply demand shifts equilibrium linear equations`, and `shutdown condition cost revenue business math`.
- [Cost, Revenue, Profit Equations and Break Even Point](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_wHuj937LS4) — **Mario's Math Tutoring**. Builds cost, revenue and profit equations from a word problem and calculates break-even, useful for exam-style equation setup and worked calculations. **NOT COVERED:** average-cost treatment is not established by the page description; search `average cost total cost output business mathematics worked examples`.

### 4. Theory of Sets

- [Intersection of Sets, Union of Sets and Venn Diagrams](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xZELQc11ACY) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Clearly demonstrates union and intersection using Venn diagrams, useful for set notation and diagram questions.
- [Set Operations in Discrete Mathematics || Union || Intersection || Difference || Complement || DMS](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0SJAMg1bD9I) — **Sundeep Saradhi Kanthety**. Its watch-page title identifies union, intersection, difference and complement, supporting notation/computation practice for these named operations.
- [Set Theory :DeMorgan's law : Written Proof (Part 1)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ro1yGNEFSaE) — **MathsSmart**. Gives a written proof of De Morgan’s law, valuable for reproducing a law-based proof rather than only memorizing a diagram.
- **NOT COVERED:** full set-type/representation survey, set properties beyond the operations above, counting elements/cardinality, Cartesian product and business/economic set applications; search `types representations of sets cardinality number of elements Cartesian product business applications`.

### 5. Mathematical Induction, Series and Sequences

- [Mathematical Induction Practice Problems](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tHNVX3e9zd0) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. The page identifies an induction tutorial with multiple practice examples, useful for rehearsing proof structure and algebraic steps. **NOT COVERED:** an explicitly verified induction proof of the sum of natural numbers; search `prove sum of first n natural numbers by mathematical induction`.
- [Sigma Notation and Summation Notation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xavgv1m9feE) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. The description covers sigma-notation summation problems and sums of arithmetic and geometric sequences, useful for translating notation and checking finite-sum calculations. The syllabus’s general nth-term and finite-series formulations are reinforced by the AP/GP lessons below.

### 6. Progressions

- [Arithmetic Sequences and Arithmetic Series - Basic Introduction](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XZJdyPkCxuE) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Covers nth terms, arithmetic-series sums and word problems, which is strong practice for identifying the first term/common difference and applying AP formulas. **NOT COVERED:** a specifically business/economics AP application; search `arithmetic progression business economics application worked problem`.
- [Geometric Series and Geometric Sequences - Basic Introduction](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zRKZ0-kOUZM) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Covers common ratio, nth term and partial geometric sums with worked examples, useful for formula selection and calculation practice.
- [Geometric Annuities | Exam FM | Financial Mathematics Lesson 16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9BlqZOIeRt4) — **JK Math**. Exam-FM financial mathematics treatment works geometric annuity valuation, connecting a GP to a business-finance calculation. **NOT COVERED:** proof/derivation of the annuity formula itself; search `derive prove annuity formula geometric progression`.

### 7. Indices and Logarithms

- [Indices, Logarithm and Surds | Mathematics](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wwEkTK-K23A) — **FlashLearners**. The opened page describes a lesson with detailed explanations and solved questions and chapter markers for index laws, logarithm laws and surd laws; this is useful for rule recall and practice with the syllabus’s three connected calculation areas. **NOT COVERED/UNCERTAIN:** the watch-page evidence does not confirm common versus natural logs or antilogarithm calculations individually; search `common natural logarithms antilogarithms solved problems`, and for index subcases `integer fractional positive negative indices surds examples`.

### 8. Permutations and Combinations

- [Permutations and Combinations Tutorial](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJnIdRXUi7A) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Explains the order-matters distinction and includes counting-principle word problems, making it useful for selecting a formula and practicing exam-style counting setups.
- [Permutation | Circular Permutation || Repeated Objects](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DznNZyhIub4) — **Mathuklasan with Sir Ram**. Its lesson title explicitly covers circular arrangements and repeated objects, the two less-routine permutation types that need separate formula practice. **NOT COVERED:** permutation of 0 and 1 as a named syllabus case, restricted permutations, and repeated/restricted combinations; search `permutation of 0 and 1 restricted permutations examples`, `combinations with repetition restricted combinations worked problems`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 8 major numbered syllabus topics. **Verified video count:** 23 unique YouTube watch-page links; no duplicate links. **Major-topic coverage:** every one of the 8 topics has at least one verified, relevant video. **Subtopic completeness:** not complete at the individual-skill level; every gap or watch-page uncertainty is explicitly marked **NOT COVERED** above with targeted search terms. The algebra videos are general international math instruction rather than University of Barishal exam recordings; no Bangladesh-specific law, tax, VAT or regulatory content appears in this mathematics course.

# 1105 — History of the Emergence of Bangladesh

**Semester:** First Year, First Semester (Semester 1), 11th-batch AIS curriculum, effective from Session 2022–2023 onward.

The 11th-batch course table (PDF p. 35) and detailed-syllabus heading (PDF p. 45) both identify 1105 by this title and semester; there is no material code/title mismatch. The course table lists **4 credits**, while the curriculum overview says course units carry 3 credits. The detailed contents below follow the 11th-batch AIS detailed-syllabus section (PDF pp. 44–45), not the earlier Management Studies curriculum. A few line-break/character artifacts occur in the source; “Al-Sham77s” appears in the text as printed/extracted and is normalized below to the familiar spelling **Al‑Shams**.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Introduction to Bangladesh**
   - **1.1** Geographical features.
   - **1.2** Ancient Janapadas.
   - **1.3** Ethnic composition.
   - **1.4** Indigenous communities.
   - **1.5** Religious process in Bengal.
   - **1.6** General overview of the economy, culture, and heritage.

2. **History of Bengal up to the Partition of India**
   - **2.1** From the Pre-Historic Period to the Guptas.
   - **2.2** Sasanka.
   - **2.3** The Palas and the Senas.
   - **2.4** The Conquest of Bakhtiyar.
   - **2.5** The Independent Bengal Sultanate (1338–1538).
   - **2.6** The growth of the Nawabs and the Battle of Palashi.
   - **2.7** Initial resistances to British rule.
   - **2.8** The Partition of Bengal in 1905 and subsequent nationalist movements.
   - **2.9** Politics in Bengal, 1937–47.
   - **2.10** The Lahore Resolution, 1940.
   - **2.11** The United Bengal Scheme.
   - **2.12** The Partition of India in 1947.

3. **Growth of Opposition, Language Movements & Attempts to Build a Democratic Government**
   - **3.1** Pakistan’s early years: state formation, bureaucratic influence, and economic, social, and cultural discrimination.
   - **3.2** Foundation of the Awami League, 1949.
   - **3.3** Language Movement of 1948 and 1952.
   - **3.4** Election of 1954 and the United Front.
   - **3.5** Constitution of 1956.

4. **Declaration of Military Rule, the Growth of Bengali Nationalism & Quest for Autonomy**
   - **4.1** Military rule in 1958 and notable measures of Ayub Khan.
   - **4.2** Constitution of 1962.
   - **4.3** Six Point Programme of the Awami League.
   - **4.4** Agartala Conspiracy Case.
   - **4.5** 11 Points Movement.
   - **4.6** Mass Upsurge of 1969 and Yahya Khan’s rise to power.
   - **4.7** Election of 1970.

5. **Liberation War of 1971**
   - **5.1** The 7 March Address of Bangabandhu.
   - **5.2** Non-cooperation Movement.
   - **5.3** Quest for a constitutional solution and Operation Searchlight.
   - **5.4** Declaration of Independence and the Mujibnagar Government.
   - **5.5** Sporadic and organized resistance (Mukti Fauj, Mukti Bahini, and guerrilla warfare).
   - **5.6** Genocide by the Pakistan Army and its local collaborators: Peace Committee, Al-Badr, Al-Shams, Razakar Bahini, and pro-Pakistani political parties.
   - **5.7** Role of the Muslim world and superpowers.

6. **The Regime of Bangabandhu (1972–1975)**
   - **6.1** Bangabandhu’s homecoming.
   - **6.2** State formation.
   - **6.3** Constitution of 1972 and subsequent amendments.
   - **6.4** Economic reconstruction.
   - **6.5** Global recognition of Bangladesh and the efforts of Bangabandhu.
   - **6.6** The killing of Bangabandhu and the ideological transition.

## Topic-to-video map

Each link below is a direct YouTube watch page opened to check its displayed title, channel, and relevance. Where a video only gives an overview or covers one part of a compound syllabus item, that limitation is stated rather than treating it as full coverage.

1. **[Geography Now! Bangladesh](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ljebhxk_9ys)** — **Geography Now**. **Topic:** 1.1, Bangladesh’s physical/geographical profile. Its country-profile format supplies a useful map-and-features refresher before writing a short descriptive answer, though it is not a Bangladesh university exam lecture.

2. **[Ancient & Medieval Bengal | Important Dynasties & Key Facts | WBCS 2026–27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VEjqqoLLb_Y)** — **Parcham PSC**. **Topics:** 2.1 (Gupta-period portion only), 2.2, and 2.3. The channel describes it as a complete revision/one-shot on ancient and medieval Bengal; the page summary names Gupta rule, Gauda/Shashanka, and the ancient dynastic frame, making it useful for chronology and recall. **Jurisdiction note:** WBCS is a West Bengal exam framing, not the Bangladesh syllabus; use it for the shared Bengal-history material and check Bangladesh-specific context separately.

3. **[When was Bengal First Partitioned? Not in 1947](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s0yPGSJuc3c)** — **The Quint**. **Topic:** 2.8, specifically the 1905 partition and its divide-and-rule context; it does **not** establish coverage of all subsequent nationalist movements. The concise explainer helps distinguish the 1905 partition from the 1947 partition in a written answer.

4. **[Bangladesh Studies | Lecture 1- History of Bangladesh from 1947 to 1972 | FR | BBA|](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QDnAfFtU5vg)** — **University of Scholars**. **Topics:** broad chronological context for 2.12 and the post-1947-to-1972 span of Units 3–6, not a verified substitute for each named subtopic. Its university BBA class format is useful for organizing a long-answer narrative; confirm all event details from the course materials.

5. **[History of the Rise of Independent Bangladesh | Timeline of 1947–1971 | History of the Rise of Ba...](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6hX3DryTEp0)** — **Moynul Islam Shah**. **Topics:** 2.12; 3.3 (1952 movement only, not 1948); 3.4; 4.4; 4.6; 4.7; and 5.1. The watch-page description explicitly names the 1947 partition, 1952 Language Movement, 1954 United Front, Agartala Case, 1969 mass uprising, 1970 election, and 7 March speech, so it is particularly useful for timeline-based recall and linking milestones. The displayed title itself ends in an ellipsis.

6. **[The Historic Six Point Movement | The Six Points: The Charter of Freedom for Bengalis Proposed by...](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Yqew6lJNUJ8)** — **Moynul Islam Shah**. **Topic:** 4.3. The description identifies the 1966 Six Point Movement and demand; use it to memorize and explain the programme’s demands in a short-answer response. The displayed title itself ends in an ellipsis.

7. **[The landmark March 7, 1971 speech by Bangabandhu Sheikh Mujibur Rahman, in our #GreatSpeeches series](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EqLHavO0HpQ)** — **ThePrint**. **Topic:** 5.1. The page frames the address in its Great Speeches series and explains its independence context; it helps build an answer around the speech’s historical significance, but it is not a full lecture on the Liberation War.

8. **[1971: Bangladesh Liberation War And The Bloody Road To Independence](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IfRcK26PBYo)** — **Timeline – World History Documentaries**. **Topic:** Unit 5’s overall 1971 war context only; no individual 5.2–5.7 detail is credited from the page information. This documentary is useful for visualizing the conflict’s broad chronology and consequences before writing an essay, not as a substitute for Bangladesh-specific named actors, operations, or diplomatic detail.

## Coverage audit

**43 syllabus subtopics** were itemized. At least one opened, relevant watch-page video directly supports **10** subtopics; **3** receive only partial coverage (2.1, 2.8, 3.3); the other **30** lack direct verified video coverage in this set. Therefore, **not every topic has a verified video**. Broad 1947–72/1971 videos are labeled as context only and do not close subtopic gaps. No direct lesson was found/verified for most early Bengal, Pakistan constitutional, 1971 operation/collaborator/diplomatic, or 1972–75 topics; the search phrases above are for targeted follow-up, not implied recommendations.

# Semester 2 — First Year, Second Semester

# 1201 Intermediate Accounting

**Semester:** Semester 2 — First Year, Second Semester (11th-batch AIS curriculum, session 2022–2023 onward).

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

Transcribed from the 11th-batch AIS curriculum’s **Detailed Syllabus**, under **FIRST YEAR SECOND SEMESTER**, heading **1201: Intermediate Accounting** (printed syllabus pp. 46–47; PDF pages 46–47). This uses the AIS detailed-syllabus section, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

1. **Cash and Receivables** — Nature of cash; reporting of cash: cash equivalents, restricted cash, bank overdrafts; cash control; differences in amount between cash book and bank statement; revenue under **IFRS 15**; nature and classifications of receivables; recognition of accounts receivable using gross and net method; valuation of accounts receivable using direct write-off and allowance method; recognition of notes receivable issued and not issued at face value, with interest-bearing and not-interest-bearing notes; valuation of notes receivable; derecognition and transfer of receivables: sale with and without guarantee and secured borrowing.
2. **Inventories** — Definition and classification of inventories; goods and costs included in inventory; cost-flow assumptions: specific identification, average-cost and first-in, first-out (**FIFO**); effect of inventory errors; lower-of-cost-or-net-realizable-value (**LCNRV**); methods of applying LCNRV; use of an allowance; recovery of inventory loss; gross-profit method of estimating inventory; retail inventory method; **IAS 2**.
3. **Property, Plant and Equipment (PPE)** — Nature and characteristics of PPE; acquisition of PPE; self-constructed assets; interest costs during construction; qualifying assets; valuation of PPE; exchanges of non-monetary assets; costs subsequent to acquisition; disposition of PPE; depreciation; impairments; depletion; revaluations; non-current assets held for sale and discontinued operations; **IAS 16, IAS 23, IAS 36 and IFRS 5**.
4. **Intangible Assets** — Nature and characteristics of intangible assets; valuation of purchased and internally created intangibles; amortization of intangibles; types of intangible assets; impairment of limited-life intangibles; reversal of impairment loss; impairment of goodwill; research and development costs; accounting for R&D activities; costs similar to R&D costs; **IAS 38 and IAS 36**.
5. **Accounting for Lease** — Nature, economic substance and advantages of lease transactions; lessee accounting; measurement of the lease liability and right-of-use asset; lessor accounting; classification of leases by the lessor; accounting measurement and presentation; special lease accounting problems; **IFRS 16**.
6. **Statement of Cash Flows** — Fundamental principle; presentation of the statement of cash flows; reporting cash flows from operating, investing and financing activities; reporting cash flows on a net basis; components of cash and cash equivalents; other disclosures; **IAS 7**.
7. **Investment Property** — Definition; recognition; measurement using the fair value model or cost model; transfers to or from investment-property classification; disposal; disclosure; **IAS 40**.

**Source-text discrepancies:** The course objective says students will account for changes in accounting policies and estimates and errors, but the detailed contents list no corresponding topic. The PPE contents tag repeats “[ILO 6]” in “[ILO 5, ILO 6, ILO 6, ILO 9].” The lease text calls the right-of-use asset “ROE” once; this fragment uses the standard abbreviation **ROU**. The course title, code and semester heading agree.

- **Topic count:** 7 numbered detailed-syllabus headings.
- **Verified video count:** 10 unique direct YouTube watch pages; title, channel and lesson relevance were checked on the watch page for every recommended video.
- **Heading coverage:** each of the 7 headings has at least one verified relevant lesson. **Complete detailed-subtopic coverage is not achieved**; the explicitly marked NOT COVERED lists identify gaps and give targeted YouTube search terms. No Bangladesh-specific law, tax, VAT or regulatory topic appears in this course’s detailed contents. The IFRS 16 lesson includes a US GAAP comparison, which is supplemental rather than a substitute for IFRS 16.

# 1202 Microeconomics — Semester 2 (First Year, Second Semester)

**Programme/curriculum:** BBA, Accounting & Information Systems (AIS), University of Barishal, 11th-batch curriculum (effective from 2022–2023). The course table and detailed-syllabus heading both identify **1202 Microeconomics** in First Year, Second Semester. This fragment uses the AIS detailed course contents, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Basic Concepts and Ideas:** Definition and relevance; positive and normative economics; scarcity; opportunity cost; production possibilities frontier; preliminary introduction to the great schools of economic thought—Classical, Neo-Classical, Keynesian, Marxist; difference between microeconomics and macroeconomics.
2. **Demand, Supply and Equilibrium Analysis:** Law of demand; ceteris paribus assumption; demand equation; shift of the demand curve; movement along the demand curve; law of supply; supply equation; shift of the supply curve; movement along the supply curve; market equilibrium.
3. **Market Regulation:** Market regulation; price ceiling; price floor; quota; tax; consumer surplus; producer surplus; changes in consumer and producer surplus after regulation.
4. **Elasticity:** Elasticity of demand; factors affecting demand elasticity; price elasticity and its determinants; computation and interpretation of price elasticity of demand (PED); point and arc elasticity; income elasticity of demand; cross-price elasticity of demand; elasticity of supply; computation and interpretation; determinants of supply elasticity.
5. **Utility Theory:** **Utility approach**—basic assumptions; marginal, average and total utility and their relationship; marginal utility and the law of diminishing marginal utility; law of equi-marginal utility. **Indifference-curve approach**—basic assumptions; characteristics of an indifference curve; indifference curves and map; budget line and its properties; marginal rate of substitution (MRS) and price ratio; price, income and substitution effects; shape of the demand curve for normal, inferior and Giffen commodity types.
6. **Theory of Production and Cost:** Factors of production; production function; law of diminishing returns; “Isocosts and Isoguant”; rate of technical substitution; cost and optimization of production level; short-run production function and law of variable proportion; internal and external economies and diseconomies of scale; long-run production function and law of returns to scale; total and marginal product; variable and fixed costs; average, marginal and total costs and shapes of various cost curves; short-run and long-run cost curves; relationship between marginal cost and price level.
7. **Market Structure and Determination of Equilibrium Price and Output Levels:** Definition and structure of market; characteristics of different markets; perfect market and imperfectly competitive markets (monopoly, duopoly, oligopoly and monopolistic markets); determination of equilibrium price and output in different markets; price discrimination and distortions; antitrust laws.
8. **Theory of Distribution: Factors of Production and Factor Pricing:** Wages, rent, interest and profit; theories of factor pricing; wage determination according to neoclassical theory.

**Syllabus wording note:** The production-and-cost line prints “Isocosts and Isoguant” (singular/misspelling as supplied); it is retained faithfully rather than silently corrected. The code/title/semester for this course agree between the 11th-batch course list and its detailed entry.

## Topic-to-video map

Every video below was checked by opening its individual `youtube.com/watch?v=...` page; title and channel are as displayed on that page. “NOT COVERED” means no direct lesson was verified for that precise syllabus item in this search, not that the topic is unimportant.

### 1. Basic Concepts and Ideas

- [Scarcity, Opportunity Cost, Trade-Offs & The Production Possibilities Curve](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lY8Lk2hB0qY) — **Dr. Lodge McCammon**. Covers scarcity, opportunity cost/trade-offs and the production possibilities curve; its graph and trade-off examples are useful for short conceptual answers.
- [Normative and positive statements | Basic economics concepts | AP Macroeconomics | Khan Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YtX6SGw7E3c) — **Khan Academy**. Directly distinguishes positive from normative claims, helping with definition-and-example questions.
- [The Most Important Economic Schools of Thought | Economics Explained](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o6UXRZ2XwgU) — **Economics Explained**. A direct schools-of-thought overview useful for review framing, but use it as a survey, not as a substitute for syllabus-specific comparisons.
- [Economic Schools of Thought: Crash Course Economics #14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tZvjh1dxz08) — **CrashCourse**. Its watch-page description identifies Keynesianism and other schools, including socialism/communism, Chicago and Austrian schools; it offers a second conceptual comparison, but the named syllabus list is not identical.

**NOT COVERED:** A verified lesson directly comparing the definition/relevance of microeconomics with macroeconomics, and an exam-ready comparison of all four exact syllabus schools (Classical, Neo-Classical, Keynesian and Marxist). Search: `microeconomics vs macroeconomics definition relevance lecture`; `Classical Neo-Classical Keynesian Marxist schools of economic thought comparison`.

The page description explicitly covers the laws of demand and supply, demand/supply shifters, shortage/surplus and changes in equilibrium; use it to revise curve shifts versus market adjustment and equilibrium.
