- [Theory of Distribution Or Theory Factor Pricing(marginal productivity theory)(land)(labour)(capital)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nN2kpvQ3LX8) — **Research Methodology Advanced Tools**. The watch page identifies this as a factor-pricing/distribution lesson and tags land, labour, capital and marginal-productivity theory; it is a narrow exam-topic match, but a relatively small, low-view channel, so cross-check terminology against course notes.

**NOT COVERED:** A verified treatment of separate wage, rent, interest and profit theories and wage determination specifically according to neoclassical theory was not established by this page. Search: `neoclassical theory of distribution wage rent interest profit factor pricing lecture`; `neoclassical wage determination marginal productivity worked example`.

## Coverage audit

**Eight** numbered detailed-syllabus topic groups were extracted. Each group has relevant, individually opened YouTube watch pages, but **not every listed subtopic has a direct verified video**: the explicit NOT COVERED items above are the gaps/uncertainties, notably equation-solving, elasticity determinants/point-arc methods, utility assumptions and full Giffen treatment, isoquant/isocost optimization and cost-curve relations, non-monopoly imperfect-market equilibrium, Bangladesh antitrust law, and the full factor-income/neoclassical wage block. The recommendations are English-language general microeconomics except where flagged; no Bangladesh-specific tax/VAT syllabus content appears in this course entry. The video count is **22 unique watch links**.

# 1203 Management — First Year, Second Semester

**Curriculum:** BBA AIS, University of Barishal, 11th batch (session 2022–2023 onward). The course list identifies 1203 as **Management** in First Year, Second Semester; it is a 3-credit course. This fragment follows that AIS course-list entry and the matching **1203: Management** entry in the Detailed Syllabus (PDF pp. 35, 50–51), not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Meaning and significance of management:** Definition; nature; principles and techniques of management; administration versus management; management skills, effectiveness and efficiency; management as a resource and as a profession; sub-areas such as operations, finance and marketing; scope across business organizations (proprietorships and corporations), government organizations, NGOs, religious organizations, etc.; theories of management.
2. **Planning:** Meaning and importance; steps; setting strategic plans; factors in formulating a sound plan; types of plans; need for long-range plans; setting vision, mission, goals and objectives; steps in planning; planning by business and non-business firms in Bangladesh; planning environment; managerial decision making.
3. **Organizing:** Meaning and importance; organization structure; organization charts and manuals; departmentation and its basis; delegation; centralization and decentralization; need for decentralization; conditions of decentralization; committee system, objectives and benefits of committees, committee types; organizing practices in Bangladesh.
4. **Directing and leading:** Nature of directing and leading; features of good directing; elements and benefits of consultative directing; leadership and leadership theories; leadership styles of Bangladesh managers; managing small groups.
5. **Motivation:** Meaning and significance; tools of motivation; what managers, employees and workers want from jobs; result-based reward; measurement of human motivation; motivation theories; applicability of Maslow’s model in Bangladesh; need-based and process-based motivation theories.
6. **Controlling:** Meaning and significance; features of a good control system; setting control standards; reporting and feedback; budgetary control system; budget as a motivation tool; Management by Objectives (MBO); Management by Exceptions (MBE); behavioral aspects of control; Management Information System (MIS); production and operation control.
7. **Coordination:** Meaning, significance and tools of coordination.
8. **Performance evaluation of managers:** Evaluation of managers’ performance and its necessity; objective and judgmental methods; judgmental errors; feedback; ACR; promotion of managers; criteria of a good promotion system; management audit.
9. **Management development:** Necessity; training and development; benefits; on-the-job and off-the-job training; higher assignments.
10. **Organizational development (OD) and managing change:** OD; quality management; internal and external environmental changes; resistance to change; managing change; managers’ economic, social and environmental responsibilities; globalization and new challenges; managers in the free-market-economy context.
11. **Managers and the international environment:** Comparative management; the Farmer–Richman model and a second model printed as “Esatphen Model”; management of international business; theories about human nature: Theory X, Theory Y and Theory Z.
12. **Management, management education and management development in Bangladesh:** Management in SOEs, NCBs, insurance corporations, private-sector enterprises, NGOs, MNCs and small business; educational institutes, hotels and hospitals. Management education in the country, including university Business Administration programs, BBA and MBA; management-development institutes in Bangladesh: BIM, BIBM, IBB and IPM.
13. **Contemporary issues in management:** Change management, knowledge management, event management, social responsibility and related issues.

## Topic-to-video map

Each linked recommendation below was opened on its YouTube watch page to check the displayed title, creator/channel and whether it is a relevant lesson. Coverage descriptions are intentionally limited to what the watch page title/description or lesson framing supports; a video is not claimed to cover an entire unit merely because its topic is adjacent.

### 1. Meaning, significance and theories of management

- [What is management? Concept of Management, Levels of management #management](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GZ2dmbDmB5I) — **Educationleaves**. Covers the concept/meaning of management, management functions, management levels and managerial skills. Its clear overview gives a compact base for short definitions and distinction-style written answers.
- [Lecture 1 - Evolution of Management Thought](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TsZukmeaewc) — **nptelhrd** (lecture by Prof. K. B. Akhilesh, IISc Bangalore; NPTEL lecture series). Covers evolution of management thought, useful for organizing historical/theoretical material into an exam answer.

**NOT COVERED:** The opened lessons do not establish administration-versus-management, the exact course’s definition/nature/principles/techniques, management as resource/profession, subareas (operations/finance/marketing), the named organizational-scope examples (proprietorships, corporations, government, NGOs, religious organizations), or a comprehensive set of management theories. Search YouTube for `administration versus management principles of management management as profession management resources scope of management`.

### 2. Planning

- [Lecture 3 : Planning - Part 1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o64PKQ2bhDY) — **IIT Roorkee July 2018**. The watch-page description identifies planning characteristics, coverage and short-, medium- and long-term planning, with strategic-planning frameworks and a case study. The lecture framing supports revision of plan types, horizons and strategic planning, rather than a Bangladesh-specific answer.

**NOT COVERED:** The page does not verify each step in the course’s planning sequence, factors for a sound plan, the specific vision/mission/goals/objectives framework, business and non-business planning in Bangladesh, planning environment, or a standalone managerial decision-making process. Search YouTube for `steps in managerial decision making vision mission goals objectives planning Bangladesh business non business firms`.

### 3. Organizing

The video explains organizing business resources; its chapter list includes the organization chart, determinants of structure and forms/bases of departmentalization. This is useful for diagram and classification questions on organizational structure.

**NOT COVERED:** Organization manuals; delegation; centralization; decentralization, its need and conditions; committee-system objectives, benefits and types; Bangladesh organizing practices. Search YouTube for `delegation centralization decentralization committees organization manuals Bangladesh management lecture`.

### 4. Directing and leading

- [Lecture 47 : Leadership Styles of Managers - I](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DTGekoI7Dug) — **IIT Roorkee July 2018**. The organized theory list helps structure comparison answers.

### 5. Motivation

The opened page shows chapters for Maslow, Herzberg, three-needs, Theory X and Y, ERG and Mayo, among others. Its concise theory-by-theory explanations are useful for recall and compare/contrast written answers.

**NOT COVERED:** Job wants of managers/employees/workers; tools of motivation; result-based rewards; measurement of human motivation; Maslow’s applicability specifically in Bangladesh; and a demonstrably complete treatment of the course’s need-based versus process-based classification. Search YouTube for `employee motivation tools result based rewards measuring motivation Maslow Bangladesh need process theories`.

The watch-page description identifies control concepts/processes, monitoring performance and corrective action. This supports a basic control-process answer and helps connect standards, monitoring and correction conceptually.

- [What Is Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR)? | Business: Explained](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZoKihFLCY0s) — **HBS Online**. The description frames CSR as business responsibility beyond profit, including people, planet and society. ### 12. Management, education and development in Bangladesh

**NOT COVERED:** No suitable, watch-page-verified lesson was found for the prescribed Bangladesh sector examples (SOEs, NCBs, insurance corporations, private enterprises, NGOs, MNCs, small businesses, educational institutes, hotels and hospitals), Bangladesh university BBA/MBA management education, or the specific institutes BIM, BIBM, IBB and IPM. General management videos above are not evidence for these local details. Search YouTube for `management in Bangladesh SOE NCB insurance NGO MNC BIM BIBM IBB IPM management education`.

Videos 11 and 13 above also cover, respectively, change management and social responsibility within this unit; their links and exam-use notes are not repeated.

## Coverage audit

There are **13 numbered syllabus units**. All 13 units appear in the map; **12 units have at least one relevant watch-page-verified video**, but that does **not** mean all their subtopics are covered. Unit 12 has no verified direct lesson. Important gaps remain across Bangladesh-specific practices, the listed comparative-management model names, specific control/appraisal techniques, and the named contemporary-management subtopics. **No unit’s full detailed subtopic list is completely video-covered.**

**Source/matching note:** PDF p. 35 places 1203 Management under First Year, Second Semester; the Detailed Syllabus’s matching entry begins p. 50. The syllabus prints “Delgation,” “Decetralisation,” “Farmer Richman Moden,” and “Esatphen Model”; obvious spelling errors were normalized in the outline, but the uncertain model label is retained as printed rather than guessed. The course identifier/title was matched using the AIS list and Detailed Syllabus, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

# 1204 Mathematics for Business Decisions–II

**Semester:** Semester 2 — First Year, Second Semester (AIS 11th-batch curriculum)

**Syllabus source and discrepancy note.** University of Barishal, BBA Accounting & Information Systems syllabus, *Detailed Course Contents*, printed p. 52 (PDF page approximately 35 onward). I matched the AIS course by title and code, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum. The detailed contents jump from heading **2** to 3–6: no heading 1 appears in this course block. The listed course content is reproduced below; I have not inferred a missing section. The source also prints “Indefinite Integral and Indefinite Integral” and “Analysis of Total Concept by Integration”; the former is a literal repeated phrase and the latter appears anomalous, so both are retained rather than silently corrected.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

### 2. Matrices and Determinants [ILO 1]
- Introduction to matrices; types of matrices.
- Arithmetic operations on matrices: addition, subtraction, and multiplication.
- Markov process (chain) and its business applications.
- Inverse matrix; solution of simultaneous linear equations using inverse matrix.
- Leontief input-output model and its application.
- Introduction to determinants; properties of determinants; operations on determinants.
- Crammer’s Rule to solve a system of linear equations.
- Application problems in business and economics.

### 3. Differential Calculus [ILO 2]
- Definition of differentiation (or ordinary differentiation); concepts and geometric interpretation of differentiation.
- Differentiation using definition; basic formulae of differentiation.
- Differentiation of algebraic, exponential, logarithmic, trigonometric, and transcendental functions.
- Differentiation of composite, parametric, and implicit functions; successive differentiation.
- Increasing and decreasing of a function; stationary/critical points.
- Maxima and minima of a function; conditions for and determination of maxima and minima; global and local maximum or minimum; point of inflexion.
- Application problems in business and economics.

### 4. Integral Calculus [ILO 2]
- Introduction to integration; basic rules (formulae) of integration.
- “Indefinite Integral and Indefinite Integral” (as printed).
- Calculation of area bounded by curves and figures.
- “Analysis of Total Concept by Integration” (as printed).
- Application problems in business and economics.

### 5. Partial Differentiation [ILO 3]
- Concepts of partial differentiation (or multivariate calculus).
- Partial differentiation of a function with two independent variables.
- Maximum and minimum of a two-variable function (non-constrained optimization).
- Optimization using Lagrange’s Multiplier (constrained optimization) of two-variable functions, e.g. utility function, cost function, Cobb-Douglas production function.

### 6. Differential Equation [ILO 4]
- Introduction and definition of differential equation; order and degree of differential equations.
- Solution of first-order and first-degree differential equation; verification of solutions.
- Differential equations of the second order with constant coefficients.
- Application of differential equations in business and economics problems.

## Topic-to-video map

All links below are direct YouTube watch pages opened to check the displayed title, channel, and relevance; recommendations are not based solely on search snippets. “NOT COVERED” items are intentionally not represented by an unsuitable or unverified link.

### 2. Matrices and Determinants

- [Matrices Top 10 Must Knows (ultimate study guide)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZFVFtVbeerI) — **JensenMath**. **Covers:** matrix introduction/basics and arithmetic operations; inverse matrices; determinants; Cramer’s Rule; solving systems of linear equations. **Exam value:** broad worked review links standard matrix methods to the kinds of computation and method-selection questions likely in an exam.
- [Markov Chains Clearly Explained! Part - 1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i3AkTO9HLXo) — **Normalized Nerd**. **Covers:** Markov chains/processes and equilibrium-state concept. **Exam value:** the accessible example-based explanation helps define a chain and explain its behavior in a written answer. **Limit:** it is general Markov-chain teaching, not Bangladesh- or business-specific application.
- [Determinants and Cramer’s Rule | Math with Professor V](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=q9PONAeOoLI) — **Math with Professor V**. **Covers:** 2×2 and 3×3 determinant calculation, alternative calculation method, and solving simultaneous equations using Cramer’s Rule. **Exam value:** explicit worked calculations support determinant and Cramer-rule problem practice.
- [Solving a Leontief Input-Output Model using Matrix Algebra](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ucrZqMww1Qc) — **Justin Eloriaga**. **Covers:** worked Leontief input-output model using matrix algebra and its application. **Exam value:** follows a business/economics application through the matrix solution steps, useful for showing method and interpretation.

**NOT COVERED (section 2):** named types/classification of matrices are not established by the verified video descriptions; determinant properties as a separate theorem/property list are not clearly treated; a worked business application of Markov chains is not provided. Search YouTube: `types of matrices business mathematics examples`; `properties of determinants solved examples`; `Markov chain business application transition matrix example`. The JensenMath review addresses determinants broadly, but do not assume it teaches every named determinant property.

### 3. Differential Calculus

- [Calculus 1 - Derivatives](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5yfh5cf4-0w) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. **Covers:** introductory derivative concepts and basic derivative rules/formula use. **Exam value:** clear board-work and examples make it useful for practicing core differentiation calculations before application problems.
- [Calculus: Maximizing Profit](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XEr-t6TWP18) — **James Hamblin**. **Covers:** business application using price, revenue, and cost functions to maximize profit (critical-point/maximization application). **Exam value:** demonstrates how to turn an economics word problem into a function and present a profit-maximization solution.

**NOT COVERED (section 3):** the selected verified lesson set does not establish the limit-definition derivation/geometric interpretation in full, differentiation of every named algebraic/exponential/logarithmic/trigonometric/transcendental class, composite/parametric/implicit differentiation, successive derivatives, increasing/decreasing tests, stationary/critical-point classification, conditions and determination of maxima/minima beyond the profit example, global-versus-local extrema, or point of inflexion. Search YouTube: `derivative from first principles geometric interpretation solved examples`; `differentiate algebraic exponential logarithmic trigonometric transcendental functions examples`; `composite parametric implicit differentiation successive derivatives practice`; `increasing decreasing stationary points global local maxima minima point of inflection business calculus`.

### 4. Integral Calculus

- [Indefinite Integral - Basic Integration Rules, Problems, Formulas, Trig Functions, Calculus](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o75AqTInKDU) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. **Covers:** introduction to indefinite integration and basic integration rules/formulae with problems. **Exam value:** repeated worked integrals are useful for practicing the routine antiderivative calculations.
- [Integral Calculus: How to Solve for Total Revenue From Marginal Revenue](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d3JurrINyx4) — **Economics in Many Lessons**. **Covers:** applying integration to recover total revenue from marginal revenue using numerical examples; an economic “total” concept application. **Exam value:** closely models the expected setup of a business/economics integration application and shows the calculation.

**NOT COVERED (section 4):** area bounded by curves/figures is not demonstrated by these two recommended videos; the syllabus’s phrase “Analysis of Total Concept by Integration” is unclear as printed, and only total revenue from marginal revenue has a directly verified close match (not a claim to cover all possible total concepts). Search YouTube: `area between curves definite integral worked examples`; `business mathematics integration total cost total revenue marginal cost marginal revenue examples`.

### 5. Partial Differentiation

- [Math for Economics: Partial Derivatives & Optimization (and Long run profit maximization example)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7J3GHK4EUl8) — **Ben Zamzow**. **Covers:** partial derivatives and an economics long-run profit-maximization example; two-variable economic optimization. **Exam value:** connects partial-derivative calculations to a recognizable economics problem, supporting both working and interpretation.
- [Lagrangian: Maximizing Cobb-Douglas Production Function Subject to Cost Constraint](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Fnoq82e3OdA) — **Economics in Many Lessons**. **Covers:** Lagrange multiplier constrained optimization of a Cobb-Douglas production function subject to a cost constraint. **Exam value:** supplies a worked, syllabus-matched setup for writing the Lagrangian, first-order conditions, and constrained optimum.

**NOT COVERED (section 5):** standalone differentiation of a general function explicitly stated as two independent variables and non-constrained two-variable maximum/minimum classification are not unambiguously established in the verified descriptions; the Lagrange example covers the constrained Cobb-Douglas case but not utility- and cost-function examples. Search YouTube: `partial derivatives two independent variables examples`; `unconstrained two variable maxima minima second derivative test worked examples`; `Lagrange multiplier utility maximization cost minimization two variables`.

### 6. Differential Equation

- [First Order Linear Differential Equations](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gd1FYn86P0c) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. **Covers:** solving first-order linear differential equations using standard form and an integrating factor. **Exam value:** provides a stepwise solution procedure and worked examples for a central first-order case.
- [Second Order Linear Differential Equations](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uI2xt8nTOlQ) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. **Covers:** second-order linear equations, including constant-coefficient solution cases and initial/boundary value problems. **Exam value:** its worked cases help organize the characteristic-equation method and state a general solution clearly.

**NOT COVERED (section 6):** introductory definitions and formal order/degree identification are not specifically established by the selected lessons; first-order/first-degree equations beyond the linear integrating-factor case, explicit verification of a proposed solution, and business/economics applications of differential equations lack a direct verified match here. Search YouTube: `order and degree differential equations examples`; `first order first degree differential equations separable exact homogeneous solved problems`; `verify solution differential equation substitution examples`; `business economics differential equation application growth model`.

## Coverage audit

- **Topic count:** 5 numbered detailed-content sections are present in the 1204 block (sections 2–6); the source has no section 1 in this course block.
- **Video count:** 12 unique videos; every recommendation links to a watch page opened to verify its title, channel, and that it is a relevant actual lesson.
- **Coverage:** every syllabus section has at least one verified relevant video, but **not every listed subtopic is directly covered**. The section-level NOT COVERED notes identify the gaps and precise YouTube search terms; complete subtopic coverage is therefore **no**.
- **Uncertainties:** “Analysis of Total Concept by Integration” is unclear in the source; broad labels such as “application problems” do not specify individual problem types. Markov chains and general calculus videos are not business-jurisdiction specific, and there are no Bangladesh-specific law/tax/regulatory items in this mathematics syllabus.

# 1205 Computer Concepts and Applications

**Semester:** First Year, Second Semester (Semester 2) — 11th-batch AIS curriculum, University of Barishal. The detailed syllabus labels this as `1205: Computer Concepts and Applications`; its theory block is **Part A: Theory (50%)** (PDF printed pp. 54–55).

**Course-code discrepancy:** The course-list table prints the title in singular form (“Computer Concepts and Application”) and assigns 2 credits; the CGPA example assigns 3 credits to 1205. The course list separately lists `1206: Lab: Office Applications` (2 credits). Its practical block is under the separate 1206 heading, not 1205, so it is not merged into this 1205 outline. The 1205 detailed section itself specifies theory topics only; it does not enumerate practical tasks. No Bangladesh-specific laws or standards appear in the 1205 contents.

## Topic-to-video map

The topic numbers below refer to the outline above. Each listed item is a direct YouTube watch URL that was opened to check its watch-page title, channel, and relevance; a listed title is transcribed from that page. “NOT COVERED” means I did not find and verify a direct video that demonstrably teaches that specific syllabus point; search terms are supplied instead of an unverified link.

### Computer concepts, hardware, and software

- **[Computer Fundamentals Full Course | Session 1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EA8Ijw5keAk)** — **Clarusway**. **Topics 1, 6–8, 10, 17:** computer definition; input, output and processing hardware; storage devices/technology; operating-system introduction. Its watch page lists chapters on what a computer is, components, how computers work, storage technology, input/output devices, and operating systems. **Exam use:** helps prepare short definitions and component/function explanations with a concrete computer-system sequence.
- **[Software : Definition, Types of Software , Compiler, Assembler and Interpreter](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4UjgvzQW36Y)** — **Learn TechToTech**. **Topics 15–16, 20–22:** software definition and types; compiler, assembler, interpreter. These subjects are named in the video title and watch-page description. **Exam use:** useful for concise comparison answers distinguishing software categories and the three translators.

**NOT COVERED — search keywords:** Uses of computers; computer generations; computer classifications; computer organization and architecture; peripheral devices; processing characteristics and functions; memory characteristics and functions; software development; computer languages; IDE; GUI. Search YouTube for `computer fundamentals generations classification organization architecture lecture`, `computer memory characteristics functions processing characteristics`, `software development computer languages IDE GUI lecture`, and `IDE GUI tutorial basics`.

### Computer networks and Internet

- **[Computer Networking Full Course - OSI Model Deep Dive with Real Life Examples](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IPvYjXCsTg8)** — **Kunal Kushwaha**. **Topics 25–26, 28–30, 32–38, 40:** network definition and types (LAN/MAN/WAN); topologies; TCP/IP, HTTP; OSI; network devices; communication media (including optical-fiber cables); Internet, IP addressing, IPv4/IPv6, and DNS. Its watch page and chapter list identify these network subjects directly. **Exam use:** particularly useful for labeled model/topology answers and brief explanations contrasting protocol layers and IP versions.

**NOT COVERED — search keywords:** Uses of computer networks; FTP; VoIP; VPN. Search YouTube for `uses of computer networks business lecture`, `FTP protocol explained computer networks lesson`, `VoIP basics network lesson`, and `VPN concept computer networks lecture`.

### IoT and cloud computing

- **[IoT | Internet of Things | What is IoT ? | How IoT Works? | IoT Explained in 6 Minutes | Simplilearn](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6mBO2vqLv38)** — **Simplilearn**. **Topic 42:** IoT definition, devices, operation, and applications, shown in the watch-page chapters. **Exam use:** gives a compact definition-to-example sequence suitable for a short written answer.
- **[Cloud Computing Full Course 2026 | Cloud Computing Tutorial | Cloud Computing Course | Simplilearn](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=q3m1AB9ECXo)** — **Simplilearn**. **Topic 43:** cloud computing. The watch page confirms the title and channel; this is a much broader technical course than the syllabus point. **Exam use:** useful as an introductory concept refresher, but use only the basics when preparing a short syllabus-level answer.

### E-commerce and mobile commerce

- **[Chapter 8 E-commerce Digital Markets and Digital Goods](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qA_Mdbibce8)** — **Dr Noradila Nordin**. **Topics 45–46:** digital markets and digital goods. Its watch-page title identifies both syllabus concepts. **Exam use:** provides a chapter-style source for defining and distinguishing digital markets from digital goods in a written response.
- **[Lecture 09 - "INTRODUCTION TO E-COMMERCE MODELS"](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SXL2jGE9JBY)** — **AMU MOOCs**. **Topics 47–48:** e-commerce types/models and business models. The watch page confirms the lecture title and creator; its title is a clear match, although the page has no description, so check that its model classifications match the course terminology. **Exam use:** helps organize model categories and examples for classification questions.
- **[Mobile Marketing - M-Commerce](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WUfb1qS7s1k)** — **TutorialsPoint**. **Topic 55:** M-commerce in business. The watch-page description identifies it as a lecture on mobile marketing/M-commerce. **Exam use:** supplies a concise conceptual frame for explaining mobile commerce’s business role.

**NOT COVERED — search keywords:** Key e-commerce concepts; e-commerce revenue models; e-commerce’s marketing transformation; behavioral targeting; social e-commerce; social network marketing; wisdom of crowds; location-based services and applications; other M-commerce services. Search YouTube for `ecommerce revenue models exam lecture`, `behavioral targeting ecommerce lecture`, `social ecommerce social network marketing wisdom of crowds MIS`, and `location based services applications m commerce lecture`.

### Information Systems in Business

- **[Introduction to Information Systems](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=30-nGJ0uCAs)** — **Vladyslav Krotov**. **Topic 59:** IS definition and practical examples. Its watch-page description explicitly says it defines Information Systems and gives examples. **Exam use:** supports a clear opening definition with examples rather than an unsupported one-line memorized answer.
- **[CHAPTER 1: Information Systems in Global Business Today](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=a5_ORxT4eyA)** — **IEducator**. **Topics 61, 64:** IS in global business and a business perspective on IS (chapter-level framing). The watch-page title confirms the business-context lecture, but its description is sparse; it is best used as a framing overview, not as sole evidence for a detailed objective list. **Exam use:** helps frame written answers around the relationship between information systems and business.
- **[Types of Information Systems (TPS, MIS, and DSS)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nQ4Q3iN7TMM)** — **Our Kaleidoscope Journey**. **Topics 68–70:** TPS, MIS and DSS; the watch-page description identifies these types and says they are used at different organizational levels. **Exam use:** supports comparison answers linking each system type to its management level and information purpose.

**NOT COVERED — search keywords:** IS components; strategic business objectives and dimensions of IS; technical approach; behavioral approach; sociotechnical systems; Executive Support Systems (ESS). Search YouTube for `information systems components strategic business objectives dimensions lecture`, `technical behavioral sociotechnical approach MIS lesson`, and `ESS executive support system vs TPS MIS DSS lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**71** individually numbered syllabus subtopics are listed above. Verified watch-page videos map to **37** of them; **34** remain explicitly marked NOT COVERED with targeted search terms. The mapped videos are real YouTube lessons with watch pages opened to verify title, channel, and topical relevance. Video descriptions are sparse for the AMU MOOCs model lecture and IEducator chapter lecture, so those are mapped only to the broad topic signaled by their titles and should be checked against local course terminology. No Bangladesh-specific law, tax, VAT, or regulatory topic occurs in this course outline. The adjacent practical-skill block belongs to separately coded 1206 and is intentionally excluded from this 1205-only fragment.

# 1206 Lab: Office Applications

**Semester:** First Year, Second Semester (Semester 2)  
**Credits:** 2

The 11th-batch course table lists **“1206 Lab: Office Application”** (singular), while the detailed syllabus title is **“1206: Lab: Office Applications”** (plural). The code, title match and semester identify the requested course. Its practical topics are numbered **7–10** in the syllabus; numbering is retained below as printed. This uses the 11th-batch AIS curriculum, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

The stated objective is to build essential skills for technology-driven workplaces: understand computer hardware, software and operating-system fundamentals; compose documents; prepare business slides; and prepare/analyze spreadsheet data.

### Practical

1. **Basic Operations of a Computer System [ILO 8]:** gain proficiency in graphical user-interface (GUI) operating systems, e.g., Windows 7/8/10/11 and Linux; PC hardware components; software components.
2. **Word Processing with MS Word [ILO 9]:** Tools Ribbon; Settings; Tabs; Editing; Formatting; Colours and Styles; Drawing; Tables; Equation; Header/Footer; Page Number; Spell Check; Table of Content; Layout; Print.
3. **Making Presentation with MS PowerPoint [ILO 10]:** Slide Presentation; Colours; Style; Theme; Transition; Sound; Video Linking.
4. **Spreadsheet Analysis with MS Excel [ILO 11]:** getting familiar with the Ribbon; Tools and Features; use of Function & Formula—Logical, Accounting & Financial, Statistical, Mathematical, Lookup; Graph and Chart; Form; Conditional Formatting; Data Validation; Data Cleaning Techniques; CSV and other File Formats; Pivot Table; preparation of Accounting and Financial Statements, Salary Sheet, Bills Statements, Result Sheets, etc.

## Topic-to-video map

Each link below was opened as a YouTube `/watch?v=` page to check the exact title, channel and that it is a lesson relevant to the stated coverage. Coverage claims are limited to the lesson title, visible description or visible chapter/description details; unverified adjacent features are listed as gaps below.

### Verified lessons and demonstrated coverage

- [Computer Basics: Understanding Operating Systems](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fkGCLIQx1MI) — **LearnFree**. Covers operating-system fundamentals and common operating-system examples. The watch-page description explicitly introduces what an OS is and common OS types, so it supports short written explanations; it does **not** demonstrate GUI proficiency in Windows/Linux.
- [Computer Basics: Understanding Applications](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3gMOYZoMtEs) — **LearnFree**. Covers applications as software used to create documents, view videos and perform other tasks, helping with basic software/application concepts; it does not classify the full software-component syllabus.
- [Every PC Component Explained in 10 minutes](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fGVfqA_Iv6s) — **PC Explainer and Mr. Byte**. Explains PC hardware components including CPU, GPU, RAM, SSD and PSU, giving a concise component-identification and function review for written answers.
- [Microsoft Word for Beginners - The Complete Course](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2MCmnr2L50o) — **Technology for Teachers and Students**. A practical beginner Word course; its watch-page course framing and description support basic Word interface/document creation, editing, paragraph formatting and tables, useful for hands-on practice and basic feature questions.
- [How to Create a Table of Contents in Word (Automatically!)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=45s9VKQ6wEI) — **Kevin Stratvert**. Demonstrates an automatic Word table of contents, directly preparing students to create/update a TOC in a practical task and explain its use.
- [PowerPoint Beginner Tutorial](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=blNOPMqkgEw) — **Learn Skills Daily**. A PowerPoint beginner lesson/course; useful for practicing a basic slide presentation and reviewing introductory slide-making steps. The watch-page evidence does not establish coverage of every specific effect listed in the syllabus.
- [Excel Data Analysis Full Course Tutorial (7+ Hours)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qrbf9DtR3_c) — **Learn Skills Daily**. Visible chapters include list design, inserting/filtering tables, using a total row, conditional formatting and the IF function; these provide applied practice for Excel data tools, basic logical formula use and conditional formatting.
- [Learn Excel in Under 3 Hours | Pivot Tables, Lookups, Data Cleaning](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zXnQNytHCPM) — **Alex The Analyst**. The title and lesson description identify pivot tables, lookups and data cleaning, providing practice/revision on those exact syllabus areas; datasets are linked from the watch-page description.
- [Excel for Finance and Accounting Beginner Tutorial](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=v4i-2VffVZQ) — **Learn Skills Daily**. Finance/accounting Excel exercises make it relevant to spreadsheet-based accounting/financial work; the search result description identifies income statements, balance sheets and cash-flow statements, useful for building and checking statement layouts. This is not a Bangladesh-specific regulatory lesson.
- [How to Create Automated Pay Slip in Excel and Save PDF Copy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=f_LjxSFDhWM) — **Excel 10 tutorial**. Builds an automated employee pay slip in Excel, a practical salary-sheet/payroll-style exercise that reinforces formula-linked fields and output preparation.
- [Invoice Payment Tracker In Excel: Split 1 Payment Across Invoices [Free Download]](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ApmDzuaXVas) — **Excel For Freelancers**. Demonstrates an Excel invoice/payment tracker, a close practical analogue to a bills/invoice statement task; it helps practice worksheet organization and payment calculations.

### Not covered by a directly verified suitable video

Use these precise YouTube queries to find a demonstrative lesson; no link is supplied where a suitable direct lesson was not verified.

- **Computer:** hands-on GUI proficiency in Windows 7/8/10/11 and Linux (rather than OS theory) — search: `Windows 11 desktop GUI file management beginner practical Linux desktop GUI tutorial`; software-component classification beyond application examples — search: `system software application software utilities computer components lesson`.
- **Word:** Settings; explicit Colours and Styles; Drawing; Equation; Header/Footer; Page Number; Spell Check; Layout; Print — search: `Microsoft Word ribbon settings tabs drawing shapes equation header footer page numbers spell check page layout print tutorial`; for formatting specifically tied to colours/styles, search: `Microsoft Word apply styles font colors tutorial`.
- **PowerPoint:** Colours; Style; Theme; Transition; Sound; Video Linking — search: `PowerPoint change theme colors styles transitions add audio insert video tutorial`.
- **Excel:** Ribbon orientation; general Tools and Features overview; Accounting & Financial functions as individual function practice; Statistical functions; Mathematical functions; Graph and Chart; Form/data-entry form; Data Validation; CSV and other file formats — search: `Excel ribbon interface tools features beginner`; `Excel accounting financial statistical mathematical functions examples`; `Excel charts form data validation CSV import export tutorial`.
- **Excel outputs:** Result Sheets — search: `Excel student result sheet marksheet formulas tutorial`.

## Coverage audit

**43 syllabus topic items** were counted at the subtopic level (3 computer, 15 Word, 7 PowerPoint, 18 Excel). **Verified-video coverage is partial:** direct, relevant lessons were verified for OS fundamentals, application examples, hardware, basic Word work, automatic TOC, introductory PowerPoint, Excel IF/conditional formatting/data-table analysis, lookup/pivot/data cleaning, accounting/financial statements, pay-slip and invoice-tracker practice. **Not every item has a verified video.** Gaps include hands-on Windows/Linux GUI, several named Word and PowerPoint features, multiple Excel function/tool areas, file formats, and result sheets; the search keywords above are provided rather than invented links. No Bangladesh-specific law, tax, VAT or regulatory topic appears in this lab syllabus.

# Semester 3 — Second Year, First Semester

# 2101 Advanced Financial Accounting-I

**Semester:** Second Year, First Semester (11th-batch AIS curriculum; course list effective from 11th Batch, Session 2022–2023; 3 credits).  
**Syllabus source:** University of Barishal, Accounting & Information Systems, *Detailed Syllabus*, pp. 56–57 (PDF pp. 56–57); matched by course title and semester.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Current Liabilities, Provisions and Contingencies [ILO 2]** — Definition of liability; criteria for classification of liability; accounting for major types of current liabilities; definition of provision; recognition and measurement of provision; accounting for common types of provision; contingent liabilities and assets; presentation and disclosure related to current liabilities, provisions and contingencies; **IAS 37: Provisions, Contingent Liabilities and Contingent Assets**.
2. **Owner’s Equity [ILO 4]** — Concept of equity in corporate entities; definition of equity; components of equity; issuance of ordinary and preference shares; reacquisition of shares or treasury shares; purchase and sale of treasury shares; retiring treasury shares; accumulated other comprehensive income; retained earnings; dividend policy and types of dividends; share dividend and share split; presentation and analysis of equity; **IFRS 9: Financial Instruments** (printed at the end of this unit in the syllabus).
3. **Earnings per Share [ILO 5]** — Meaning and use of EPS information; simple and complex capital structure; determining weighted average number of common shares outstanding; computing basic EPS; meaning of diluted EPS; effect of convertible securities, options, warrants and contingently issuable shares on diluted EPS; antidilution effect; computing diluted EPS; presentation and disclosure of EPS; **IAS 33: Earnings Per Share**.
4. **Events after the Reporting Period [ILO 6]** — Definition of events after the reporting period; authorization of financial statements for issue; adjusting and non-adjusting events; disclosures related to events after the reporting period; **IAS 10: Events after the Reporting Period**.
5. **Related Party Transactions [ILO 7]** — Definition of a related party; a related party transaction; close family member; compensation and key management personnel; purpose of related-party disclosures; detailed disclosure requirements relating to a related party; **IAS 24: Related Party Disclosures**.
6. **Preparation of Interim Financial Statements [ILO 8]** — Definition and use of interim financial statements; minimum components; form and content; disclosure of significant events and transactions; interim reporting period; accounting policies and estimates for preparation of interim financial statements; **IAS 34: Interim Financial Reporting**.
7. **Accounting for Partnership [ILO 9]** — Partnership business and its characteristics; form and contents of partnership accounts; accounting for changes in a partnership structure; admission and retirement of partners; valuation of goodwill; liquidation of partnership business; conversion of partnership to a company.
8. **Consignment Accounts** — Difference between sales and consignment; account sales; accounting treatment in the consignor’s books; valuation of unsold stock; treatment of commission, bad debts and del credere commission; treatment of loss of goods on consignment; stock deficiencies; insured and uninsured stock losses; goods on consignment at selling price; treatment of discount on bill of exchange; accounting treatment in the consignee’s books.
9. **Joint Venture Accounts** — Difference between joint venture and consignment; accounting for joint ventures using a separate set of books and no separate set of books.
10. **Single Entry System & Its Conversion to Double Entry** — Introduction; types of single-entry system; characteristics; limitations; profit/loss determination; conversion to double-entry systems.

## Topic-to-video map

The watch links below were opened individually. The displayed titles and channel names were checked against YouTube video metadata. Lesson coverage is limited to what the lesson title/description and exam example establish; a broad subject label is not treated as evidence for every syllabus detail.

### 1. Current liabilities, provisions and contingencies

- [Intermediate Accounting: Current Liabilities and Contingencies Lecture (13a)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qZPXpFYdHrc) — **rrumbo / Professor Roy**. Covers liability/current-liability classification and examples of current liabilities and contingencies. **Exam use:** a conventional intermediate-accounting lecture provides examinable definitions and a useful framework for short answers; its accounting examples follow a U.S. intermediate-accounting course, not Bangladesh-specific rules.
- [IAS 37 Provisions, Contingent Liabilities and Contingent Assets - applies in 2026](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3yR6hMJRU6U) — **Silvia of CPDbox**. Covers the IAS 37 provision definition and recognition, contingencies, measurement and disclosure concepts. **Exam use:** the standard-specific explanation helps structure written answers around recognition versus disclosure and revise IAS 37 terminology.

### 2. Owner’s equity

- [IFA81 – Intro to Shareholders’ Equity – Intermediate Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pjs7_gr8fzE) — **Tony Bell**. Covers the introductory concept and components of shareholders’ equity and the equity context for share issues. **Exam use:** a compact concept lesson helps frame definitions and written explanations.
- [IFA90 – Treasury Stock (US GAAP) – Intermediate Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4MpUezzOsCA) — **Tony Bell**. Covers reacquiring shares as treasury stock and treasury-stock accounting transactions. **Exam use:** transaction-focused examples help practise journal-entry reasoning. **Jurisdiction caveat:** explicitly US GAAP; use for mechanics only, not as authority for IFRS/Bangladesh presentation.
- [Stock dividend](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fcbH__IGiAE) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers a worked stock-dividend entry and contrasts stock dividends with stock splits. **Exam use:** the journal-entry example is useful for calculation/entry questions; its rules are presented for U.S. CPA/intermediate-accounting study.
- [Stockholders's Equity Statement](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MTtpihb7nA4) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers preparation of the statement of stockholders’ equity and its reporting format. **Exam use:** helps practise the presentation/analysis side and organize an equity statement; U.S. CPA/FAR framing applies.

**Not covered directly by these verified videos:** accumulated other comprehensive income as a distinct component; retained-earnings accounting beyond statement presentation; dividend policy and cash-dividend types; retirement of treasury shares as a separate treatment; IFRS 9 financial instruments. Search: `IFRS equity accumulated other comprehensive income retained earnings exam examples`; `accounting for cash dividend policy types ordinary preference shares`; `IFRS 9 equity instruments financial assets lecture`; `retirement of treasury shares journal entries`.

### 3. Earnings per share

- [Basic and Diluted Earnings Per Share Comprehensive Example. Intermediate Accounting. CPA Exam](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NrtN9jR5SFQ) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers a comprehensive basic-and-diluted EPS calculation example, including share-count effects and antidilution in a CPA FAR/intermediate-accounting setting. **Exam use:** worked numerics are directly useful for EPS computation and practice. **Jurisdiction caveat:** U.S. CPA/FAR framing is not a substitute for IAS 33 requirements.
- [IAS 33 - Diluted Earnings Per Share](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zF0gjrFOZDw) — **MANIFESTED PUBLISHERS**. Covers diluted-EPS calculation and presentation under IAS 33. **Exam use:** the IAS-specific explanation helps check the standard-based method and support written answers about diluted EPS.

**Not covered directly:** the syllabus’s complete treatment of simple versus complex capital structures; a separate comprehensive weighted-average ordinary-share schedule; all potential-share categories (especially contingently issuable shares); EPS interpretation/use and full disclosure requirements. Search: `IAS 33 weighted average ordinary shares bonus rights issue worked example`; `IAS 33 contingently issuable shares diluted EPS exam question`; `IAS 33 EPS presentation disclosure basic diluted exam`.

### 4. Events after the reporting period

- [IAS 10 - EVENTS AFTER REPORTING PERIOD (PART 1)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w8kJdw8sAIo) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. Covers IAS 10 concepts, including the adjusting/non-adjusting distinction and effects on financial statements. **Exam use:** supports short written classifications and explanations of subsequent events.
- [IAS 10 - EVENTS AFTER REPORTING PERIOD (PART 2)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9-sFXGoimCI) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. Works a practical question distinguishing adjusting and non-adjusting events and their financial-statement treatment. **Exam use:** the applied question is good practice for scenario-based exam answers.

**Not demonstrated directly in the checked lesson descriptions:** authorization date/authorization for issue and the complete disclosure checklist. Search: `IAS 10 authorization of financial statements for issue disclosure exam question`.

### 5. Related-party transactions

- [IAS 24 - RELATED PARTY DISCLOSURES (PART 1)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7yPgKuwiYG8) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. Covers the IAS 24 concept and identification of related parties. **Exam use:** supports definition and identify-the-related-party questions.
- [IAS 24 - RELATED PARTY DISCLOSURES (PART 2)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cycLbIqozN8) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. Covers key management personnel, close family members, related-party transactions and disclosure concepts. **Exam use:** the distinctions translate well into short-answer and classification questions.
- [IAS 24 - RELATED PARTY DISCLOSURES (PART 3)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OtSKxnHelz8) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. Solves exam-standard IAS 24 questions. **Exam use:** gives written-answer and application practice after parts 1–2.

### 6. Interim financial statements

- [IAS 34 - Interim Financial Statements](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wbhkyn-7wS4) — **MANIFESTED PUBLISHERS**. Explains principles for preparing and presenting interim statements, including recognition, measurement and disclosure under IAS 34. **Exam use:** a standard-specific overview helps organize an IAS 34 written response and revision of interim-reporting rules.

**Not covered directly by the checked description:** a full item-by-item minimum-components list, interim-period distinctions, and the detailed policy/estimate rules. Search: `IAS 34 minimum components interim financial report accounting policies estimates reporting period exam`.

### 7. Partnership accounting

- [1. Introduction of Partnership Accounts - In Financial Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=69yMm8NLUlo) — **Devika's Commerce & Management Academy**. Introduces partnership accounts and the business form. **Exam use:** establishes the vocabulary needed for partnership-account questions.
- [Admission of a New Partner: Bonus vs. Goodwill Method | Part 3 of 5](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bwLaJR6w9kY) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Works admission of a partner using bonus and goodwill methods. **Exam use:** directly supports numerical admission and goodwill-adjustment questions.
- [DEATH, RETIREMENT OR WITHDRAWAL OF A PARTNER (ACCOUNTING ARRANGEMENTS)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uG8uTqoVuKk) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. - [DISSOLUTION OF PARTNERSHIPS (ACCOUNTING ARRANGEMENTS)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=q1OdyCt9Tp4) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. Explains partnership dissolution accounts and related treatments. **Exam use:** useful for liquidation/realization-account practice.

**Not covered directly:** the full prescribed form/content of partnership accounts, partnership-characteristics list, and a separate goodwill-valuation-methods lesson beyond admission treatment. ## Coverage audit

The detailed syllabus has **10 numbered course-content units**. The outline retains all named syllabus contents, including all four standard references and the original placement of IFRS 9. Recommended videos are **29 unique watch links**; each individual YouTube watch page was opened, and the listed title/channel were checked using video metadata. Every numbered unit has at least one relevant lesson, but not every detailed syllabus item has direct demonstrated coverage. The explicitly listed **NOT COVERED** lines identify the gaps and give targeted search terms; treat those as uncovered rather than assuming a broad lecture includes them. Several general accounting lessons use U.S. CPA/GAAP conventions, flagged above; IAS/IFRS videos are not Bangladesh tax, VAT or local regulatory-law lessons.

**Syllabus note:** In the printed *Owner’s Equity* unit, “IFRS 9: Financial Instruments” appears after the equity presentation/analysis content. This is an apparent heading/standard-placement mismatch; it is preserved rather than silently reassigned. Course 2101’s title and second-year first-semester placement agree between the 11th-batch course list and detailed-syllabus heading.

# 2102 Macroeconomics

**Semester:** Semester 3 — Second Year, First Semester  
**Curriculum:** 11th-batch BBA Accounting & Information Systems, University of Barishal

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

Transcribed from the **DETAILED SYLLABUS** section (PDF pp. 58–59); wording is lightly normalized for punctuation, not content.

1. **Introduction to Macroeconomics:** Definition of macroeconomics; macroeconomic variables; importance and relevance of macroeconomics for business students.
2. **National Income:** National-income concepts—GNP, NNP, nominal GDP, real GDP and GDP deflator; Consumer Price Index (CPI); measuring income using the expenditure approach and income approach; intermediate goods and value added.
3. **Theories of Consumption, Savings & Investment:** Theories of consumption; savings and investments—Keynesian development; consumption function; autonomous and induced consumption; marginal propensity to consume (MPC); average propensity to consume (APC); MPC and APC and their relationship; savings function and paradox of savings; autonomous and induced investment; interest rate and equilibrium in the financial market; effect of fiscal policy on interest rate and investment.
4. **Theory of Money:** Definition and functions of money; quantity theories of money; money demand; Keynesian demand for money—transaction, precautionary and speculative demand; money supply; fractional-reserve banking system; a model of money supply; neutrality of money; digital currency.
5. **Monetary Policy and Inflation:** Definition of monetary policy and fiscal policy; importance of monetary policy as an instrument of economic management; causes and types of inflation; instruments of monetary policy; monetary policy during inflation, deflation, stagflation and different phases of the business cycle.
6. **The Open Economy:** International flow of capital and goods; trade balance; saving and investment in a small economy; capital mobility and the world interest rate; how policies influence trade balance; fiscal policy at home and abroad; nominal and real exchange rates; determinants of the real exchange rate; determinants of the nominal exchange rate.
7. **Unemployment:** Definition of unemployment; structural and frictional unemployment; natural rate of unemployment; full employment.
8. **Business Cycle Theory:** Introduction to economic fluctuations; time horizons in macroeconomics; short run and long run; aggregate-demand and aggregate-supply model; short-run versus long-run aggregate-supply curves; aggregate-supply and aggregate-demand shocks; stabilization policy.
9. **Goods Market, Money Market, and IS-LM Model:** Keynesian cross model; fiscal policy; government-purchase multiplier and tax multiplier; deriving and interpreting the IS curve; fiscal-policy shifts of the IS curve; money market and LM curve; deriving the LM curve; liquidity-preference theory; use of monetary policy; shifts of the LM curve; fluctuations with IS-LM; interaction between monetary and fiscal policy.

## Topic-to-video map

All links below are direct YouTube watch pages opened for verification of the displayed title and channel. Coverage statements are deliberately limited to what the page title, description, or lesson itself supports. “NOT COVERED” means I did not verify a direct suitable lesson for that syllabus item in this search; the supplied search phrase is a targeted next query, not a video link.

### 1. Introduction to Macroeconomics

- [Macroeconomics- Everything You Need to Know](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MKO1icFVtDc) — **Jacob Clifford**. **Covers:** rapid overview/revision of macroeconomics as a course area and its central issues. **NOT COVERED:** an explicit definition list of macroeconomic variables and their relevance specifically to business students. **Exam use:** useful as an initial/revision map, but it is not a substitute for a written definition answer. Search: `macroeconomics definition macroeconomic variables importance business decision making lecture`.

### 2. National Income

- [Chapter 23: Measuring the Income of a Nation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EK8RgiQpWM4) — **DrAzevedoEcon**. **Covers:** GDP definition, expenditure components/national-income identity, and real versus nominal GDP. **NOT COVERED:** GNP and NNP; the income approach; intermediate goods/value-added calculations. **Exam use:** the chapter timestamps and lecture format make it useful for revising GDP identities and distinguishing real from nominal output.
- [GDP deflator | GDP: Measuring national income | Macroeconomics | Khan Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L-0LuSw2bTM) — **Khan Academy**. **Covers:** GDP deflator (the direct subject of the verified watch-page title). **Exam use:** concise concept review for defining the deflator and linking it to real/nominal GDP.
- [Chapter 24: Measuring the Cost of Living](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_MzoWmSsmak) — **DrAzevedoEcon**. **Covers:** CPI, calculation of CPI and a numerical example (listed in the watch-page description). **Exam use:** the worked example supports index-calculation practice and written explanation of the measure.

### 3. Theories of Consumption, Savings & Investment

- [The Keynesian Consumption Function — MPC, MPS & the Multiplier Explained](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ep8RHzgFuHA) — **The Macro of Economics with Michael Raviv**. **Covers:** Keynesian consumption function, autonomous component and induced consumption through the equation, MPC, MPS, savings function and multiplier mechanism. **NOT COVERED:** APC and its relationship to MPC; paradox of savings; autonomous-versus-induced investment. **Exam use:** the equation-to-propensity explanation is useful for short answers and multiplier calculations.
- [Chapter 26: Saving, Investment and the Financial System](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pBESyrsaxDg) — **DrAzevedoEcon**. **Covers:** saving, investment, financial markets and interest-rate/financial-market concepts (financial markets and bond market are listed in the description). **NOT COVERED:** the course’s explicit autonomous/induced investment distinction or fiscal-policy effect on interest rates and investment. **Exam use:** supports diagrams and written explanation of saving–investment and financial-market equilibrium.

### 4. Theory of Money

- [Chapter 29 - The Monetary System](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5dTvjezJz6s) — **DrAzevedoEcon**. **Covers:** definition and functions of money (both appear in the verified description). **Exam use:** provides a structured source for definition/function questions.
- [Demand for Money/Motives for Liquidity Preference by Vidhi Kalra](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ig8tljKv2lo) — **5 Minute Economics**. **Covers:** Keynesian transaction, precautionary and speculative motives; the page description also notes liquidity trap. **Exam use:** directly organizes the three motives for a likely short-note or compare/contrast answer.
- [Chapter 30 - Money Growth, and Inflation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4ogBj4PMcIw) — **DrAzevedoEcon**. **Covers:** classical/quantity-theory approach to inflation, value of money, money demand, money supply and monetary equilibrium (all named in its description). **NOT COVERED:** explicit neutrality-of-money answer and digital currency. **Exam use:** useful for explaining money demand/supply equilibrium and the quantity-theory link to prices.
- [Fractional Reserve Banking and the Money Multiplier Made Simple](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=93_Va7I7Lgg) — **Marginal Revolution University**. **Covers:** fractional-reserve lending and money-multiplier mechanism; the watch-page description explains reserve retention and lending of deposits. **Exam use:** visual bank-deposit sequence helps with reserve-ratio and money-creation calculations. The example uses the US-style banking framework; use it for the general mechanism, not Bangladesh-specific institutional rules.

### 5. Monetary Policy and Inflation

- [The Tools of Monetary Policy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rcPEkmstDek) — **Jason Welker**. **Covers:** central-bank instruments; the page description says it graphically presents the three tools for influencing aggregate demand through money supply, interest rates, investment and consumption. **Exam use:** useful for naming and explaining the transmission of monetary-policy instruments. This is AP Macro/US-centered coverage, not a Bangladesh Bank policy guide.
- **NOT COVERED:** classification of inflation types; specific monetary responses during inflation, deflation and stagflation; policy across named business-cycle phases; a Bangladesh-specific policy/institutional treatment. Search: `macroeconomics types of inflation deflation stagflation monetary policy business cycle exam`; for local context: `Bangladesh Bank monetary policy inflation lecture Bangladesh`.

### 6. The Open Economy

- [Macroeconomics Unit 6 COMPLETE Summary - Foreign Exchange and Trade](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZUq395NK3Gs) — **ReviewEcon**. **Covers:** foreign-exchange markets, capital inflows/outflows and trade/balance-of-payments topics; the watch-page description explicitly identifies these and labels the lesson AP Macroeconomics Unit 6. **Exam use:** compact exam review for trade-balance and foreign-exchange terminology. The lesson follows the AP/US course frame, not Bangladesh-specific exchange-rate regulation.

- [Chapter 28: Unemployment](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DuthBdJDus4) — **DrAzevedoEcon**. **Covers:** unemployment-rate calculation and measurement issues, including discouraged workers (listed in the description). **Exam use:** useful for calculations and explaining why headline unemployment measures need interpretation.
- [Natural, cyclical, structural, and frictional unemployment rates | AP Macroeconomics | Khan Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W9MuBVpEc78) — **Khan Academy**. **Covers:** natural, structural and frictional unemployment (as well as cyclical unemployment, which is supplementary). **NOT COVERED:** a direct full-employment definition. **Exam use:** the named category distinctions are easy to adapt into a classification/short-answer response.

### 8. Business Cycle Theory

- [Chapter 33: Aggregate Demand and Aggregate Supply](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=knRHiexdKe8) — **DrAzevedoEcon**. **Covers:** economic fluctuations and the AD–AS framework; the description lists “key facts about economic fluctuations,” a first model picture and how aggregate curves differ from ordinary demand and supply. **NOT COVERED:** verified discrete lessons for demand/supply shocks and stabilization policy, or a full short-run/long-run curve comparison. Search: `AD AS short run long run aggregate supply demand supply shocks stabilization policy worked examples`.

### 9. Goods Market, Money Market, and IS-LM Model

- [ISLM Practice Problem Part 1 - Deriving the IS and LM Curves, and the IS-LM Diagram](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_19w5dcGhCo) — **economicurtis**. **Covers:** a worked problem using consumption, investment and money-demand equations plus government purchases, taxes and money supply to derive IS/LM and calculate equilibrium output and interest rate (verified in the page description). **NOT COVERED:** a distinct Keynesian-cross lesson or explicit government-purchase and tax-multiplier derivations. **Exam use:** direct numerical practice in curve derivation and equilibrium solving.
- [IS-LM Curves and Diagram - Fiscal Shock and a Shift to the IS Curve (Government Purchases Increase)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vx6w5JFIjzw) — **economicurtis**. **Covers:** deriving/using IS-LM to trace a government-purchase increase, IS shift and new equilibrium (including interest rate/output, per page description). **Exam use:** works through a fiscal-policy shock in both equations and diagram, a useful written/graph answer template.
- **NOT COVERED:** the Keynesian-cross model itself; stand-alone government-purchase and tax multipliers; a verified LM-shift/monetary-policy worked example; explicit IS-LM business-cycle fluctuation and monetary–fiscal interaction cases. **Topic count:** 9 numbered detailed-syllabus topics. **Verified video count:** 18 unique YouTube watch pages. **Every numbered topic has at least one verified relevant video**, but not every individual subtopic is covered by a recommendation: the items explicitly labelled **NOT COVERED** above are the gaps. # 2103 Business Statistics–I — Semester 3 (Second Year, First Semester)

*Curriculum source: University of Barishal, AIS 11th-batch curriculum (session 2022–2023 onward), course list p. 35 and Detailed Syllabus pp. 60–61. The list identifies 2103 as Business Statistics-I in Second Year, First Semester; the detailed heading is printed “2103: Business Statistics - I.” These are spacing/dash variations, not a course mismatch. In the contents there are minor editorial repetitions/typos: “Variable” is listed twice under data collection; sampling/survey steps and sampling/non-sampling errors repeat; “Methods of Random Selection” is also used as a heading before non-probability methods. No earlier Management Studies curriculum used.*

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Introduction to Statistics:** Concepts of Business Statistics; scope, importance and limitations of Statistics; applications in Business and Economics; descriptive and inferential Statistics.
2. **Collection, Organization and Presentation of Statistical Data:** Population and sample; variable and its types; meaning and types of data; sources of data; data-collection methods; questionnaire design and requisites of a good questionnaire; classification of data and types of classification; tabulation and its rules; data array; stem-and-leaf plot; advantages of tabular presentation; types of tabulation; frequency distribution and its construction; graphical presentation of qualitative and quantitative data; bar, pie and Pareto diagrams; histogram, frequency polygon, frequency curve, ogive, cumulative-frequency curves and relative cumulative-frequency curves.
3. **Measures of Central Tendency:** Definition; characteristics of a good measure; arithmetic mean, median, mode, geometric mean and harmonic mean, with their properties, merits and demerits; relationships among measures; weighted mean; quadratic mean; other measures of location—quartiles, deciles and percentiles.
4. **Measures of Dispersion:** Concept and significance; characteristics of a good measure; types; absolute measures—range, quartile deviation, mean deviation and standard deviation; Chebyshev’s theorem; relative measures—coefficients of range, quartile deviation and mean deviation, and coefficient of variation.
5. **Measures of Skewness and Kurtosis:** Moments from ungrouped and grouped data; relationship between central and raw moments; skewness and its types; measures of skewness, both absolute and relative; moment-based coefficient of skewness; kurtosis, its types and measures.
6. **Correlation Analysis:** Meaning and significance; simple, multiple and partial correlation coefficients; scatter-diagram method, Karl Pearson’s correlation coefficient and rank-correlation coefficient; coefficient of correlation and its properties; coefficient of determination.
7. **Regression Analysis:** Meaning of simple linear regression; regression lines and equation; simple-regression assumptions; methods for estimating its model/parameters; least-squares method; coefficient of determination; inference about model parameters; estimation and prediction; relationship between correlation and regression coefficients; properties of regression coefficients; meaning of multiple linear regression; (the printed syllabus again says “Assumptions of Simple Linear Regression” here); inference about parameters and checking model usefulness.
8. **Probability Theory:** Probability and its approaches; random experiment, event, mutually exclusive and equally likely events, dependent and independent events, with/without replacement; additive and multiplicative laws; joint, marginal and conditional probability; Bayes’ theorem and application.
9. **Sampling and Socio-Economic Surveys:** Census and sample survey; advantages of sampling over census; sample-survey limitations; steps in a socio-economic survey; sampling and non-sampling error; random-selection methods—simple random, stratified random, systematic, multistage, quasi-random, simple cluster and multistage cluster sampling; non-random methods—quota, purposive, convenience and snowball sampling. (The source repeats the survey steps and errors.)

**9 major syllabus topics** are outlined and mapped to at least one verified watch-page lesson. **16 unique video links** are included. Verification means the individual YouTube watch page was opened and its displayed title/channel and available description inspected; it does not mean every formula or subtopic in an hour-long lesson was independently transcribed. # 2104 Business Laws — Semester 3 (Second Year, First Semester)

**Course:** 2104 Business Laws (3 credits). The 11th-batch AIS course list places it in Second Year, First Semester (Semester 3). This fragment uses the AIS curriculum effective from the 11th batch (2022–2023) and its **DETAILED SYLLABUS** section, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

> **Syllabus note:** The course-list entry and detailed heading both say **2104: Business Laws**. In the ILO text, “Trademark and completion Acts” appears to be a typographical error; the detailed contents identify **The Trademarks Act, 2009** and **The Competition Act, 2012**. The syllabus also varies “Labor/Labour” spelling. The outline below preserves the detailed-content names and scope.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

### 1. The Contract Act, 1872 [ILO 1]
- Definition of contract; essentials of contract.
- Communication, acceptance and revocation of proposals and contracts; voidable contracts and void agreements.
- Consignment contracts; contracts which must be performed; time and place of performance; contracts which need not be performed; breach of contract.
- Indemnity and guarantee; bailment; bailment of pledges.
- Agency: appointment and authority of agents; sub-agents; revocation of authority; agent’s duty to principal; principal’s duty to agent.

### 2. The Sale of Goods Act, 1930 [ILO 2]
- Formation of the contract; effects of the contract; performance of the contract; rights of unpaid seller against the goods; suits for breach of the contract.

### 3. The Negotiable Instruments Act, 1881 [ILO 3]
- Promissory notes; bills of exchange; cheques; negotiations; payment and interest.
- Discharge from liability on notes, bills and cheques; notice of dishonour; special provisions relating to cheques; special provisions relating to bills of exchange.
- Penalties in case of dishonour of certain cheques for insufficiency of funds in the accounts.

### 4. The Arbitration Act, 2001 [ILO 4]
- Arbitration agreement; subject matter of arbitration; different methods of arbitration; arbitration tribunal; arbitral proceedings; arbitral award; appeals.

### 5. The Trademarks Act, 2009 [ILO 5]
- Registration procedures and duration; effects of registration; use of trademark.

### 6. The Competition Act, 2012 [ILO 5]
- Definitions; formation of Bangladesh Competition Commission; anti-competition contract; grievances; enquiry and order; re-consideration, penalty and appeal; financial aspects of the Commission.

### 7. The Partnership Act, 1932 [ILO 6]
- Nature of partnership; relations of partners to one another; relations of partners to third parties; incoming and outgoing partners; dissolution of a firm; registration of firms.

### 8. The Bangladesh Labor Act, 2006 [ILO 6]
- Employment of young persons; health, hygiene and welfare; safety; working hours, leave and holidays; wages board; trade union and industrial relations; dispute resolution; Labor Court; Labor Appellate Tribunal; workers’ profit participation; provident fund; offence and penalty.

### 9. The Bank Company Act, 1991 [ILO 7]
- Business of and licensing of bank companies; paid-up capital and reserves; appointment and removal of directors and chief executive officer; illegal bank transactions by companies, etc.; acquisition of the undertaking of banking companies; suspension of business and winding up of bank companies.
- Restriction on loans and advances; issuance of policy directions; remission of loans; operation and management; inspection and investigation.

### 10. The Insurance Act, 2010 [ILO 7]
- Evolution of insurance laws in Bangladesh; statutory definitions; classification of business and policies; registration and renewals; determination of premium rates; capital and deposits; margin of safety; insurable interests; investment of assets; solvency margin; loans and management; investigation and power of regulatory authority; assignment and transfer of policies; licensing of agents; survey and surveyors.

### 11. The Financial Institutions Act, 1993 [ILO 7]
- Licensing of financial institutions; minimum capital, reserve fund and liquid assets; restriction on credit facilities; investment.

### 12. The Securities and Exchange Commission Ordinance, 1969 [ILO 8]
- Issue of capital; registration and regulation of stock exchanges; regulation of issuers; prohibitions and restrictions; enquiries, penalties, orders and appeals; miscellaneous.

### 13. The Securities and Exchange Rules, 1987 [ILO 8]
- Qualification, etc., of members of stock exchange; maintenance of accounts and audit; maintenance of books of account, etc., by members; listing of a security; submission of annual report by issuers; risk-based capital adequacy requirements.

### 14. The Exchanges Demutualisation Act, 2013 [ILO 8]
- Process of converting a mutualized exchange into a demutualized one; issuance of TREC (Trading Right Entitlement Certificate); operations and management of a demutualized exchange.

## Topic-to-video map

The watch pages below were opened to verify each visible title, channel and lesson relevance. “Covered” describes the act-level scope evidenced by the chapter/one-shot lesson title; no chapter timestamps were exposed on the page, so check the linked lecture against the syllabus wording during revision.

1. **Contract Act, 1872 — all detailed syllabus items under Topic 1:** definition and essentials; communication/acceptance/revocation; voidable contracts and void agreements; consignment contracts; performance, time/place, non-performance and breach; indemnity/guarantee; bailment/pledges; and the listed agency matters. [CL- Business Laws- Chapter 04- The Contract Act, 1872- Anwaruzzaman FCA](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DAz0kbOA92o) — **CA Chamber**. This long, chapter-specific CA class is a Bangladesh-focused exam lecture and gives a structured way to revise the Act’s doctrines for written answers and application questions.

2. **Sale of Goods Act, 1930 — formation, effects and performance of the sale contract; unpaid seller’s rights; suits for breach (Topic 2).** [Sale of Goods Act, 1930 | Complete One Shot | Sections 1–66 Explained | Judiciary | Law Exams ⚖️](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TjeDWUTw44k) — **STUDY GO With ZEENAT**. A full-Act, exam-oriented revision lesson can help organize the syllabus’s sale-contract and remedy topics. **Jurisdiction warning:** the channel/video is judiciary-exam oriented in a different jurisdiction (India), not Bangladesh; use it only for general concepts and verify all statutory wording and amendments against the Bangladesh Act before an exam. No Bangladesh-specific full-Act lesson was verified.

3. **Negotiable Instruments Act, 1881 — promissory notes, bills of exchange, cheques, negotiation, payment/interest, discharge, notice of dishonour, special cheque/bill provisions and cheque-dishonour penalties (Topic 3).** [CL- Business Laws- Chapter 02 -The Negotiable Instruments Act,1881-Anwaruzzaman FCA](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3KC6JZealcM) — **CA Chamber**. This Bangladesh ICAB Certificate Level chapter lecture is directly aligned to the named Act and is useful for learning statutory distinctions and framing written answers; verify current Bangladesh provisions for cheque dishonour.

4. **Partnership Act, 1932 — nature; partner-to-partner and partner-to-third-party relations; incoming/outgoing partners; dissolution; firm registration (Topic 7).** [CL- Business Laws- Chapter 03- The Partnership Act, 1932- Anwaruzzaman FCA](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jC_hveAuBU4) — **CA Chamber**. The chapter-specific Bangladesh CA lecture supports exam revision of partner rights/liabilities, changes in membership, dissolution and registration as connected legal issues.
