5. **Bank Company Act, 1991 — all detailed syllabus items under Topic 9:** business/licensing; capital/reserves; directors/CEO; illegal transactions; acquisition; suspension/winding-up; lending restrictions; policy directions; loan remission; operation/management; inspection/investigation. [Lecture on Bank Companies Act for ICMAB LT 125 - Corporate & Business Law Paper | Analyst Skill](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1U1cBHrqtvc) — **Analyst Skill**. The title identifies an act-specific corporate/business-law lecture for ICMAB, making it a useful Bangladesh-oriented revision aid for organizing provisions into likely descriptive exam headings.

6. **Bangladesh Labour Act, 2006 — introductory context only; the watch-page description says the lecture covers the Act’s contents, organizations to which it does not apply, and statutory definitions.** [Dr. Nazrul Islam - Lecture on "Introduction to Bangladesh Labour Act 2006"](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=McaA49Cne2k) — **Dr Nazrul Islam**. The university lecturer’s Bangladesh-specific overview helps orient students to the Act and its vocabulary, but it is **not evidence of coverage** of the detailed syllabus’s specific labour subtopics below; do not treat it as a substitute for those provisions.

### NOT COVERED: no direct suitable, verified video found for these detailed subtopics

- **Topic 4 — Arbitration Act, 2001:** arbitration agreement; subject matter; methods; tribunal; proceedings; award; appeals. Search: `Bangladesh Arbitration Act 2001 arbitration agreement tribunal award lecture Bangla`.
- **Topic 5 — Trademarks Act, 2009:** registration procedures/duration; effects of registration; use of trademark. Search: `Bangladesh Trademarks Act 2009 registration duration effects lecture`.
- **Topic 6 — Competition Act, 2012:** definitions; Bangladesh Competition Commission; anti-competition contract; grievances; enquiry/order; reconsideration, penalty and appeal; Commission finances. Search: `Bangladesh Competition Act 2012 Competition Commission anti competition lecture`.
- **Topic 8 — Bangladesh Labor Act, 2006:** employment of young persons; health/hygiene/welfare; safety; working hours/leave/holidays; wages board; trade union/industrial relations; dispute resolution; Labor Court and Labor Appellate Tribunal; workers’ profit participation; provident fund; offences/penalties. The introductory video above does not verify instruction on these listed details. Search: `Bangladesh Labour Act 2006 working hours leave wages board trade union labour court provident fund lecture`.
- **Topic 10 — Insurance Act, 2010:** all 15 items in the outline (Bangladesh insurance-law evolution through survey/surveyors). Search: `Bangladesh Insurance Act 2010 registration premium solvency margin agents surveyors lecture`.
- **Topic 11 — Financial Institutions Act, 1993:** licensing; minimum capital/reserve/liquid assets; credit-facility restrictions; investment. Search: `Bangladesh Financial Institutions Act 1993 licensing capital reserve liquid assets lecture`.
- **Topic 12 — Securities and Exchange Commission Ordinance, 1969:** issue of capital; exchange registration/regulation; issuer regulation; prohibitions/restrictions; enquiries/penalties/orders/appeals; miscellaneous. Search: `Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Commission Ordinance 1969 lecture issue of capital issuers`.
- **Topic 13 — Securities and Exchange Rules, 1987:** member qualifications; accounts/audit; books; security listing; issuer annual reports; risk-based capital adequacy. Search: `Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Rules 1987 listing annual report risk based capital adequacy lecture`.
- **Topic 14 — Exchanges Demutualisation Act, 2013:** conversion; TREC issuance; demutualized-exchange operations/management. Search: `Bangladesh Exchanges Demutualisation Act 2013 TREC lecture`.

## Coverage audit

- **Topic count:** 14 statutory topics; **112 checklist subtopics** (individual syllabus provisions/subjects, with instrument types separated for counting).
- **Verified video coverage:** 44 checklist subtopics are mapped to Bangladesh-focused act-specific chapter lectures (Contract Act 17; Negotiable Instruments Act 10; Partnership Act 6; Bank Company Act 11). Five Sale of Goods subtopics have a full-Act video, but it is from another jurisdiction and is not a Bangladesh-law substitute. The labour overview is relevant but does not establish coverage of any of the 12 detailed labour items.
- **Does every syllabus subtopic have at least one verified, jurisdiction-appropriate video? No.** The nine uncovered statutory topics above account for 63 checklist subtopics. Sale of Goods has only a jurisdiction-mismatched video. The exact syllabus language is preserved above; lecture-page descriptions do not provide timestamps to prove each listed provision individually.

# 2105 Financial Management

**Semester:** Second Year — First Semester (Semester 3), 11th-batch AIS curriculum. The course list identifies 2105 as Financial Management (3 credits); the detailed-syllabus heading also reads “2105: Financial Management.” The requested code/title match, with no material discrepancy in this course entry. Source: attached University of Barishal AIS syllabus, effective from 11th Batch (Session 2022–2023), course list PDF p. 35 and detailed syllabus PDF pp. 65–66.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

The following preserves the detailed-syllabus wording and order. IDs are used in the video map. Topic count below means the 75 source-listed topic clauses/items; compound clauses retain their internal subtopic lists.

### 1. Introduction

- **1.1** Goals and functions of business finance.
- **1.2** The financial manager.
- **1.3** Objectives of finance.
- **1.4** The financial strategy process for a corporation.
- **1.5** Impact of financial markets and other external factors on a business’s financial strategy.
- **1.6** Functions of business finance.
- **1.7** Functions of financial officers.
- **1.8** Goal of finance: profit maximization versus shareholders’ wealth maximization.
- **1.9** Growth in share price.
- **1.10** Ethics in business finance.
- **1.11** Agency problems; ways to minimize agency cost: profit percentage, share ownerships, and share options.

### 2. Time Value of Money

- **2.1** Time value of money.
- **2.2** Timelines.
- **2.3** Future value and compounding.
- **2.4** Present value and discounting.
- **2.5** Opportunity cost rate.
- **2.6** Fair/equilibrium value.
- **2.7** Net present value (NPV).
- **2.8** Future and present values of an annuity.
- **2.9** Perpetuities; amortized loan and amortization schedule.
- **2.10** Market value, economic value, and internal rate of return (IRR) of an investment.

### 3. Concepts of Return and Risk

- **3.1** Uncertainty, risk, and return.
- **3.2** Risk-free and risk-adjusted return.
- **3.3** Bond returns.
- **3.4** Return from a stock investment.
- **3.5** Types of risks: business risk and financial risk; total risk and specific risk; diversifiable risk and systematic risk; single-asset risk and portfolio risk.
- **3.6** Risk measurement: variance, standard deviation, and coefficient of variation.
- **3.7** Decision-making methods under risk and uncertainty.
- **3.8** Sensitivity analysis.
- **3.9** Simulation.
- **3.10** Expected value and attitude to risk.

### 4. Introduction to Security Valuation

- **4.1** Valuation of bonds and debentures.
- **4.2** Valuation of preferred stock.
- **4.3** Valuation of common stock: Dividend Discount Model (DDM), Capital Assets Pricing Model (CAPM), Gordon Model (GM).
- **4.4** Free Cash Flow to Equity (FCE), as written in the syllabus.

### 5. Cost of Capital

- **5.1** Capital structure and its costs.
- **5.2** Comparison.
- **5.3** Calculating and interpreting the costs of different financing methods.
- **5.4** Cost of debt.
- **5.5** Cost of irredeemable debentures.
- **5.6** Cost of redeemable debentures.
- **5.7** Cost of bank loans and overdrafts.
- **5.8** Cost of preferred stock.
- **5.9** Cost of common equity.
- **5.10** Cost of retained earnings.
- **5.11** CAPM approach.
- **5.12** Bond: yield-plus-risk-premium approach.
- **5.13** Dividend-yield-plus-growth-rate approach.
- **5.14** Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) approach.
- **5.15** Cost of newly issued common stock or external equity.
- **5.16** Weighted Average Cost of Capital (WACC).
- **5.17** Rationale for WACC.
- **5.18** Marginal Cost of Capital (MCC).
- **5.19** MCC schedule and break points.
- **5.20** Factors affecting the cost of capital.
- **5.21** Different uses of cost of capital.

### 6. Capital Budgeting and Investment Appraisal

- **6.1** Overview of capital budgeting.
- **6.2** Methods of evaluating mutually exclusive projects and independent projects.
- **6.3** Investment appraisal techniques/tools: non-discounted Payback Period (PB); discounted Discounted Payback Period (DPBP), Net Present Value (NPV), Internal Rate of Return (IRR), Modified Internal Rate of Return (MIRR), and Profitability Index (PI).
- **6.4** Merits and demerits of the investment-appraisal techniques.
- **6.5** Ranking the different investment-appraisal techniques.

### 7. Sources of Financing

- **7.1** Financing for small- and medium-sized entities.
- **7.2** Short-term financing.
- **7.3** Intermediate-term financing.
- **7.4** Long-term financing.
- **7.5** Lease financing: operating lease and capital lease.
- **7.6** Factors affecting a company’s choice of finance.
- **7.7** Concepts of warranties, covenants, and guarantees.
- **7.8** Financing problems of small firms.
- **7.9** Notion of venture capital.
- **7.10** Business plan.
- **7.11** Process of venture-capital financing.
- **7.12** Methods of venture financing.
- **7.13** Problems and sources of financing small and medium enterprises and micro industries.
- **7.14** Role of banks and non-banking institutions in development of SMEs in Bangladesh.

## Topic-to-video map

Every recommended item below was opened as a direct YouTube watch page to verify its displayed title and channel and to confirm that it is a real lesson relevant to the stated syllabus topics. The coverage statements are deliberately limited to what the watch-page title/description establishes; unsupported subtopics are marked **NOT COVERED** below.

1. [Financial Management Introduction: Scope, Functions, and Profit vs. Wealth Maximization | Lecture 01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N6gnXeWjjHM) — **Riyath Ismail**. Covers **1.1–1.3, 1.6, 1.8**: scope/functions, role/objectives of financial management, and profit-versus-wealth maximization. Its exam value is the introductory written-answer framing and clear distinction between the two financial goals.
2. [Time Value of Money - Present Value vs Future Value](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cy4PiY5ERTI) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Covers **2.1, 2.3, 2.4**: TVM and worked present-/future-value calculations through compounding and discounting. The step-by-step examples are useful for numerical questions.
3. [How To Calculate The Present Value of an Annuity](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RU-osjAs6hE) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Covers the present-value portion of **2.8**. It walks through discounting a stream of periodic payments, making it a direct numerical-practice aid; the future-value-annuity portion is not established by this lesson.
4. [Chapter 8 a: Risk and Return](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=A2Kn7GKEx-o) — **Finance Lectures**. Covers **3.1** and the standard-deviation/coefficient-of-variation and expected-value portions of **3.6, 3.10**; the watch-page description explicitly lists expected return, standard deviation, coefficient of variation, and an expected-value calculation spreadsheet. Its worked quantitative measures support exam calculations and interpretation.
5. [Uncertainty and Risk in Decision Making | CIMA P2 C11 | [Summary] | #sensitivity #decisiontree #VAR](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=h8ECL9yaMcQ) — **Finance Study Tutor**. Covers **3.7, 3.8**, and the expected-value portion of **3.10**: uncertainty, sensitivity analysis, expected values, and decision trees. The concise revision format helps structure decision-analysis answers and practice expected-value calculations.
6. [Introduction to Bond Valuation | CPA Exam BAR | Finance Course](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gykgWtBLyXg) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers **4.1** and the bond-price concept relevant to **3.3**: bond terms and pricing as present value of future cash flows. Its exam-oriented explanation supports valuation computations; it is not Bangladesh-specific and does not cover Bangladesh debt-market regulation.
7. [Dividend Discount Model DDM Explained with Examples.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b7_4JEF-1uM) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers **4.2** and the DDM, CAPM-required-return, and constant-growth/Gordon-model portions of **4.3**; the watch-page description says it includes worked examples for preferred stock and dividend-paying firms. The calculations directly rehearse stock valuation and the model assumptions commonly tested.
8. [Brigham, Stock Valuation (Part 1), Financial Management](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OxegP60QvhM) — **Kirby R. Cundiff, Ph.D., CFA, CFP®**. Covers the common-stock-features/valuation introduction in **4.3**. Its financial-management-course framing provides a written-answer starting point; use the preceding DDM video for the named model calculations.
9. [Cost of Capital (WACC)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QqMvEgnTeg4) — **Kaplan UK**. Covers **5.16–5.17** and the basic weighted-cost concept in **5.1**. The watch page identifies it as a cost-of-capital masterclass about WACC, useful for explaining the weighted average and its role; it does not establish coverage of every individual financing-cost formula below.
10. [Capital Budgeting Techniques in English - NPV, IRR , Payback Period and PI, accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CO8LDV2sO6M) — **pmtycoon**. Covers **2.7, 2.10 (IRR portion), and 6.3** for PB, NPV, IRR, and PI. The lesson explicitly names these techniques and is suitable for worked-method comparison and exam practice.
11. [Sources of Business Finance Explained | Bank Loans, Trade Credit, Share Capital, Overdrafts & More](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DAZi6XcTZzE) — **Two Teachers**. Covers **7.2, 7.4** at a basic classification level, including the named short-term examples overdrafts/trade credit and longer-term bank loans/share capital. The compact overview helps frame written answers comparing business-finance sources; it is general business education, not Bangladesh-specific.
12. [Operating lease Versus a Finance Lease. CPA Exam FAR](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=909yQl92sHc) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers the operating-versus-capital/finance lease concepts in **7.5**. Its classification explanation is useful for short comparison answers, **but it teaches US GAAP/ASC 842 CPA-exam lease classification**; do not treat its criteria as Bangladesh law or as a Bangladesh-specific regulatory lesson.
13. [What is Venture Capital? | Entrepreneurship | From A Business Professor](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HMnjP6rHNdo) — **Business School 101**. Covers **7.9, 7.11** at an introductory level: what venture capital is, its stages, and examples, as identified in the watch-page description. It gives an accessible conceptual base for written answers; it does not establish the syllabus’s detailed venture-financing methods or business-plan requirements.

### Clearly uncovered or only partially covered

- **NOT COVERED — 1.4–1.5, 1.7, 1.9–1.11:** corporate financial-strategy process and external influences; financial officers’ duties; share-price growth; finance ethics; agency problems and the specified incentive mechanisms (profit percentage, share ownership, share options). Search YouTube for: `corporate financial strategy process external factors financial markets lecture`, `financial manager ethics agency problem agency costs profit sharing stock options lecture`.
- **NOT COVERED — 2.2, 2.5–2.6, market/economic-value portion of 2.10, 2.9:** timelines; opportunity-cost/discount rate; fair/equilibrium value; market and economic value; perpetuities; amortized-loan calculations and amortization schedules. **Partially covered — 2.8:** present value of an annuity is covered, but future value of an annuity is not verified. IRR within 2.10 is covered via capital budgeting, but market/economic valuation is not. Search: `finance timelines opportunity cost rate equilibrium value market value economic value perpetuity amortized loan amortization schedule worked examples`, `future value of ordinary annuity worked examples`.
- **NOT COVERED — 3.2–3.5, 3.9:** risk-free versus risk-adjusted return; bond-return calculation (as distinct from bond valuation); stock-return calculation; the syllabus’s full risk taxonomy (business/financial, total/specific, diversifiable/systematic, single-asset/portfolio); and simulation. **Partially covered — 3.6:** standard deviation and coefficient of variation are verified, but variance is not explicitly established. **Partially covered — 3.7 and 3.10:** expected value/decision-tree practice is covered, but the full set of decision rules and risk attitudes is not. Search: `risk free risk adjusted return bond holding period return stock return business financial systematic diversifiable risk examples`, `variance risk measurement expected return portfolio single asset lecture`, `simulation decision making under risk finance examples`, `risk attitude expected value decision rules`.
- **PARTIALLY COVERED — 4.3:** DDM, Gordon growth, and CAPM as required-return input have direct coverage; a separate CAPM-based valuation exercise beyond that input is not verified. **NOT COVERED — 4.4:** Free Cash Flow to Equity (FCE). Search: `CAPM stock valuation worked example`, `free cash flow to equity FCFE valuation worked example`.
- **NOT COVERED — 5.2–5.15, 5.18–5.21:** comparison of financing costs; cost calculations for debt, irredeemable/redeemable debentures, bank loans/overdrafts, preferred stock, common equity, retained earnings, new equity; CAPM, bond-yield-plus-risk-premium, dividend-yield-plus-growth, and DCF approaches; MCC, MCC schedule/break points; factors affecting and uses of cost of capital. The WACC recommendation is only a conceptual starting point, not evidence of this full syllabus. Search: `cost of capital numerical problems redeemable irredeemable debentures bank loan overdraft preferred shares retained earnings`, `CAPM bond yield risk premium dividend growth DCF cost of equity problems`, `marginal cost of capital schedule break points factors uses worked example`.
- **PARTIALLY COVERED — 6.3:** PB, NPV, IRR and PI have a direct lesson, but DPBP and MIRR are not verified. **NOT COVERED — 6.1–6.2, 6.4–6.5:** capital-budgeting overview, mutually exclusive versus independent project choice, method merits/demerits, and ranking. Search: `capital budgeting mutually exclusive independent projects ranking appraisal techniques advantages disadvantages`, `discounted payback period MIRR worked examples`.
- **NOT COVERED — 7.1, 7.3, 7.6–7.8, 7.10, 7.12–7.14:** SME financing as a whole; intermediate-term finance; financing-choice factors; warranties/covenants/guarantees; small-firm financing problems; business plans; venture-financing methods; SME/micro-industry financing problems and sources; and Bangladesh banks/NBFIs’ SME-development role. The general sources and venture-capital videos above are not Bangladesh regulatory or institution-specific lessons. Search: `Bangladesh SME financing Bangladesh Bank SME Foundation banks NBFIs micro industries lecture`, `Bangladesh SME finance role banks non bank financial institutions training Bangla`, `intermediate term finance warranties covenants guarantees venture financing methods business plan lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 75 source-listed syllabus clauses/items across the seven detailed-syllabus headings. **Verified video coverage:** yes for the clearly stated portions above; **no**, not every detailed syllabus topic has a verified video. Gaps and partial coverage are individually called out above with precise YouTube search keywords; in particular, no suitable Bangladesh-specific exam lesson was verified for SME institutions/financing. All 13 listed video links were opened as YouTube watch pages; watch-page titles and channels are transcribed from those pages, not inferred from search snippets.

# Semester 4 — Second Year, Second Semester

# 2201 Advanced Financial Accounting–II

**Semester:** Semester 4 — Second Year, Second Semester (BBA, 11th-batch AIS curriculum)

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

Transcribed from the **Detailed Course Contents** under “SECOND YEAR SECOND SEMESTER” in the attached University of Barishal AIS syllabus. The outline below retains all seven listed units and their detailed items.

1. **Financial Instruments** (ILO 3, ILO 4)
   - Meaning of “Financial Instrument”; classification of Financial Assets; classification of Financial Instruments as Liability or Equity; Compound Financial Instrument.
   - Recognition and Derecognition of Financial Assets and Liabilities; Measurement of Financial Assets and Liabilities; considerations on Fair Value Measurement; Impairment of Financial Assets; Reclassification of Financial Assets; accounting treatment for gain or loss on Financial Assets and Liabilities; Ethical and Judgement Issues.
   - Presentation and Disclosure Requirements as per IAS 32 and IFRS 7; named standards: IAS 32, IFRS 7, IFRS 9 and IFRS 13.
2. **Liquidation and Bankruptcy** (ILO 5)
   - Individual; Partnership Firms and Company; Bankruptcy Act 1997; Winding up in Insolvency and by the Court; Voluntary Winding Up; Powers of the Liquidators; Liquidator’s Accounts; Proof of Debts; Priority of Payment of Debts; Accounting for Liquidation and Bankruptcy.
3. **Branch and Departmental Accounts**
   - Benefits of Branches; Branches; pricing of goods sent to Branches by Head Office; keeps Branch books within its own accounting system; Stock and Debtors Systems; Wholesale Branch System; Branch keeping separate records; incorporation of Trial Balance into Head Office book; preparation of Final Accounts.
4. **Hire Purchase and Installment Accounting**
   - **Hire purchase:** Definition; accounting treatment in the books of buyer and seller; treatment of repossession.
   - **Installment:** Hire purchase vs. Installment; determination of Installments; accounting treatment for installment in both purchaser and seller books.
5. **Royalty Accounts**
   - Royalty; Minimum Rent and Short working defined; Entries in Books of Accounts; Royalty Receivable and Payable.
6. **Non-Current Liabilities** (ILO 3)
   - Nature and Types of Non-Current Liabilities; Types and Issuance of Bonds; Valuation and Accounting for Bonds Payable; Cost of Issuing Bonds; accounting for Long-term Notes Payable and Mortgage Payable; Extinguishment of Non-Current Liabilities; Presentation, Disclosure and Analysis of Non-Current Liabilities; IFRS 9: Financial Instruments.
7. **The Effects of Changes in Foreign Exchange Rates**
   - Definitions; foreign currency transactions; translation from the functional currency to the presentation currency; disposal of a foreign operation; tax effects of exchange differences; convenience translations; disclosures; IAS 21.

> **Syllabus discrepancy:** The course-list table and detailed-syllabus heading both place course **2201** in Second Year, Second Semester and give the title Advanced Financial Accounting–II. The objectives/ILOs also describe business combination, investments in associates and joint ventures, separate/consolidated financial statements, segment reporting and related-party disclosure, but those subjects are not in the detailed-content list (except liquidation); the detailed list instead includes Branch/Departmental Accounts, Hire Purchase/Installment, Royalty, Non-Current Liabilities and IAS 21. This fragment follows the requested detailed-content section, not the objectives or the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

## Topic-to-video map

All links below are direct YouTube watch pages opened to verify the displayed title, channel and lesson relevance. “Not covered” means no direct suitable lesson was verified for that specific syllabus point in this search; suggested search phrases are given rather than guessed links.

### 1. Financial Instruments

- [Financial Instrument |Intro & Recognition| (IAS 32, IFRS 9 & IFRS 7) #ican #ias #icag](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ROcoKPsQpzs) — **RONAS Academy**. Covers the instrument concept, introductory classification and recognition under the named standards; the actual lesson description discusses financial-asset and financial-liability categories. Its exam value is a focused standards-based introduction to the vocabulary and recognition starting point.
- [IFRS 9 Explained: Amortized Cost, FVOCI & FVTPL Examples](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=515mnGgSrgk) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers classification and measurement examples for amortized cost, FVOCI and FVTPL, with journal-entry examples for assets/liabilities and bond transaction costs. Worked classifications and entries are directly useful for numerical and treatment questions.
- [IFRS 9 Expected Credit Losses: The 3 Stages Explained](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x0OsNn-qm6c) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers the IFRS 9 impairment model, 12-month versus lifetime expected credit losses and interest treatment as credit risk changes. It helps structure an impairment answer and distinguish stages in an exam question.
- [Financial Instrument Pt.3 |Compound Instrument| (IAS 32, IFRS 9 & IFRS 7) #ican #ias #icag](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DxWcxE7ffMo) — **RONAS Academy**. Covers compound financial instruments under IAS 32/IFRS 9/IFRS 7. It is a course-like lecture dedicated to a common classification-and-components written or calculation problem.
- [IFRS 13 : Fair Value Measurement |All You Need To Know About Fair Value| #acca #icag #icai #ican](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-Xn297VcL6c) — **RONAS Academy**. Covers fair-value measurement under IFRS 13. It gives a standards-specific conceptual base for the syllabus’s fair-value measurement considerations.
- [Financial instruments – Disclosure (IFRS 7) - CIMA F3 lecture](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WkivsztUoks) — **OpenTuition**. Covers IFRS 7 financial-instrument disclosure. It helps organize a written disclosure answer around the standard’s disclosure focus.

**NOT COVERED:** Derecognition of financial assets/liabilities (search: `IFRS 9 derecognition financial assets liabilities worked example`); reclassification of financial assets (search: `IFRS 9 reclassification financial assets accounting example`); ethical and judgement issues (search: `ethical judgement issues financial instruments IFRS 9 accounting`). The selected lessons address recognition, classification, measurement, impairment and IFRS 7 disclosure, but do not demonstrably teach these three syllabus points.

### 2. Liquidation and Bankruptcy

- [Chapter 7 Bankruptcy | Liquidation Accounting | Advanced Accounting CPA REG](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WuEpCXDqZk4) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers liquidation accounting in a **U.S. Chapter 7** bankruptcy context, including the process, voluntary/involuntary petitions and trustee role/duties. It offers a useful conceptual comparison for liquidation and written discussion, **not Bangladesh law or the Bankruptcy Act 1997**.

**NOT COVERED — Bangladesh law and detailed syllabus procedures:** individual bankruptcy; partnership-firm bankruptcy; Bangladesh Bankruptcy Act 1997; winding up in insolvency; winding up by the Court; voluntary winding up under Bangladesh law; Bangladesh liquidator powers; liquidator’s accounts/statement; proof of debts; priority of payment of debts under Bangladesh law. No Bangladesh-specific suitable YouTube lesson was verified. Search phrases: `Bangladesh Bankruptcy Act 1997 accounting liquidator statement proof of debts`; `Bangladesh Bankruptcy Act 1997 priority of debts liquidation`; `Bangladesh company winding up insolvency court voluntary liquidator powers lecture`. The video above teaches U.S. federal Chapter 7, so its legal rules and trustee terminology must not be used as Bangladesh rules.

### 3. Branch and Departmental Accounts

- [Branch Account - Format & Concept - Financial Accounting - By Saheb Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F-p8g1TsSGw) — **Saheb Academy**. Covers branch-account concepts, types of branches and the debtor-system format. The clear format-led introduction is useful for explaining basic branch accounts and setting out a written answer.
- [Branch Account - Invoice Price - By Saheb Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1n99ZPbdjwY) — **Saheb Academy**. Covers the invoice-price concept and accounting treatment when goods are sent to a branch at invoice price. Its stated problem-solving focus supports calculation and journal/account practice.
- [Branch Accounting part-3 | Branch Stock & Debtors Method | Financial Accounting | Sem 1st | CWG](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SVE6cY8d_-I) — **CWG for BCom**. Covers the branch stock-and-debtors method. It is a method-specific lecture for practicing the system’s account structure.
- [#25 WHOLESALE BRANCH SYSTEM - BRANCH ACCOUNTS](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WbAC-1S7dPA) — **COMMERCE CLARIFIED**. Covers the Wholesale Branch System. Its narrowly titled lesson is useful for revising that named method separately from stock-and-debtors accounting.
- [CA Inter| Advanced Accounting| Branch Accounts| Independent Branches| CA Suraj lakhotia](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nZybKlMtM5Q) — **AVINASH LEARNING - CA Foundation, Inter & Final**. Covers independent-branch concepts, transactions and entries; independent branches keep separate accounting records. The worked treatment supports exam practice on branches maintaining their own books.
- [Branch Accounts/Incorporation of branch Trial Balance in Head office book/Problem 1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hgPgUeoR2ts) — **Commerce Class Partner**. Covers incorporation of a branch trial balance into the head-office book through a stated problem. It gives a direct worked format for the incorporation question.

**NOT COVERED:** Benefits of branches (search: `advantages benefits of branch accounting exam answer`); preparation of branch final accounts as a complete final-account problem (search: `branch accounting final accounts solved problem head office`). The incorporation lesson is directly about trial-balance incorporation; its watch-page description does not establish full final-account preparation. Departmental-account allocation is not separately listed in the detailed contents, and no extra topic has been added for it.

### 4. Hire Purchase and Installment Accounting

- [#3 Hire Purchase System - Problem 1 -By Saheb Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=h4AEZJSgUJ8) — **Saheb Academy**. Solves a hire-purchase accounting problem and demonstrates working-note/problem technique. It is useful practice for setting out the calculation and account treatment in an exam answer.
- [Hire Purchase - Partial Repossession ~ For B.Com / M.Com](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=B-Ak1pWu4D8) — **CA. Naresh Aggarwal**. Explains the procedure for partial repossession in hire purchase. It is especially useful for a stepwise repossession treatment question.
- [Accounting for Hire Purchase Transactions and Payment by Installment System (Financial Accounting)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zmR4077w_rY) — **EZIKAN ACADEMY**. Covers accounting for hire-purchase transactions and the installment system. The paired treatment offers revision of both named systems, although its watch-page description does not specify entries separately for both counterparties.

**NOT COVERED:** Formal definition of hire purchase (search: `hire purchase definition accounting rights ownership exam`); hire purchase versus installment comparison (search: `hire purchase versus installment system differences accounting`); determination/calculation of installments (search: `installment purchase accounting calculate installments worked problem`); accounting treatment specifically in both buyer/purchaser and seller/vendor books for both systems (search: `hire purchase installment system journal entries purchaser vendor books worked problem`). The verified lessons are relevant to the systems and repossession, but their watch-page descriptions do not establish all these requested distinctions and party-by-party entries.

### 5. Royalty Accounts

- [ACCOUNTING FOR ROYALTIES (PART 1)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NeAA0zculW0) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. Explains royalty-account concepts and a working schedule for the tenant/landlord books, supporting the meaning of royalty, entries, and receivable/payable treatment. Its step-by-step schedule is useful for setting up the main exam accounts.
- [Royalty Account Part-5 | Numerical Question :- Solution | B.Com 1st Year](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=T9q22baVGq4) — **CWG for BCom**. Works a minimum-rent question with journal entries in the lessor’s books and shortworking. It is useful for rehearsing the syllabus’s main royalty calculation and entry pattern.

**Coverage:** royalty; minimum rent; shortworking; entries in books of accounts; royalty receivable/payable are represented by these two lessons. The royalty-accounting examples are from Indian B.Com teaching; they address accounting methods, not Bangladesh-specific law.

### 6. Non-Current Liabilities

- [Bonds Payable: Effective Interest Method, Discount & Premium](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TGOinJSwZHE) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Works bond pricing, discount/premium amortization schedules, issuance and interest journal entries. It is strong numerical practice for valuation and accounting of bonds payable.
- [Long Term Liabilities: Notes payable](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KCCpyWT-pS0) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers interest-bearing and zero-interest-bearing long-term notes, present-value measurement, effective-interest discount amortization and imputed interest. The examples help with note valuation and subsequent accounting.
- [How to Account for Early Retirement of Bonds](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QZBwaSQqRSs) — **Edspira**. Covers early retirement/extinguishment of bonds and the accounting steps at redemption before maturity. It is useful practice for the carrying-value, reacquisition and gain/loss logic.

**NOT COVERED:** Types of bonds beyond issuance/discount-premium mechanics (search: `types of bonds accounting debentures secured unsecured redeemable lecture`); mortgage payable accounting (search: `mortgage payable accounting long term liabilities worked example`); presentation, disclosure and analysis of non-current liabilities (search: `IFRS non-current liabilities presentation disclosure ratio analysis exam`). The IFRS 9 classification/measurement lesson above also addresses bond transaction costs, supporting the course’s cost-of-issuing-bonds item; the mortgage and presentation/disclosure/analysis items remain gaps.

### 7. The Effects of Changes in Foreign Exchange Rates

- [IAS 21 - Lecture - Part 1 of 2 (20 Min)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=k2kyRxtXIRc) — **CA Campus**. Part one of a two-part IAS 21 lecture, addressing foreign-currency translation and financial-statement effects. It provides a concise standards overview for exam revision.
- [IAS 21 - Lecture - Part 2 of 2 (13 Min)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=divHmebGsuQ) — **CA Campus**. Completes the same IAS 21 lecture series on currency translation and reporting effects. Using both parts gives students the linked lesson rather than an isolated search result.
- [IAS 21 Journal Entries | Effects of Changes in Foreign Exchange Rates](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eLSmIwHq6DA) — **Counttuts**. Explains IAS 21 and works journal entries for changes in foreign-exchange rates. It is the most directly problem-oriented selection for foreign-currency transaction entries.
- [IAS 21: Overseas Subsidiaries and Exchange Differences | Old But Gold Series](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=p5-YYMHh4IE) — **Tom Clendon ACCA SBR**. Discusses overseas subsidiaries and exchange differences under IAS 21. Its exam-focused explanation is useful for foreign-operation translation and exchange-difference treatment.

**NOT COVERED:** disposal of a foreign operation as a distinct exam question (search: `IAS 21 disposal of foreign operation exchange differences worked example`); tax effects of exchange differences (search: `IAS 21 tax effects exchange differences example`); convenience translations (search: `IAS 21 convenience translation foreign currency financial statements`); IAS 21 disclosure requirements (search: `IAS 21 disclosure requirements foreign currency operations exam`). The selected videos directly address IAS 21, transactions/journal entries, translation and overseas subsidiaries, but these four narrower items were not demonstrably covered by the opened watch-page descriptions.

## Coverage audit

- **Detailed syllabus topic count:** 61 listed content items across the seven detailed units (counting each semicolon-separated syllabus item as one; the standards are named within the relevant disclosure item).
- **Unique video count:** 25 direct watch-page links; each selected watch page was opened and checked for its displayed title, channel and relevant lesson context. No links are repeated.
- **Is every topic covered by a verified video?** **No.** Every syllabus item is nevertheless accounted for above as mapped coverage or explicitly **NOT COVERED**. Major gaps are Bangladesh-specific Bankruptcy Act 1997/winding-up law and priority/proof procedures; selected financial-instrument judgment/derecognition/reclassification points; several exact hire-purchase party/comparison/calculation requirements; branch benefits/full final accounts; mortgage-liability and liability-disclosure/analysis content; and specific IAS 21 disposal/tax/convenience/disclosure points.
- **Jurisdiction caveat:** the liquidation-accounting lesson teaches U.S. Chapter 7, not Bangladesh’s Bankruptcy Act 1997. Other regional accounting tutorials are used only for general accounting procedures, not Bangladesh law.

# 2202 Corporate Laws, Governance and Secretarial Practices

**Semester:** Semester 4 — Second Year, Second Semester (3 credits)

**Source and discrepancy:** University of Barishal, BBA in Accounting & Information Systems, 11th-batch curriculum (effective 2022–2023), *Detailed Syllabus*, PDF pp. 70–71. I used the 11th-batch AIS detailed syllabus, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum. The curriculum table labels code 2202 “Corporate Laws and Secretarial Practices,” while the detailed-syllabus heading for the same code is “Corporate Laws, Governance and Secretarial Practices”; the detailed-syllabus title is used here. In the director list, “Disqualification” appears twice; both occurrences are retained below.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

### Corporate Laws and Governance (Weight 80%)

1. **Overview, Constitution and Incorporation [ILO 1]:** Background and evolution of company law in Bangladesh; statutory definitions under the Companies Act 1994; Memorandum of Association (MoA); Articles of Association (AoA); companies limited by guarantee.
2. **Share Capital and Registration [ILO 2]:** Distribution of share capital; reduction of share capital; variation of shareholders’ rights; registration of an unlimited company as limited; reserve capital of a limited company; unlimited liability of directors.
3. **Management, Administration and Books of Records [ILO 3]:** Meetings and proceedings—Annual General Meeting (AGM), Statutory Meeting, Extraordinary General Meeting (EGM), Board Meeting; provision for vote; appointment of directors; duties of directors; disqualification of directors; removal of directors; disqualification; vacation of office of director; loan to director; director not to hold the office of profit; conflicts of interest; penalties; offences; return of allotment; balance sheet; profit and loss accounts; authentication of balance sheet and profit and loss accounts; right of members to copies of account and report.
4. **Winding Up [ILO 4]:** Mode of winding up; winding up by court; voluntary winding up; winding up by creditors.
5. **Corporate Governance (CG) Concept and Conceptual Framework [ILO 5]:** Definition and importance of CG; theoretical frameworks—Agency Theory, Stewardship Theory, Transaction Cost Economics, Stakeholder Theory, Resource Dependence Theory, Theory of Managerial Hegemony; CG systems—insider system and outsider system; CG elements—internal mechanisms and external mechanisms.
6. **Corporate Governance Mechanisms [ILO 6]:** Formation and size of the Board of Directors (BoD); role of directors; types of BoDs; women’s representation on the board; power and duties of directors; proceedings of directors; splitting the roles of Chairman and Chief Executive Officer; board committees—Audit Committee, Nomination Committee, Remuneration Committee; training and orientation of board members; performance evaluation of the BoD; internal control—definition, components of internal-control systems, limitations of internal-control systems; assessing the role of internal audit; ownership structure; external mechanisms—monitoring by blockholders and analysts, external auditors, competition and takeovers, regulation and enforcement.
7. **Corporate Governance Reforms [ILO 7]:** “Shareholder-led” (“principles-based”) or “regulator-led” (“rules-based”) approach to CG; global best practices—OECD Principles, SAFA best practices, UNCTAD disclosure requirements; CG reforms in Bangladesh; voluntary Code of BEI; BSEC CG Guidelines—contents and implementation status.
8. **Corporate Ethics [ILO 8]:** Corporate ethics versus corporate responsibility; promoting and reinforcing corporate ethical culture; ethical norms; ethical behavior and internal governance mechanisms; ethical leadership from the BoD; code of ethics; whistle-blowing policies and procedures to support ethical behavior; ethical audit.

### Secretarial Practices (Weight 20%)

9. **The Role and Authority of Company Secretary [ILO 9]:** Need for and importance of a company secretary; qualifications; independence; appointment; general provisions; developing corporate-governance policies and best practices; legal and organizational support of the Board of Directors; protecting shareholder rights; disclosures and transparency.
10. **The Meeting Processes [ILO 10]:** Convening meetings—AGM, Statutory Meeting, EGM, Board Meeting; frequency; quorum; attendance; chairman; passing resolutions by circulation; minutes; preservation of minutes and other records; share transfer and transmission; AGM/EGM; meetings through tele/video conferencing.
11. **Appointment of Statutory Auditor [ILO 11]:** Appointment, removal and remuneration of the statutory auditor under the Companies Act 1994; scope of audit; duties and rights of auditors.
12. **Appointment of CG Auditor [ILO 11]:** Appointment of a Corporate Governance Auditor under the CG Code.
13. **Corporate Reporting [ILO 12]:** Director’s Report under the Companies Act 1994; submission of return to RJSC&F; disclosure of Price Sensitive Information (PSI); disclosure on appointment or reappointment of directors; declaration/certification by CEO and CFO to the Board on the year-end financial statements; IPO issue under the Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Commission (Public Issue) Rules, 2015; BSEC Notification of 20 June 2018.

## Topic-to-video map

Each link below is a direct YouTube watch page opened during research; title and channel were checked on that page. Bangladesh statute/regulation videos are prioritized for Bangladesh-law topics. The broader governance, ethics and internal-control lessons are supplementary concepts, not Bangladesh legal authority.

1. **[Overview of Bangladesh Companies Act, 1994](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=f8f2Vvw8Fv0)** — **Md. Shafiqul Alam LL.B, FCS, FCA, FCMA.** Covers the Act’s preliminary definitions and the formation/incorporation framework, including MoA, AoA and registration (introductory treatment; not every formation form is confirmed). **Exam use:** A Bangladesh-law foundation for defining key terms and organizing an answer on company constitution and incorporation.
2. **[Overview of Bangladesh Companies Act, 1994; Section 11-29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rLJyyoLdYBU)** — **Md. Shafiqul Alam LL.B, FCS, FCA, FCMA.** A Bangladesh Companies Act lesson specifically identified by section range; use it alongside the overview for the course’s constitution/incorporation/registration material, not as evidence that every subtopic in those units is covered. **Exam use:** Reading the statute by section can help anchor short answers to the Bangladesh Act; the page description is broad, so verify individual section details against the Act and class notes.
3. **[Authorized Capital Vs Paid Up Capital In Bangla With Important Fact In Company Registration](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QWVi0Y4zHkk)** — **LAWSNLAWYERS.** Covers authorized versus paid-up capital and company-registration context; this is only a terminology supplement for share-capital distribution, not coverage of the full statutory rules on capital. **Exam use:** The direct comparison helps distinguish basic capital terms in definition questions.
4. **[Discussion on The Companies Act (Section-83 to 90) in Bangla | Discussion on The Companies Act-1994](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ih1z9X6hiec)** — **Md. Shafiqul Alam LL.B, FCS, FCA, FCMA.** Its description quotes section 83 on statutory meetings and reports, directly matching the Statutory Meeting part of the syllabus. **Exam use:** A Bangladesh Act-based explanation supports a written answer on the statutory meeting and statutory report.
5. **[Practical Discussion on The Companies Act (Section-90 to110)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TlufankINPI)** — **Md. Shafiqul Alam LL.B, FCS, FCA, FCMA.** The page identifies Bangladesh Companies Act sections 90–110 and opens with section 90 on directors being obligatory; useful for the director-appointment/board-composition portion, but not verified for every listed director duty or disqualification. **Exam use:** The statutory-section sequence and the explicit board-size provision give a concrete basis for director-composition answers.
6. **[Provisions Related to Books of Accounts U/S:181-185 of Companies Act-94 & Financial Reporting Act-15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xFlTlE-pzkU)** — **Md. Shafiqul Alam LL.B, FCS, FCA, FCMA.** Bangladesh-focused lesson on books of accounts under Companies Act 1994 sections 181–185 and the Financial Reporting Act 2015. **Exam use:** It gives a statute-linked starting point for written answers about company books and record-keeping; do not treat the title alone as coverage of all balance-sheet/report authentication requirements.
7. **[Exploring Corporate Governance Theories: Agency, Stakeholder, Stewardship, and More](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JzDdCgwWDFM)** — **The Business Learning Hub.** The description expressly covers Agency Theory, Stakeholder Theory, Stewardship Theory and Resource Dependency Theory (the syllabus calls this Resource Dependence Theory). **Exam use:** A concise compare-and-contrast review supports theory-definition and distinction questions; the description also names Institutional Theory, which is not a syllabus substitute for the two missing theories.
8. **[The basics of Corporate Governance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7CMUfDZ0Kzk)** — **Practice Tests Academy.** Explains why corporate governance is needed, what it is and what constitutes best practice; general, not Bangladesh-code-specific. **Exam use:** Useful for framing definition-and-importance answers before writing the Bangladesh-specific application.
9. **[AA Topic Explainer: Corporate Governance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-ELI7YRwUek)** — **ACCA Student Study Resources.** An ACCA expert-tutor lesson on the corporate-governance topic; general governance review, not Bangladesh-specific regulation. **Exam use:** The tutor-led exam-topic format helps structure a concise governance explanation; supplement it with Bangladesh law/code notes.
10. **[AA Topic Explainer: Internal Controls and Tests of Control](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=A73ujYVw_WQ)** — **ACCA Student Study Resources.** Covers systems of internal controls and tests of controls; this is a general ACCA audit lesson, not Bangladesh company-law guidance. **Exam use:** It gives a practical conceptual explanation useful for internal-control-system answers, but does not establish full syllabus coverage of components, limitations, or Bangladesh requirements.
11. **[10 Business Ethics Concepts You Need to Know (4-Minute Overview)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TCbn2mwTpnQ)** — **The Business Learning Hub.** The description covers business ethics concepts including CSR, sustainability and anti-corruption; general, not jurisdiction-specific. **Exam use:** Helps distinguish corporate ethics and responsibility and gives examples for short written answers; the brief overview is not a replacement for the syllabus’s governance mechanisms.
12. **[Amendments of Public Issue Rules'15 | IPO Rules in Bangladesh | Changes in IPO Rules | IPO in Bangla](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Zwm8KkXLVbs)** — **Md. Shafiqul Alam LL.B, FCS, FCA, FCMA.** Bangladesh-specific discussion of Public Issue Rules 2015 amendments; relevant only to the syllabus’s IPO/Public Issue Rules reference. **Exam use:** It provides a named-rule anchor for IPO disclosure/compliance answers. Its 5-year-old upload may not reflect later amendments; the syllabus’s dated rule and 20 June 2018 notification should be checked against current BSEC material before relying on current compliance details.
13. **[Art of Conducting General Meeting as per Secretarial Standard -2](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=igAF7OwMWNs)** — **The Institute of Company Secretaries of India.** A meeting-conduct lesson under India’s Secretarial Standard-2; it is not Bangladesh law and should be used only as a process illustration, not for Bangladesh notice, quorum, voting or minute-taking requirements. **Exam use:** The meeting-conduct framing may help visualize a general meeting sequence, while all legal answers must follow Bangladesh provisions.

### Direct-video gaps and precise YouTube searches

The following syllabus details did not have a direct, suitable video verified in this search. The cited videos above cover only the portions identified in their coverage statements.

- **Unit 1:** Background/evolution of Bangladesh company law and companies limited by guarantee — **NOT COVERED**. Search: `Bangladesh Companies Act 1994 history companies limited by guarantee lecture`.
- **Unit 2:** Full statutory distribution/allotment of share capital; reduction of capital; variation of shareholder rights; registering an unlimited company as limited; reserve capital; unlimited director liability — **NOT COVERED** (authorized-versus-paid-up comparison is only a terminology supplement). Search: `Bangladesh Companies Act 1994 share capital reduction variation rights reserve capital unlimited company limited lecture`.
- **Unit 3:** AGM, EGM and Board Meeting provisions beyond statutory meeting; voting; director duties, disqualification, removal, repeat “disqualification,” vacation of office, loans, office of profit, conflicts, penalties/offences; return of allotment; balance sheet and profit-and-loss accounts; authentication; members’ right to copies of accounts/reports — **NOT COVERED**. Search: `Bangladesh Companies Act 1994 directors duties disqualification removal loan office of profit sections lecture`; `Bangladesh Companies Act 1994 annual general meeting voting return of allotment accounts authentication lesson`.
- **Unit 4:** All modes of winding up, including court, voluntary and creditors’ winding up — **NOT COVERED**. Search: `Bangladesh Companies Act 1994 winding up by court voluntary creditors lecture`.
- **Unit 5:** Transaction Cost Economics; Theory of Managerial Hegemony; insider/outsider systems; internal/external CG mechanisms — **NOT COVERED**. The theories video covers only the four named syllabus theories in item 7. Search: `corporate governance transaction cost economics managerial hegemony insider outsider systems lecture`.
- **Unit 6:** Board formation, role/types/power/duties/proceedings, women’s representation, chair–CEO split; audit/nomination/remuneration committees; board training/orientation and performance evaluation; internal-control components and limitations; internal-audit role; ownership structure; blockholders/analysts, external auditors, competition/takeovers, regulation/enforcement — **NOT COVERED** by a video verified to teach all these specific items. The ACCA internal-control lesson covers only systems/tests at a general level. Search: `corporate governance board mechanisms women directors committees chairman CEO split internal audit ownership lecture`; `corporate governance external mechanisms blockholders analysts takeovers enforcement lecture`.
- **Unit 7:** Principles-based versus rules-based reform; OECD Principles; SAFA best practices; UNCTAD disclosure requirements; Bangladesh CG reforms; BEI voluntary code; BSEC CG Guidelines’ contents and implementation — **NOT COVERED**. The IPO video is about Public Issue Rules, not the BSEC CG Code. Search: `Bangladesh BSEC Corporate Governance Code 2018 contents implementation lecture`; `OECD principles SAFA UNCTAD corporate governance disclosure lecture Bangladesh BEI code`.
- **Unit 8:** Promoting/reinforcing ethical culture; ethical norms in detail; behavior and internal governance mechanisms; ethical leadership from the board; code of ethics; whistle-blowing policies/procedures; ethical audit — **NOT COVERED**. Search: `corporate ethical culture board leadership code ethics whistleblowing policy ethical audit lecture`.
- **Unit 9:** All Bangladesh-specific company-secretary role/authority topics—importance, qualifications, independence, appointment, general provisions, governance-policy development, board support, shareholder rights, disclosure/transparency — **NOT COVERED**. Search: `Bangladesh company secretary role qualifications appointment independence board support lecture`.
- **Unit 10:** Bangladesh-specific meeting frequency, quorum, attendance, chair, circular resolutions, minutes/records, share transfer/transmission, AGM/EGM and tele/video meetings — **NOT COVERED**. The India Secretarial Standard video is only a process illustration and is excluded from Bangladesh-law coverage. Search: `Bangladesh Companies Act 1994 meeting quorum minutes resolution by circulation share transfer video conferencing lecture`.
- **Unit 11:** Appointment/removal/remuneration of statutory auditors under the Companies Act 1994; scope, duties and rights — **NOT COVERED**. Search: `Bangladesh Companies Act 1994 section 210 auditor appointment removal remuneration rights duties lecture`.
- **Unit 12:** Appointment of a Corporate Governance Auditor under the CG Code — **NOT COVERED**. Search: `Bangladesh BSEC Corporate Governance Code corporate governance auditor appointment lecture`.
- **Unit 13:** Director’s Report; RJSC&F return submission; PSI disclosure; appointment/reappointment disclosures; CEO/CFO financial-statement certification; 20 June 2018 BSEC Notification — **NOT COVERED**, except the IPO/Public Issue Rules 2015 amendment lesson (item 12). Search: `Bangladesh Companies Act 1994 directors report RJSC return PSI CEO CFO certification lecture`; `BSEC 20 June 2018 notification corporate governance CEO CFO certification Bangladesh lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 13 numbered syllabus units (8 Corporate Laws and Governance + 5 Secretarial Practices). **Verified videos:** 13 unique direct watch pages; each title and channel was checked on its YouTube watch page. **Does every detailed syllabus subtopic have a verified video? No.** Some portions of Units 1–3, 5–8 and 13 have direct matches; the gaps above remain uncovered. Units 4, 9, 11 and 12 have no Bangladesh-specific suitable video verified. Unit 10 has only a cross-jurisdiction process illustration, not Bangladesh-law coverage. The BSEC Public Issue Rules lesson is dated; no BSEC Code/2018 notification lesson was verified.

# 2203 Business Statistics–II

**Semester:** Semester 4 — Second Year, Second Semester (11th-batch AIS curriculum, 3 credits)

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

Transcribed from the PDF’s **DETAILED SYLLABUS** entry headed **“2203: Business Statistics-II”** (not the earlier Management Studies curriculum). The outline preserves its topic order and unusual wording where that may matter:

1. **Random Variables and Probability Distribution:** definition of random variable and its types; probability distributions of a random variable; discrete and continuous probability distributions; binomial, Poisson, normal and standard normal distributions; standard normal variate; important properties of these distributions; fitting binomial and Poisson distributions; numerical problems.
2. **Sampling Distribution:** concept of sampling distribution; Central Limit Theorem (CLT) and business applications; sampling distribution of samples drawn with and without replacement; sampling distribution of difference between two sample means (with and without replacement); sampling distribution of sample proportion (with and without replacement); sampling distribution of difference between two sample proportions (with and without replacement); numerical problems.
3. **Estimation and Confidence Intervals:** estimation and its types; point and interval estimation; confidence interval; properties of a good estimator; estimation methods; confidence-interval estimation for a mean (known/unknown, large/small *n*); for difference between two population means (known/unknown, large/small *n*); for population proportion *P*; for difference between two population proportions; sample-size determination for mean and proportion; numerical problems.
4. **Large-Sample Inferences about Population Parameters:** hypothesis testing and its types; null and alternative hypotheses; steps in hypothesis testing; one-tailed and two-tailed tests; Type I and Type II errors; significance level; power; *p*-value and steps for calculating it from a test statistic; decision to reject using *p*-value; standard normal test; tests for population mean and proportion; equality tests for two population means and two population proportions in large samples (Z-test); numerical problems.
5. **Small-Sample Inferences about Population Parameters:** comparison of *z* and *t* distributions; Student *t*-test; small-sample test of a population mean (*t*-test); equality of two means for independent and dependent samples; test of zero correlation coefficient; numerical problems.
6. **Analysis of Variance:** meaning of analysis of variances; precision and efficiency of a design; Completely Randomized Design (CRD), one-way classification, significance test of two treatment means in CRD, CRD advantages/disadvantages; Randomized Block Design (RBD), two-way classification, significance test of two treatment means in RBD, relative efficiency of RBD to CRD; Latin Square Design (LSD), analysis of LSD, significance test of two treatment means in LSD, relative efficiency of LSD to RBD; numerical problems.
7. **Index Number:** definition and importance; price, quantity and value indices; factors affecting index numbers; construction problems; uses; unweighted and weighted construction methods; consistency tests; cost-of-living index numbers, construction steps, construction methods and uses; chain-base indices and link relatives; important properties; numerical problems.
8. **Business Forecasting and Time Series Analysis:** concepts, significance and uses; components—secular trend (long-term movement), seasonal, cyclical and irregular variation; mathematical models; trend measurement by graphical method and least-squares method; seasonal measurement by simple averages, ratio-to-trend and ratio-to-moving-average methods; numerical problems.
9. **Nonparametric Tests:** meaning and advantages; chi-square test, applications and conditions for applying it; goodness-of-fit tests; contingency tables and determination of expected cell frequencies; test of independence; one-sample sign test; Mann–Whitney U test for independent samples; test of randomness; numerical problems.
10. **Statistical Quality Control:** concept and characteristics of quality control; causes of variation; process control; control charts listed as “x chart, R-chart, Pie-chart, C-chart.”

**Material source discrepancies:** the semester/course list identifies code 2203 as Business Statistics-II in second-year second semester, matching the detailed-syllabus title, although the detailed heading itself omits the semester label. The source text has apparent transcription/heading errors (“Two Two-TailedTailed Test,” “Applicationof,” and “Pie-chart”). The last is probably *p*-chart in context, but the PDF literally says “Pie-chart”; it is retained above rather than silently corrected. “LSD” appears in the CRD/RBD/LSD design sequence and is treated here as Latin Square Design.

## Topic-to-video map

Every link below points to a YouTube **watch** page opened for this check; title and channel were read from the watch page. Video titles are copied as displayed. Coverage is deliberately limited to what the opened page’s title/description supports; an item marked **NOT COVERED** is not implied to be taught by a nearby video.

### 1. Random variables and probability distributions

- [Discrete Probability Distributions (Binomial, Poisson, Hypergeometric) in Business Statistics (WK 9)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b77QtbnnWRY) — **Research By Design**. Covers discrete random-variable/probability-distribution introduction and binomial and Poisson examples. Its business-statistics framing and worked examples support formula recognition and numerical practice.
- **NOT COVERED:** continuous and normal/standard-normal distributions, standard-normal variate and properties of all the named distributions are not established by this lesson’s page; fitting binomial/Poisson specifically is also not verified. Search YouTube: `normal standard normal variate properties solved problems business statistics`; `fitting binomial Poisson distribution solved examples`.

### 2. Sampling distributions

- [Central Limit Theorem - Sampling Distribution of Sample Means - Stats & Probability](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4YLtvNeRIrg) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Explains CLT and sampling distributions of sample means, useful for conceptual questions and interpreting the normal approximation.
- [Sampling distribution of the difference in sample proportions | AP Statistics | Khan Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oThlns-qBaI) — **Khan Academy**. Directly treats the difference in sample proportions and the sampling-distribution concept, useful for understanding the two-group setup.
- **NOT COVERED:** sample-distribution calculations with and without replacement, finite-population corrections, distribution of the difference between two means, and sample-proportion distributions under both replacement conditions are not all demonstrated by these two pages. Search YouTube: `sampling distribution difference between two sample means with and without replacement solved`; `sampling distribution sample proportion finite population correction with replacement examples`.

### 3. Estimation and confidence intervals

- [Large Sample Confidence Intervals for Means and Proportions [STAT 2593]](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wBomIRyZ3d0) — **Dr. Dylan Spicker**. Page description explicitly lists large-sample intervals for means and proportions plus sample-size calculations, making it useful for formula selection and calculations.
- **NOT COVERED:** estimation types, point versus interval estimation, estimator properties/methods; known versus unknown population standard deviation and small-*n* intervals; confidence intervals for the difference of two population means or two proportions; and all associated exercises are not established by this large-sample lesson. Search YouTube: `confidence interval difference of two means known unknown sigma solved`; `confidence interval difference two proportions sample size worked problem`; `properties good estimator point interval estimation lecture`.

### 4. Large-sample inferences

- [Hypothesis Testing Problems - Z Test & T Statistics - One & Two Tailed Tests 2](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zJ8e_wAWUzE) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. The description confirms worked Z- and *t*-test examples, one- and two-tailed decisions, and accepting/rejecting a null; this is strong practice for showing the test procedure and conclusion.
- [Hypothesis Testing With Two Proportions](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pCbNUnZ98oE) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Works a two-proportion Z-test, supporting the large-sample equality-of-proportions calculation and written decision.
- **NOT COVERED:** the page evidence does not establish power, Type I/II errors, significance-level definitions, the *p*-value calculation/decision method, a separate one-population proportion test, or a large-sample two-mean test. Search YouTube: `p value method hypothesis test type I type II power examples`; `large sample z test difference two means worked problems`.

### 5. Small-sample inferences

- [Matched or Paired Samples T-Test - Hypothesis Testing](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JiQR0lHLe74) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Demonstrates matched/paired *t*-testing and a confidence interval, helping with dependent-sample calculation and null/alternative decision framing.
- [Hypothesis Testing Problems - Z Test & T Statistics - One & Two Tailed Tests 2](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zJ8e_wAWUzE) — **The Organic Chemistry Tutor**. Its *t*-statistic example gives extra practice distinguishing a small-sample *t* procedure from a Z test.
- **NOT COVERED:** independent two-mean *t* test, zero-correlation-coefficient test and a direct *z* versus Student-*t* distribution comparison are not specifically established. Search YouTube: `independent two sample t test worked example`; `test significance zero correlation coefficient t test solved`; `z distribution versus student t distribution comparison`.

### 6. Analysis of variance

- [Different types of Experimental Designs | CRD | RCBD | LSD](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gVPbYCx9HA4) — **WInspire**. Explicitly names CRD, randomized complete block design and Latin square design, helping distinguish the three design structures for theory questions.
- **NOT COVERED:** a verified lesson was not found here for the syllabus’s ANOVA computations and significance tests of two treatment means in CRD/RBD/LSD; one-way/two-way classification calculations; design precision/efficiency; CRD advantages/disadvantages; relative efficiency (RBD:CRD and LSD:RBD); or numerical exercises. Search YouTube: `CRD RBD Latin square ANOVA treatment means solved relative efficiency`; `precision efficiency of experimental design CRD RBD LSD problems`.

### 7. Index numbers

- [Business Statistics II I Index Numbers I Introduction Theory I Khans Commerce Tutorial I](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qitoat27PQI) — **Hasham Ali Khan**. A course-specific index-number theory lesson; it is a useful starting point for concise written definitions and theory review.
- [Business Statistics II I Index Numbers I Important Theory for Exam I Khans Commerce Tutorial I](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=h23rc__cS8E) — **Hasham Ali Khan**. The page title explicitly identifies exam theory, consistency tests and chain-based indices, useful for recalling test/property statements and chain-index ideas.
- **NOT COVERED:** the opened pages do not verify worked construction of price/quantity/value indices, weighted/unweighted formula comparisons, construction factors/problems, cost-of-living index steps/methods/uses, link-relative calculations or numerical applications. Search YouTube: `business statistics index numbers weighted unweighted cost of living index solved problems`; `chain base index link relatives calculations Fisher tests time reversal factor reversal`.

### 8. Business forecasting and time series

- [Business Statistics 2 I Time Series Analysis I Problems I Part 1 I Khans Commerce Tutorial I](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m7efOr5kp74) — **Hasham Ali Khan**. Course-specific worked time-series problems; its page identifies trend analysis and seasonal variation, so it is useful for practicing the course’s calculation style.
- **NOT COVERED:** concepts/significance/uses, all four components, mathematical models, graphical trend, least squares, and each seasonal method (simple averages, ratio-to-trend, ratio-to-moving-average) are not individually verified from the opened page. Search YouTube: `time series graphical least squares trend simple averages ratio to trend ratio moving average solved business statistics`.

### 9. Nonparametric tests

- [Non-parametric tests - Sign test, Wilcoxon signed rank, Mann-Whitney](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IcLSKko2tsg) — **zedstatistics**. The page description/chapter list explicitly includes nonparametric meaning and the sign and Mann–Whitney tests; clear definitions and rank/test procedure help short-answer and method-choice preparation.
- [Chi Square Test - with contingency table](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=misMgRRV3jQ) — **Math Meeting**. Directly demonstrates a chi-square test with a contingency table, useful for expected-frequency and test-statistic calculations for association/independence.
- **NOT COVERED:** chi-square goodness-of-fit applications/conditions, a separately verified expected-cell-frequency procedure, and test of randomness are not established by the opened pages. Search YouTube: `chi square goodness of fit conditions expected frequencies worked examples`; `runs test randomness nonparametric solved statistics`.

### 10. Statistical quality control

- [CONTROL CHART BASICS and the X-BAR AND R CHART +++++ EXAMPLE](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Aj7lJLR-7b4) — **CQE Academy**. Explains common/special causes of variation and works an *x*-bar and R chart, useful for interpreting variation and practicing variable-chart limits.
- [Attribute Data Control Chart Examples!! How to select/create the P, NP, C and U Charts](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=p-gvwkHePaU) — **CQE Academy**. Demonstrates selection/construction of P and C charts (as well as NP and U), giving calculation practice for the likely intended P chart and C chart.
- **NOT COVERED:** the PDF’s “Pie-chart” cannot be verified as a statistical control chart name; if the intended item is *p*-chart, the linked P-chart lesson is relevant. General quality-control characteristics and causes beyond the common/special-cause introduction are not exhaustively covered. Search YouTube: `statistical quality control characteristics process control x bar R p c chart solved`.

## Coverage audit

**10 syllabus content headings; 15 distinct verified videos.** Each of the ten broad headings has at least one relevant video opened on its actual watch page. **Not every syllabus subtopic has a verified direct lesson**: gaps are called out under each heading, particularly sampling with/without replacement, detailed confidence-interval variants, design-specific ANOVA calculations and efficiency, cost-of-living/index-number construction, specific time-series methods, goodness-of-fit/randomness tests, and the “Pie-chart” wording. No Bangladesh-specific legal, tax or regulatory topics occur in this statistics syllabus.

# 2204 Marketing

**Semester:** Semester 4 — Second Year, Second Semester  
**Curriculum scope:** 11th-batch AIS curriculum (Session 2022–2023 onward), Detailed Syllabus  
**Source:** `/home/ubuntu/upload/Syllabus.pdf`, course 2204, “Detailed Course Contents.”

> Video lectures below are supplemental study aids, not AIS course materials. Their visible YouTube watch-page titles, channels, and relevance were checked. The course outline below follows the AIS curriculum; the video recommendations do not replace its prescribed readings or guarantee exam coverage.

## Course objective

This course introduces students to the fundamental building blocks of marketing, stressing the underlying principles of the “marketing concept” and integrating concepts in relation to consumer needs, market information, product development, pricing, distribution, selling, advertising, and promotions.

## Intended learning outcomes

Upon completion of this course, students will be able to:

1. Understand the core concepts of marketing, marketing process, and marketing management.
2. Develop strategic marketing and business planning.
3. Analyze market opportunities by scanning the marketing environment and consumer buying behavior.
4. Develop and apply marketing strategies.
5. Plan and design marketing mix and programs including product, price, and promotions.
6. Create a competitive advantage and understand the global marketplace.
7. Learn sustainable and social marketing and ethics.

## Detailed course contents

The following outline transcribes the AIS curriculum's Detailed Course Contents. Spacing has been normalized where PDF line breaks split words.

1. **Understanding Marketing** [ILO 1, ILO 2]
   - **1.1. Assessing Marketing’s Critical Role in Organizational Performance:** What is marketing; core concepts; the marketing process, marketing management; marketing orientations; and demarketing.
   - **1.2. Building Customer Satisfaction through Quality, Service & Value:** define customer value & satisfaction; customer relationship management (CRM); delivering customer value & satisfaction; attracting & retaining customers.
   - **1.3. Winning Markets through Market-oriented Strategic Planning:** Corporate and division planning; business strategic planning.
2. **Analyzing Marketing Opportunities** [ILO 1, ILO 3]
   - **2.1. Scanning the Marketing Environment:** Analyzing needs & trends in the microenvironment; identifying & responding to the major macro-environmental forces.
   - **2.2. Analyzing Consumer Markets and Buying Behavior:** A model of consumer behavior; major factors influencing buying behavior; the buying process; identifying market segments and selecting market targets; market targeting.
3. **Developing Marketing Strategies** [ILO 1, ILO 2, ILO 4]
   - **3.1. Differentiating and Positioning the Market Offering:** Tools for competitive differentiation; developing a positioning strategy; communicating the company’s position.
   - **3.2. Developing New Products:** Process the of new product development, challenges, and way forwards [sic].
   - **3.3. Managing Life Cycle Strategies:** The product life cycle; marketing strategies throughout the PLC.
4. **Planning Marketing Programs** [ILO 1, ILO 2, ILO 5]
   - **4.1. Managing Product Lines, Brands & Packaging:** What is a product; levels of a product; product classifications; product line decisions; product mix decisions; brand decisions; packaging & labelling decisions.
   - **4.2. Managing Service Business and Product Support Services:** Nature of and classification of services; characteristics of services and their marketing implications; marketing strategies for service firms; managing product support services.
   - **4.3. Designing Pricing Strategies and Programs:** Selecting the pricing objective; determining demand; estimating costs; analyzing company competitors’ prices and offers; selecting a pricing method; selecting the final price; price discounts and allowances; promotional pricing; product mix pricing; initiating & responding to price changes.
   - **4.4. Selecting & Managing Marketing Channels:** What are marketing channels; channel functions; channel levels; channel design decisions; channel management decisions. **Managing Retailing, Wholesaling, and Market Logistics:** Retailing; wholesaling; marketing logistics.
   - **4.5. Designing and Managing Integrated Marketing Communications:** Identify the target audience; determine the communication objective; design the message; select the communication channel; establish the total promotional budget; decide on the promotion mix.
   - **4.6. Managing Advertising, Sales Promotion, and Public Relations:** Developing and managing an advertising program; the purpose of sales promotion; major decisions in sales promotion; major decisions in marketing PR.
   - **4.7. Managing the Sales Force:** Designing the sales force; managing the sales force; principles of personal selling.
   - **4.8. Managing Direct & Online Marketing:** Growth and benefits of direct marketing; major channels for direct marketing; marketing in the 21st century.
5. **Extending Marketing** [ILO 1, ILO 6, ILO 7]
   - **5.1. Creating Competitive Advantage:** Competitor analysis, competitive strategies, and balancing customer and competitor orientations.
   - **5.2. The Global Marketplace:** Global marketing today, global marketing environment, going global, global marketing program, global marketing organization.
   - **5.3. Sustainable and Social Marketing and Ethics:** Sustainable marketing, social marketing, social criticism of marketing, ethical issues in marketing.
