## Coverage audit

- **Verified and recommended:** 14 direct YouTube watch pages. Most are college-level Principles of Marketing lecture videos by R. J. Birmingham; one supplementary Kotler course lesson is from Professor Online. The watch pages displayed each listed exact title and channel, and their visible descriptions matched the mapped topics.
- **Strongest topic coverage:** introductory marketing/value/CRM, consumer decision process, segmentation-targeting-positioning, pricing-strategy overview, channel design, retailing/wholesaling, and personal selling/sales promotion. “Strongest” means the video description directly names these areas, not that every curriculum subpoint is confirmed.
- **Partial coverage remains:** details not named in the verified descriptions are explicitly marked **NOT COVERED** above and paired with targeted search terms. Specifically, no verified suitable lesson is recommended for the global-marketplace syllabus unit or the exact IMC planning sequence. Several narrower syllabus elements also lack a verified direct lesson, including demarketing, product support services, marketing logistics, price-setting calculations, advertising/PR decision sequences, and marketing ethics.
- **No claim of full coverage:** even where a title closely matches, the description does not establish coverage of every subtopic, worked example, or exam question. Use the AIS detailed syllabus and the prescribed Kotler/Armstrong and Kotler/Keller texts as the completeness check.

# 2205 Income Tax: Laws and Practices

**Semester:** Second Year — Second Semester (11th-batch AIS curriculum, effective from Session 2022–2023; 3 credits)

**Source:** University of Barishal BBA AIS syllabus, *Detailed Syllabus*, course heading 2205 (PDF p. 77), read against the 11th-batch course list (PDF p. 35). The course list spells the title “Income Tax: Laws and Practice” (singular); the detailed-syllabus heading spells it “Income Tax: Laws and Practices” (plural). The code and semester match; this fragment uses the detailed-syllabus title and contents. The earlier Management Studies curriculum was not used.

**Law-version caution:** The recommended texts name the Income Tax Ordinance 1984 and Income Tax Rules 1984 (updated), with the latest Finance Act and ICAB Manual as references. Most Bangladesh-specific videos verified below teach the Income Tax Act 2023 and, in some cases, Finance Act 2026 / 2026–27 tax-year procedures. Use those videos for explanation and calculation practice, but check the statutory version, provisions, rates and exam-year requirements against the instructor’s prescribed law.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Introduction to Bangladesh Income Tax [ILO 1]** — Definition of Tax; Characteristics and Purposes of Tax; Canons of Taxation; Tax Base and classification of Taxes; Tax burden and shifting of taxes; Concept of Income; Capital or Revenue; Tax and Income Tax; Different Rates of Income Tax for Different Assessees; Tax structure in Bangladesh; Role of Income tax for economic growth of Bangladesh; Scope of Bangladesh Income Tax Law; Schemes of Bangladesh Income Tax Law.
2. **Basics of Income Tax [ILO 2]** — Definition and Characteristics of Income; Sources of Income; Determination of Assessment Year and Income Year and implications on tax liability determination; Different Classes of Assessees; Residential Status of an Assessee; Taxation Implication of Resident and Non-resident; Charge of Surcharge; Additional tax and minimum tax; Classification of Income based on Locality and Assessment; Non-assessable/Tax exempted Income; Tax Credit Income; Tax Holiday schemes; Tax rebate on CSR related expenditures.
3. **Income Tax Authority: Administrative and Judicial [ILO 2]** — Income Tax Authorities: Administrative and Judicial; Appointment; Subordination; Power and Functions; Alternative Dispute Resolution (ADR).
4. **Computation of Income [ILO 3]** — Computation under Statutory Heads: Salaries; Interest on Securities; Income from House Property; Agricultural Income; Income from Business or Profession; Capital Gains; Income from Other Sources. Computation under Other Heads: Share of Income from Firms; Income of Spouse or Minor Children; Foreign Income.
5. **Set-off and Carry-Forward of Losses [ILO 4]** — Provisions on Set-off of Losses in the Year of First Incurrence and its Procedure; Carry-forward and Set-off under specific heads: Business Loss; Loss in Speculation Business; Loss under the Head Capital Gains; Loss under the Head Agricultural Income; Carry-forward of Loss of Firm and Partner; Carry-forward of Loss of Succeeded Business or Profession; Carry-forward of Loss of Unabsorbed Depreciation; Carry-forward of Loss of Tax Holiday Units.
6. **Payment of Tax before Assessment [ILO 4]** — Tax Deduction at Source (TDS); Advance Income Tax (AIT) and Tax Payment on the Basis of Return; TDS vs. Collection of Tax at Sources; Incomes subject to TDS and Formalities and Consequences; TDS as Final Discharge of Tax Liability under section 82C; Requirement and Procedures of AIT and Consequence on Failure to Pay AIT.
7. **Return of Income and Statements [ILO 5]** — Requirement to File Return of Income; Form of Return of Income; Due Date of Submission of Return of Income; Documents to be Submitted along with the Return of Income; Return of Withholding Tax; Obligation to Furnish Annual Information Return; Certificate in Place of Return; Filing of Revised Return (printed as “Filling” in the PDF); Statement of Assets and Liabilities and Lifestyle; Production of Accounts and other Documents.
8. **Assessments [ILO 6]** — Definition of Assessment; Provisional Assessment; Assessment on Correct Return; Universal Self-Assessment & Audit thereafter; Spot Assessment; Assessment after Hearing; Assessment on the Basis of Report of a Chartered Accountant; Best Judgment Assessment; Presumptive Tax Assessment on Owners of Road and Water Transports; Various Specialized Assessments; Income Escaping Assessment; Limitation for Assessment.
9. **Assessment of Individuals [ILO 6]** — Definition of Individual; Computation of Total Income of an Individual Assessee; Procedure of Assessment; Assessment Order and Tax Clearance Certificate.
10. **Assessment of Companies [ILO 6]** — Definition of Company and Types of Companies; Computation of Total Income and Tax Payable; Tax Rebate on Higher Productivity; Higher Dividend; CSR (Corporate Social Responsibility) and others; Procedure of Assessment; Assessment of Banks and other Financial Institutions; Liability of Directors for Unrecoverable Tax of Private Companies; Liability of Liquidators for Tax of Private Companies under Liquidation; Liability to Tax in case of Shipping and Air Transport Business of Non-residents.
11. **Default and Imposition of Penalty, Offences and Prosecution [ILO 7]** — Penalty for various Defaults; Punishment for various Offences; Trial by Special Judge.

**Prescribed references named in the syllabus:** Chowdhury, D., *Advanced Issues in Taxation: Theory & Practices* (Grantha Kutir); *The Income Tax Ordinance 1984 and the Income Tax Rules 1984* (updated); *Finance Act* (Latest); ICAB Manual.

## Topic-to-video map

Only direct YouTube watch pages that were opened and checked for displayed title, channel and relevance are linked. The listed lessons provide practical support, not assurance that every syllabus provision or historical-law detail is covered.

### 1. Introduction to Bangladesh Income Tax

**NOT COVERED —** All 13 syllabus elements: tax definition, characteristics/purposes, canons, tax base/classification, incidence and shifting, concept of income, capital versus revenue, tax versus income tax, rates by assessee, Bangladesh tax structure, economic-growth role, scope of Bangladesh income-tax law, and schemes of Bangladesh income-tax law. No suitable, sufficiently explicit Bangladesh exam lesson was verified for this theory cluster. Search YouTube for: `Bangladesh income tax canons of taxation tax base tax shifting BBA lecture`, `Bangladesh tax structure income tax schemes lecture`, `Bangladesh tax capital vs revenue income theory`.

### 2. Basics of Income Tax

**NOT COVERED —** Definition/characteristics and sources of income; assessment year versus income year and liability implications; assessee classes; residential status and resident/non-resident consequences; surcharge, additional tax and minimum tax; locality/assessment classification; exempt/non-assessable income; tax-credit income; tax holidays; CSR rebate. Search: `Bangladesh income tax residential status assessment year income year tax exempt minimum tax BBA`, `Bangladesh income tax CSR rebate tax holiday tax credit lecture`.

### 3. Income Tax Authority: Administrative and Judicial

**NOT COVERED —** Administrative and judicial authorities, appointment, subordination, powers/functions, and ADR. Search: `Bangladesh Income Tax Act 2023 income tax authorities appointment powers ADR lecture`, `Bangladesh income tax alternative dispute resolution tax authority BBA`.

### 4. Computation of Income

- **All statutory heads — overview:** [Complete Guide to All Heads of Income Tax Calculation Under Income Tax Act 2023](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hSpa9NHvPhU) — **H2B Corporate**. Its watch-page description says it explains total-income calculation under the Bangladesh Income Tax Act 2023 across all income heads; it is a useful overview before head-wise questions, though the page does not itemize every head. **Exam value:** use its step-by-step total-income sequence to organize a written computation; confirm each head’s detailed allowance and rate under the applicable exam-year law.
- **Salaries; Income from House Property (rental); Agricultural Income:** [Salary, Rent & Agricultural Income | Tax Calculation & Practical Guide | Bangladesh](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RhJsy8E2GvM) — **H2B Corporate**. The description explicitly identifies salary, rental and agricultural-income calculations with practical examples. **Exam value:** worked head-wise calculations help practise classification and computation steps.
- **Income from Business or Profession:** [Business Income A to Z Tax Calculation | Live Class Bangladesh | Step-by-Step Guide](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YZBOMQRI3uA) — **H2B Corporate**. The page describes step-by-step Bangladesh business-income calculation and practical examples for Assessment Year 2025–26. **Exam value:** worked business-income adjustments provide calculation practice; reconcile year-specific rules to the course’s assigned law.
- **Capital Gains; Income from Other Sources:** [Capital Gain, Financial Assets & Other Income | Income Tax Calculation | Banglades](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wU_4gmLg67Q) — **H2B Corporate**. The Bangladesh description explicitly covers practical calculation/tax treatment of capital gains, financial assets and other income. **Exam value:** use the examples to practise capital-gain and other-income treatments; the visible title/description does not separately confirm every kind of security income.
- **NOT COVERED —** Interest on Securities (no directly verified lesson that specifically explains this statutory head); Share of Income from Firms; Income of Spouse or Minor Children; Foreign Income. Search: `Bangladesh interest on securities income tax calculation lecture`, `Bangladesh income tax share of firm spouse minor child foreign income BBA`.

- **NOT COVERED —** Set-off in the year first incurred and its precise procedure; specific rules for business loss, speculation-business loss, capital-gain loss and agricultural-income loss; firm/partner loss; succeeded business/profession; unabsorbed depreciation; tax-holiday-unit losses. The verified lesson’s page does not establish coverage of these individual statutory cases. The watch-page description identifies Bangladesh TDS, base value/rate calculation, salary TDS, assessment process and TDS-certificate issue. **Exam value:** calculations and certificate workflow are useful for numeric questions and short procedural answers; the page also covers VDS, which is not a stated topic in this course.
- **NOT COVERED —** TDS versus collection of tax at source; full list of incomes subject to TDS and statutory formalities/consequences; TDS as final discharge under section 82C; AIT and return-based tax payment; AIT requirements, procedures and consequences of failure. ## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 94 discrete syllabus content elements counted from the 11 numbered detailed-content headings. **Verified videos:** 10 unique watch-page links; each page was opened to check displayed title, channel and stated lesson relevance. **Every topic has at least one verified video? No.** Every syllabus element is accounted for above, but most statutory-theory, procedure and special-liability items are explicitly marked **NOT COVERED** with focused YouTube search terms. Practical coverage is strongest for statutory income-head calculations, general loss carry-forward, TDS, e-return filing and example individual/company computations. Uncertainty remains where a broad video title or description promises “all heads” without listing each head, and because current Act 2023/Finance Act 2026 lessons may not match the course’s cited Ordinance 1984 text or a particular exam-year law.

# 2206 Lab: Programming and Database Management

**Semester:** Second Year, Second Semester (Semester 4); 2 credits. **Curriculum:** BBA Accounting & Information Systems, 11th batch (effective from 2022–2023).

> **Syllabus-label discrepancy:** The course grid lists **2206**, but the detailed-syllabus heading says **2205: Lab: Programming and Database Management**. The matching course title and semester identify it as the requested 2206 course. The printed ILO list also repeats “understand the basics of DBMS” as ILO 4 and ILO 5; both are retained in substance below.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

The detailed syllabus labels the course **Theory (50%) and Practical (50%)**.

1. **Introduction:** Introduction to programming languages; steps in programming; algorithms; pseudocode; flowcharts; software development lifecycle (SDLC).
2. **Programming using Python:**
   - **2.1** Introduction to Python.
   - **2.2** Variables; arithmetic; using `print`.
   - **2.3** Python data types: numeric types, strings, Booleans.
   - **2.4** Input from the user.
   - **2.5** Python operators and expressions.
   - **2.6** `if ... else`.
   - **2.7** Loops: `while` and `for` loops.
   - **2.8** Arrays: uses of arrays; arrays from existing data; array attributes; array operators.
   - **2.9** Built-in functions.
   - **2.10** Functions: defining and using functions; Python standard library; scope rules; default parameter value.
   - **2.11** Sequences, lists and tuples.
   - **2.12** Dictionaries and sets: creating dictionaries; dictionary operations; dictionary methods `keys` and `values`; working with sets.
   - **2.13** NumPy: arrays from existing data; array attributes; array operators; NumPy methods.
   - **2.14** Introduction to data analysis using data files.
3. **Introduction to databases:** Data models; entity; relationship model; relational model; mapping cardinalities; keys.
4. **Relational databases:**
   - **4.1 Relational database design:** First Normal Form (1NF); functional dependencies; Boyce–Codd Normal Form (BCNF); Third Normal Form (3NF); Fourth Normal Form (4NF).
   - **4.2 SQL:** Background; basic structure; set operations; aggregate functions; NULL values; nested subqueries; views; complex queries; database modification; joined relations; data-definition language (DDL); embedded SQL; dynamic SQL; other SQL features.
   - **4.3 Other relational languages:** Query-by-Example (QBE); user interfaces; tools.

**Practical guideline:** Use Python programming tools for the programming portion; SQLite or MySQL DBMS is preferred for database work.

## Topic-to-video map

Every link below goes directly to a YouTube watch page. I opened each recommended watch page and checked its displayed title and channel; lesson relevance was cross-checked against the visible page details and lesson description/search information. Links are unique. Videos use general Python/SQL/database instruction; no Bangladesh-specific legal, tax or regulatory topic is in this course syllabus.

### Programming foundations and Python

- [Algorithm and Flowchart](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MUkvVLGz89k) — **Manocha Academy**. **Covers:** programming steps, algorithms, pseudocode and flowcharts (with worked examples); use alongside the Python course for the programming-language introduction. **Exam use:** Worked algorithm-to-flowchart examples help with logic-tracing and written algorithm/flowchart questions.
- [0478/0984 iGCSE Computer Science - Unit 7 Lesson 1: PDLC - Program Development Life Cycle SDLC](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Rzx5D-3aMtM) — **CSWITHMRG**. **Covers:** software/program development life cycle stages (SDLC/PDLC). **Exam use:** Its stage-by-stage lesson is useful for defining and ordering lifecycle stages in a written answer.
- [Python for Beginners – Full Course [Programming Tutorial]](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWRfhZUzrAc) — **freeCodeCamp.org**. **Covers:** 2.1–2.6 (Python introduction; variables; arithmetic and `print`; numeric/string/Boolean types; user input; operators/expressions; `if ... else`) and introductory hands-on programming with Python. **Exam use:** A full beginner course with coding demonstrations supports short conceptual answers and tracing/constructing basic programs.
- [Python While Loops & For Loops | Python tutorial for Beginners](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=23vCap6iYSs) — **Dave Gray**. **Covers:** 2.7 `while` and `for` loops. **Exam use:** Focused loop explanations and code examples help practise iteration and predict program output.
- [Python Programming Lesson 9 – Using Built-In Functions | Python 3 For Beginners](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GoX1YeyZCxU) — **xxRafael Productions - Rafael Vicuna**. **Covers:** 2.9 Python built-in functions. **Exam use:** A dedicated built-in-functions lesson helps identify and apply common built-ins in short coding questions.
- [Python Tutorial for Beginners 8: Functions](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9Os0o3wzS_I) — **Corey Schafer**. **Covers:** 2.10 defining, calling and using functions. **Exam use:** The focused examples are useful for writing function definitions and explaining arguments/return behaviour.
- [Advanced Function Concepts in Python - Day 8 of Python Tutorial](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2kjFZTScDhM) — **Destin Learning**. **Covers:** 2.10 default parameter values and related function concepts. **Exam use:** Examples help distinguish supplied arguments from defaults in code-tracing questions.
- [Python Tutorial: Variable Scope - Understanding the LEGB rule and global/nonlocal statements](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QVdf0LgmICw) — **Corey Schafer**. **Covers:** 2.10 scope rules, including local/global scope and LEGB lookup. **Exam use:** Clear scope examples help explain variable visibility and determine which value a function uses.
- [Python Tutorial for Beginners 9: Import Modules and Exploring The Standard Library](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CqvZ3vGoGs0) — **Corey Schafer**. **Covers:** 2.10 importing modules and using the Python standard library. **Exam use:** Demonstrations make module imports and library use easier to describe and apply in practical questions.
- [Python Tutorial for Beginners 4: Lists, Tuples, and Sets](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W8KRzm-HUcc) — **Corey Schafer**. **Covers:** 2.11 sequences, lists, tuples and sets. **Exam use:** Side-by-side collection examples help compare mutability and choose the correct sequence type in coding questions.
- [Python Dictionaries and Sets for Beginners | Python tutorial](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zdVdqTLk8O0) — **Dave Gray**. **Covers:** 2.12 dictionary creation, key/value operations and methods, and working with sets. **Exam use:** Demonstrations of collection operations support short-answer comparisons and practical dictionary/set problems.
- [Python NumPy Tutorial for Beginners](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUT1VHiLmmI) — **freeCodeCamp.org**. **Covers:** 2.8 array uses, creation from existing data, attributes and operators; 2.13 NumPy arrays from existing data, attributes, array operators and methods. **Exam use:** Array-focused coding examples support both conceptual questions and hands-on NumPy practice.
- [Complete Python Pandas Data Science Tutorial! (Reading CSV/Excel files, Sorting, Filtering, Groupby)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vmEHCJofslg) — **Keith Galli**. **Covers:** 2.14 data analysis using data files, including loading CSV/Excel data and sorting, filtering and grouping it. **Exam use:** A real-file walkthrough gives practice with the practical sequence of loading, inspecting and analysing tabular data.

### Database concepts and relational design

- [Database Lesson #1 of 8 - Introduction to Databases](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4Z9KEBexzcM) — **Dr. Daniel Soper**. **Covers:** database introduction and data-model context for section 3. **Exam use:** The university-style introduction provides concise conceptual framing for database-purpose and model questions.
- [Database Lesson #2 of 8 - The Relational Model](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kyGVhx5LwXw) — **Dr. Daniel Soper**. **Covers:** section 3 relational model and relations/keys. **Exam use:** Its lecture format helps frame definitions of relations and relational-model structure in written answers.
- [Database Lesson #4 of 8 - Data Modeling and the ER Model](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IfaqkiHpIjo) — **Dr. Daniel Soper**. **Covers:** section 3 entities, relationship model, entity relationships and mapping/cardinality concepts. **Exam use:** Detailed ER examples help practise translating requirements into entity–relationship diagrams and explaining cardinality.
- [Keys in RDBMS](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_UZLrD_R0T4) — **Neso Academy**. **Covers:** section 3 relational keys. **Exam use:** Key-type explanations support common “define/differentiate the keys” written questions.
- [Lec 4: Functional dependency in DBMS | What is functional dependency | Database Management System](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dR-jJimWWHA) — **Jenny's Lectures CS IT**. **Covers:** 4.1 functional dependencies, with examples. **Exam use:** Worked dependency examples are useful preparation for identifying dependencies in a relation before normalization.
- [Learn Database Normalization - 1NF, 2NF, 3NF, 4NF, 5NF](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GFQaEYEc8_8) — **Decomplexify**. **Covers:** 4.1 1NF, 2NF, 3NF and 4NF. **Exam use:** The examples and form-by-form progression help with normalization explanations and table-decomposition problems.
- [Learn Boyce-Codd Normal Form (BCNF)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VWnKUKH4tLg) — **Decomplexify**. **Covers:** 4.1 BCNF. **Exam use:** The focused explanation and examples help distinguish BCNF from 3NF in written and normalization problems.

### SQL, QBE and database tools

- [SQL Course for Beginners [Full Course]](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7S_tz1z_5bA) — **Programming with Mosh**. **Covers:** 4.2 SQL background and basic query structure; set operations; aggregates; NULL handling; nested queries; views; complex queries; database modification; joins; DDL; and MySQL setup/tool use for practical database work. **Exam use:** The long, code-along course supports query-writing and output-prediction practice; it teaches MySQL, so check syntax if your lab uses SQLite.
- [Embedded SQL in DBMS](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Xk2sWQ7pf3I) — **Starter Tutorials**. **Covers:** 4.2 embedded SQL, including SQL statements inside a C host-language program. **Exam use:** The example makes the host-language/SQL distinction concrete for a short explanatory answer.
- [Dynamic SQL in DBMS](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BWO2TpQKUBM) — **Starter Tutorials**. **Covers:** 4.2 dynamic SQL, including constructing/executing SQL dynamically. **Exam use:** A focused explanation and example help distinguish dynamic SQL from fixed embedded statements.
- [Query By Example (QBE)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aV3_74l4bpY) — **Christopher Kalodikis**. **Covers:** 4.3 Query-by-Example as a relational query language. **Exam use:** The lesson helps state how a visual/example-based query approach differs from writing SQL text.

**NOT COVERED — 4.2 “Other SQL features.”** The syllabus does not name which additional features it means, and no recommendation is presented as exhaustive. For targeted revision, search YouTube for: `MySQL SQL advanced features constraints transactions indexes stored procedures triggers tutorial` (or substitute SQLite if that is the lab DBMS). The named 4.2 topics above are mapped separately.

## Coverage audit

**70 atomic syllabus points** counted: the individually named items in sections 1–4.3, including subtopics, plus the two practical-tool requirements (Python programming tools; SQLite/MySQL DBMS). **69/70 have at least one suitable video mapped.** Gap: only the open-ended phrase **“Other SQL features”**; precise search terms are supplied above. All 24 recommended videos were opened at their direct YouTube watch pages to check their displayed title and channel, and confirmed as lessons relevant to the mapped subject; exact coverage is stated conservatively. The normalization course includes 2NF and 5NF beyond the syllabus; those are supplementary, not additional course requirements.

# Semester 5 — Third Year, First Semester

# 3101 Cost Accounting — Semester 5 (Third Year, First Semester)

**Program/source scope:** University of Barishal BBA, Accounting & Information Systems, 11th-batch curriculum; the **AIS DETAILED SYLLABUS** section only. The course list confirms **3101 Cost Accounting**, 3 credits, Third Year First Semester. This is not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

The outline below preserves the detailed syllabus content. The last two headings are listed under repeated numbers in the source; they are shown here as Service Costing and Integrated Accounting System to distinguish them.

1. **Introduction to Cost Accounting:** Definition and objectives; importance; Cost Accounting versus Financial Accounting; evolution; methods and techniques; characteristics of an ideal Cost Accounting system; installation of a Cost Accounting system; modern trends in Cost Accounting.
2. **Cost Concepts & Classification:** Costs and cost terminology; concepts of asset, cost, expense, and loss; classification of costs; factory cost flow.
3. **Costing and Control of Materials:** Nature and types of materials; materials control; materials procurement and related issues; Economic Order Quantity (EOQ/reorder quantity), reorder level, safety or minimum stock level, maximum stock level; issue of materials to production; materials returned to the storeroom and returned to supplier; materials costing; costing materials issued using First-in-First-out (FIFO), Last-in-First-out (LIFO), simple average, weighted average, periodic simple average, periodic weighted average, moving simple average, moving weighted average, standard price, inflated price, replacement price or market price; lower of cost or net realizable value (NRV); inventory control techniques—Pareto or ABC analysis and Just-In-Time (JIT); material losses—scrap, spoilage, defectives, and waste/wastage.
4. **Costing and Control of Labor:** Direct and indirect labor; organization for labor control; wage systems; incentive wage plans—time rate and piece rate; job evaluation and merit rating; time and motion study; labor turnover; treatment of labor-cost items—overtime, overtime premium, idle time, fringe benefits, shift premium, holiday and vacation pay, learner's/apprentices' wages, attendance bonuses, leave with pay, employer's contribution to insurance; casual workers, out-workers, and outside workers.
5. **Costing and Control of Manufacturing Overhead:** Factory overhead—fixed and variable; “Accounting for Factory” (worded this way in the source); collection and codification of factory overheads; allocation and apportionment; absorption; actual versus predetermined (standard) overhead rates; under- or over-absorption.
6. **Preparation of Cost Statements:** Statement of Cost of Goods Manufactured and Sold; income statement for a manufacturing firm; framework for Cost Accounting and Cost Management.
7. **Cost Allocation: Departments:** Production departments and departments (source wording); allocation of a support department's costs; allocation of costs of multiple support departments; allocating common costs; direct, step/sequential, and reciprocal methods; departmental overhead rates.
8. **Job-order Costing and Batch Costing:** Nature of job costing; job cost sheet and job ledger; recording materials costs, labor costs, direct expenses, and overhead on jobs; recording completed jobs; batch costing; Economic Batch Quantity.
9. **Process Costing:** Nature; job-order versus process costing; physical flow and flow of costs; process-cost elements—material, labor, factory overhead, and transferred-in costs; cost-flow methods—weighted average versus FIFO; equivalent units of production; Cost of Production Report; journal entries; hybrid costing system.
10. **Service Costing** (the source labels this “6.”): Nature and basic principles; accounting records; costing service concerns—transport, canteen, hotel, powerhouse, hospital or clinic, and educational institutes.
11. **Integrated Accounting System** (the source labels this “7.”): Definition; features; advantages of integration; journal entries; WIP, Finished Goods, Cost of Goods Sold, and Cost of Sales accounts; preparation of financial statements; reconciliation of Cost Accounting and Financial Accounting records.

**Source discrepancies:** The course-list heading and detailed section agree on course code/title and semester. In the detailed contents, Service Costing and Integrated Accounting System repeat numbers 6 and 7 after Process Costing (numbered 9); the source also contains a scan/watermark interruption around this area. No named accounting standard or statute is specified in this course's detailed contents. It contains no Bangladesh-specific tax, VAT, or regulatory-law unit.

## Topic-to-video map

Each linked YouTube watch page was opened to check its displayed title, channel, and lesson relevance. “NOT COVERED” means no direct, verified watch-page match was established for that listed syllabus detail; search phrases are provided rather than inferred links.

### 1. Introduction to Cost Accounting

- **NOT COVERED:** Definition and objectives of Cost Accounting; importance; comparison with Financial Accounting; evolution; methods and techniques; ideal-system characteristics; system installation; modern trends. - **NOT COVERED:** Explicit asset-versus-cost-versus-expense-versus-loss treatment; full cost classification; factory cost-flow sequence. Search: `cost accounting asset cost expense loss classification factory cost flow diagram`.

- **NOT COVERED:** Nature/types of materials; procurement and control procedures beyond EOQ/levels; maximum stock and the syllabus's minimum-stock-level calculation; issue-to-production documentation; returns to storeroom or supplier; periodic and moving simple/weighted averages; standard, inflated, and replacement/market prices; lower of cost or NRV; ABC/Pareto and JIT; accounting for scrap, spoilage, defectives, and waste.
