### 6. Preparation of Cost Statements

- [How to Prepare a Cost of Goods Manufactured Statement (Cost Accounting Tutorial #24)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ycezt5Hu06M) — **Notepirate**. **Covers:** preparing a Cost of Goods Manufactured statement. **Exam use:** a worked schedule gives practice arranging manufacturing-cost components into a formal statement.
- **NOT COVERED:** A complete combined Cost of Goods Manufactured and Sold statement; manufacturing-firm income statement; the stated framework for Cost Accounting and Cost Management. Search: `manufacturing cost accounting cost of goods manufactured sold income statement worked example cost management framework`.

### 7. Cost Allocation: Departments

- [Service Department Cost Allocation | Direct, Step-Down & Reciprocal Methods](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LeCeJHAQzYA) — **Joefessor**. **Covers:** the three titled support/service-department allocation methods—direct, step-down, and reciprocal—and moving support costs to production. **Exam use:** the side-by-side methods aid written comparison and method-selection problems.
- **NOT COVERED:** Allocation of multiple support-department costs in full, common-cost allocation, and departmental overhead-rate calculations. Search: `departmental overhead rates common costs multiple support departments direct step down reciprocal worked problem`.

### 8. Job-order Costing and Batch Costing

- [Job Order Costing: Follow the Costs & Journal Entries](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d-EWG8MMocU) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** a manufacturing job-order cost flow from raw materials through WIP and Finished Goods to Cost of Goods Sold, with direct materials, direct labor, applied overhead, and journal entries. **Exam use:** tracing the entries account by account is useful for job-cost-flow problems and short explanations.
- **NOT COVERED:** Job cost sheet and job ledger formats; direct-expense treatment; distinct recording of completed jobs beyond the stated cost flow; batch costing and Economic Batch Quantity. Search: `job cost sheet job ledger direct expenses completed jobs batch costing economic batch quantity worked problem`.

### 9. Process Costing

- [Process Costing: Weighted Average Example in 4 Steps | CPA & CMA](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sjUEpL9RhRQ) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** weighted-average process costing, physical-unit flow, equivalent units, cost per equivalent unit, cost assignment, and movement to the next department/ending WIP. **Exam use:** a worked departmental example follows the main unit-costing calculation sequence.
- [Process Costing FIFO Method](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lZzHw00akvw) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** FIFO process-costing steps, equivalent-unit differences from weighted average, cost per equivalent unit, and cost reconciliation. **Exam use:** supports stepwise FIFO calculations and comparison with weighted average.
- **NOT COVERED:** When process costing is appropriate; direct job-versus-process comparison; separate material/labor/overhead/transferred-in cost-element treatment; the exact syllabus-format Cost of Production Report; process-cost journal entries; hybrid costing. Search: `process costing suitability job vs process transferred in costs journal entries hybrid costing production report`.

### 10. Service Costing

- [#1 Service or Operating Costing - Concept - B.COM / CMA / CA INTER](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1iRda9uKq9w) — **Saheb Academy**. **Covers:** conceptual introduction to service/operating costing. **Exam use:** provides a chapter-level starting point for defining the method and framing a basic written answer.
- [#4 Service or Operating Costing - Problem 3 - ICAI Practical Question 2 - By Saheb Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SyIu_9qAESg) — **Saheb Academy**. **Covers:** a transport-costing numerical calculating passenger-kilometres and an operating cost sheet. **Exam use:** worked unit-cost and cost-sheet steps suit practical-question revision. **Jurisdiction note:** the watch-page description identifies an ICAI practical question and passenger-tax adjustment (Indian exam context); use the costing method only, not that tax treatment as Bangladesh guidance.
- **NOT COVERED:** Canteen, hotel, powerhouse, hospital/clinic, and educational-institute costing examples; syllabus-specific accounting-record formats beyond the transport problem. Search: `service costing canteen hotel powerhouse hospital clinic educational institute cost sheet solved problem`.

### 11. Integrated Accounting System

- **NOT COVERED:** Definition, features, benefits, integrated journal entries, WIP/Finished Goods/Cost of Goods Sold/Cost of Sales accounts, financial-statement preparation under an integrated system, and reconciliation with Financial Accounting records. Search: `integrated cost accounting system journal entries WIP finished goods cost of sales reconciliation financial accounts`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 11 syllabus units (thematic headings; the source's two duplicated numbers are normalized above). **Verified videos:** 12 unique direct watch-page links. **Every syllabus subtopic is accounted for** in the coverage map, either as video coverage or an explicit NOT COVERED gap with targeted search terms. **Every subtopic does not have a verified video:** 9 of 11 units have at least one relevant verified video, but only partial coverage within several; Introduction to Cost Accounting and Integrated Accounting System have no direct verified lesson in this map. No Bangladesh-specific law/tax/VAT item occurs in this course outline; one transport example is explicitly from an Indian ICAI problem and its passenger-tax adjustment should not be applied as Bangladesh guidance.

# 3102 Corporate Finance — Semester 5 (Third Year, First Semester)

**Program/version:** University of Barishal, BBA Accounting & Information Systems, curriculum effective from the 11th batch (session 2022–2023).  
**Source scope:** the 11th-batch AIS curriculum’s *Detailed Syllabus*, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum. The course list on PDF p. 35 prints `302 Corporate Finance` under Third Year, First Semester; the detailed-syllabus heading on PDF p. 82 is `3102: Corporate Finance`. This appears to be a code/heading inconsistency; the course title and semester match the requested course, so the detailed-syllabus entry is used below.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

The outline below retains the syllabus wording and order. Bracketed ILO references are reproduced from the source.

1. **Introduction to Corporate Finance [ILO 1]** — Definition of Corporate Finance; Corporate Finance Decisions; Legal Forms of Business Organization; The Goals of Corporate Finance Manager; The Agency Problem and Control of the Corporation; Regulation and Corporate Governance; Financial Markets and the Corporation; Financial Statements Analysis; Financial Planning Models; Determinants of Growth; Caveats of Financial Planning Models.
2. **Capital Investment Decisions and Risk Analysis [ILO 2]** — Capital Investment Evaluation Methods; Incremental or Relevant Cash Flows; Estimating Investment and Operating Cash Flows; Special Cases of Discounted Cash Flow Analysis; Capital Rationing; Inflation and Capital Investment Decisions; Capital Investment Decisions Under Risk; Sensitivity Analysis; Scenario Analysis and Break-Even Analysis; Real Options; Decision Trees.
3. **Capital Structure Decisions [ILO 3, ILO 4]** — Financing Decision and Firm Value; Efficient Capital Markets; Different Types of Market Efficiency; Operating Leverage; Financial Leverage and Total Leverage; Impact of Leverage on Firm Value and Risk; Modigliani and Miller (MM) Theory of Capital Structure: Proposition I and II; Capital Structure and Cost of Capital; Limits to the Use of Debt; Financial Distress Costs; Agency Cost of Debt; Integration of Tax Effect and Financial Distress Costs; Debt Signaling Hypothesis; Agency Cost of Equity; The Pecking-Order Theory; How Firms Establish Capital Structure.
4. **Valuation and Capital Budgeting for the Levered Firm [ILO 5]** — Adjusted Present Value Approach; Flow to Equity Approach; Weighted Average Cost of Capital Approach; A Comparison of APV, FTE and WACC Approach; Estimation of the Discount Rate for Valuation; Beta and Leverage.
5. **Dividend and Other Payouts Decisions [ILO 6]** — Different Types of Payouts; Standard Method of Cash Dividend Payment; Dividend Relevance Theory; Walter and Gordon Model; Modigliani and Miller (MM) Dividend Policy Irrelevance Theory; Repurchase of Stock; Taxes; Dividend and Stock Repurchases; Factors Affecting Dividend Policy; Different Types of Dividend Policy; Dividend Signaling Hypothesis; Information Content of Dividend; Clientele Effect; Stock Dividends and Stock Splits.
6. **Working Capital Management [ILO 7]** — Concepts of Working Capital; Importance of Working Capital Management; Factors Determining Working Capital; Hedging; Aggressive and Conservative Approach to Working Capital Financing; Estimating Working Capital Requirements; Cash and Marketable Securities Management; Receivable Management and Credit Policy; Inventory Management; Short-term Financing Management.
7. **International Corporate Finance [ILO 8]** — Domestic versus Foreign Investment; Foreign Exchange Markets, Transactions and Participants; Different Types of Exchange Rates; International Monetary System; Trading in Foreign Exchange; Factors Influencing Exchange Rates; Purchasing Power Parity; Interest Rate Parity; The International Fisher Effect; International Capital Budgeting; Capital Structure and Working Capital Management; Political Risk.

## Topic-to-video map

Only the watch pages listed below were opened to verify each displayed title, channel, and lesson relevance. The page’s description/title was used conservatively; a video is not credited for syllabus details it does not demonstrate. Every course-content item appears either in a coverage statement or under **NOT COVERED**. “Covered” means the page/title substantiates useful study of that listed topic, not that a short video substitutes for the full course.

### 1. Introduction to Corporate Finance

- **The Goals of Corporate Finance Manager** — [Session 2: The Objective in Corporate Finance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RZYuuX6FOBg), **Aswath Damodaran**. The watch page identifies it as a corporate-finance decision-making objective lecture; it is useful for an exam answer explaining the goal behind financial decisions.
- **Financial Markets and the Corporation; Corporate Finance Decisions (capital raising/financing and valuation decisions)** — [Introduction to Corporate Finance: Course Video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5eGRi66iUfU), **Corporate Finance Institute**. Its watch-page description explicitly includes capital markets, capital raising, valuation and equity securities, giving a structured introduction to firm financing and markets.
- **Financial Planning Models** — [Financial Planning Models | Pro Format Financial Statements | Corporate Finance | Chp 4 p 1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=owHNQYb2DQU), **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. The page says it covers sales forecasts, pro-forma statements, asset requirements and the plug variable, making it useful for explaining and setting out a financial-planning model.
- **NOT COVERED:** Definition of Corporate Finance (as a distinct examinable definition); Legal Forms of Business Organization; The Agency Problem and Control of the Corporation; Regulation and Corporate Governance; Financial Statements Analysis; Determinants of Growth; Caveats of Financial Planning Models. Search YouTube: `corporate finance definition legal forms agency problem control corporation financial statement analysis growth financial planning model caveats lecture`; for regulation, `Bangladesh corporate governance BSEC directives capital market regulation lecture` (no Bangladesh-specific regulatory video was verified).

### 2. Capital Investment Decisions and Risk Analysis

- **Capital Investment Evaluation Methods (NPV, IRR and payback)** — [Capital Budgeting: NPV, IRR, Payback | MUST-KNOW for Finance Roles](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7gG0Evugubo), **Kenji Explains**. The watch-page description promises NPV, IRR and payback calculations using real examples in Excel, so it is useful for practising core capital-budgeting computations.
- **Incremental or Relevant Cash Flows** — [Capital Budgeting Analysis: What cash flows should you use?](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5w1m87pWBqM), **David Hillier**. This investment-appraisal lesson is explicitly about selecting the cash flows for capital-budgeting analysis, helping with relevant-cash-flow reasoning in problem questions.
- **Sensitivity Analysis; Scenario Analysis; Break-Even Analysis** — [Capital Budgeting: Sensitivity Analysis, Scenario Analysis, Break-Even Analysis](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=REPnSbR_D04), **David Hillier**. The verified lesson title names all three syllabus techniques, supporting comparison questions and revision of project-risk analysis.
- **NOT COVERED:** Estimating Investment and Operating Cash Flows (as separate step-by-step estimates); Special Cases of Discounted Cash Flow Analysis; Capital Rationing; Inflation and Capital Investment Decisions; Capital Investment Decisions Under Risk (broader risk treatment beyond the sensitivity/scenario/break-even tools above); Real Options; Decision Trees. Search YouTube: `capital budgeting initial investment operating cash flow working capital terminal cash flows special cases capital rationing inflation real options decision tree worked example`.

### 3. Capital Structure Decisions

- **Financing Decision and Firm Value (debt/equity financing choice and capital-structure effects)** — [Capital Structure (2025 CFA® Level I Exam – Corporate Issuers – Learning Module 6)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Un8dX7RGxIg), **AnalystPrep**. The page identifies an exam-prep lesson on corporate financing choices using debt and equity and capital-structure effects, useful for framing financing-choice and risk/value answers.
- **NOT COVERED:** Efficient Capital Markets; Different Types of Market Efficiency; Operating Leverage; Financial Leverage; Total Leverage; a worked treatment of the Impact of Leverage on Firm Value and Risk; Modigliani and Miller (MM) Theory of Capital Structure—Proposition I and II; Capital Structure and Cost of Capital; Limits to the Use of Debt; Financial Distress Costs; Agency Cost of Debt; Integration of Tax Effect and Financial Distress Costs; Debt Signaling Hypothesis; Agency Cost of Equity; The Pecking-Order Theory; How Firms Establish Capital Structure. Search YouTube: `corporate finance capital structure MM proposition I II taxes financial distress agency costs debt signaling pecking order leverage worked lecture`.

### 4. Valuation and Capital Budgeting for the Levered Firm

- **Adjusted Present Value Approach; Flow to Equity Approach; Weighted Average Cost of Capital Approach; A Comparison of APV, FTE and WACC Approach** — [FTE, APV & WACC Part 1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=v7gSUOUnn9g), **Corporate Finance By Anwar**; and [FTE, APV & WACC Part 2](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=a71Q-3RoU0A), **Corporate Finance By Anwar**. Both watch pages identify this as a comparison of capital-expenditure evaluation methods; together the titled parts provide a short method-comparison series useful for exam method selection and calculation practice.
- **NOT COVERED:** Estimation of the Discount Rate for Valuation; Beta and Leverage. Search YouTube: `levered firm valuation estimate discount rate beta leverage adjusted beta worked example APV FTE WACC`.

### 5. Dividend and Other Payouts Decisions

- **Dividend Relevance Theory; Walter and Gordon Model; Modigliani and Miller (MM) Dividend Policy Irrelevance Theory** — [MM Gordon Walter Theory of Dividend | Unit 4 Part 2 | Financial Management and Corporate Finance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=089S3A2bdaE), **Learning with Dr. Anand Vyas**. The verified title expressly names MM, Gordon and Walter dividend theories, making it a direct theory-revision aid for written comparisons and model-based answers.
- **NOT COVERED:** Different Types of Payouts; Standard Method of Cash Dividend Payment; Repurchase of Stock; Taxes; Dividend and Stock Repurchases; Factors Affecting Dividend Policy; Different Types of Dividend Policy; Dividend Signaling Hypothesis; Information Content of Dividend; Clientele Effect; Stock Dividends; Stock Splits. Search YouTube: `corporate dividend policy cash dividend payment stock repurchase taxes payout types signaling information content clientele effect stock dividend split exam lecture`.

### 6. Working Capital Management

- **Concepts of Working Capital; Importance of Working Capital Management; Working Capital Financing Policies; Cash Management** — [Working Capital and Financing Policies | Cash Management | CMA (US)-PART 2 Lec 56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bFLgg4s5gy0), **Qasim Riaz**. The watch-page description names working capital, its management objective, financing policies and cash management, which helps with conceptual exam answers and financing-policy revision. **Jurisdiction note:** this is a US CMA course; use it for general finance concepts, not Bangladesh-specific rules.
- **NOT COVERED:** Factors Determining Working Capital; Hedging; the specific Aggressive and Conservative Approach to Working Capital Financing; Estimating Working Capital Requirements; Marketable Securities Management; Receivable Management; Credit Policy; Inventory Management; Short-term Financing Management. Search YouTube: `working capital requirements determinants hedging aggressive conservative financing receivables credit policy inventory short term finance worked problems`.

### 7. International Corporate Finance

- **Foreign Exchange Markets / exchange rates and general parity conditions** — [Exchange Rates, Parity Conditions, and Global Financial Prices](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YYyfQyw44zc), **Understand Finance with Dr. Dicle**. The watch page presents a lesson on exchange-rate movements and parity conditions, useful as a conceptual introduction before practising the syllabus’s named parity relationships. **Quality caveat:** YouTube displayed an AI badge on this watch page; treat it as supplementary overview, not the sole authority for exam formulae.
- **International Capital Budgeting; foreign-exchange exposure relevant to foreign investment** — [Foreign Exchange Exposure and International Capital Budgeting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vvfbAYuLp88), **Understand Finance with Dr. Dicle**. Its page describes currency effects on international investment cash flows and explicitly names international capital budgeting, helping with a written explanation of cross-border investment risk. **Quality caveat:** the page displayed an AI badge. No Bangladesh-specific law or regulatory claim is made by this lesson.
- **NOT COVERED:** Domestic versus Foreign Investment (direct comparison); Foreign Exchange Markets—Transactions and Participants; Different Types of Exchange Rates; International Monetary System; Trading in Foreign Exchange; Factors Influencing Exchange Rates; Purchasing Power Parity (as a named method); Interest Rate Parity (as a named method); The International Fisher Effect; Capital Structure and Working Capital Management in an international setting; Political Risk. Search YouTube: `international corporate finance domestic foreign investment foreign exchange transactions participants exchange rate types monetary system PPP IRP international Fisher effect political risk capital budgeting`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 91 discrete syllabus subtopics, counting paired methods/tools separately while retaining the source’s course-unit headings. **Verified video count:** 13 distinct YouTube watch pages opened; each recommended link is a direct `youtube.com/watch?v=...` link, with displayed title and channel checked on-page. **Does every topic have at least one verified video? No.** Uncovered items are explicitly listed by unit with targeted YouTube search terms; the biggest gaps are capital-structure theory, dividend/payout mechanics, working-capital subareas, international-finance detail and Bangladesh-specific regulatory coverage. The videos are supplementary study aids, not a complete one-to-one syllabus replacement. The international-finance pair is visibly AI-badged, and the working-capital lesson is explicitly a US CMA series; the syllabus itself does not list a Bangladesh statute by name in this course’s detailed-content section.

# 3103 Audit and Assurance

**Semester:** Semester 5 — Third Year, First Semester (11th-batch AIS curriculum)

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

Transcribed from the 11th-batch Accounting and Information Systems curriculum's **Detailed Syllabus** section, matching by course title and semester (not the earlier Management Studies curriculum). The course list identifies **3103 Audit and Assurance** in the third-year first semester.

1. **Audit and assurance engagement (ILO 1).** Concept of audit and assurance engagements; assurance process; levels of assurance; importance and limitations of assurance services; framework for assurance engagements; purposes and characteristics of different assurance engagements; auditing and its characteristics; reasonable assurance; scope and objectives of auditing; economic demand for auditing; reduction of information risk; historical background of audit. Types of audit: operational; financial statements; compliance; interim and final; cost; management; social; performance; value-for-money; 3E; due diligence; forensic; system; continuous; internal; external; government; and tax audit.

2. **Regulatory framework of statutory audit in Bangladesh (ILO 2).** Legal provisions of audit under the Companies Act 1994, sections 210–220; legal provisions for keeping books of records under the Companies Act 1994, sections 181, 183–187 and 189; Securities and Exchange Rules, 1987; Bank Companies Act, 1991; Insurance Act, 2010; Income Tax Ordinance, 1984.

3. **Professional bodies and professional standards (ILO 3).** Regulatory body versus standard-setting body. Regulatory bodies: Financial Reporting Council (FRC), Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Commission (BSEC), Bangladesh Bank (BB), Insurance Development and Regulatory Authority (IDRA). Standard-setting bodies: International Federation of Accountants (IFAC), International Accounting Standards Board (IASB), Financial Accounting Standards Board (FASB). Professional bodies: Institute of Chartered Accountants of Bangladesh (ICAB), Institute of Cost and Management Accountants of Bangladesh (ICMAB), and the syllabus's printed name “Institute of Chartered Secretariat of Bangladesh (ICSB).” Auditing profession in Bangladesh; need for professional standards; International Auditing and Assurance Standards Board (IAASB); International Standards on Auditing (ISA), International Standards on Assurance Engagements (ISAE), International Standards on Review Engagements (ISRE), International Standards on Related Services (ISRS), International Standards on Quality Control (ISQC), International Auditing Practice Notes (IAPN); adoption of auditing standards in Bangladesh.

4. **Overview of audit of historical financial information (ILO 4).** Objective of financial statements audit (ISA 200); management's responsibilities; auditor's responsibilities; professional skepticism; professional judgment; management assertions; setting audit objectives, including transaction- and balance-related audit objectives; expectation gap in audit service; audit limitations.

5. **Process for audit of historical financial information (ILOs 5–9).**
   - **Accepting the audit engagement / pre-engagement procedures (ILO 6):** client intimation; evaluation of management's integrity; identification of unusual issues; professional clearance; Form 23B; evaluation of independence; due care; agreeing terms of the engagement letter (ISA 210); ensuring quality control for an audit (ISA 220).
   - **Audit planning (ILO 7):** planning an audit of financial statements (ISA 300)—set overall audit strategy, benefits of an audit plan, develop an audit plan, audit-planning checklist; identifying and assessing risks of material misstatement through understanding the entity and its environment (ISA 315); understanding the client's internal control systems; COSO components of internal control; impact of IT infrastructure on internal control; walkthrough test and assessing control risk; preliminary analytical procedure; materiality for financial statements as a whole and performance materiality (ISA 320); risk-based audit approach; audit risk model and assessing acceptable audit risk; considering fraud risk (ISA 240); audit-planning memorandum and audit program.
   - **Performing audit tests (ILO 8):** test of controls; substantive tests—substantive tests of transactions, substantive analytical procedures, tests of details of balances; evidence (ISA 500); persuasiveness of audit evidence—sufficiency and appropriateness; evidence mix; analytical procedures (ISA 520); audit sampling (ISA 530); written representations (ISA 580); documentation and working papers (ISA 230).
   - **Completing the audit and issuing an audit report (ILO 9):** complete field work; evaluate findings; subsequent events (ISA 560); communicating with those charged with governance and management (ISA 265); form the final audit opinion and issue the report (ISA 700, ISA 701); components of an audit report; key audit matters; auditor's report decision process—materiality, true and fair view/fairly presented; clean/unqualified opinion; modified opinions—qualified, adverse and disclaimer (ISA 705); emphasis-of-matter and other-matter paragraphs (ISA 706); going-concern issue (ISA 570).

6. **Professional ethics (ILO 10).** Definition of ethics; role and professional importance of ethical codes; rule-based versus principle-based ethical codes; IFAC Code of Ethics; fundamental principles—integrity, objectivity, professional competence and due care, confidentiality and independence; resolving ethical conflicts; conflict of interest; confidentiality and its importance, safeguards to confidentiality, and disclosure of confidential information; independence requirements in an assurance engagement; threats to fundamental ethical principles and assurance-provider independence—self-interest, self-review, “management” (as printed), advocacy, familiarity and intimidation threats; safeguards to eliminate or reduce threats.

7. **Liabilities of auditors (ILO 11).** Nature of professional accountants' liabilities; business failure; audit failure; audit risk; auditor liability under the Companies Act; liabilities under the Income Tax Ordinance; contractual liability; civil liability—negligence and misfeasance; criminal liability; liability to clients; liability to third parties; auditor liability for libel; professional response to legal liability; notable legal cases.

## Topic-to-video map

Each linked YouTube watch page below was opened directly to check its displayed title, channel and lesson relevance. “NOT COVERED” means no directly suitable video was verified for that named syllabus content; search phrases are supplied rather than an unverified link.

### Verified lessons

- [**Topic 1 - Introduction and overview of audit and assurance**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XnwldL6Jpsg) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** Covers introductory audit/assurance concepts, the role and purpose of audit, assurance, and broad conceptual framing for assurance levels and reasonable assurance. Its lecture format helps with concise definitions and written-answer framing for foundational questions.

- [**Topic 2 - Ethics, legal liability and client acceptance**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BPd7_8NExVk) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** Covers the syllabus's broad themes of professional ethics, auditor legal liability and client acceptance; use it for a conceptual review, not as a Bangladesh-law authority. The lecture format gives a revision structure for short theory answers, but its page does not establish coverage of every IFAC principle, threat, safeguard or Bangladeshi statute.

- [**Topic 3 - Risk and the audit**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=INw7djAmtv4) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** Covers audit risk and the relationship between risk and the audit process. It helps students explain risk-based audit thinking in exam answers; consult the separate planning lecture below for plan/risk-model review.

- [**2020 audit lectures - Module 2 - standards, assertions and the audit risk model**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iDW4TetOCSM) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** The title identifies standards, management assertions and the audit risk model. It is useful for revising assertions and connecting them to audit risk, though it does not demonstrate coverage of the syllabus's full list of standard-setting and regulatory bodies or all named standards.

- [**Lecture 4 - audit planning, the audit risk model and fraud**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_pYx96UBzyA) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** The watch-page description says it discusses the importance of audit planning, use of the audit-risk model in audit strategy, and the auditor's fraud responsibilities. It supports exam answers on planning rationale, risk model and fraud risk; it is not evidence of coverage of every ISA 300/315/320 planning requirement.

- [**Topic 4 - Audit Evidence**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nd8ibLwfs_I) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** Covers audit evidence and procedures at a topic level. It helps structure written answers about obtaining evidence; check the syllabus gaps below for the specific evidence standards and documentation points not verified by the page.

- [**Topic 6 - Sampling**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DoiGOpP4nF0) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** The page description asks how auditors determine how much evidence to collect and says the lecture examines sampling. It is useful for explaining the purpose of sampling and evidence-quantity decisions in exam answers.

- [**Topic 7 - Testing internal controls**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o_Nhe7sA4Ak) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** Covers tests of internal controls. Its dedicated lesson title is useful for distinguishing control-test questions from substantive-test questions; the page alone does not demonstrate coverage of all COSO or IT-control subtopics.

- [**Topic 8 - Basics of substantive testing**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uBQYIZukwO8) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** Covers substantive testing at an introductory level. It helps with the distinction between substantive tests and control tests, but is not verified as covering every transaction, analytical-procedure or balance-detail method listed in the syllabus.

- [**Topic 11 - Completing the audit**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=h0iTWwheYtM) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** A dedicated audit-completion lecture. It helps organize a completion-stage written answer and review the idea of closing an audit; the watch page does not establish coverage of each ISA 560/265/570 requirement.

- [**Audit opinions - wrapping up the audit with ISA/ASA700**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jrlJ8so0RRk) — **AmandaLovesToAudit.** Covers audit opinions and reporting under ISA/ASA 700. It is useful for structuring an answer on forming and communicating an audit opinion; ISA/ASA means the lecture may use Australian terminology, so use Bangladesh-adopted wording where required.

- [**Appointment of Auditor & Remuneration || Company Act 1994 || Section 210 _ ICSB**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NBHhs3xyG6c) — **ICSB & ICMAB SchooL.** Bangladesh-specific lesson on appointment and remuneration of auditors under Companies Act 1994, section 210. Its local-law focus supports an exam answer on that provision; it covers section 210, not the syllabus's entire statutory-law list.

### Explicit gaps: NOT COVERED by a verified direct lesson

- **Audit types:** operational, financial-statement, compliance, interim/final, cost, management, social, performance, value-for-money, 3E, due-diligence, forensic, system, continuous, internal, external, government and tax audits. Search: `auditing types operational compliance performance forensic 3E due diligence lecture examples`.
- **Bangladesh statutory audit law beyond Companies Act 1994 section 210:** Companies Act sections 211–220 and record-keeping sections 181, 183–187, 189; Securities and Exchange Rules 1987; Bank Companies Act 1991; Insurance Act 2010; Income Tax Ordinance 1984; auditor liabilities under the Companies Act and Income Tax Ordinance. Search each instrument with `Bangladesh auditor audit lecture` plus its exact act/section; do not substitute another country's law. Section 210 appointment/remuneration alone is covered above.
- **Named bodies and standards:** regulatory-vs-standard-setter distinction and the named FRC, BSEC, BB, IDRA, IFAC, IASB, FASB, ICAB, ICMAB and ICSB roles; Bangladesh auditing profession and its need for standards; IAASB's ISA, ISAE, ISRE, ISRS, ISQC and IAPN series; and adoption of auditing standards in Bangladesh. The standards/assertions video is only a general lead-in. Search: `Bangladesh ICAB IAASB BSA ISA ISAE ISRE ISRS ISQC IAPN adoption lecture`, `FRC BSEC Bangladesh audit regulators ICAB ICMAB ICSB lecture`.
- **Engagement fundamentals beyond the introductory overview:** assurance-process stages, detailed levels/framework/limitations and purposes/characteristics of different assurance engagements; detailed history; economic demand and information-risk reduction. The introductory video is a useful starting point, but these detailed subtopics are not individually verified. Search: `assurance engagement framework levels reasonable limited assurance purpose characteristics audit history lecture`.
- **ISA 200 and audit overview detail:** management's and auditor's responsibilities under ISA 200; professional skepticism and judgment; transaction- and balance-related audit objectives; expectation gap; and detailed audit limitations. Assertions and risk have videos above, but no verified ISA 200-specific lesson was found. Search: `ISA 200 overall objectives independent auditor management responsibilities skepticism judgment expectation gap assertions lecture`.
- **Pre-engagement particulars:** client intimation; management-integrity evaluation; unusual issues; professional clearance; Form 23B; independence evaluation; due care; ISA 210 engagement-letter terms; and ISA 220 audit quality control. Client acceptance is a broad theme in the ethics/liability/client-acceptance lesson, but these procedures and standards were not verified in detail. Search: `Bangladesh Form 23B audit client acceptance ISA 210 ISA 220 professional clearance lecture`.
- **Detailed planning:** ISA 300 overall strategy, plan benefits/development/checklist; ISA 315 entity/environment risk assessment; internal-control-system understanding, COSO components, IT infrastructure, walkthrough and control risk; preliminary analytical procedures; ISA 320 overall and performance materiality; acceptable audit risk; ISA 240 fraud-risk requirements; audit-planning memorandum and program. Risk, planning and fraud concepts have introductory lessons above; these named requirements and practical artifacts are not demonstrated. Search: `ISA 300 audit plan checklist ISA 315 COSO IT walkthrough ISA 320 performance materiality ISA 240 fraud audit program worked example`.
- **Detailed audit-test methods and documentation:** substantive tests of transactions, substantive analytical procedures, tests of details of balances; ISA 500 sufficiency/appropriateness and evidence mix; ISA 520 analytical procedures; ISA 580 written representations; ISA 230 documentation and working papers. General evidence, control testing, substantive testing and sampling have lessons above, but these named ISA methods and workpapers lack a verified focused lesson. Search: `ISA 500 sufficiency appropriateness audit evidence mix ISA 520 analytics ISA 580 written representations ISA 230 working papers exam`.
- **Detailed completion/reporting requirements:** field-work completion and evaluation of findings; ISA 560 subsequent events; ISA 265 governance communications; ISA 700/701 report components and key audit matters; decision process/materiality and true-and-fair/fair-presentation; clean/unqualified opinion; ISA 705 qualified/adverse/disclaimer; ISA 706 emphasis-of-matter/other-matter; ISA 570 going concern. The completion and ISA/ASA 700 videos are broad support only; no focused page-verified video was found for the listed detailed standards and report modifications. Search: `ISA 560 subsequent events ISA 265 governance ISA 701 key audit matters ISA 705 opinions ISA 706 ISA 570 going concern audit lecture`.
- **Detailed ethics code application:** IFAC code, rule- vs principle-based codes, each fundamental principle, ethical-conflict resolution, conflict of interest, confidentiality safeguards/disclosure, independence requirements, each listed threat (including the syllabus's “management” threat) and safeguards. The ethics lesson is a broad introduction, not verified as covering this full list. Search: `IFAC IESBA code five fundamental principles threats safeguards independence conflict confidentiality audit exam`.
- **Detailed legal-liability doctrine and cases:** business failure versus audit failure and audit risk; contractual, negligence, misfeasance and criminal liability; liability to clients and third parties; libel; professional response and notable cases. The broad liability lesson is not Bangladesh-specific and is not verified for these detailed categories or cases. Search: `auditor liability negligence misfeasance criminal third party libel notable cases Bangladesh audit lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 7 numbered detailed-syllabus units. **Verified videos:** 12 distinct watch pages opened and checked for title, channel and relevance. **Does every syllabus subtopic have a directly suitable verified video? No.** Some broad foundations and audit-process stages are supported, but the gaps above remain substantial—especially Bangladesh laws beyond section 210, the named standards/body catalogue, detailed ISA requirements, ethics-code application and auditor-liability doctrines/cases. The syllabus print has minor wording/formatting anomalies: “Competing the Audit” (used above as “Completing” for readability), “Management Threat,” “Secretariat” in the printed ICSB body name, and “PerformanceMateriality” without a space. The target code/title and third-year first-semester placement match the course list; no target-course code mismatch was found.

# 3104 Banking and Insurance

**Semester:** Semester 5 — Third Year, First Semester (BBA AIS, 11th-batch curriculum; 3 credits).

**Source:** University of Barishal BBA Accounting & Information Systems syllabus, *Detailed Syllabus*, course header and contents on PDF p. 87 onward. The course list confirms code/title and semester in the 11th-batch curriculum. The outline below retains the syllabus wording and named laws/standards, with line wrapping normalized.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

### Part A — Banking Financial Institution

1. **Evolution of Banking Financial Institutions:** introduction to banking; origin, growth and development of banking; historical development of banking systems; banking history of Bangladesh and its present status.
2. **Classification of Banks:** central banks; commercial banks; investment or industrial banks; exchange banks; co-operative banks; land mortgage banks; savings banks; merchant banks; specialized banks; mixed banking; group banking; chain banking; unit banking; branch banking; offshore and onshore banking.
3. **Central Bank:** characteristics; nature and functions; necessity; central banking theory; comparison between central and commercial banks; clearing-house system.
4. **Commercial Bank:** theories; functions and services; sound commercial banking principles; employment of funds; multiple credit expansion; risks and objectives of risk management; elements of sound risk-management systems; bank risk-management guidelines; regulation by Bangladesh Bank (BB) and Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Commission (BSEC); classified loans and loan-loss provisions; operation and management; inspection and investigation; accounts and audit; submission of returns and reports; corporate-governance guidelines and practices in banks; green banking; SME banking; corporate social responsibility; enterprise risk management; BASEL III; risk-based capital adequacy; stress testing.
5. **Preparation and Presentation of Bank’s Financial Statements:** bank reporting framework; voluntary- and mandatory-reporting regulations; relevant provisions of the Bank Companies Act 1991; preparation of banking-company financial statements under BB guidelines; presentation and disclosure in Islamic-bank financial statements; ideas on IAS 39/IFRS 9 in preparing bank financial statements.

### Part B — Insurance

6. **Insurance Company in Bangladesh:** concept of insurance; development and present status of insurance companies in Bangladesh; role and functions; principles; classification of insurance business and policies; Insurance Development and Regulatory Authority (IDRA); accounts, audit, actuary report and statements (IFRS 4, IAS 19 and IAS 26); actuary and actuarial valuation; commissions, rebates and management expenses; dividend distribution; bonus and profit; registers; submission of returns; re-insurance business in Bangladesh; role of Insurance Academy; Shariah-based insurance companies and Shariah board.
7. **Life Insurance:** life-assurance contract; types of policies; certain aspects of life assurance; actuarial valuation; premium, valuation and surplus calculation; surrender value; settlement of claims; life fund; present status of life-insurance business in Bangladesh; methods of spreading insurance in rural areas; loan against life policy; policy lapsation; policy renewal.
8. **Fire Insurance:** material-loss insurance; consequential-loss insurance; settlement of claims; extent of loss by fire in Bangladesh; moral hazard; management problems of fire insurance.
9. **Marine Insurance:** marine-insurance contract; types of policies; maritime perils; marine losses; settlement of claims; Act of God.
10. **Other Insurance:** health insurance; postal life insurance; crop insurance; cattle insurance; civil aviation insurance; export credit guarantee insurance; group insurance; bank-deposit insurance; fidelity insurance, etc.
11. **Financial Statements of Insurance Companies:** computation of insurance claims; preparation of insurance-company financial statements under IDRA guidelines and the Insurance Act 2010.
12. **Accounting for Islamic Insurance Companies:** presentation and disclosure in Islamic-insurance-company financial statements; disclosure of bases for determining and allocating surplus or deficit; investment funds; provisions; reserves.

### Part C — Other Financial Institutions

13. **Non-Banking Financial Institutions (NBFI):** introduction; origin, growth and development; historical development and present status; types of NBFI; accounting for lease finance; Islamic financing; “Preparation of Financial Statements of Banking Companies” (wording as printed); Bangladesh Bank guideline for NBFI; SED and other relevant regulatory authorities; applicable laws.

**Syllabus numbering note:** the detailed contents number both “Accounting for Islamic Insurance Companies” and “Non-Banking Financial Institutions” as 12. I list the latter as item 13 only to distinguish the two blocks. Under NBFI, “Preparation of Financial Statements of Banking Companies” appears as printed and seems to be a heading/copying mismatch; it has not been silently replaced with a guessed NBFI equivalent. The course list and detailed-syllabus header both identify the requested course as **3104 Banking and Insurance**; the correct semester is Third Year, First Semester.

## Topic-to-video map

Each linked item below was opened on its direct YouTube watch page to check the displayed title, channel and relevance. Coverage descriptions are deliberately limited to what the video/page establishes. “NOT COVERED” means I did not find a direct, suitable lesson among the verified items; search terms are supplied instead. Non-Bangladesh lessons are explicitly flagged.

### Verified lessons

- [15. Central Banks & Commercial Banking, Part 1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lPD9fx8fK1k) — **MIT OpenCourseWare**. **Coverage:** conceptual central-bank and commercial-banking material, including central-bank role/approach; useful for framing short conceptual comparisons. **Exam value:** a university lecture provides a structured conceptual basis, but it is part of MIT’s *Blockchain and Money* course, not a Bangladesh banking-law lecture; it does not establish coverage of every central-bank syllabus subtopic.
- [An Overview of Basel I,II,III Implementation in Bangladesh.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kLkxK8Tl1nY) — **Professional Journey...**. **Coverage:** overview of Basel I/II/III implementation in Bangladesh (the title and description identify an AIBB risk-management module). **Exam value:** supports a Bangladesh-context written overview of the Basel framework; not a substitute for BB circulars or detailed capital/stress-test calculations.
- [FINANCIAL STATEMENTS OF BANKS (PART 1)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ar7eLmiVVtc) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. **Coverage:** general preparation and formats of bank annual financial statements. **Exam value:** the lesson explicitly describes statement formats and preparation guidance, making it useful for presentation-format questions; it is not demonstrated as a Bangladesh BB-guideline solution.
- [IFRS 9 ECL Model Explained: Simple Walkthrough with Example](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Rx_RzRcgvlk) — **Silvia of CPDbox**. **Coverage:** IFRS 9 expected-credit-loss model with numerical examples. **Exam value:** step-by-step ECL examples help with impairment/provisioning concepts and calculations; international/general IFRS context, not Bangladesh-specific loan-classification rules, and not IAS 39.
- [Corporate Accounting I Accounts of Life Insurance Companies I Theory 1 I Khans Commerce Tutorial I](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lShTze5Oegw) — **Hasham Ali Khan**. **Coverage:** introductory theory lesson on accounts of life-insurance companies. **Exam value:** offers a starting framework for written answers on life-insurer accounting; the watch page does not demonstrate Bangladesh-specific rules or detailed actuarial/policy calculations. **Jurisdiction caveat:** Indian/B.Com-oriented corporate-accounting context; do not treat as Bangladesh regulation.
- [1. Insurance Claims Chapter - Introduction & Most Important 3 Steps To Solve Insurance Claims](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7oHKBGfEbas) — **Devika's Commerce & Management Academy**. **Coverage:** introductory claim-solving workflow and steps. **Exam value:** helps organize a written solution before calculations; this is a general corporate-accounting lesson and not Bangladesh-specific.
- [Fire Insurance Claim Problem 1 - Financial Accounting - B.COM / BBA / IPCC By Saheb Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=66aTH3VE8-0) — **Saheb Academy**. **Coverage:** worked fire-claim problem, including preparation of a trading account, memorandum trading account and fire-insurance claim statement. **Exam value:** directly useful calculation practice for fire-claim questions and statement layout. **Jurisdiction caveat:** general Indian B.Com/BBA/IPCC accounting exercise, not Bangladesh fire regulation or loss data.
- [Marine Insurance Contract Part I | Types of Marine Policies | Lectures on Insurance Law.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y9VQr6_TPH4) — **Waseem Khan**. **Coverage:** marine-insurance contract and types of marine policies. **Exam value:** supports concise definitions and classification answers. **Jurisdiction caveat:** general insurance-law lesson, not Bangladesh-specific; it does not establish coverage of maritime perils, claims calculations or Bangladesh law.
- [What is Takaful? Islamic Insurance Meaning, Principles, Models & Types | AIMS Education](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tuSS61eKC48) — **AIMS Education**. **Coverage:** takaful meaning, principles, models/types and distinction from conventional insurance. **Exam value:** gives an organized conceptual comparison for Islamic-insurance written answers. **Jurisdiction caveat:** general/international treatment, not Bangladesh IDRA/Shariah-board requirements or financial-statement calculations.

### Mapped syllabus coverage and explicit gaps

- **A1 Evolution of banking institutions:** **NOT COVERED**—all listed points: introduction, origin/growth/development, historical development of banking systems, Bangladesh banking history and present status. Search YouTube: `Bangladesh banking history evolution banking system lecture Bangladesh BBA`.
- **A2 Classification of banks:** **NOT COVERED**—central/commercial/investment or industrial/exchange/co-operative/land-mortgage/savings/merchant/specialized banks; mixed/group/chain/unit/branch banking; offshore/onshore banking. MIT’s lecture is a limited conceptual supplement for central vs. commercial banking, not a classification lesson. Search: `classification of banks types unit branch merchant exchange cooperative offshore onshore lecture`.
- **A3 Central bank:** MIT lecture offers conceptual support for central-bank role and central/commercial-bank comparison only. **NOT COVERED:** full characteristics; nature/functions and necessity; central-banking theory; clearing-house system. Search: `central bank functions characteristics necessity clearing house system Bangladesh Bank lecture`.
- **A4 Commercial bank:** MIT lecture is a limited conceptual support for commercial banking; IFRS 9 ECL video supports the general ECL concept only; Basel video supports Basel framework/implementation. **NOT COVERED:** commercial-banking theories; full functions/services; sound principles; employment of funds; multiple credit expansion; complete risk objectives/elements/guidelines; BB/BSEC regulatory requirements; Bangladesh classified-loan categories and provisions; operations/management; inspection/investigation; bank accounts/audit; returns/reports; governance; green banking; SME banking; CSR; ERM; Basel-specific capital adequacy calculations and stress testing. Search: `Bangladesh Bank commercial banking principles credit creation classified loan provisioning risk management green banking SME Basel III stress testing lecture`.
- **A5 Bank financial statements:** FOG lesson supports general bank-statement preparation and formats. IFRS 9 video supports expected-credit-loss accounting only. **NOT COVERED:** voluntary/mandatory reporting rules; Bank Companies Act 1991; Bangladesh Bank statement guidelines; Islamic-bank statement disclosures; IAS 39; broader IFRS 9 bank-statement preparation. Search: `Bangladesh bank financial statements Bangladesh Bank guidelines Bank Companies Act 1991 IAS 39 IFRS 9 lecture`.
- **B6 Insurance company in Bangladesh:** Takaful video supports general Shariah-based insurance concepts; the life-accounts theory lesson provides only a general accounting introduction. **NOT COVERED:** insurance concept and conventional insurance principles as a systematic exam lesson; Bangladesh sector development/status; roles/functions; classification and policies; IDRA; IFRS 4, IAS 19, IAS 26; actuarial report/valuation; commissions/rebates/management expenses; dividends/bonus/profit; registers/returns; Bangladesh reinsurance; Insurance Academy; Bangladesh Shariah companies and Shariah boards. Search: `Bangladesh insurance business IDRA Insurance Act 2010 principles IFRS 4 IAS 19 actuarial accounts lecture Bangla`.
- **B7 Life insurance:** life-company accounts theory video is only a general orientation to insurer accounting. **NOT COVERED:** life-assurance contract; policy types and special aspects; actuarial valuation; premium/valuation/surplus calculations; surrender value; life-claim settlement; life fund; Bangladesh present status; rural insurance distribution; policy loans; lapsation and renewal. Search: `life insurance actuarial valuation premium surplus surrender value life fund policy loan lapsation renewal problem lecture Bangladesh`.
- **B8 Fire insurance:** Saheb Academy video supports a worked material/fire-claim calculation and claim-statement method. **NOT COVERED:** consequential-loss insurance; the syllabus-specific Bangladesh extent of fire loss; moral hazard; fire-insurance management problems. Search: `fire insurance consequential loss business interruption moral hazard Bangladesh fire insurance lecture`.
- **B9 Marine insurance:** Waseem Khan video supports marine contract basics and policy types. **NOT COVERED:** maritime perils; marine-loss classifications; settlement-of-claim calculations; Act of God. Search: `marine insurance maritime perils marine losses claim settlement act of God worked example`.
- **B10 Other insurance:** **NOT COVERED**—health; postal life; crop; cattle; civil aviation; export-credit guarantee; group; bank-deposit; fidelity insurance. Search: `health postal crop cattle aviation export credit group bank deposit fidelity insurance types lecture`.
- **B11 Insurance-company financial statements:** FOG/Hasham lessons are not insurer statements under Bangladesh rules; claim videos support only general claim workflow and a fire-claim calculation. **NOT COVERED:** complete insurance-claim computations across policy types and financial-statement preparation under IDRA guidelines and Insurance Act 2010. Search: `Bangladesh insurance company financial statements IDRA guidelines Insurance Act 2010 claim calculation lecture`.
- **B12 Islamic-insurance accounting:** Takaful lesson supports concepts/models only. **NOT COVERED:** financial-statement presentation/disclosure; disclosure of bases for determining/allocating surplus or deficit; investment funds; provisions and reserves. Search: `takaful accounting financial statements surplus deficit allocation investment funds reserves provisions Bangladesh`.
- **C NBFI:** **NOT COVERED**—NBFI introduction/origin/growth/history/status/types; lease-finance accounting; Islamic financing; the printed “Preparation of Financial Statements of Banking Companies” item; BB NBFI guidelines; SED/other regulators and applicable laws. Search: `Bangladesh NBFI Bangladesh Bank guidelines accounting lease finance Islamic financing financial statements lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 13 distinguishable content blocks in the detailed course contents (five Part A, seven Part B, one Part C); the source duplicates the printed number 12 for two blocks. **Verified videos:** 9 unique direct YouTube watch pages. **Is every detailed subtopic covered by a verified video? No.** All syllabus subtopics appear above in the outline and are either linked to a narrowly described lesson or explicitly marked NOT COVERED in the mapping. Major gaps are Bangladesh-specific bank/insurance laws and regulatory reporting, much of bank-risk governance and insurance theory, life actuarial methods, marine/other-insurance details, Islamic-insurance accounting, and NBFIs. Videos outside Bangladesh are flagged; none should be used as an authority on Bangladesh statutes or regulatory compliance.

## Topic-to-video map

All links below are direct YouTube watch pages opened to verify the displayed title, creator/channel, and that the item is an actual relevant lesson. Coverage statements are deliberately limited to what the watch-page title/description establishes; a general/theoretical lesson is not presented as a Bangladesh-law substitute.

### VAT and VAT accounting

1. [Input and Output VAT Calculation in Bangladesh | Step-by-Step with Example](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_RFd2N3tPsA) — **H2B Corporate**. **Covers #21 (input tax):** the Bangladesh VAT & SD Act 2012 example explains input/output VAT credit adjustment and net VAT, useful for practicing the input-credit calculation.
2. [Costing for Input Output Coefficient Calculation | Mushak 4.3 Costing](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=q9O6AjX-lAQ) — **H2B Corporate**. **Covers #31:** a worked cost-sheet and input-output coefficient calculation gives direct practice for filling and explaining Mushak 4.3.
3. [Most Used Mushak Forms 6.1 to 6.10 A to Z Online Class | Complete VAT Forms Training](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wYuzhC8_SDU) — **H2B Corporate**. **Covers #32–33 and #35–40 (forms 6.1, 6.2, 6.3–6.8):** a Bangladesh VAT forms overview supports remembering each book, invoice, certificate, and note’s exam function; see the separate 6.2.1 lesson below.
4. [Mushak 6.2.1 Form Fill-Up | How to prepare purchase and sale account? (Bangla Tutorial)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_wKCnjB2qpI) — **H2B Corporate**. **Covers #34:** the practical purchase-and-sale account form walkthrough helps rehearse the entries and format likely required in a written/practical answer.
5. [Session-9: VAT Deduction at Source-7_Examples-1-3](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=d-j3Yojk22U) — **VAT with Dr Rouf**. **Covers #13:** worked VDS decisions for vehicle, consultancy-service, and furniture purchases help with withholding examples and explain when deduction is due.
6. [Online VAT Return Mushak 9.1 A to Z | Complete Guide to Complete VAT Return | Bangladesh VAT](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ae_FEgX4Jtw) — **H2B Corporate**. **Covers #41:** the step-by-step Bangladesh online Mushak 9.1 return demonstration is useful for learning the return’s completion sequence and practical filing entries.

### Cross-border income taxation and transfer pricing

7. [Baker Tilly International - Introduction to Double Tax Treaties](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F2yxJsd7OWQ) — **Baker Tilly**. **Covers #47 (DTTA purpose and benefits only):** the treaty introduction provides a lecture-length conceptual basis for explaining why double-tax treaties are used; it is not Bangladesh-specific.
8. [Credit Method to Relief International Double Taxation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AS4QztHlUcE) — **University of Mannheim Business School**. **Covers #49–50 (foreign-income relief by credit method):** its focused explanation helps distinguish and frame the foreign-tax-credit method in an exam answer; it does not teach Bangladesh sections.
9. [Exemption Method to Relief International Double Taxation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sdoR9tqz3gg) — **University of Mannheim Business School**. **Covers #49–50 (foreign-income relief by exemption method):** the paired method lesson makes it easier to contrast exemption relief with credit relief in a written comparison; it is general international-tax instruction.
10. [Permanent Establishment International Taxation. Tax Compliance and Planning TCP.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QgVdsxqLXdI) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers #55 (PE concept):** the lecture explains when overseas business activity triggers tax presence and contrasts foreign branches with subsidiaries, useful for a PE-definition answer; **US CPA-oriented, not Bangladesh law**.
11. [Transfer Pricing Methods Explained: The 5 OECD Pillars](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hYlf5bXHY0Y) — **Taxplained**. **Covers #60–64:** the lesson breaks down the five OECD transfer-pricing methods, helping students recall and compare CUP, resale, cost-plus, profit-split, and TNMM; it does not cover Bangladesh’s statutory filing/penalty sections.

### International trade, documents, and customs

12. [International Trade 101 | Economics Explained](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YiZn5fpPHgY) — **Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis**. **Covers #78–80:** the import/export coffee example explains trade’s benefits and costs, useful for short exam answers on gains and why imports/exports matter; it is general economics, not Bangladesh procedure.

Shafiqul Alam LL.B, FCS, FCA, FCMA**. **Covers #90 (bill of entry):** the Bangladesh import-duty lesson explicitly identifies bill-of-entry fields/process alongside assessed value and total tax incidence, useful for explaining the declaration and computation.

### Public finance and fiscal policy

22. [Public Goods and Externalities](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=K4qvB7KXAw4) — **Professor Dave Explains**. **Covers #106–110:** the lesson explains public goods, free riding, and whether externalities are beneficial or harmful, a clear conceptual basis for definitions and examples.
23. [(IME2) Chapter 6 - Video 12: First and Second Theorem of Welfare Economics and How to Verify.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JuGMV4kW8h8) — **selcuk ozyurt**. **Covers #104–105:** the lecture explicitly explains and verifies both welfare theorems, aiding precise statement-and-significance answers; it is intermediate microeconomics theory.
24. [80. CANONS OF TAXATION -Public Economics | Adam Smith | Meaning and Types | Taxation Laws (Part 1)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9mXc_9GTLbQ) — **ECONOMICS PEDIA**. **Covers #111:** an exam-oriented canons/principles lesson supplies a concise framework for defining and explaining taxation principles.
25. [MIT 14.41 Lecture 18: Tax Incidence, General Equilibrium Tax Incidence, Tax Efficiency](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qJ83KbqLQFA) — **Students for Open and Universal Learning** (MIT 14.41, Professor Jonathan Gruber). **Covers #112, #115–118, #122:** the public-finance lecture covers incidence, general-equilibrium incidence, consumer/producer burden, and tax efficiency, supporting diagrammatic and written explanations; it is not Bangladesh tax law.
26. [MIT 14.41 Lecture 19: Optimal Commodity Taxation, Optimal Income Taxes, Tax-Benefit Linkages](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6YGHPl92IFA) — **Students for Open and Universal Learning** (MIT 14.41, Professor Jonathan Gruber). **Covers #121, #123–124:** this advanced lecture directly addresses optimal commodity and income-tax design, useful for explaining the logic behind optimal tax structures.
27. [MIT 14.41 Lecture 20: Taxation and Labor Supply](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2KNIPUhUbxg) — **Students for Open and Universal Learning** (MIT 14.41, Professor Jonathan Gruber). **Covers #119:** the lecture focuses on how taxation affects labor supply, supporting an analytical answer rather than a definition-only response.
28. [Balanced, Surplus & Deficit Budget | Class 12 Economics Chapter 5 | CBSE 2025-26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FprSLEf0Fts) — **Magnet Brains**. **Covers #125–128:** the lesson distinguishes balanced, surplus, and deficit budgets for quick definitions and classification; **CBSE/India course framing, not Bangladesh budget policy**.
