## Coverage audit

- **Topics itemized:** 128.
- **Verified video coverage:** 58 topics have at least one mapped video whose actual YouTube watch page was opened; **70 topics remain NOT COVERED** as listed above.
- **Every topic has at least one verified video?** **No.** There are important gaps, especially statutory Bangladesh tax/VAT/customs detail, treaty-model distinctions, and specific fiscal-theory subtopics; use the specified searches rather than infer coverage.
- **Source/course notes:** matched the exact **3105** title in the 11th-batch AIS DETAILED SYLLABUS; no code/title conflict was found. The source’s phrase “VAT recordingin” is normalized to “VAT recording in.” The source unusually prints “UN DTTA Model Vs. EU DTTA Model”; wording is retained verbatim in substance, but confirm that comparison with the course instructor. The reference list cites the **Income Tax Ordinance 1984**, **Customs Act 1969**, and “Latest Finance Act”; these are reproduced as course references, not represented as current-law advice. Watch-page verification establishes titles/channels/relevance, not whether every lesson’s law/rates remain current.

# 3106 Entrepreneurship and Business Development

**Semester:** Third Year, First Semester (Semester 5) — 11th-batch BBA, Accounting & Information Systems, University of Barishal. The 11th-batch course roster places 3106 in the third-year first-semester column (PDF p. 35); the detailed course heading is `3106: Entrepreneurship and Business Development` (PDF pp. 92–93). The roster breaks “Business Development” onto a second line and assigns the course 1 credit; the detailed heading agrees on the code and title. No conflicting code/title was found in the AIS section. This uses the AIS 11th-batch section, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

Transcribed from the detailed course contents; punctuation/capitalization are lightly normalized, with slash-linked study activities retained together. No named law or technical standard is specified in this course entry.

1. **Introduction to Entrepreneurship Development:** Introduction; nature and importance of entrepreneurship; historical development of entrepreneurship; personal characteristic of entrepreneurship; function of entrepreneurship; distinction between an entrepreneur and a manager; role of entrepreneurship in economic development; summary/case study analysis/application of knowledge.
2. **Emerging Issues in Entrepreneurship:** Entrepreneurship myths; theories of entrepreneurship; culture of entrepreneurship; gender and entrepreneurship; summary/case study analysis/application of knowledge.
3. **Forms of Entrepreneurship:** Small business entrepreneurship; types of business ownership; classification of family business; partnership business; cooperatives; limited company; franchise; types of franchise methods; corporate entrepreneurship; case study analysis/application of knowledge.
4. **Micro & Small Scale Enterprises:** Classification of business firms; role of SME in economic development; advantages and disadvantages of SME; challenges facing the development of SME in developing countries; role of government in the development of SMEs in students’ respective countries; case study analysis/application of knowledge.
5. **Creating & Starting the Venture / Creativity and Innovation:** Definition of creativity and innovation; some of new ideas; methods of generating ideas; purposeful innovation; sources of innovative opportunity; the bright idea; principles of innovation; case study analysis/application of knowledge.
6. **Environmental Analysis / Entrepreneurial Environment:** Introduction to environmental analysis; internal environmental analysis; external environmental analysis; challenges in industrial analysis; case study analysis/application of knowledge.
7. **Feasibility Studies / Analysis:** Meaning of a feasibility study/analysis; preparing concept statement; dimensions of feasibility studies; who carries out a feasibility study?; when is it valuable?; when to conduct a feasibility analysis; forms of feasibility analysis; case study analysis/application of knowledge.
8. **Business Model Canvas:** Meaning of a business model canvas; rationale for business model canvas; nine elements of a business model canvas; why a good value proposition is central to a successful business model canvas; case study analysis/application of knowledge.
9. **Financing New Ventures:** Introduction; “Financing means and some of finance” (wording as printed); venture capital; case study analysis/application of knowledge.
10. **The Business Plan:** Introduction; essentials of a good business plan; scope and value of the business plan; elements of a business plan; business plan format; case study analysis/application of knowledge.
11. **Industry Tour and Business Development:** A week industry tour and developing a business based on the industry experience.

## Topic-to-video map

Each direct YouTube watch page below was opened to verify its displayed title and channel and to check that it is an actual lesson relevant to the stated topic. “NOT COVERED” means I did not find a suitable direct lesson that I could verify for that syllabus item; search phrases are provided instead. These are study aids, not University of Barishal past-paper predictions.

### 1. Introduction to Entrepreneurship Development

- **[What is Entrepreneurship? definition, characteristics and entrepreneurial process](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MdNNGfoxrqA)** — **Key Differences**. **Covered:** introduction; nature/meaning of entrepreneurship; personal characteristic of entrepreneurship. The description identifies the meaning of entrepreneurship and entrepreneur, qualities/characteristics, and the entrepreneurial process; that compact explanation is useful for definition and short-note answers.
- **[entrepreneur, entrepreneurship, function, characteristics, barrier, innovation and entrepreneurship](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_C2LlvX9s5w)** — **DWIVEDI GUIDANCE**. **Covered:** function of entrepreneurship; personal characteristics (reinforcement). Its explicit function/characteristics lesson framing provides an exam-revision prompt for organizing function and trait answers.
- **NOT COVERED:** historical development; distinction between entrepreneur and manager; role in economic development; summary/case study analysis/application. Search: `history development of entrepreneurship lecture`, `entrepreneur vs manager differences exam answer`, `role of entrepreneurship in economic development lecture`, `entrepreneurship case study analysis for students`.
- **NOT COVERED:** importance as a distinct examinable topic beyond the verified video’s definition/meaning. Search: `importance of entrepreneurship economic development lecture exam`.

### 2. Emerging Issues in Entrepreneurship

- **[Theories of Entrepreneurship](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bBydFrDNugI)** — **Lets Understand Business**. **Covered:** theories of entrepreneurship, including economic, sociological, psychological, innovation, and development perspectives. The lecture names the major theory families, giving a useful scaffold for comparative written answers.
- **NOT COVERED:** entrepreneurship myths; culture of entrepreneurship; gender and entrepreneurship; summary/case study analysis/application. Search: `myths of entrepreneurship lecture exam`, `entrepreneurial culture lecture`, `gender and entrepreneurship lecture women entrepreneurs`, `entrepreneurship theories myths gender case study`.

### 3. Forms of Entrepreneurship

- **[Forms of Business Organization Sole Proprietorship, Partnership, Corporations](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sGHxNuj-4MA)** — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covered:** types of business ownership at the level of sole proprietorship, partnership, and corporation; partnership business. Its compare-and-contrast lesson helps frame ownership-form answers around liability, tax, capital, and continuity. **Jurisdiction caution:** this lesson explicitly frames legal/tax points for U.S. CPA exams; do not transfer its jurisdiction-specific rules to Bangladesh.
- **NOT COVERED:** small business entrepreneurship as a distinct form; family-business classification; cooperatives; limited company under Bangladesh law; franchise; franchise methods; corporate entrepreneurship; case-study application. Search: `types of franchise methods lecture`, `cooperative business form entrepreneurship lecture`, `family business classification lecture`, `corporate entrepreneurship intrapreneurship exam`, `Bangladesh Companies Act limited company business form lecture`, `small business entrepreneurship forms lecture`.

### 4. Micro & Small Scale Enterprises

- **NOT COVERED:** classification of business firms; SME contribution to economic development; SME advantages/disadvantages; SME-development challenges in developing countries; role of government in SME development in Bangladesh; case study/application. No Bangladesh-specific SME policy lesson was verified. Search: `SME classification advantages disadvantages developing countries lecture`, `role of SMEs in economic development lecture`, `Bangladesh SME Foundation government support SMEs lecture`, `SME development challenges Bangladesh case study`.

### 5. Creating & Starting the Venture / Creativity and Innovation

- **[Ch 4 Creativity and the Business Idea](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4Zv3SbUGWdA)** — **Jonathan Keisler, PhD**. **Covered:** definition/concept of creativity and innovation; new business ideas (introductory connection). The chapter lecture links creativity with business-idea development, which supports concise concept answers and idea examples. The page notes that the video starts on its second or third slide, with a minimal portion missed.
- **Covered only at an introductory level:** creativity and business-idea development. The chapter title establishes relevance, but the watch-page information does not demonstrate the syllabus’s specific innovation definitions or techniques.
- **NOT COVERED:** definition of innovation as a distinct syllabus point; some of new ideas beyond the introductory connection; methods of generating ideas; purposeful innovation; sources of innovative opportunity; the bright idea; principles of innovation; case study analysis/application. Search: `entrepreneurship methods of generating business ideas lecture`, `sources of innovation opportunities principles of innovation lecture`, `purposeful innovation Drucker exam lecture`, `creativity business idea case study exercise`.

### 6. Environmental Analysis / Entrepreneurial Environment

- **[PESTEL Analysis EXPLAINED | B2U | Business To You](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bYn4CyL3r5w)** — **Business To You**. **Covered:** introduction to environmental analysis; external environmental analysis (political, economic, social, technological, environmental, and legal forces). Its labeled PESTEL framework is easy to reproduce as a written checklist and apply to a venture.
- **[Porter's 5 Forces EXPLAINED | B2U | Business To You](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Dfp23xSqpdk)** — **Business To You**. **Covered:** external/industry environment analysis (industry attractiveness and competitive forces). The lesson’s five-force structure is a clear framework for industry-analysis questions.
- **NOT COVERED:** internal environmental analysis; challenges in industrial analysis as a distinct topic; case study analysis/application. Search: `internal environmental analysis entrepreneurship resources capabilities lecture`, `challenges in industry analysis lecture`, `entrepreneurial environment case study PESTEL SWOT`.

### 7. Feasibility Studies / Analysis

- **[Feasibility Analysis | Entrepreneurship (Chapter 3)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IZcpCfskoys)** — **Get Knowledge**. **Covered:** meaning/purpose of feasibility analysis; when to conduct it and why it is valuable. The entrepreneurship chapter’s feasibility focus and timing discussion help structure a short answer around whether and when to screen a venture idea.
- **NOT COVERED:** preparing a concept statement; dimensions of feasibility studies; who carries one out; forms of feasibility analysis; case study analysis/application. Search: `feasibility study concept statement market technical organizational financial dimensions lecture`, `who conducts feasibility study entrepreneurship`, `forms of feasibility analysis startup case study`.

### 8. Business Model Canvas

- **[Business Model Canvas Explained with Examples](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CakUeC1sCSs)** — **EPM**. **Covered:** meaning and rationale of the Business Model Canvas; all nine elements (customer segments, value propositions, channels, customer relationships, revenue streams, key resources, key activities, key partnerships, and cost structure); centrality of value proposition; case/example application. The lesson’s nine-block walkthrough and examples are directly useful for labeling, explaining, and practicing a canvas in an exam.

### 9. Financing New Ventures

- **[CA Final - AFM - Theory - Startup Finance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FQ-zJj8UKyM)** — **Dinesh Jain**. **Covered:** introduction to startup finance; financing sources/concepts. Its timestamped theory/revision lesson gives a structure for describing funding choices. **Caution:** it is CA Final AFM content, not Bangladesh-specific guidance on local funders, rules, or availability.
- **[Venture Capital for Beginners | How VC Works](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gA2lb0W7Qi8)** — **Logan Burchett**. **Covered:** venture capital. Its focused startup lesson is useful for defining VC and outlining how that funding route works in a written answer; it is general startup education, not Bangladesh-specific investor guidance.
- **NOT COVERED:** case study analysis/application. Search: `startup finance venture capital case study classroom`.

### 10. The Business Plan

- **[Business Plan Writing 101: Wharton Entrepreneurship Series](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zlrb_X6fYZ0)** — **Wharton School** (workshop featuring Patrick FitzGerald). **Covered:** introduction to planning; essentials of an effective business plan. The workshop discusses what is required for an effective plan, useful for exam answers on plan quality.
- **NOT COVERED:** scope and value of the business plan as a distinct topic; specific elements and format; case study analysis/application. Search: `scope and value of business plan entrepreneurship lecture`, `elements and format of business plan entrepreneurship lecture exam`, `business plan format worked example startup case study`.

### 11. Industry Tour and Business Development

- **NOT COVERED:** the one-week industry tour and developing a business from the tour experience are applied, course-specific activities; no verified direct YouTube lesson can substitute for the local tour. Search: `Bangladesh industry visit entrepreneurship business development student project`, `industry analysis to business idea case study Bangladesh`.

## Coverage audit

**67 syllabus topic units** were audited (each numbered syllabus topic/phrase counted once; slash-linked summary/case/application activities count as one unit). **12 unique videos** have direct watch-page title/channel verification. Each unit is assigned above to verified lesson coverage or an explicit **NOT COVERED** gap; some broad/composite items are only partly covered. Thus, **not every topic has a verified video**. Biggest gaps are SME/developing-country and Bangladesh government coverage, ownership forms beyond sole proprietorship/partnership/corporation, detailed innovation methods, feasibility dimensions/forms, business-plan scope/elements/format, and the field tour. The ownership lesson is U.S.-framed; the startup-finance and VC lessons are not Bangladesh-specific. The detailed syllabus itself lists no named laws or standards for this course.

# Semester 6 — Third Year, Second Semester

# 3201 Management Accounting

**Semester:** Semester 6 — Third Year, Second Semester  
**Curriculum scope:** University of Barishal, Department of Accounting & Information Systems, 11th Batch (Session 2022–2023) onward; Detailed Syllabus entry for **3201: Management Accounting**. This fragment uses that AIS curriculum entry, not the older Management Studies curriculum.

## Detailed course-content outline

1. **Management Accounting, Business Organizations and Professional Ethics [ILO 1]** — Definition of Management Accounting; Scope and Functions of Management Accounting; Evolution of Management Accounting; Financial Accounting vs. Management Accounting; Cost Accounting vs. Management Accounting; Expanding Role of Management Accountants; the Management Accounting Profession; Code of Ethics for Management Accountants.
2. **Cost Behavior [ILO 2]** — Cost Behavior; Analysis and Measurement of Cost Behavior: Variable, Fixed, Mixed; Methods of Segregating Mixed Costs into Fixed and Variable Costs (High-Low Method; Inspection of Accounts/Accounts Classification Method; Engineering Method; Scatter graph Method; Ordinary Least Squares or Regression Method); Contribution Format of Income Statement.
3. **Cost-Volume-Profit Analysis [ILO 2, ILO 3, ILO 4]** — Assumption and Uses of Cost-Volume-Profit (CVP) Analysis; Graphical Approach to CVP Analysis; Breakeven Analysis; Computations of Breakeven Point: Equation Method, Formula Method and Graphical Method; Target Net Operating Income with and without taxes; Sensitivity Analysis; Margin of Safety; Degree of Operating Leverage (DOL); CVP Analysis Under Multi-Product Situations; Limitations of CVP Analysis.
4. **Variable Costing and Absorption Costing [ILO 2, ILO 5]** — Nature and Comparison; Product Cost and Income Statement Under Different Costing System; Reconciliation of Absorption Costing Income with Variable Costing Income; Transfer of Fixed Manufacturing Overhead from one Period to Another; Over/Under-Production Compared to Standard; Uses and Limitations of Absorption Costing and Variable Costing.
5. **Activity-Based Costing and Activity-Based Management [ILO 6]** — Traditional Costing System and its Application and Limitation; Definition of Activity-Based Costing (ABC); Steps of ABC; Treatment of Cost under Traditional and ABC System; Comparison of Traditional and ABC Product Costs; Designing an ABC System; Benefits and Criticisms of ABC; Meaning of Activity-Based Management (ABM).
6. **Introduction to Budgets and Preparation of the Master Budget [ILO 2, ILO 7]** — Budget and Budgeting; Purpose of Budgeting; Types of Budgets and their Relations; Budget as a Planning and Control Tool; Budgeting Process; Preparation of Functional Budgets; Cash Budget; Master Budget; Budgeted Income Statement; Budgeted Balance Sheet; Behavioral Aspects of Budgeting.
7. **Flexible Budgets and Performance Analysis [ILO 2, ILO 8]** — Limitations of a Static Budget and the Need for a Flexible Budget; The Preparation of Flexible Budget; Flexible Budget and Performance Report.
8. **Standard Costing and Variance Analysis [ILO 9, ILO 10]** — Meaning and Purposes of Standard Cost; Need for the Setting up of Standard; Consideration in Establishing Standard; Types of Standards; Calculation of Variances: Material Price and Usage Variances, Labor Rate and Efficiency Variances, Overhead Volume and Spending Variances; Interpretations of Different Variances; Recording Journal Entries Under the Standard Costing System; Performance Report.
9. **Cost Allocation: Joint Products and By-Products [ILO 11]** — Basic Concepts: Joint Products and By-Products, Common Costs and Joint Costs, Split Off Point and Separable Costs; Allocation of Joint Costs; Assignment of Costs to Joint Products: Market or Sales Value Method, Quantitative or Physical Unit Method, Average Unit Cost Method, Weighted Average Cost Method; Irrelevance of Joint Costs for Decision Making; Accounting for By-Products: Production Method and Sales Method; Journal Entries for By-Products.
10. **An Introduction to Responsibility Accounting [ILO 12]** — Meaning and Objectives of Responsibility Accounting; Financial Control and its Relationship with Operations Control; Centralization vs. Decentralization; Advantages and Disadvantages of Decentralization; Decentralization and Responsibility Centers: Cost Center, Profit Center and Investment Center.
11. **Total Quality Management** — Concepts, History and Evolution, Benefits, Implementing TQM, TQM practices in different countries.

## Topic-to-video mapping

“Coverage” refers to what the linked lesson’s indexed description/title supports, not a claim that it replaces the full syllabus. Videos are linked to their direct YouTube watch pages. Exact displayed titles and channels were cross-checked against YouTube’s own video metadata; relevance was checked against the indexed lesson descriptions. The text-only page extractor returned YouTube footer text rather than full watch-page descriptions, so where a lesson description did not substantiate a syllabus detail, that detail is explicitly left uncovered below.

### 1. Management Accounting, Business Organizations and Professional Ethics

- **[Introduction to Management Accounting - B.COM / BBA / MBA - By Saheb Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MTXDi0nDeI0)** — **Saheb Academy.** Coverage: introductory management-accounting concepts and basic definition. Exam value: useful for a concise definition and an opening conceptual answer. It does not verify all scope, functions, history, profession, or role-expansion details.
- **[Difference between Financial Accounting and Managerial Accounting.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zeL6uilxlBo)** — **Educationleaves.** Coverage: financial accounting versus managerial accounting. Exam value: direct comparison/revision for the distinction asked in the outline.
- **[IMA Ethical Standards of Professional Conduct Explained.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7Pbf3WOu5jM)** — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses.** Coverage: IMA professional ethics standards. Exam value: directly supports ethics principles and professional conduct. The course outline’s broader Bangladesh-specific professional-body context is not verified by this video.
- **NOT COVERED:** scope and functions as a complete examinable list; evolution/history; cost accounting versus management accounting; expanding role of management accountants; profession and organizations/business-organization context. Search terms: `management accounting scope functions evolution expanding role profession lecture`; `cost accounting vs management accounting differences`; `management accountant profession Bangladesh code ethics AIS`.

### 2. Cost Behavior

- **[MA17 - Cost Behavior - Managerial Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Axeecq5TByU)** — **Tony Bell.** Coverage: cost behavior and mixed-cost estimation, including high-low, scattergraph, and least-squares regression methods (as identified in the lesson listing). Exam value: good conceptual and calculation review across the principal quantitative methods.
- **[High Low Method Explained.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FZ72zhjxODw)** — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses.** Coverage: worked separation of mixed cost into fixed and variable components using high-low. Exam value: focused practice for a frequently tested calculation.
- **[Least Square Regression For Variable And Fixed Costs || Cost Behavior Analysis || Lecture 09 ||](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=H5wxf7-5AdQ)** — **Muhammad Umar Mughal.** Coverage: regression for estimating fixed and variable cost components. Exam value: extra practice reading/applying regression-based cost estimates.

- **NOT COVERED:** inspection of accounts/accounts-classification method and engineering method. Search terms: `managerial accounting account classification method mixed cost estimation example`; `engineering method cost estimation managerial accounting example`.

Exam value: directly useful for single-product CVP calculations.

- **[Cost Volume Profit (CVP) Analysis: Target After Tax Profit](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UUTv8OMr5os)** — **Edspira.** Coverage: target profit after income taxes. Exam value: focused support for the syllabus’s with-tax target-income calculation.

- **[MA44 – Flexible Budget Example Problem: Fix the Flawed Variance Report](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8t33wGuta9Y)** — **Tony Bell.** Coverage: worked flexible-budget performance report and correction of a flawed variance report. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses.** Coverage: material price/usage and labor rate/efficiency variances. Exam value: focused formulas and worked examples for the four named input variances.
- **[MA42 – Fixed Overhead Variances: Budget and Volume Variances (Step-By-Step)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBVsHmPSZYg)** — **Tony Bell.** Coverage: fixed-overhead budget/spending and volume variances. Exam value: worked practice for overhead variance calculations.
- **[Variances Using Journal Entries. CPA Exam BAR](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Fm5_j5M49Sg)** — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses.** Coverage: journal entries for material price/usage and labor rate/efficiency variances. Exam value: supports the recording-entry part of the syllabus.
- **NOT COVERED:** meaning/purposes, standard-setting considerations and types of standards as a complete theory topic; overhead variance coverage beyond the linked fixed-overhead lesson (including a complete variable-overhead volume/spending treatment); full interpretation of every variance and a complete integrated performance report. Search terms: `standard costing purposes setting standards considerations types of standards managerial accounting`; `variable overhead spending efficiency variance journal entry performance report`; `interpret favorable unfavorable material labor overhead variances management performance`.

### 9. Cost Allocation: Joint Products and By-Products

- **[Managerial Accounting 7.10: Allocating Joint Costs](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RkWLfSMHwkc)** — **Kurt Heisinger.** Coverage: physical-quantity and sales-value methods for allocating joint cost. Exam value: concise direct practice for two listed allocation bases.
- **[#2 Joint and By Product - Average Unit Cost Method - Problem 2 - By Saheb Academy](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F_ISUgxFbJg)** — **Saheb Academy.** Coverage: average-unit-cost method worked example. Exam value: targeted numerical practice.
- **[Byproduct Journal Entries: Sales Method vs. Production Method](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IJORXlFA-zI)** — **Edspira.** Coverage: accounting for by-products using the sales and production methods, including journal-entry treatment. Exam value: direct support for the syllabus’s by-product methods and entries.
- **NOT COVERED:** weighted-average cost method of joint-product allocation; explicit irrelevance of joint costs in decision-making/sell-or-process-further analysis; full treatment of common versus joint/separable costs and split-off concepts beyond the introductory allocation lessons. Search terms: `weighted average method joint cost allocation joint products worked problem`; `irrelevance of joint costs decision making sell process further`; `joint products common joint separable costs split off point accounting`.

### 10. An Introduction to Responsibility Accounting

- **[Managerial Accounting 11.2: Maintaining Control Over Decentralized Organizations](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XlkN9tY7-kI)** — **Kurt Heisinger.** Coverage: decentralized control and the cost, profit and investment responsibility-center distinctions. Exam value: direct review of responsibility-center classification and decentralization.
- **NOT COVERED:** responsibility accounting’s meaning/objectives as a full theory answer; the relationship between financial and operations control; a verified advantages/disadvantages comparison of decentralization. Search terms: `responsibility accounting meaning objectives financial control operations control`; `advantages disadvantages decentralization responsibility accounting cost profit investment centers`.

### 11. Total Quality Management

- **[Total Quality Management Explained](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=njrF7gKrVeQ)** — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses.** Coverage: TQM definition/principles and basic organizational quality philosophy. Exam value: useful introductory conceptual review.
- **NOT COVERED:** a verified complete history/evolution, implementation process, benefits list, and country-by-country practices. Search terms: `total quality management history evolution benefits implementation practices countries lecture`; `TQM practices different countries comparative management`.

## Coverage audit

**Coverage is partial; do not treat this playlist as full syllabus coverage.** Verified, directly relevant lessons are available for introductory management accounting concepts and professional ethics; several cost-behavior tools; core single- and multiproduct CVP calculations; variable-versus-absorption costing; an ABC worked example; master budgets; flexible-budget reports; key standard variances and some journal entries; selected joint-cost methods and by-product entries; responsibility centers; and introductory TQM. The exact uncovered subtopics are marked **NOT COVERED** above with targeted search terms. Highest-priority gaps before an exam are the account-classification and engineering cost-estimation methods; CVP graphing/assumptions/limitations/sensitivity; ABM and full ABC design; budget behavior; standards-setting theory and complete overhead variance/performance-report coverage; weighted-average joint allocation and joint-cost decision irrelevance; responsibility-accounting control theory; and TQM history/implementation/country practices.

**Source for the outline:** *Curriculum of the Department of Accounting and Information Systems, University of Barishal, BBA Program, 11th Batch (Session 2022–2023) onward*, Detailed Syllabus, “THIRD YEAR SECOND SEMESTER,” course 3201, “Detailed Course Contents” (provided as `/home/ubuntu/upload/Syllabus.pdf`).

# 3202 Corporate Reporting — Semester 6 (Third Year, Second Semester)

**Source and scope.** Transcribed from the University of Barishal BBA Accounting & Information Systems curriculum effective from the 11th batch (session 2022–2023), using its **Detailed Syllabus** course entry (PDF p. 96), not the earlier Management Studies curriculum. The course-list table places 3202 Corporate Reporting in Third Year, Second Semester. The detailed entry assigns **Part 1: Financial Reporting (75% weight)** and **Part 2: Non-Financial Reporting (25% weight)**.

**Source notes.** The title and code agree in the course list and detailed entry. In item 9, “Counterbalancing Errors” is repeated; preserved as one topic rather than treating the repeated words as a distinct subject. The source also has minor spacing/typing defects (“Issuesrelated,” “partiesto”) and a stray comma after “GRI guideline”; normalized only for readability. The content below otherwise retains the source’s named standards and scope.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

### Part 1: Financial Reporting (75%)

1. **Reporting Framework and Ethics:** IASB Conceptual Framework; regulatory framework; convergence process; inherent limitations of financial statements; ethical and professional issues.
2. **Presentation of Financial Statements:** Statements for a company according to IAS 1: Statement of Financial Position; Statement of Profit or Loss and Other Comprehensive Income; Statement of Changes in Equity; Statement of Cash Flows; and Notes, comprising significant accounting policies and other explanatory information.
3. **Reporting Financial Performance:** Revenue from Contracts with Customers (IFRS 15); Earnings Per Share (IAS 33); Related Party Disclosure (IAS 24); effect of changes in foreign exchange rates (IAS 21).
4. **Reporting of Assets:** Inventory (IAS 2); Property, Plant & Equipment (IAS 16); Impairment of Assets (IAS 36); Intangibles (IAS 38); Non-current Assets Held for Sale and Discontinued Operations (IFRS 5).
5. **Financial Instruments:** Meaning of a financial instrument; classification of financial assets; classification of financial instruments as liability or equity; compound financial instrument; recognition and derecognition of financial assets and liabilities; measurement of financial assets and liabilities; considerations on fair value measurement; impairment of financial assets; reclassification of financial assets; accounting treatment for gain or loss on financial assets and liabilities; ethical and judgement issues; presentation and disclosure requirements under IAS 32 and IFRS 7 (standards named: IAS 32, IFRS 7, IFRS 9 and IFRS 13).
6. **Accounting for Interests in Subsidiaries (IAS 27, IFRS 3, IFRS 10 and IFRS 12):**
   - **6.1 Group Accounts—Basic Principles:** Context for group accounts; single entity concept; control and ownership; nature of business combinations; identifying the acquirer; measuring and allocating the cost of a business combination; goodwill calculation; non-controlling interest; applying IAS 27 to separate financial statements; disclosure requirements.
   - **6.2 Group Accounts—Consolidated Statement of Financial Position:** Goodwill calculation; preparation of consolidated statement of financial position; mid-year acquisitions; adjustments of intra-group balances; unrealized intra-group profit; fair-value adjustments; other consolidation adjustments.
   - **6.3 Group Accounts—Consolidated Statement of Financial Performance and Statement of Changes in Equity:** Preparation of consolidated financial performance; adjustments for intra-group transactions and unrealized profit; mid-year acquisitions; dividends; other consolidation adjustments; preparation of consolidated statement of changes in equity.
   - **6.4 Group Cash Flow Statement:** Basic principle; cash flows to minority interest and associates; acquisition and disposal of subsidiaries; acquisition and disposal of associates.
7. **Accounting for an Entity’s Interests in Other Entities (IAS 28, IFRS 10, IFRS 11 and IFRS 12):**
   - **Associates:** Meaning of associate; criteria for significant influence; equity method for investments in associates; exemptions from applying the equity method; discontinuing the equity method; impairment losses; preparing Statement of Profit or Loss and Other Comprehensive Income under the equity method; accounting for associate losses; accounting for different transactions between a group and its associate; IFRS 12 disclosure requirements.
   - **Joint arrangements:** Introduction; meaning of joint control; types and structure of joint arrangement; accounting for acquisitions and sales of interests in joint operations; impairment loss when applying the equity method in a joint venture; preparing financial statements of parties to a joint arrangement (IFRS 11); IFRS 12 disclosure requirements.
8. **Operating Segments:** Meaning and importance of segment reporting; managerial approach to identifying and reporting operating segments; disclosure requirements; IFRS 8.
9. **Accounting Policies, Changes and Errors (IAS 8):** Accounting changes; changes in accounting policy; retrospective accounting-change approach; changes in accounting estimates; prospective reporting; accounting errors; error analysis; non-counterbalancing errors; counterbalancing errors.
10. **Income Taxes (IAS 12):** Definitions; current tax; deferred taxes; tax bases; recognition and measurement of deferred taxes; presentation and disclosures.
11. **Employee Benefits and Retirement Benefit Plans:** Basic principle; short-term employee benefits; profit-sharing and bonus payments; types of post-employment benefit plans; contribution plans; benefits plans; termination benefits; IAS 19; benefit plans and disclosure; IAS 26.
12. **Other standards:** IFRS 1, First-time Adoption of International Financial Reporting Standards; Government Grants (IAS 20); Financial Reporting in Hyperinflationary Economies (IAS 29); Agriculture (IAS 41); IFRS 6, Exploration for and Evaluation of Mineral Resources; IFRS 2, Share-based Payment; IFRS 14, Regulatory Deferral Accounts; IFRS 17, Insurance Contracts.

### Part 2: Non-Financial Reporting (25%)

13. **Non-Financial Reporting:** Origin, history and nature; types—Corporate Social Responsibility Reporting, The Triple Bottom Line Report, XBRL, Sustainability Reporting, GRI and Integrated Reporting; regulatory framework—non-financial reporting standard and GRI guideline; IFRS S1, General Requirements for Disclosure of Sustainability-related Financial Information; IFRS S2, Climate-related Disclosures.

## Topic-to-video map

Each recommended item below was checked by opening its direct YouTube watch page. Coverage claims are limited to what the watch-page title and description establish; the videos are IFRS/ACCA/ICAG-oriented rather than University of Barishal past-paper solutions.

1. [**The Conceptual Framework for Financial Reporting**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F4A9UGI9Guk) — **Kaplan UK**. **Coverage:** IASB Conceptual Framework, including financial-reporting purpose, qualitative characteristics, elements, recognition/derecognition, measurement, presentation and disclosure (Part 1.1). **Exam value:** The chaptered tutor explanation gives a compact framework for written conceptual questions and terminology recall.
2. [**ACCA P2 Presentation of Financial Statements (IAS 1)**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DePzPPRgYEQ) — **OpenTuition**. **Coverage:** IAS 1 presentation of company financial statements (Part 1.2), including the required primary statements and notes at the standard level. **Exam value:** A focused corporate-reporting lecture helps organize a written answer around statement presentation and disclosure requirements.
3. [**ICAG Lectures: IFRS 15 How To Recognise Revenue PART 1| ICAG |ACCA| CPA| CFA - Nhyira Premium**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=genzeDRE0O8) — **Nhyira Premium**. **Coverage:** Revenue from contracts with customers, IFRS 15 (Part 1.3). **Exam value:** A dedicated accounting-standards lesson is useful for explaining revenue recognition and structuring IFRS 15 exam responses.
4. [**ACCA I Financial Reporting (FR) I IAS 21 IAS 33 Earnings Per Share - FR Lecture 21**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=plHNxGGGYDk) — **Sabi Akther**. **Coverage:** IAS 21 foreign-currency effects and IAS 33 earnings per share (Part 1.3). **Exam value:** The combined FR lecture directly targets two separately examinable standards; use it to rehearse core treatment and terminology.
5. [**IAS 24 - RELATED PARTY DISCLOSURES (PART 2)**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cycLbIqozN8) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. **Coverage:** IAS 24 related parties, key management personnel, close family members, transactions and disclosure requirements (Part 1.3). **Exam value:** Its stated focus on identifying parties and required disclosures supports concise definition-and-disclosure answers.
6. [**ACCA SBR Financial instruments bitesize ( IFRS9 & IAS 32)**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gVQGuskiFfw) — **Jo Tuffill ACCA Online Tutor**. **Coverage:** Financial-asset classification and measurement, compound instruments, impairments, derivatives and hedging under IFRS 9/IAS 32 (Part 1.5). **Exam value:** The tutor identifies regularly tested areas and breaks difficult instrument classifications into exam-relevant concepts; it does not establish full coverage of IFRS 7 disclosure or every financial-instrument syllabus bullet.
7. [**IAS 2 - INVENTORIES (PART 1)**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3S6ulr7qTiU) — **FOG Accountancy Tutorials**. **Coverage:** IAS 2 inventories (Part 1.4). **Exam value:** This is a focused standard lesson with concepts and the fundamental IAS 2 principle, useful for definitions and inventory-treatment questions.
8. [**IFRS Lectures: IFRS 5 | IAS 16 | IAS 38 - Practice Questions**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PWUWmHJEe5c) — **Nhyira Premium**. **Coverage:** IFRS 5 held-for-sale/discontinued-operation topics, IAS 16 PPE and IAS 38 intangible assets (Part 1.4). **Exam value:** The title identifies practice questions across the three syllabus standards, supporting application and calculation practice rather than passive summary.
9. [**IFRS Solved Past Exam Questions - IAS 36 Impairment of Assets || Financial Reporting Lectures**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EQQ1WC1hCv4) — **MSL Business School**. **Coverage:** IAS 36 impairment (Part 1.4). **Exam value:** The watch-page description confirms a fully solved past exam question, ideal for practising impairment calculations and answer method.
10. [**How to consolidate under IFRS - step by step EXAMPLE**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mpUem0dWEmg) — **Silvia of CPDbox**. **Coverage:** Introductory step-by-step consolidation process and basic group-account consolidation (Part 1.6). **Exam value:** The worked example helps build an ordered consolidation method, but is not evidence of coverage of every advanced consolidation adjustment listed in the syllabus.
11. [**IAS 8 Accounting Policies, Changes in Accounting Estimates**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vPDe0jexRHA) — **EZIKAN ACADEMY**. **Coverage:** IAS 8 policy changes, estimate changes and errors (Part 1.9). **Exam value:** It explains the three IAS 8 categories that students must distinguish when deciding retrospective versus prospective treatment.
12. [**Operating segments (IFRS 8) - ACCA (SBR) lectures**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FB3rEKPtR5s) — **OpenTuition**. **Coverage:** Operating-segment reporting under IFRS 8 (Part 1.8). **Exam value:** A focused SBR lesson supports written responses on the standard’s management/managerial approach and segment disclosure logic.
13. [**The fundamentals of IAS 12**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3H4FJwCP5XA) — **ACCA**. **Coverage:** Basic principles of income taxes under IAS 12 (Part 1.10). **Exam value:** ACCA lecturer Adam Deller’s focused explanation helps clarify tax-accounting concepts before attempting current/deferred tax calculations.

### Not covered by the verified recommendations

The following syllabus subtopics did not have a direct suitable watch-page-verified lesson in this shortlist. Search using the exact phrases shown rather than treating a general standard overview as proof of coverage:

- **1. Reporting Framework and Ethics:** Regulatory framework, convergence process, inherent limitations of financial statements, ethical and professional issues — search: `IFRS regulatory framework convergence financial statements limitations ethics professional issues exam lecture`.
- **2. Presentation:** Individual statement components (Statement of Financial Position; Statement of Profit or Loss and OCI; Statement of Changes in Equity; Statement of Cash Flows; notes/significant policies/explanatory information) are named in the IAS 1 recommendation, but were not individually checked for worked preparation coverage — search: `IAS 1 complete financial statements worked example statement of changes in equity cash flow notes ACCA`.
- **3. Financial performance:** IFRS 15 performance obligations/contract applications and IAS 21/IAS 33 advanced worked computations beyond the standards named in the verified lessons — search: `IFRS 15 five step exam question IAS 21 foreign currency worked example IAS 33 EPS diluted exam question`.
- **4. Assets:** Full IAS 16 measurement/depreciation/revaluation methods; IAS 38 recognition and amortisation details; IFRS 5 classification/measurement and discontinued-operation presentation are not individually confirmed in the combined-practice video — search: `IAS 16 IAS 38 IFRS 5 solved exam question revaluation amortisation held for sale`.
- **5. Financial instruments:** Complete IAS 32/IFRS 7 presentation and disclosure requirements; derecognition, reclassification, all measurement categories, gains/losses and ethical/judgement issues are not all demonstrated — search: `IFRS 7 disclosure IAS 32 presentation derecognition reclassification financial assets solved exam question`.
- **6. Subsidiaries and group accounts:** IAS 27 separate financial statements and disclosures; IFRS 3 acquirer/cost allocation/goodwill/NCI; mid-year acquisition; intra-group balances and unrealized profit; fair-value and other consolidation adjustments; consolidated performance, dividends and changes in equity; group cash flows including minority, associates and acquisition/disposal — search: `IFRS 3 IFRS 10 consolidated accounts mid year acquisition unrealized profit NCI consolidated cash flows worked question`.
- **7. Other entities:** IAS 28 associate significant influence/equity-method exemptions and discontinuance/losses/transactions/disclosures; IFRS 11 joint-control/types/structure/joint-operation acquisitions and sales/joint-venture impairment/financial statements; IFRS 12 disclosures — search: `IAS 28 equity method associate losses transactions IFRS 11 joint arrangement worked example IFRS 12 disclosures ACCA`.
- **8. Segments:** Meaning and importance of segment reporting, managerial approach, identification thresholds and detailed disclosure tests are not itemized in the verified video description — search: `IFRS 8 managerial approach identify reportable segments disclosure exam question`.
- **9. IAS 8:** Explicit retrospective accounting-change method, prospective estimate method, error analysis and counterbalancing/non-counterbalancing error calculations — search: `IAS 8 retrospective prospective accounting changes counterbalancing errors worked examples`.
- **10. IAS 12:** Definitions, current-tax calculation, tax bases, deferred-tax recognition/measurement and presentation/disclosure problems beyond the fundamentals overview — search: `IAS 12 current tax tax base deferred tax recognition measurement worked ACCA question`.
- **11. Employee benefits:** Short-term benefits, profit-sharing/bonuses, post-employment contribution/benefit plans, termination benefits (IAS 19), and retirement-plan reporting/disclosure (IAS 26) — search: `IAS 19 employee benefits IAS 26 retirement benefit plans exam question defined contribution defined benefit`.
- **12. Other standards:** IFRS 1 first adoption; IAS 20 grants; IAS 29 hyperinflation; IAS 41 agriculture; IFRS 6 mineral-resource exploration/evaluation; IFRS 2 share-based payment; IFRS 14 regulatory deferral accounts; IFRS 17 insurance contracts — search: `IFRS 1 IAS 20 IAS 29 IAS 41 IFRS 6 IFRS 2 IFRS 14 IFRS 17 exam lecture solved questions`.
- **13. Non-financial reporting:** Origin/history/nature; CSR; triple bottom line; XBRL; sustainability reporting; GRI; integrated reporting; non-financial reporting regulation and GRI guideline; IFRS S1 and IFRS S2 — search: `GRI sustainability reporting corporate social responsibility triple bottom line XBRL integrated reporting IFRS S1 S2 lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 13 top-level syllabus units (12 Financial Reporting units plus Non-Financial Reporting); all listed subtopics are transcribed above and accounted for in the map or the explicit NOT COVERED list. **Verified-video count:** 13 unique YouTube watch links. **Every topic has at least one verified video?** No. Direct verified lessons cover IASB conceptual framework, IAS 1 at a general presentation level, IFRS 15, IAS 21, IAS 33, IAS 24, selected IFRS 9/IAS 32 instrument areas, IAS 2, IAS 36, IFRS 5/IAS 16/IAS 38 as practice-question topics, basic consolidation, IAS 8 at overview level, IFRS 8 at overview level and IAS 12 fundamentals. Other subtopics are explicitly marked **NOT COVERED** above. No Bangladesh-specific law/tax/VAT/regulatory syllabus topic occurs in this course entry; the recommended foreign exam-prep jurisdictions/channels should not be mistaken for Bangladesh law coverage. Direct watch pages were opened for all 13 listed recommendations; where the watch-page evidence identified only a broad standard lesson, detailed treatment is qualified rather than assumed.

# 3203 Forensic Accounting — Semester 6 (Third Year, Second Semester)

**Curriculum basis:** University of Barishal BBA AIS, effective from the 11th batch (session 2022–2023), using the AIS course grid and the `DETAILED SYLLABUS` section—not the earlier Management Studies curriculum. The grid places **3203 Forensic Accounting** in Third Year, Second Semester, and the detailed entry also reads **3203: Forensic Accounting**; no target-course code/title discrepancy was found. The printed phrase **“Benfold Law Technique”** is retained below as written; it appears to mean **Benford’s Law**.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Introduction to Fraud and Forensic Accounting** [ILO 1]: Introduction to Crime; Actus Reus and Mens Rea; Legal System of a Country; Taxonomy of Crime; Conceptual Framework of the Fraud Deterrence cycle; Definition of Forensic Accounting; Historical Perspective of Forensic Accounting; Difference between Forensic Accounting and External Auditing; Emergence of Forensic Accounting; Controversy surrounding the Accountant’s role in fraud detection and handling; How to become Forensic Accountants; Forensic Accountant’s investigative role; Different dimensions of Forensic Accounting; Required knowledge, skills and abilities of Forensic Accountant.
2. **Potential Red Flags and Fraud Detection Techniques** [ILO 1]: Fraud; Types of Frauds; Occupational Fraud; Fraud Detection; Assessing the risks of frauds; Interpreting potential red flags; Fraud Triangles; The social consequences of economic crime; Identifying and evaluating risk factors; Identifying Techniques—Analytical Procedures, Observation and Inspection; Financial Statements Fraud Detection Techniques.
3. **Financial Statement Fraud: Revenues and Receivables** [ILO 2]: Revenue Recognition Principle; Improper Revenue Detection techniques; Improper Revenue Recognition Detection Techniques; Related part Transactions; Detection of Receivables Misappropriations.
4. **Financial Statement Fraud: Assets, Liabilities and Expenses** [ILO 2]: Types of Assets Misstatements; Detection Techniques of Misappropriated Assets; Understatement of Liabilities and Expenses; Employee Fraud; Off-Balance Sheet transactions; Disbursement Schemes; Expenses Reimbursement Schemes; Payroll Schemes.
5. **Teaming with Forensic Accounting Investigators** [ILO 3]: Scope of Internal Audit; Role of Internal Audit to detect Complex Corporate Fraud; Forensic Accounting Investigators’ cooperation with internal auditors; Forensic Accounting Investigators’ cooperation with external auditors.
6. **Investigative Techniques** [ILO 3]: Case initiation and evaluation; Solvability Factors; Goal setting and planning; Investigation; Timing of Investigation; Understanding the Entity; Document Review; Development and Implementation of the investigative strategy; Prioritize of allegations; interviewing the Financially sophisticated Witness and employees; Documenting the interview.
7. **Analyzing the Financial Statements** [ILO 3]: Horizontal Analysis; Vertical Analysis; Ratio Analysis; Reasonableness Test; Data Mining through Computer Aided Investigation Techniques; Other Analytical Tools; Identifying the signs of Earnings Management; Benfold Law Technique.
8. **Analysis Tools for Investigators** [ILO 3]: Importance of Analysis; Associational Analysis; Temporal Analysis; Inferential Analysis; Investigative Inference Analysis; Constructing an Investigative Inference Chart; Applying Chart for the investigative process.
9. **Data Mining: Computer-Aided Forensic Accounting Investigation** [ILO 3]: Meaning of Data Mining; Effective Data Mining procedures; Data Cleaning; Eliminating Duplicate Information; Effective Use of Data Analysis Results; Data Sorting.
10. **Evidence** [ILO 4]: The Evidence Act, 1872; Meaning of Evidence; Fact in Issue; Characteristics of Evidence—Relevance, Admissibility, Reliable; Types of Evidence—Documentary Evidence, Real Evidence, Testimony, Circumstantial Evidence, Hearsay as Evidence; Beyond Reasonable Doubt Standard; Document collection and organization; Critical Steps of Gathering Evidence; Management of evidence; Burden of Proof.
11. **Money Laundering** [ILO 5]: Money Laundering Prevention Act, 2012; Meaning of Money Laundering; Laundering Process—Placement, layering, Integration; Reporting Entity; Predicate Offence; Relationship between Fraud and Money Laundering; Varying the Impacts of Money Laundering on Companies; Impact of Money Laundering on Financial Statements; Anti-Money Laundering and Forensic Accounting Investigation; Offence of Money Laundering and Punishment; Investigation and Trial; Bangladesh Financial Intelligence Unit (BFIU) and its functions.
12. **Cyber-crimes** [ILO 6]: Definition of cyber-crime; Profiling the cyber-criminals; The auditor’s role in computer forensics; Management of cyber-crime; Criminality of cyber-crimes; cyber-crime statutes; Guidelines for cyber-crime management.

### 7. Analyzing the Financial Statements

- [FA52 – Horizontal Analysis Example - Step-by-Step Financial Analysis](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZkI9Tejs3uU) — **Tony Bell**. Walks through horizontal analysis with a workbook; useful for practicing the calculation and explaining period-to-period changes in an exam.
- [How to Detect Fraud Using Benford's Law](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7uhAn19V1EY) — **Robert A. Bonavito, CPA**. A forensic accountant explains Benford’s Law for screening large numerical datasets such as tax returns and general ledgers; useful for a concise method explanation. The syllabus spells the name “Benfold.”
- [CAATS and its Application](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=N9ml0zexqxo) — **MANIFESTED PUBLISHERS**. Introduces computer-assisted audit techniques and their application; useful for explaining the role of computer-aided techniques in financial examination.

**NOT COVERED:** Vertical Analysis; Ratio Analysis; Reasonableness Test; “Other Analytical Tools” beyond the specifically mapped Benford/CAAT examples; Identifying the signs of Earnings Management. Search YouTube: `vertical analysis ratio analysis reasonableness test earnings management forensic accounting examples`.

### 8. Analysis Tools for Investigators

**NOT COVERED:** Importance of Analysis; Associational Analysis; Temporal Analysis; Inferential Analysis; Investigative Inference Analysis; Constructing an Investigative Inference Chart; Applying Chart for the investigative process. The mapped financial-analysis and CAAT lessons are not demonstrated coverage of these named inference methods/charts. Search YouTube: `forensic accounting associational temporal inferential analysis investigative inference chart lesson`.

### 9. Data Mining: Computer-Aided Forensic Accounting Investigation

See the single **CAATS and its Application** entry under topic 7 for general computer-assisted technique context; it is not a demonstrated forensic data-mining or data-cleaning walkthrough.

**NOT COVERED:** Meaning of Data Mining; Effective Data Mining procedures; Data Cleaning; Eliminating Duplicate Information; Effective Use of Data Analysis Results; Data Sorting. Search YouTube: `forensic accounting data mining data cleaning duplicate records sorting tutorial`; `audit fraud data analytics effective procedure interpret results lesson`.

### 10. Evidence

- [সাক্ষ্য আইন | ল এন্ড অর্ডার | Law and Order | Law Program| ATN Bangla Talkshow](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xuh6YesLyFM) — **ATN Bangla Talk Show**. A Bangladesh Bangla-language legal-program episode on the Evidence Act (“সাক্ষ্য আইন”); useful as local-law orientation, but the page does not establish that it teaches every listed evidence doctrine.
- [Introduction to Digital Evidence Collection](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WIV0aa_DnXw) — **CyberOffense**. The lesson is specifically about digital evidence collection; useful as an introductory practical prompt for gathering digital records, not as a Bangladesh Evidence Act lecture.

**NOT COVERED:** The Evidence Act, 1872 provisions in detail; Meaning of Evidence; Fact in Issue; Characteristics of Evidence—Relevance, Admissibility, Reliable; Types of Evidence—Documentary Evidence, Real Evidence, Testimony, Circumstantial Evidence, Hearsay as Evidence; Beyond Reasonable Doubt Standard; Document collection and organization (general/non-digital); Critical Steps of Gathering Evidence (general); Management of evidence (general); Burden of Proof. Search YouTube: `Bangladesh Evidence Act 1872 relevance admissibility burden of proof documentary circumstantial hearsay lecture`; `Bangladesh Evidence Act fact in issue beyond reasonable doubt types of evidence class`.

### 11. Money Laundering

- [Money Laundering Act 2012 | Law and Order | Law and Order | ATN Bangla Talkshow](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BCzzCg8DQBg) — **ATN Bangla Talk Show**. A Bangladesh legal-program episode titled for the Money Laundering Act 2012, giving Bangladesh-specific statutory context; the watch-page description does not establish a section-by-section statutory review.
- [What Do You Know About Placement, Layering and Integration ?](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c9FPcJp1ClU) — **FinCrime Agent**. Explains the three stages with practical examples; useful for memorizing and distinguishing placement, layering and integration in a written response. This is general AML instruction, not a Bangladesh-law lesson.

**NOT COVERED:** Meaning of Money Laundering; Reporting Entity; Predicate Offence; Relationship between Fraud and Money Laundering; Varying the Impacts of Money Laundering on Companies; Impact of Money Laundering on Financial Statements; Anti-Money Laundering and Forensic Accounting Investigation; Offence of Money Laundering and Punishment; Investigation and Trial; Bangladesh Financial Intelligence Unit (BFIU) and its functions. The Act video is local but its page does not verify these individual provisions or BFIU functions. Search YouTube: `Bangladesh Money Laundering Prevention Act 2012 reporting entity predicate offence punishment investigation trial BFIU`; `BFIU functions money laundering financial statement forensic investigation Bangladesh lecture`.

### 12. Cyber-crimes

- [Introduction to Cybercrime and Digital Forensics](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uQW9SM6jhJY) — **CyberOffense**. An introductory cybercrime/digital-forensics lesson; useful for initial concept orientation, but its watch page has no description supporting coverage of the specific auditor or statutory syllabus details. No Bangladesh jurisdiction is identified on the page.

**NOT COVERED:** Profiling the cyber-criminals; The auditor’s role in computer forensics; Management of cyber-crime; Criminality of cyber-crimes; cyber-crime statutes; Guidelines for cyber-crime management. Search YouTube: `Bangladesh cybercrime law cyber security act auditor computer forensics lecture`; `cybercriminal profiling organizational cybercrime management guidelines auditor computer forensics lesson`.

## Coverage audit

**12** detailed syllabus headings are outlined. **15** unique YouTube videos are mapped, and each recommended watch page was opened to check its displayed title, channel and relevance. **No:** every syllabus subtopic does not have a direct verified video; many narrow topics are explicitly marked **NOT COVERED** with search terms. Bangladesh Evidence Act and MLPA videos are Bangladesh-specific, but their watch-page information does not verify every named provision; the general AML-stages and CPA/auditing lessons should not be treated as Bangladesh legal instruction.

# 3204 Organizational Behavior

**Semester:** Semester 6 — Third Year, Second Semester  
**Syllabus source:** University of Barishal, BBA Accounting & Information Systems, 11th-batch curriculum, *Detailed Syllabus*, course entry on PDF p. 101 (recommended textbooks follow on p. 102).

> **Source-text note:** The course heading reads “3204: Organizational Behavior,” matching the requested title/code; the course page itself does not repeat the semester. The semester above follows the task specification. The PDF contains apparent spelling/heading errors—among them “Burtrand Ressell,” “Theory V,” “Kenneth Arrow’s Signature theory,” “Committees, schemes Model,” “Law and high morale,” and “Human hector analysis.” These are retained as printed where they occur rather than silently corrected.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Introduction.** Meaning of behavior; significance of studying behavior; scope of the subject; its relation with Economics, Philosophy and Business.

2. **Human Psychology & Industrial Psychology.** The syllabus names Aristotle, Adam Smith, Rabindranath Tagore and “Burtrand Ressell.” It then lists:
   - **a) Agency relationship:** separation of ownership and control; Kenneth Arrow’s “Signature theory” and uncertainties.
   - **b) Perception and attitudes:** determination and change; “Committees, schemes Model.”
   - **c) History of Industrial Psychology:** psychologists’ roles in selection, training, placement, transfer and performance appraisal; experts’ role in developing the subject as a discipline of knowledge.
   - **d) Job attitudes, motivation concepts and applications, emotion and modes.**
   - **e) Frustration:** significance, causes and remedies.
   - **f) Group dynamics:** group formation, group norms, benefits of group membership and membership types; Hawthorn study; work-group names in the USA, UK, Japan, India and Bangladesh.
   - **g) Work and work environment:** job satisfaction, productivity, group solidarity and cohesiveness; effects of environment on workers’ performance, including job satisfaction, productivity, group solidarity and cohesiveness; effects of repetitive work, rest and recreational facilities; eliminating boredom; length of work period.
   - **h) Personality and values:** determinants, personality traits, introvert and extrovert.
   - **i) Leadership:** Theory V, Theory Y and Theory Z; Maslow and Herzberg; leadership patterns in “sectaries” and organizations in Bangladesh; whether leaders are born or made; sources of power.
   - **j) Conflict and negotiation, power and politics.**
   - **k) Effectiveness:** management effectiveness; successful versus effective leader; employee commitments; employee turnover.
   - **l) Culture:** cultural variables, effective performance and behavior, aestheticism, values, independence, arts, humanity, self-control and development.
   - **m) Morale:** “Law and high morale,” productivity, measurement and diagnosis.
   - **n) Counseling:** types, meaning and significance; directive versus non-directive counseling.
   - **o) Engineering psychology:** “Human hector analysis” for equipment design, layout, systems study, input and output factors.

## Topic-to-video map

Each linked item below was opened as a direct YouTube watch page. The title and channel are transcribed from that page; coverage is limited to the titled lesson and its displayed description. A video is not treated as evidence for unmentioned syllabus details.

**NOT COVERED:** a direct lesson on the full course scope or OB’s specific relations with Economics, Philosophy and Business. Search: `organizational behavior scope relation economics philosophy business lecture`.

**NOT COVERED:** syllabus-specific accounts of Aristotle, Adam Smith, Rabindranath Tagore and “Burtrand Ressell.” Search: `Aristotle Adam Smith Rabindranath Tagore Bertrand Russell human psychology organizational behavior Bangladesh syllabus`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count: 17** (the introduction plus the named psychology unit and its a–o subtopics). At least one relevant, verified video is mapped to **13 of 17** units; the agency-relationship, frustration, effectiveness and morale units have no direct suitable verified lesson here. Even in partially covered units, numerous exact syllabus items are explicitly marked **NOT COVERED** with search terms rather than inferred from broad video titles. **Video count: 15 unique watch-page links**, opened to verify the displayed title, channel and lesson relevance; YouTube playback itself may require sign-in.

# 3205 Accounting Information Systems

**Semester:** Semester 6 — Third Year, Second Semester (11th-batch AIS curriculum; 2 credits).

**Source and matching note:** The course is matched by title and semester in the BBA Accounting & Information Systems curriculum effective from the 11th batch, then by the `3205: Accounting Information Systems` heading in its Detailed Course Contents. The earlier Management Studies curriculum was not used. The detailed syllabus cites `[ILO 8]` for topics 4 and 11, but the course's own ILO list contains only ILOs 1–7; this appears to be a cross-reference error. Topic names and syllabus phrases below retain the source wording, including abbreviations and apparent typos.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

**PART A: Theory (50%)**

1. **The Information System [ILO 1]:** An Accountant's Perspective; the Information Environment; the Framework of AIS and the Various Sub Systems; Organizational Structure; the Role of the Accountant; Accounting Information Systems; AIS and MIS; Accounting as a System; Users of Accounting Information System; Management Structure; Information Technology and Corporate Strategy; the AIS and Corporate Strategy; How AIS can Add Value to an Organization; Models of Decision Making; Human Aspect of the AIS; Transaction Processing; Computer Hardware and Software in AIS; Computerized Accounting Systems; Tools to Implement Accounting System.
2. **Introduction to Transaction Processing [ILO 2]:** An Overview of Transaction Processing; Transaction Cycles: Expenditure Cycle; Accounting Record Keeping; Audit Trail; Chart of Accounts; Different Methods Coding; Documentation Techniques; Computer Based Accounting Systems.
3. **Documenting AIS [ILO 3]:** Importance of Documentation; Document Flow Charts; Guidelines for Drawing DFC; System Flow Chart & DFD; System FC & Guidelines; Program FC; Decision Table; Case Tool.
4. **Ethics, Fraud, and Internal Control [ILO 4, ILO 8]:** Ethical Issues in Business; Fraud and Accountants; Internal Control Concepts and Procedures; COSO Framework; Assessing and Managing Risk Related to AIS; Computer Fraud and Security; Auditing; Computer-Based Information Systems.
5. **Revenue and Expenditure Cycles [ILO 2]:** Overview of the Manual Revenue System and Study of CBAS; Study of the Sales and Purchases Sub Systems of an Organization; Overview of Purchases and Cash Disbursements; Computer Based Purchases and Cash Disbursements; Payroll Activities; Computerized Payroll System; Threats, Exposures and Control Procedures in the Revenue and Expenditure Cycles.
6. **General Ledger, Financial Reporting, and Management Reporting Systems [ILO 2]:** Data Coding Systems; Financial Reporting Systems; Management Reporting Systems; Threats; Exposures and Control Procedures in the Revenue and Expenditure Cycles.
7. **Strategic Decisions [ILO 5]:** DSS; AIS and AI/ES; AIS in Corporate Planning; Decision Support System (DSS); Artificial Intelligence (AI) and Expert Systems Operation and Evaluation.
8. **Systems Organizations, Development, and Management [ILO 7]:** Approaches to System Development; AIS Development Strategies; System Development Life Cycle (SDLC).
9. **Designing and Implementing Systems [ILO 7]:** System Acquisition Methodology; Investigation and Feasibility Study; Requirements Analysis and Initial Design; System Design; Selection; Acquisition and Development; Systems Implementation; System Maintenance and Program Changes; Project Management: Planning, Control Methods, and Standards.
10. **Controls and Standards [ILO 4]:** Control Environment; System Acquisition Process and Controls; Risk Assessment Processes and Activities; Information System Processing Operations and Controls; Monitoring Processes and Activities; Controls Over Personal Systems.
11. **Information Security [ILO 5, ILO 8]:** Importance of Security; Security of Computer Hardware and Software; Data Security; Network and Web Security; Malicious Software; Hackers and Cyber Vandalism; Computer Crime and Cyber Terrorism.
