Search: `AIS computer hardware software data network web security malware hackers cyber vandalism computer crime lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**11 top-level syllabus topics; 10/11 have at least one verified, relevant lesson, with partial coverage.** Topic 8 has no directly suitable verified video identified. For the other topics, the page metadata does not establish every syllabus subtopic; gaps and precise search keywords are listed above. In particular, COSO is named in the syllabus but not demonstrated by the selected AIS-control video's visible page, and documentation artifacts, processing/control standards, and advanced security threats need targeted follow-up. All recommended videos teach general AIS/CPA concepts; none is presented as Bangladesh-specific legal/regulatory instruction. The syllabus has no named local statute in this course's detailed contents.

# 3206 Lab: Enterprise Resource Planning

**Semester:** Third Year, Second Semester (Semester 6)  
**Program:** BBA Accounting & Information Systems, University of Barishal, 11th-batch curriculum (2022–2023 onward)

The semester course list labels this **“3206 Lab: Enterprise Resource Planning”** (2 credits). The Detailed Syllabus heading instead reads **“3206: Enterprise Resource Planning”**; the course code matches, but the heading omits “Lab.” The detailed contents also tag “Introduction to Modern Accounting Packages” as **[ILO 9]**, although this entry lists only ILOs 1–3. Both are retained as source discrepancies rather than silently corrected.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

Source: attached *AIS Syllabus.pdf*, 11th-batch course list and **DETAILED SYLLABUS**, entry 3206 (not the earlier Management Studies curriculum).

1. **Historical Development of Accounting Packages & ERP Systems** [ILO 1].
2. **Introduction to Modern Accounting Packages** [ILO 9]:
   - Practical demonstration of contemporary accounting and business software; suggested packages: **Tally Prime, QuickBooks, Xero, etc.**
   - Features of an accounting package.
   - Making a chart of accounts; general ledger.
   - Payables: vendors, purchase, payments.
   - Receivables: customers, sales receivables.
   - Maintaining payroll.
   - Inventory management.
   - Point of Sales (POS).
   - Reporting.
   - Miscellaneous.
3. **Project:**
   - Accounting Information System (AIS) / ERP design and development, using ERD, DFD, and designing an AIS.
   - Project based on practical demonstrations of contemporary accounting and business package.

## Topic-to-video map

The entries below point to individual YouTube **watch pages** opened to check the displayed title, channel, and lesson relevance. Videos demonstrate TallyPrime rather than every package named in the syllabus; examples are software practice aids, not Barishal-specific configuration guidance.

### ERP and package demonstrations

- [**ERP Systems Explained in Under 8 Minutes**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bb4jVGiQKKA) — **Software Connect**. **Covers:** ERP as a modern integrated business-software concept, relevant to the course’s ERP introduction and package-feature context; it does **not** teach the historical development timeline. A short conceptual overview helps frame written answers on what ERP does and why organizations use it.
- [**How to Create Ledgers in TallyPrime | Tally Learning Hub**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=leyl5w8GAmo) — **Tally Solutions**. **Covers:** practical ledger-master setup as an entry point to making a chart of accounts and establishing general-ledger accounts; it does not demonstrate a full chart-of-accounts design exercise. The step-by-step screen practice is useful for practical questions on setting up accounting records.
- [**How to Record Purchases and Sales in TallyPrime | Tally Learning Hub**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-xgd6H2Iqsc) — **Tally Solutions**. **Covers:** purchase and sales transaction entry, supporting the purchase and sales-receivables workflow items. Its transaction walk-through is good practice for practical voucher-entry questions.
- [**How to Record Payments and Receipts in TallyPrime | Tally Learning Hub**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZNQheFhTIfU) — **Tally Solutions**. **Covers:** payment and receipt recording, including the cash-settlement steps connected to vendor payables and customer receipts. Repeating the demonstrated entries gives practice for lab-based voucher questions.
- [**How to Manage Receivables and Payables using Billwise Details in TallyPrime | Tally Learning Hub**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lrFlqv5Jnk0) — **Tally Solutions**. **Covers:** bill-wise receivables and payables tracking for customer/vendor accounts. It helps with exam questions that ask how outstanding invoices and settlements are tracked against parties.
- [**How to Create Stock Groups & Items in TallyPrime | Tally Learning Hub**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1MHWbYNDirc) — **Tally Solutions**. **Covers:** stock groups and stock items as inventory setup. The software demonstration supports practical questions on structuring inventory records.
- [**Payroll Management with TallyPrime | CA Deepanshu Goel | Tally CA Connect**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CoRGKIPc-Fk) — **Tally Solutions**. **Covers:** payroll setup, salary processing/accounting, and payroll reports. Its applied payroll workflow helps with practical questions on maintaining payroll in an accounting package. **Jurisdiction note:** the description mentions statutory compliance; do not treat any country-specific compliance discussion as Bangladesh payroll law.
- [**Point of Sale Invoice 🧾 in Tally Prime | POS Invoice in Hindi**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ulFfkWBWfSw) — **Tally Tutorial**. **Covers:** practical POS invoice entry. The worked screen example helps rehearse POS steps for a lab/practical question. **Language/jurisdiction note:** the lesson is in Hindi and shows Tally configuration; no Bangladesh VAT rule is established by this video.
- [**How to View and Analyse Accounting Reports in TallyPrime | Tally Learning Hub**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=27DCHv_mQJw) — **Tally Solutions**. **Covers:** viewing and analysing accounting reports. It is useful for practical/report-reading questions and interpreting package outputs; its page description does not confirm a general-ledger-specific report exercise.

### AIS/ERP project diagrams

- [**Entity Relationship Diagram (ERD) Tutorial and EXAMPLE**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wMgirP7z4k8) — **The Business Analysis Doctor - IIBA Certification**. **Covers:** ERD concepts, notations, components, and an example, supporting the project’s “use of ERD” item. The example-based explanation is useful for drawing and labeling an ERD in a written or project question.
- [**Data Flow Diagram EXAMPLE [How to Create Data Flow Diagrams]**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ab1DZ6o7QBs) — **The Business Analysis Doctor - IIBA Certification**. **Covers:** DFD purpose and levels, with an example, supporting the project’s “use of DFD” item. It gives a model for explaining levels and constructing a DFD in an exam answer.

### NOT COVERED — no directly suitable video verified

- **Historical development of accounting packages and ERP systems:** the ERP overview above is conceptual, not a development/history lesson. Search YouTube for: `history evolution of ERP systems MRP MRP II accounting packages lecture timeline`.
- **Miscellaneous package features:** the syllabus does not define what “Miscellaneous” includes, and no bounded examinable subtopic can be matched. Search with the specific feature named by the instructor, e.g. `TallyPrime [feature name] practical tutorial`.
- **Integrated AIS/ERP design-and-development project:** the ERD and DFD videos teach those artifacts separately, but no verified lesson was found that designs and develops a complete AIS/ERP project end to end. Search YouTube for: `accounting information system project design ERD DFD ERP implementation tutorial`.
- **Designing an AIS:** the diagram lessons are useful components, but neither directly demonstrates AIS-specific design from requirements through design. Search for: `AIS design project accounting information system ERD DFD worked example`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count: 20** (counting each syllabus bullet/sub-bullet once; package examples remain one item). **Direct verified-video coverage: 14/20; partial/limited coverage: 2/20; NOT COVERED: 4/20.** The limited items are the package example list: TallyPrime is demonstrated, but QuickBooks and Xero are not individually covered; and “general ledger,” represented by ledger setup and accounting-report demonstrations, without a verified dedicated general-ledger report exercise. The four uncovered topics are historical development, undefined miscellaneous features, integrated AIS/ERP project development, and AIS-specific design. No Bangladesh law, tax, VAT, or regulatory topic is stated in this course’s detailed contents; India GST/statutory compliance from software tutorials should not be substituted for Bangladesh rules. All 11 recommended links above were opened as YouTube watch pages; their visible titles, channels, and descriptions identified them as actual, relevant lessons.

# Semester 7 — Fourth Year, First Semester

# 4101 Advanced Management Accounting

**Semester:** Semester 7 — Fourth Year, First Semester (3 credits)  
**Curriculum:** University of Barishal BBA, Accounting & Information Systems, effective from 11th batch (2022–2023).

The 11th-batch course table and its detailed-syllabus heading both identify this course as **4101: Advanced Management Accounting**. This extract uses the AIS Detailed Syllabus section, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum. One editorial quirk: the separate **“10. Balanced Scorecard”** heading repeats material already listed under performance measurement in item 6 and has no ILO label; it is retained as printed. The source also has occasional spacing/numbering irregularities.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Traditions and Innovations in Management Accounting [ILO 1]:** An Overview of Conventional Management Accounting; Relevance Lost; Relevance Regained; Management Accounting in Post-Modern Era Business; Cost Management; Management Accounting Innovations.
2. **Relevant Information for Decision Making with a Focus on Pricing Decisions [ILO 2]:** The Concept of Relevance; Decision Model; Major Factors that Affect Pricing Decisions; The Concept of Pricing: Perfect and Imperfect Competition; Market-Based Approach: Target Costing for Target Pricing; Cost-Plus Pricing; Non-Cost Factors in Pricing Decisions; Antitrust Laws and Pricing Decisions.
3. **Relevant Information for Decision Making with a Focus on Operational Decisions [ILO 3]:** Opportunity, Outlay and Differential Costs; Avoidable and Unavoidable Costs; Insourcing Versus Outsourcing; Make or Buy Decisions; Dropping or Continuing with a Segment; Optimal Use of Limited Resources; Special Sales Order; Keep or Replace a Machine/Equipment; Irrelevant or Misspecified Costs; Decisions and Performance Evaluation.
4. **Responsibility Accounting and Segment Reporting [ILO 4]:** Decentralization and Corporate Segments; Traceable vs Common Fixed Costs; Segment Margin; Segmented Income Statements; Levels of Segmented Income Statements; Segmented Financial Information in External Reports.
5. **Management Control Systems, Transfer Pricing, and Multinational Considerations [ILO 5]:** Management Control Systems: Nature and Type; Evaluating Management Control Systems; Decentralization and Transfer Pricing; Criteria for Evaluating Transfer Prices; Calculating Transfer Prices; Market-Based Transfer Prices; Cost-Based Transfer Prices; Negotiated Transfer Prices; Dual Transfer Pricing; General guideline for determining a minimum transfer price; Goal Congruence and Sub-optimality; How Multinationals Use Transfer Pricing to Minimize Their Taxes; OECD Guidelines on Transfer Pricing.
6. **Performance Measurement, Compensation, and Multi-National Considerations [ILO 6]:** Financial and Nonfinancial Performance Measures; Accounting-Based Measures for Business; Approaches to Measuring Performance; Return on Investment (ROI) and DuPont Method; Residual Income (RI) Method; Economic Value Added (EVA) Method; Return on Sales (ROS); Balanced Scorecard; Choosing the Details of the Performance; Target Levels of Performance and Feedback; Performance Measurement in Multinational Companies; Role of Management Accountant in Designing a Reward System.
7. **The Management and Control of Quality [ILO 7]:** Definition of Quality; Costs of Quality: Prevention Costs, Appraisal Costs, Internal Failure Costs and External Failure Costs; Measuring and Controlling Quality Cost; Distribution of Quality Costs; Quality Cost Reports; Uses of Quality Cost Information; International Aspects of Quality: The ISO Standards.
8. **Accounting, Time and Efficiency [ILO 8]:** Just-In-Time Systems; Enterprise Resource Planning (ERP) Systems; Backflush Costing; Managing Goods for Sale in Retail Organizations; Challenges in Estimating Stock-Related Costs and their Effects; Just-In-Time Purchasing; Stock Costs and their Management in Manufacturing Organizations; Theory of Constraints; Balanced Scorecards and Time-Based Measures.
9. **Data Analytic Thinking and Prediction [ILO 9]:** Data Science Basics and Management Accounting; Outcome Prediction; Value Creation; Data Science Framework—Defining the Problem and the Relevant Data, Data Algorithms and Models, Refining the Decision Tree, Validating and Choosing Models, Evaluating Data Science Models.
10. **Balanced Scorecard:** Concepts; Origin of BSC; Implementation of BSC.
11. **Emerging Issues: Digital Technologies, Governance and Sustainability [ILO 10]:** Digital technologies and accounting; The digitally advanced enterprise control loop; Enterprise governance; Environmental management accounting; Management accounting changes highlight managerial context.

## Topic-to-video map

All listed links below were opened as YouTube `watch?v=` pages to check the displayed title, channel, and lesson relevance. Coverage is stated narrowly; a general video is not treated as proof of a detailed syllabus subtopic.

### 1. Traditions and innovations

**NOT COVERED:** No suitable, exam-oriented lesson was verified for the full historical/conceptual sequence: conventional management accounting; Relevance Lost; Relevance Regained; management accounting in post-modern-era business; cost management; and management accounting innovations.  
**Search keywords:** `management accounting relevance lost relevance regained conventional postmodern innovations lecture`; `management accounting history Relevance Lost Relevance Regained lecture Kaplan Johnson`.

### 2. Relevant information and pricing

- [Target Costing Price Takers. Cost Accounting & Managerial Accounting Courses. CPA Exam BAR.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Few7HYGL8NQ) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses.** Covers target costing/market-price-first target pricing and contrasts it with cost-plus pricing; its worked explanation is useful for a pricing calculation and short written comparison.
- [Relevant Costs & Decision-Making | Chapter 12 | Managerial Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IaG9MjuF3Y8) — **Samantha Taylor.** Covers the relevance framework and relevant-cost decision making; it is a focused lecture useful for defining relevance and framing a decision answer.

**NOT COVERED:** The decision model as a named formal model; all major pricing-decision factors; perfect versus imperfect competition; a full cost-plus pricing calculation; non-cost pricing factors; antitrust laws. Neither video is Bangladesh-law instruction; no jurisdiction-specific antitrust lesson was verified.  
**Search keywords:** `pricing decisions perfect imperfect competition non-cost factors antitrust management accounting lecture`; `cost plus pricing worked example managerial accounting`; `antitrust law pricing decisions accounting exam`.

### 3. Relevant information and operational decisions

- The **Relevant Costs & Decision-Making | Chapter 12 | Managerial Accounting** lesson by **Samantha Taylor** (linked above under topic 2) supplies general relevant/irrelevant-cost decision framing; use it to revise the decision rule before attempting case calculations.

**NOT COVERED:** The course’s full case set is not demonstrated by this general lecture: opportunity, outlay and differential cost distinctions; avoidable/unavoidable costs; insourcing versus outsourcing; make-or-buy; drop/continue a segment; optimal use of limited resources; special sales order; keep/replace equipment; irrelevant or misspecified costs as a separate case; and decisions/performance evaluation.  
**Search keywords:** `relevant costing make or buy special order keep replace drop segment limited resource worked problems managerial accounting`; `opportunity outlay differential avoidable unavoidable costs lecture`.

### 4. Responsibility accounting and segment reporting

- [Segmented Income Statement: Traceable Fixed Cost & Common Fixed Cost](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eSUlt3qlqn0) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses.** Explicitly explains the meaning of a segment, traceable versus common fixed costs, segment margin, and a segmented income statement; the worked format helps with a likely statement-preparation question.

**NOT COVERED:** Decentralization/corporate-segment responsibility accounting beyond the introductory framing; levels of segmented income statements; segmented financial information in external reports.  
**Search keywords:** `responsibility accounting levels segmented income statements external reporting lecture`.

### 5. Management control systems, transfer pricing and multinationals

- [Transfer Pricing Methods: Market, Cost-Based & Negotiated](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GO06fD45VpE) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses.** Covers market-, cost-based and negotiated methods and their effects on divisional profit, autonomy and incentives; the method comparison supports exam explanations and transfer-price problem setup.

**NOT COVERED:** Nature/types and evaluation of management control systems; criteria/calculation details for transfer prices beyond the named method overview; dual transfer pricing; the minimum-transfer-price guideline; goal congruence and sub-optimality as a worked case; multinational tax minimization; OECD Guidelines on Transfer Pricing. Farhat’s channel is CPA/CMA-oriented and the course context is not Bangladesh tax law; do not use it as Bangladesh regulatory advice.  
**Search keywords:** `minimum transfer price dual transfer pricing goal congruence multinational tax worked example`; `OECD Transfer Pricing Guidelines arm length methods educational lecture`; `Bangladesh transfer pricing NBR rules lesson`.

### 6. Performance measurement and compensation

- [Managerial Accounting 11.4: Using Return on Investment (ROI) to Evaluate Performance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=l4XnG4cO36I) — **Kurt Heisinger.** Explains ROI for evaluating investment centers; useful for formula recall and a short performance-evaluation calculation.
- [Managerial Accounting 11.6: Using Economic Value Added (EVA) to Evaluate Performance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DLr0PqR1aS0) — **Kurt Heisinger.** Explains EVA for investment-center evaluation; it is a focused supplement for interpreting an EVA result in a written or numerical answer.

**NOT COVERED:** Financial/nonfinancial measure classifications and broad measurement approaches; accounting measures generally beyond the two lessons; DuPont method; RI; ROS; choosing performance details; target levels and feedback; multinational performance measurement; management accountant’s role in reward-system design. The Balanced Scorecard is treated under topic 10.  
**Search keywords:** `DuPont ROI residual income ROS worked example management accounting performance measurement`; `multinational performance measurement compensation reward system management accounting lecture`.

### 7. Management and control of quality

- [Explaining the Cost of Quality, the 4 Cost Categories and Juran’s Quality Cost Curve](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LA5B5g1kLkM) — **CQE Academy.** Explicitly covers prevention, appraisal, internal-failure and external-failure costs; the examples and cost-curve framing help with classification and theory questions.
- [Preparing a Cost of Quality Report Demonstration Problem](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_sZVwRhfnlI) — **From Thought to Thriving.** Demonstrates preparation of a cost-of-quality report; it is the most directly exam-oriented item here for organizing and calculating a report.

**NOT COVERED:** Measuring/controlling and distributing quality costs in a full accounting problem; uses of quality-cost information; international quality aspects and ISO standards.  
**Search keywords:** `quality cost report measuring controlling distribution uses ISO standards management accounting worked example`.

### 8. Accounting, time and efficiency

- [JUST IN TIME SYSTEM | Accounting and Backflush Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6tFl2wMxeko) — **Angela Besino.** The lesson title directly identifies JIT and backflush accounting; it is useful for reviewing the two linked concepts, though no description or worked-problem claim is made here.

**NOT COVERED:** ERP systems; managing goods for sale in retail; challenges estimating stock-related costs and their effects; JIT purchasing specifically; manufacturing stock-cost management; Theory of Constraints; time-based measures. The Balanced Scorecard is covered only at the conceptual/origin/implementation level by the video under topic 10.  
**Search keywords:** `ERP management accounting JIT purchasing retail inventory stock cost manufacturing theory of constraints time based measures lecture`.

### 9. Data analytic thinking and prediction

- [APM Topic Explainer: Data Analytics](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=k3BkZgHBvFA) — **ACCA Student Study Resources.** Provides an APM-context introduction to data analytics; it is useful for conceptual revision but does not demonstrate the whole syllabus framework.
- [Data Analytics for Accounting Chapter 3 Performing the tests and analyzing results](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3XxwObQqzWU) — **Dr. Veronica Paz.** An accounting data-analytics lecture focused on performing tests and analyzing results; it can help frame analytical-model evaluation, but the watch page does not establish coverage of the exact syllabus methods.

**NOT COVERED:** Data-science basics specifically within management accounting; outcome prediction and value creation; defining the problem/relevant data; data algorithms and models; refining a decision tree; validating/choosing models; evaluating data-science models as a complete process.  
**Search keywords:** `management accounting data science framework prediction value creation decision tree validation model evaluation lecture`; `accounting predictive analytics decision tree worked example validate choose model`.

### 10. Balanced scorecard

**NOT COVERED:** No Bangladesh-specific or course-specific BSC case was verified.  
**Search keywords:** `balanced scorecard implementation example management accounting exam answer`.

### 11. Digital technologies, governance and sustainability

- [Current Developments in Management Accounting - ACCA Advanced Performance Management (APM)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DueL6cQkRJk) — **OpenTuition.** A reputable exam-preparation lecture explicitly focused on current management-accounting developments; it supports high-level written discussion of emerging managerial context, not every digital-control detail.
- [APM topic Explainer: Environmental Management Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=50XjLB_s3DA) — **ACCA Student Study Resources.** Explains environmental management accounting; this is useful for defining the environmental-cost information angle in a sustainability answer.

**NOT COVERED:** Digital technologies and accounting in detail; the digitally advanced enterprise control loop; enterprise governance; the syllabus’s specific “management accounting changes highlight managerial context” treatment beyond general emerging developments. Environmental management accounting is covered, but not the full governance/sustainability scope.  
**Search keywords:** `digital technologies accounting enterprise control loop management accounting governance lecture`; `enterprise governance sustainability management accounting current developments lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 11 detailed-syllabus headings. **Verified videos:** 14 distinct direct YouTube watch pages; no link is duplicated. **Coverage:** at least one relevant lesson is available for 10 of 11 headings; heading 1 has no suitable lesson verified. No heading’s full detailed subtopic list is covered. The uncovered subtopics are explicitly listed above with targeted search terms. # 4102 Financial Market and Investment Analysis

**Semester:** Fourth Year — First Semester (Semester 7) · **Credits:** 3

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

Source: attached *University of Barishal BBA Accounting & Information Systems syllabus*, 11th-batch curriculum (effective 2022–2023), Detailed Syllabus, PDF pp. 107–111. The course-list table uses code **4102** with title “Financial Market and Investment Analysis”; that matches the detailed heading. The PDF has minor spacing/OCR blemishes (for example, “StockExchange”); names below are normalized without changing the syllabus meaning.

### Part A: Financial Market (40%)

1. **Overview of the Financial System:** Components of the financial system of Bangladesh; financial markets—their types, structure and functions; internationalization of financial markets; financial intermediaries/institutions—their types, importance and functions; securities traded in financial markets; regulators of the financial system.
2. **The Money Market:** Definition, purpose, participants and regulator of the money market; central bank structure, governance and functions; monetary policy and implications; commercial-bank operations, regulation, management and performance; banks’ sources and uses of funds; digital banking; Islamic banking; non-bank financial institutions (NBFIs); insurance companies; types and valuation of money-market securities; globalization of money markets.
3. **The Capital Market:** Definition, functions and participants; primary market and process for issuing new securities; private placement; secondary market and trading mechanism; capital-market instruments—stock, bond, debenture and mutual fund; stock types and characteristics; stock-market transaction order types, margin trading and short selling; stock-market indexes; regulation of stock trading; over-the-counter (OTC) markets; Alternative Trading Board; SME Platform; bond market and bond types/features; intermediaries—investment banks, issue managers, underwriters, broker-dealers, pension funds, professional fund managers and venture-capital firms; financial innovation and FinTech; regulators/institutions: Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Commission (BSEC), Dhaka Stock Exchange (DSE), Chittagong Stock Exchange (CSE), Central Depository Bangladesh Limited (CDBL), and Central Counterparty Bangladesh Limited (CCBL).
4. **Other markets/institutions:** Commodity exchange; mortgage market; asset-backed securities market; foreign-exchange market; derivative-security market; micro-finance institutions.

### Part B: Investment Analysis (60%)

5. **The Investment Environment:** Definition of investment; measures of return and risk; determinants of required rate of return; risk–return relationship; real and financial assets; financial markets and the economy; investment-management process; Investment Policy Statement; investments in a global market.
6. **An Introduction to Portfolio Theory and Practice:** Definition and measurement of risk and return; probability distribution; expected return and variance of return; risk aversion; mean–variance portfolio theory; measuring a portfolio’s expected return and risk; portfolios of one risky asset and a risk-free asset; diversification and portfolio risk; portfolios of two risky assets; asset allocation with stocks, bonds and bills; Markowitz Portfolio Optimization Model; minimum-variance efficient frontier; portfolio-performance evaluation; composite portfolio-performance measures; performance-attribution analysis; factors affecting the use of performance measures.
7. **Asset Pricing Models:** Overview of Capital Market Theory; Capital Asset Pricing Model (CAPM) and its assumptions; systematic and unsystematic risk; derivation of the Capital Market Line (CML) and Security Market Line (SML); empirical tests of CAPM; theory and practice of the market portfolio; Arbitrage Pricing Theory (APT); multifactor models of risk and return; Fama–French (FF) Three-Factor Model; Efficient Market Hypothesis (EMH), alternative forms, implications and empirical tests.
8. **Stock Analysis and Equity Portfolio Management:** Global and domestic macroeconomic analysis; business-cycle and industry analysis; company analysis and stock valuation; fundamental analysis; technical analysis; active and passive equity-portfolio-management strategies; value versus growth investing; style analysis; asset-allocation strategies; portfolio-manager performance evaluation.
9. **Bond Analysis and Portfolio Management:** Bond fundamentals—characteristics, types, pricing, price volatility, yields and ratings; determinants of bond interest rates; yield curve and future interest rates; theories of the term structure of interest rates; managing interest-rate risk; bond-portfolio strategies—passive, active, core-plus, matched-funding, and contingent/structured management.
10. **Derivative Security Analysis:** Overview of derivative markets and securities; option contract; call and put options; payoff structures; option values at expiration; option-trading strategies; put–call parity; fundamentals of option valuation; binomial option-pricing model; Black–Scholes option-valuation model; forwards and futures—basic concepts, trading mechanics, hedging, pricing and valuation; foreign-exchange, stock-index, interest-rate and commodity futures; swaps; warrants and convertible securities; other embedded derivatives.

## Topic-to-video map

Coverage is limited to what the linked lesson/page identifies as its subject; a general video is not treated as proof of Bangladesh-specific law or of every course subtopic. Each link below was opened as a YouTube watch page to verify that it is a real video and to check its displayed title, channel and relevance.

### 1. Financial system and Bangladesh institutions

- [Financial Markets and Institutions - Lecture 01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tDHwUh8fbsQ) — **Krassimir Petrov**. **Covers:** financial-system basics, financial instruments/claims, financial markets and institutions, intermediation, and primary/secondary-market concepts. **Exam use:** the university-style opening lecture gives definitions and conceptual framing suitable for short explanatory answers; it is not Bangladesh-specific.
- [Important Institutions of the Bangladesh Stock Market and Their Responsibilities](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yFz4XfANr7g) — **BRAC EPL Stock Brokerage Ltd**. **Covers:** Bangladesh stock-market institutions and their responsibilities (use for the local exchange/depository/regulator context only). **Exam use:** local examples can strengthen institution/function answers; check the lecture against current Bangladesh rules because it is not a substitute for the named statutes/rules.

**NOT COVERED:** Bangladesh financial-system component taxonomy; Bangladesh-wide regulator map; internationalization of financial markets. For the institutions video, do not infer that it covers every syllabus body—especially CCBL—unless confirmed in your notes. Search: `Bangladesh financial system components formal semi formal informal sector lecture`; `Bangladesh CCBL central counterparty functions lecture`; `Bangladesh Securities and Exchange Commission securities laws rules stock trading educational lecture`.

The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** T-bills and their pricing, negotiable CDs, commercial paper, bankers’ acceptances, Eurodollars, repos and federal funds (U.S. instruments/market context). **Exam use:** named instruments plus a T-bill calculation provide recall and worked-practice support.

**NOT COVERED:** money-market definition, purpose, participants and regulator as a complete unit; central-bank structure/governance/functions; monetary policy implications; commercial-bank regulation, management and performance; bank funding sources/uses; digital banking; Islamic banking; NBFIs; insurance companies; globalization; Bangladesh-specific money-market practice. This lesson is U.S.-oriented and should not be used as Bangladesh law/regulation instruction. Search: `Bangladesh money market Bangladesh Bank treasury bill repo lecture`; `Bangladesh Bank structure governance monetary policy commercial banks NBFI Islamic banking lecture`; `Bangladesh insurance companies role financial system lecture`.

### 3. Capital market

- [What are Capital Markets? | Intro to Capital Markets (Part 1)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SZZG9UIRaXs) — **Corporate Finance Institute**. **Covers:** introductory capital-market concept and purpose (as identified by the lesson title/series). **Exam use:** a concise conceptual primer can help frame written answers; it is a general, not Bangladesh-regulatory, lesson.
- [Important Institutions of the Bangladesh Stock Market and Their Responsibilities](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yFz4XfANr7g) — **BRAC EPL Stock Brokerage Ltd**. **Covers:** the Bangladesh institution/function aspect identified above. **Exam use:** provides local context to pair with generic market-mechanism theory.

**NOT COVERED:** market participants in syllabus breadth; primary-issue process; private placements; secondary trading mechanics in exam detail; stock/bond/debenture/mutual-fund features beyond the intro; stock types/features; order types; margin trading and short selling; stock indexes; stock-trading rules; OTC markets; Alternative Trading Board; SME Platform; full bond-market features; the complete intermediary list (investment banks, issue managers, underwriters, broker-dealers, pension funds, professional fund managers and venture-capital firms); FinTech/financial innovation; a verified role-by-role treatment of BSEC, DSE, CSE, CDBL and CCBL. The general CFI lesson is not Bangladesh law. Search: `Bangladesh IPO process primary market private placement BSEC lecture`; `DSE trading order types margin trading short selling Bangladesh lesson`; `Bangladesh Alternative Trading Board SME platform DSE BSEC educational video`; `BSEC DSE CSE CDBL CCBL roles Bangladesh lecture`; `bond debenture mutual fund types capital market intermediaries Bangladesh lecture`.

### 4. Commodity, mortgage, asset-backed, FX, derivative markets and microfinance

**NOT COVERED:** commodity exchanges; mortgage markets; asset-backed securities markets; foreign-exchange markets; derivative-security-market overview; microfinance institutions. Search: `commodity exchange mortgage market asset backed securities lecture`; `foreign exchange market structure participants lecture`; `Bangladesh microfinance institutions financial market lecture`.

### 5. Investment environment

- [Expected Return, Variance & Standard Deviation | Excel Example](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-bq6vNXHbnQ) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** scenario probabilities, expected return, variance and standard deviation using a worked portfolio example. **Exam use:** follows the calculation steps students need for return/risk numerical questions.
- [Explaining the Capital Asset Pricing Model (CAPM) & Security Market Line (SML)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XIXd7pUt4cg) — **Ryan O'Connell, CFA, FRM**. **Covers:** CAPM expected-return calculation, risk-free rate, beta and systematic risk (also mapped under Topic 7). **Exam use:** worked formula interpretation helps answer risk–required-return questions.

**NOT COVERED:** investment definition; full return measures; determinants of required return beyond the CAPM lesson; real versus financial assets; markets/economy relationship; investment-management process; Investment Policy Statement; global-market investing. Search: `investment management process investment policy statement global investing lecture`; `real assets versus financial assets investment environment lecture`; `required rate of return determinants risk return relationship lecture`.

### 6. Portfolio theory and practice

- [Expected Return, Variance & Standard Deviation | Excel Example](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-bq6vNXHbnQ) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** expected return and variance/standard deviation calculations from scenario probabilities. **Exam use:** worked calculations build accuracy on core portfolio risk/return measures.
- [Efficient Frontier Explained | Minimum Variance Portfolio & CAL](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kgPU9uPTXPc) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** modern portfolio theory, efficient frontier, minimum-variance portfolio and capital-allocation line. **Exam use:** visual explanation helps label and interpret frontier/CAL diagrams and explain efficient choice.

**NOT COVERED:** risk aversion; diversification and its effect on portfolio risk; full mean–variance optimization/model computation; explicit worked combinations of one risky plus risk-free asset and two risky assets; explicit stock/bond/bill allocation; portfolio-performance evaluation; composite measures; performance attribution; factors affecting measure choice. Search: `two risky assets portfolio expected return covariance worked problem`; `diversification portfolio risk worked example`; `Markowitz portfolio optimization numerical minimum variance weights`; `Sharpe Treynor Jensen portfolio evaluation worked example`; `performance attribution analysis portfolio managers factors performance measures lecture`.

### 7. Asset pricing models

- [Explaining the Capital Asset Pricing Model (CAPM) & Security Market Line (SML)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XIXd7pUt4cg) — **Ryan O'Connell, CFA, FRM**. **Covers:** CAPM, expected return, beta/systematic risk and SML. **Exam use:** formula application and interpretation are useful for numerical and short-answer questions.
- [Arbitrage Pricing Theory and Multifactor Models of Risk and Return (FRM Part 1 2025 – Bk 1 – Chpt 6)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=B_wlmRB9ODA) — **AnalystPrep**. **Covers:** APT and multifactor risk/return models. **Exam use:** the FRM-oriented explanation provides a focused basis for comparing factor-pricing logic with CAPM.
- [Are markets efficient?](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bM9bYOBuKF4) — **Chicago Booth Review**. **Covers:** EMH question—whether market prices reflect information—and debate about market behavior, bubbles, and value/growth. **Exam use:** a discussion between Eugene Fama and Richard Thaler supplies contrasting arguments for written evaluation of EMH implications.

**NOT COVERED:** formal Capital Market Theory overview; CAPM assumptions and empirical tests; unsystematic risk as a separately explained concept; CML derivation; market-portfolio theory/practice; Fama–French Three-Factor Model specifically; EMH alternative forms and empirical tests. The EMH video is a discussion, not a substitute for formal forms/tests. Search: `capital market theory investment portfolio overview lecture`; `CAPM assumptions empirical tests capital market line derivation lecture`; `systematic versus unsystematic risk finance lesson`; `Fama French three factor model worked example lecture`; `weak semi strong strong form EMH empirical tests lecture`.

### 8. Stock analysis and equity-portfolio management

- [Investments - Fundamental Analysis, Lecture 01 - Introduction](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5hGJ1cn_nxw) — **Krassimir Petrov**. **Covers:** the role of fundamental analysis, fundamental/valuation concepts, and introductory macro-investment analysis. **Exam use:** the lecture format supports explanatory answers linking macro factors to fundamental valuation.

**NOT COVERED:** global versus domestic macro analysis in detail; business-cycle phases and industry analysis; full company analysis/stock valuation; technical analysis; active/passive equity strategies; value versus growth strategy comparison; style analysis; asset-allocation strategies; portfolio-manager performance evaluation. Search: `business cycle industry analysis stock valuation investment lecture`; `technical analysis chart indicators lecture exam`; `active passive equity portfolio management value growth style analysis asset allocation lecture`; `portfolio manager performance evaluation attribution lecture`.

### 9. Bond analysis and portfolio management

- [Bond Valuation & Interest Rate Risk | Chapter 3 – Principles of Corporate Finance (14th)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WjrsdDa9wQk) — **Last Minute Lecture**. **Covers:** bond pricing/valuation and interest-rate risk (bond-price sensitivity); general corporate-finance bond context. **Exam use:** chapter-summary format is a focused revision aid for price–rate relationships and valuation concepts.
- [Money Market Instruments Explained | T-Bills, CDs, Repos & Federal Funds](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=z9A9JJujEuY) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** T-bill pricing, a short-security valuation example relevant to money-market instruments, not the full bond unit. **Exam use:** supplies a practice calculation for discount-security pricing.

**NOT COVERED:** bond characteristics/types and ratings; yield calculations in depth; determinants of bond interest rates; yield-curve/future-rate theories; complete interest-rate-risk management; passive, active, core-plus, matched-funding and contingent/structured portfolio strategies. Search: `bond yield ratings determinants interest rates yield curve term structure theories lecture`; `bond portfolio passive active core plus matched funding contingent structured strategies lecture`; `duration immunization bond interest rate risk worked problems`.

### 10. Derivative security analysis

- [Black Scholes Model and Put Call Parity](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kK1oPxOeu7c) — **IFT**. **Covers:** Black–Scholes option pricing and put–call parity. **Exam use:** focuses on two named valuation relationships likely to appear in formula-based questions.
- [Introduction to Derivatives - Futures & Forwards - Revision Class1](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t7tJpp3f2Rs) — **Patrick Boyle**. **Covers:** revision lesson on forward and futures contracts (general, non-Bangladesh jurisdiction). **Exam use:** a dedicated revision slideshow helps compare contract mechanics and prepare concise definitions; use a separate worked example for pricing/hedging calculations.

**NOT COVERED:** derivative-market overview; option-contract details/call-put payoffs and expiration values; option strategies; binomial model; fuller option-trading/valuation practice; detailed forward/futures pricing, trading, and hedging examples; FX, stock-index, interest-rate and commodity futures as separate applications; swaps; warrants; convertible securities; other embedded derivatives. Search: `option payoff expiration call put strategies binomial tree worked problems`; `forward futures pricing hedging worked examples FX stock index interest rate commodity futures`; `swaps pricing valuation lecture warrants convertible securities embedded derivatives`.

## Coverage audit

- **Topic count:** 10 numbered detailed-content units (4 in Part A; 6 in Part B); the outline retains the syllabus subtopics within each unit.
- **Verified video count:** 13 unique YouTube watch links. Each watch page was opened; the displayed title, uploader/channel and relevance were checked. No search-result snippet is being presented as watch-page verification.
- **Coverage:** **No, not every syllabus subtopic has a verified suitable lesson in this map.** The NOT COVERED notes enumerate the gaps within every affected unit and give targeted YouTube search terms. Bangladesh-specific regulatory/law content is especially sparse; general/U.S.-based lessons are flagged and should not be used to learn Bangladesh law. No Bangladesh statute is named in the course’s detailed contents, but BSEC/DSE/CSE/CDBL/CCBL are explicitly named.

# 4103 Public Sector and NGO Accounting

**Semester:** Semester 7 — Fourth Year, First Semester (11th-batch AIS curriculum; 3 credits)

Source: University of Barishal BBA AIS syllabus, 11th-batch course list (PDF p. 35) and *Detailed Syllabus* (PDF pp. 110–111). The outline below retains the syllabus’s wording and named Bangladesh institutions, laws, standards, and systems.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Constitutional and Legal Framework of Government Accounting Practice in Bangladesh [ILO 1].** Historical evolution of public sector accounting in Bangladesh; relationship between central and local government; relevant constitutional provisions (Section 84(1 & 2)); General Financial Rules; Treasury Rules and Subsidiary Rules; forms and manners of government accounting—Account Code and other divisional account codes.

2. **Role of Central Bank in Treasury and Cash Management [ILO 2].** Relationships among Bangladesh Bank, Sonali Bank, and Accounts Offices; role of the Central Bank in Treasury Functions and Cash Management; cash management, forecasting, and reporting.

3. **Budget and Accounting Classification System (BACS) [ILO 3].** Features of Chart of Accounts (COA); introduction to BACS; structure of the Classification Chart in BACS; 13-digit Classification Structure and its limitation; new Classification Structure; benefits of new classification; levels of classification and their significance; application of BACS; Volume-1, 2, 3 & 4 of Account Code.

4. **Integrated Financial Management Information System (IFMIS) [ILO 4].** Information to IFMIS; features of a standard IFMIS; iBAS++ as an IFMIS (Budget Preparation Module, Budget Execution Module, Accounting Module, General Ledger); integration of data/information using iBAS++ from Accounts Offices; integration among Bangladesh Bank, Sonali Bank, Economic Relations Division (ERD), NBR, and National Savings Directorate; flow of accounting data among government offices.

5. **Controller General of Accounts [ILO 5].** Objectives and activities of CGA Office; organogram of CGA Office; introduction to Finance Accounts and Appropriation Accounts.

6. **Preparation of Annual Accounts [ILO 6].** Basis of Accounting; preparation of Consolidated Fund; classification of Consolidated Fund; Public Account of the Republic; classification of Public Account of the Republic; forms and manner of keeping Public Accounts; Appropriation Accounts; Appropriation Accounts by Civil Department, Defense Services, Bangladesh Post Office, and Bangladesh Railway; new Appropriation Account format from 2017–18; preparation of Finance Account.

7. **Implementation of IPSAS in Bangladesh [ILO 12].** International Public Sector Accounting Standards Board (IPSASB); brief history of IPSASB; IPSASB activities; introduction to IPSASs; benefits and criticisms; need for harmonization around the world; introduction to Cash Basis IPSAS; introduction to Accrual Basis IPAS; implementation status of IPSAS in Bangladesh; major challenges of IPSASs in Bangladesh.

8. **Supreme Audit Institution (SAI) of Bangladesh [ILO 7].** Institutional models of Supreme Audit Institutions; Comptroller and Auditor General (C&AG) of Bangladesh; authorities and responsibilities of C&AG; audits of SAI; International Public Sector Accounting Standards (IPSAS); International Organization of Supreme Audit Institutions (INTOSAI); International Standards of Supreme Audit Institutions (ISSAIS); Asian Association of Supreme Audit Institutions (ASOSAI); Public Expenditure and Financial Accountability (PEFA); public access to the information of PFM.

9. **Management and Execution of Public Expenditure [ILO 9].** Legal framework of public procurement—salient features of PPA 2006 and PPR 2008; Budgeting and Accounting Classification System (BACS); Integrated Budgetary and Accounting System (iBAS++); Supplementary Rules (SR); management of public procurement—key risks in public procurement and mitigation; procurement management under Public Private Partnership (PPP); Public Procurement Reform Project II (PPRP-II); Digitizing Implementation Monitoring and Public Procurement Project (DIMAPPP).

11. **Accounting for NGOs.** Nature and accounting procedures of NGOs.

## Topic-to-video map

Every link below is a direct YouTube watch page opened during research; its displayed title and channel were checked on that page. Jurisdiction warnings are included where the lesson is not Bangladesh-specific.

### Bangladesh-specific procurement and government system

- [Annual Procurement Plan (APP) - বার্ষিক ক্রয় পরিকল্পনা | PPR 2008](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=R6ec2oly_T8) — **Technophile**. **Coverage:** Unit 9’s APP/planning application of PPA 2006 and PPR 2008: the page description says it explains APP rules and acts, guidelines, and an example for manual tender under those laws. **Exam use:** Its worked planning example can support a written answer on how procurement planning applies the named Bangladesh rules. It does not establish coverage of every PPA/PPR provision or the other procurement topics listed below.

- [Form 8 Entry | BC-1 | Budget Preparation | iBAS++](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dvNVHY4uBZk) — **iBAS++**. **Coverage:** Unit 4, practical budget-preparation workflow within iBAS++; it is an official-channel tutorial showing Form 8 entry/BC-1. **Exam use:** A concrete on-screen process helps distinguish the budget-preparation function from the system’s other modules. It does not demonstrate all iBAS++ modules or the named agency integrations/data flow.

### Public-sector accounting concepts and statements — not Bangladesh-specific

- [Public Sector Accounting: Chart of Accounts, Treasury Single Account & Public Expenditure](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4ZyLYm9XmnI) — **Pinnacle Professional College**. **Coverage:** Unit 2’s general treasury/cash-management concepts (Treasury Single Account) and Unit 3’s general Chart of Accounts concept; the lesson also provides a public-expenditure overview relevant to Unit 9. **Exam use:** The lecture’s named concepts help build short-answer definitions and conceptual links between treasury, account classification, and expenditure. **Jurisdiction:** It is not a Bangladesh-specific explanation; it does not evidence the Bangladesh Bank–Sonali Bank–Accounts Offices arrangement or Bangladesh’s BACS particulars.

- [Budget With National Chart of Accounts (Budgeting & Budgetary Control): Public Sector Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=G69JReum-Qc) — **EZIKAN ACADEMY**. **Coverage:** Unit 3’s general national Chart of Accounts and budgeting/budgetary-control concepts. **Exam use:** The extended lecture offers conceptual material for explaining how classifications support a public budget. **Jurisdiction:** The watch page associates the lesson with ICAN Office Annex, not Bangladesh; use for concepts only, not Bangladesh’s 13-digit/new structures, account-code volumes, or laws.

- [Cash Basis IPSAS Financial Statements (Statement of Assets & Liabilities)- Public Sector Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gPYAUGtCn4Y) — **EZIKAN ACADEMY**. **Coverage:** Unit 6’s general basis/annual-statement preparation concepts and Unit 7’s Cash Basis IPSAS; the page description specifically says it explains the statement of assets and liabilities and consolidated recurrent and capital accounts, including recurrent revenue/expenditure and capital receipts/expenditure. **Exam use:** The described statement components give useful structure for written answers and practice preparing cash-basis public-sector statements. **Jurisdiction:** The lesson is under an ICAN Centre context, not Bangladesh; do not substitute its format for Bangladesh’s Consolidated Fund/Public Account or local account-code rules.

- [Public Sector Accounting| PMG Accounts| Exchequer Accounts | Appropriation Account| GAV|A.I.A](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mUS0T85uujA) — **ERICKSON Lectures**. **Coverage:** Unit 6’s general preparation of Appropriation Accounts (the page description identifies preparation of major accounts, including the Appropriation Account). **Exam use:** Its account-preparation demonstration is useful for learning the structure and steps of an appropriation-account answer. **Jurisdiction:** Not presented as Bangladesh-specific; it cannot verify the Bangladesh departments/services or the 2017–18 format.

- [Introduction to International Public Sector Accounting Standards (IPSAS)webinar](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jFOmijy41AY) — **CIPFA**. **Coverage:** Unit 7’s introductory IPSAS topic, as identified by the title. **Exam use:** A professional-body webinar is a suitable starting point for framing an IPSAS definition/overview answer. The watch page has no description, so coverage beyond the IPSAS introduction is not asserted; it does not establish Bangladesh implementation status.

### NGO accounting

- [Nonprofit Accounting Overview for Accountants (Webinar)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pDenzG5IBHI) — **Aplos**. **Coverage:** Unit 11’s general nature and accounting-procedure overview for nonprofit/NGO accounting; the page describes it as a multi-day accounting course for nonprofits. **Exam use:** The overview is a useful framework for explaining why nonprofit accounting procedures differ from ordinary commercial bookkeeping. It is a vendor-led nonprofit lesson, not Bangladesh NGO regulation or Bangladesh-specific reporting requirements.

**Syllabus discrepancy:** The detailed contents jump from item 9 to item 11; no item 10 is printed. Unit 7 prints “Accrual Basis IPAS,” while its heading and the ILO use “IPSAS”; this outline preserves the printed item and treats “IPAS” as an apparent typo for IPSAS. The detailed heading and course list both identify 4103, and the 11th-batch course list places it in Fourth Year, First Semester (Semester 7).

# 4104 Supply Chain and Operation Management

**Semester:** Fourth Year, First Semester (Semester 7)  
**Curriculum:** University of Barishal, BBA Accounting & Information Systems, effective from 11th batch (2022–2023).

The curriculum course table and the Detailed Syllabus both identify course 4104 as “Supply Chain and Operation Management”; the code and title match. The singular “Operation” is the syllabus wording. No material code/title discrepancy was found for this course. The outline below transcribes the Detailed Course Contents, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Operations and Supply Chain Management:** Operations Management; Supply Chain Management; important trends such as agility; information technologies; people.
2. **Operations and Supply Chain Strategies:** Elements of the Business; Strategy; Operations and Supply Chain Strategies; Customer Value; Performance Dimensions; Order Winners and Order Qualifiers; Stages of Alignment with the Business Strategy; Core Competencies in Operations and Supply Chain.
3. **Process Choice and Layout Decisions in Manufacturing and Services:** Manufacturing Processes; Product Customization within the Supply Chain; Service Processes; Layout decision models.
4. **Business Processes:** Business Processes; Mapping Business Process; Managing and Improving Business Processes; Business Process Challenges and the SCOR Model.
5. **Managing Quality and Capacity:** Quality defined; total cost of quality; Total Quality Management (TQM); Six Sigma; statistical quality control; managing quality across the supply chain; capacity; measures of capacity; factors affecting capacity; supply chain considerations; capacity strategies; methods of evaluating capacity alternatives; process capacity (The Theory of Constraints, Waiting Line Theory and Little’s Law).
6. **Supply Management and Logistics:** Why supply management is critical; the strategic sourcing process; the procure-to-pay cycle; sustainable supply; supply chain disruptions; why logistics is critical; logistics decision areas; logistics strategy; logistics decision models.
7. **Forecasting:** Forecast types; laws of forecasting; selecting a forecasting model; qualitative forecasting methods; time-series forecasting models; causal forecasting models; measures of forecast accuracy; Collaborative Planning, Forecasting, and Replenishment (CPFR).
8. **Sales and Operations Planning:** Sales and Operations Planning (S&OP) in the planning cycle; major approaches to S&OP; organizing for and implementing S&OP; services considerations; linking S&OP throughout the supply chain; applying optimization modeling to S&OP.
9. **Managing Inventory and Production:** The role of inventory; inventory types; inventory drivers; independent versus dependent demand inventory; periodic review systems; restocking levels; continuous review systems; The Economic Order Quantity (EOQ); reorder points and safety stock; quantity discounts; single-period inventory system; the Bullwhip effect; inventory positioning; transportation, packaging, and material handling considerations; master scheduling; Material Requirements Planning (MRP); production activity control and vendor order management system; job sequencing; monitoring and tracking technologies; synchronizing planning and control across the supply chain; distribution requirements planning.
10. **Supply Chain Information Systems:** Understanding supply chain information needs; supply chain information systems; BPM tools; cloud computing; Internet of Things (IoT).
11. **JIT/Lean Production:** The lean perspective on waste and inventory; recent developments in lean thinking; Kanban systems; controlling inventory levels using Kanban; synchronizing the supply chain using Kanban; using MRP and Kanban together.
12. **Quantitative Techniques in SCM:** Linear Programming model formulation; optimization using graphical method; Simplex method; Dual Simplex Method; Transportation Problem; PERT and CPM; Decision Tree Analysis.

- [Materials Requirements Planning (MRP) Explained — Dependent Demand in Operations](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GXb9D-k-0rM) — **Operations & Supply Chain Management University**. Explains MRP and dependent demand, with the description pointing to lot sizing, BOM, MPS, and system integration; helpful for explaining MRP logic and planning inputs. **Watch page verified.**
- **NOT COVERED:** Role, types, and drivers of inventory; independent versus dependent-demand distinctions beyond MRP; periodic and continuous review systems; EOQ; quantity discounts; single-period inventory; Bullwhip effect; inventory positioning; transportation, packaging, material handling; master scheduling; production activity control; vendor order management; job sequencing; monitoring/tracking technologies; synchronizing planning/control; distribution requirements planning. Search YouTube: `inventory management EOQ quantity discount single period bullwhip master scheduling production activity control job sequencing DRP worked problems`.

### 10. Supply Chain Information Systems

- **NOT COVERED:** Understanding supply-chain information needs; supply-chain information systems; BPM tools; cloud computing; IoT. Search YouTube: `supply chain information systems BPM tools cloud computing IoT lecture`.

### 11. JIT/Lean Production

- [Lecture 14 JIT Lean Maintenance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3SugEuEwJAo) — **Eddy Witzel**. The course lecture page identifies Just-in-Time, Lean Operations, and maintenance; useful for an exam overview of lean/JIT and its operational purpose. **Watch page verified.**
- **NOT COVERED:** Recent developments in lean thinking; Kanban systems; controlling inventory with Kanban; synchronizing the supply chain using Kanban; using MRP and Kanban together. Search YouTube: `Kanban inventory control supply chain synchronization MRP Kanban together lean lecture`.

Works through linear programming by the graphical method; useful for practicing feasible-region/optimal-solution steps. **Watch page verified.**

Solves a maximization LPP with the Simplex Method; useful for following tableau iterations in an exam-style worked problem. **Watch page verified.**

- **Syllabus topic count:** 12 numbered Detailed Course Contents sections.

# 4105 IT Governance and Information System Audit

**Semester:** Semester 7 — Fourth Year, First Semester  
**Source scope:** University of Barishal, AIS 11th-batch curriculum, *Detailed Course Contents* (PDF pp. 35–36; printed pp. 35–36). The earlier Management Studies curriculum is not used. The semester course-list row wraps “Audit” onto the next line; the detailed heading gives the full title under code 4105, so this is a layout wrap, not a conflicting course code/title.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

This outline preserves the detailed-syllabus terms, with compound terms split where the syllabus names distinct concepts. The coverage audit counts **118 listed terms/skills**, counting repeated items in different syllabus units separately.

1. **IT Audit Fundamentals**
   - **1.1 Information Technology Environment and IT Audit:** IT environment; ERP; cloud computing; blockchain; fintech technologies; artificial-intelligence technologies; DSS; definition of IT audit; audit objectives; need for audit; IT audit functions; internal IT audit; external IT audit; role of IT auditors; IT-auditor profile (experience and skill); career opportunities.
   - **1.2 Internal and External IT Audit:** building an effective internal IT-audit function; benefits of internal IT auditing; internal IT-audit challenges; external IT-audit process.
   - **1.3 Legal, Ethical, and Social Issues in Information Systems:** IT crimes; cyberattacks; security legislation; privacy legislation; information security; IT policies; standards and procedures; relevant laws, regulations, and industry standards affecting the organization; impact of ethical and social issues in information systems; National IT Policy 2009; Information and Communication Technology Act 2006; SOX 2002.
2. **IS Auditing Standards and Guidelines:** ISACA Code of Professional Ethics; ISACA IS Auditing Standards Framework; ISACA IS Auditing Guidelines; ISACA IS Auditing Procedures; relationship among standards, guidelines, and procedures; Information Technology Assurance Framework (ITAF™).
3. **IT Governance and Strategy:** alignment of IT with business objectives; COSO; ITIL; COBIT; ISO 27001; ISO/IEC 27002; joint framework; IT performance metrics; IT Balanced Scorecard; regulatory compliance; internal controls; IT strategy; IT Steering Committee; communication; operational planning.
4. **Risk Management and Internal Controls**
   - **4.1 Risk Management:** risk-management basics; qualitative risk analysis; quantitative risk analysis; enterprise risk management; risk assessment; IT risk assessment; guidelines; IT risk-management life cycle.
   - **4.2 Application Systems: Risks and Controls:** application-system risks; end-user-development application risks; risks to systems exchanging electronic business information; web-application risks; application controls—input, processing, output; IT auditor’s involvement.
   - **4.3 Change Control Management:** importance of a change-control system; change-control-management process; change-control-management procedures; configuration management; organizational change management.
5. **The Information System Audit Process, Tools, and Techniques**
   - **5.1 IT Audit Process:** audit universe; COBIT; risk assessment; audit plan; design audit procedure; test controls; substantive tests; document results; communications; auditing entity-level controls; auditing operating systems; storage; applications; cloud computing; outsourced operations; company projects; data center; disaster-recovery plan; communicating audit results; other IT audits.
   - **5.2 Tools and Techniques used in IT Audit:** audit productivity tools; system-documentation techniques; flowcharting; CAAT for sampling; CAAT for application review; CAAT for auditing application control; CAAT for operational review; computer-forensic tools.
6. **System Development and Implementation**
   - **6.1 System Development Lifecycle:** system development life cycle (SDLC); planning; system analysis and requirements; system design; development; testing; implementation; additional risks and associated controls related to the SDLC phases; approaches to system development (traditional, prototyping, and end-user approaches, as also specified in the ILOs); IT auditor’s involvement in system development and implementation.
   - **6.2 Systems Acquisition, Service Management, and Outsourcing:** systems-acquisition strategy; systems-acquisition process; service management; outsourcing IT systems; IT-audit involvement.

Video 7 is ERM, not a complete risk-analysis lesson; video 11 verifies change-control testing only.
- **Unit 5:** audit universe; COBIT as an audit-planning method; detailed audit-plan contents; design of audit procedures; control tests and substantive tests as distinct techniques; document results; communications; entity-level controls; audits of operating systems, storage, and applications; cloud and outsourced operations; company projects; data centers; disaster-recovery plans; other IT audits; productivity tools; system-documentation techniques; flowcharting; each CAAT subtype (sampling, application review, application-control audit, operational review); computer-forensic tools. Videos 8–9 are broad process-review lessons and video 12 is CAAT-general only.
- **Unit 6:** SDLC planning, requirements analysis, system design, development, testing, implementation; phase-specific risks/controls; traditional, prototyping, and end-user development approaches; IT-auditor involvement through SDLC; detailed acquisition strategy/process; service management; IT outsourcing; and IT-audit involvement in service/outsourcing decisions. Video 10 establishes general CISA acquisition/development relevance, not every listed phase/subtopic.
