# Semester 8 — Fourth Year, Second Semester

# 4201 Accounting Theory — Semester 8 (Fourth Year, Second Semester)

Source: University of Barishal, Department of Accounting & Information Systems, **11th-batch BBA curriculum**, *Detailed Syllabus* section. The course is matched by its heading and semester in that section, not the earlier Management Studies curriculum.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Introduction to Accounting Theory** — Definition of theory; importance of studying accounting theory; overview of accounting theories: pragmatic, normative, and positive; theory-development process; alternative theoretical paradigms: positivism, interpretivism, and critical.
2. **Accounting Regulation** — Definition of accounting regulation; accounting regulatory environment; arguments against regulation under the free-market perspective: private agency, market for manager, market for corporate takeover, and market for lemon; arguments for regulation; theories of regulation: public interest, regulatory capture, and private interest; application of regulation theories to accounting practice; accounting regulation as an output of a political process.
3. **International Accounting** — Differences in accounting across nations; harmonization and standardization; pros and cons of standardization; globalization and the IASB’s role in standardization; obstacles to standardization.
4. **Measurement Issues: Accounting for the Effects of Changing Prices and Market Conditions** — Meaning of measurement; pros and cons of commonly used measurement bases; factors in selecting among alternative measurement bases; explanation and evaluation of normative theories for accounting for changing prices; fair-value accounting’s advantages and disadvantages, including its procyclicality.
5. **Normative Theories of Accounting: The Case of the Conceptual Framework Project** — Meaning, benefits, and criticisms of conceptual frameworks; pros and cons of the current IASB Conceptual Framework versus prior frameworks; meaning of accountability and its relevance to and relationship with conceptual frameworks; decision-usefulness and stewardship functions of financial reporting and their influence on current selection among competing measurement approaches.
6. **Positive Accounting Theory (PAT)** — Definition, origins, and development; agency theory and its relevance to PAT; efficiency versus opportunistic perspectives on accounting-method selection; accounting-based manager bonus schemes (Bonus Plan Hypothesis); accounting-based contracts addressing manager–creditor conflicts (Debt Covenant Hypothesis); political costs and accounting choice (Political Cost Hypothesis); criticisms of PAT; relevance to IASB and FASB efforts to determine “best” measurement approaches. The detailed syllabus also names **Transaction Cost Theory; Oliver Williamson’s Market and Hierarchy Theory; Kenneth Arrow’s General Impossibility Theorem; Agency Conflict Type I and Type II; Ostrom’s Common Pool Resource Governance theory; and the Coase Theorem—private negotiation and governance outside the court system**.
7. **Sustainable Accounting/Corporate Narratives and System-Oriented Theories** — Definition and issues of sustainable accounting; regulatory efforts; system theories for understanding sustainable accounting; social contract and corporate sustainability disclosures; legitimacy theory (legitimacy, legitimacy gap, legitimation process); impression-management approaches and their relationship to corporate legitimacy and disclosures; stakeholder theory (managerial versus normative branches); media agenda-setting theory and its relationship to media, corporate legitimacy, and disclosures; institutional theory (Old Institutional Economics and New Institutional Sociology, isomorphism, decoupling); integrated reporting and ESG reporting.
8. **Accounting and Capital Markets** — Fama’s Efficient Market Hypothesis (EMH) and tests of market efficiency concerning accounting information; effects of earnings announcements on stock-price level and variability; association between accounting performance measures and stock returns; implications for capital-markets research when key market-efficiency assumptions are relaxed; Robert Shiller’s criticism of EMH (“Irrational Exuberance”).
9. **Accounting and Behavioral Theories** — Full-rationality assumption and criticism; bounded rationality; criticism of expected utility theory; Kahneman and Tversky’s decision-making under risk and Prospect Theory; Prospect Theory assumptions: utility derived from a reference point, loss aversion, diminishing sensitivity to gains and losses, and probability weighting; Thaler’s Mental Accounting Theory.
10. **Critical Perspectives of Accounting** — Definition of critical accounting; possible impact of critical-accounting research on social practice; role of state and society in accounting practice; social theories used in critical accounting, including ideas of Marx, Foucault, Bourdieu, Gramsci, and Latour.

**Syllabus discrepancies worth noting.** The ILO list includes ILO 15 on governance theories, but the detailed topics jump from ILO 14 (unit 8) to ILO 16 (unit 9); there is no separate detailed governance unit. ILO 12 mentions creative accounting, but the detailed contents do not specify creative-accounting subtopics beyond placing ILO 12 alongside PAT. The course heading itself reads **4201: Accounting Theory** under **FOURTH YEAR SECOND SEMESTER**. The detailed course content lists no Bangladesh-specific tax, VAT, or statutory-law module.

## Topic-to-video map

Only YouTube watch pages actually opened during this research are listed as verified. Coverage below is deliberately limited to what the page’s title, description, or lesson framing supports; a relevant title alone is not treated as proof that every syllabus subtopic is taught.

### 1. Introduction to Accounting Theory

- [🎓 Approaches to Accounting Theory Explained | Descriptive, Normative & Positive Approaches](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CAtPzyX7DmY) — **Setindiabiz**. **Covers:** overview of normative and positive approaches (the title also identifies descriptive approaches). **Exam value:** concise contrast of major approaches can help structure definition-and-comparison answers. It does not establish coverage of pragmatic theory, the theory-development process, or the positivist/interpretivist/critical paradigms.
- **NOT COVERED:** definition and importance of accounting theory; pragmatic theory; theory-development process; positivism, interpretivism, and critical paradigms. Search YouTube: `accounting theory definition importance pragmatic normative positive theory development positivism interpretivism critical lecture`.

### 2. Accounting Regulation

- [Public Interest Theory in Accounting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BfKLbhFCiHs) — **Tetracarbon (Phillip Wong)**. **Covers:** public-interest theory, a rationale for accounting regulation. **Exam value:** a focused theory lesson supports a concise written explanation of why regulation may serve the public interest.
- **NOT COVERED:** regulatory definition/environment; the full pro- and anti-regulation arguments; the free-market mechanisms (private agency, market for manager, takeover market, and market for lemon); regulatory capture and private-interest theory; applying the theories to accounting practice; political-process explanation. Search YouTube: `accounting regulation free market perspective private agency market for managers takeovers lemon market capture private interest political process lecture`.

### 3. International Accounting

- [Reasons For Accounting Diversity | International Accounting Course](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mczb_NZazrc) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** reasons for differences across national accounting practices, including legal systems, taxation, financing, inflation, political/economic ties, and culture. **Exam value:** its cross-country factors give a clear framework for comparative short answers.
- [International Accounting Course | Harmonization | International Accounting Standard Committee |IASC](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=haLA-2_ZRjk) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** harmonization, historical efforts to reduce cross-border diversity, challenges, IASC-to-IASB evolution, and the IASB’s role. **Exam value:** its historical narrative helps organize an answer on harmonization and international standard-setting.
- **NOT COVERED:** a direct, balanced account of the pros and cons of accounting standardization and a separate treatment distinguishing standardization from harmonization. Search YouTube: `accounting standardization versus harmonization advantages disadvantages obstacles IASB exam lecture`.

### 4. Measurement Issues: Changing Prices, Market Conditions, and Fair Value

- [Accounting in Three Minutes: Fair Value](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2tHXItejIM4) — **GAAP Dynamics**. **Covers:** introductory explanation of fair-value accounting. **Exam value:** a short concept refresher helps define fair value before writing an evaluative answer. This is not a full worked treatment of measurement theory.
- **NOT COVERED:** normative theories for accounting for changing prices; common alternative measurement bases and their respective pros/cons; criteria for choosing a basis; fair-value advantages/disadvantages and procyclicality. Search YouTube: `accounting theory measurement bases changing prices normative theories fair value advantages disadvantages procyclicality lecture`.

### 5. Conceptual Framework Project

- [The Conceptual Framework for Financial Reporting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F4A9UGI9Guk) — **Kaplan UK**. **Covers:** why a framework is needed, its purpose, financial-reporting objectives, qualitative characteristics, elements, recognition, measurement, and presentation/disclosure. **Exam value:** the ordered lesson provides a strong outline for explaining the framework and how it guides reporting choices.
- **NOT COVERED:** benefits and criticisms as an explicit evaluation; current IASB versus prior frameworks; accountability and its relationship to the framework; a focused comparison of stewardship versus decision-usefulness and how those functions influence competing measurement approaches. Search YouTube: `IASB conceptual framework criticism prior frameworks accountability stewardship decision usefulness measurement accounting theory lecture`.

### 6. Positive Accounting Theory and Related Theories

- [Positive Accounting Theory & Agency Theory](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J3mh4XO0QMM) — **Tetracarbon (Phillip Wong)**. **Covers:** the named connection between PAT and agency theory. **Exam value:** the focused pairing is useful for framing agency relationships before explaining PAT’s explanatory approach.
- [ACC518 - Positive Accounting Theory](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VsMnvH2O6Y0) — **Carmen Quade**. **Covers:** a dedicated Positive Accounting Theory lesson. **Exam value:** an additional course lecture is useful for revising PAT’s basic framing alongside the agency-theory lesson.
- [What is transaction cost theory?](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=K_FRm1yNcAU) — **FNEGE Médias**. **Covers:** Oliver Williamson’s transaction-cost theory and how governance choice/internalization relates to transaction costs. **Exam value:** the governance-choice explanation helps turn this named theory into a compare-and-explain answer.
- **NOT COVERED:** proven coverage of PAT’s origins/development; efficiency versus opportunistic perspectives; the Bonus Plan, Debt Covenant, and Political Cost hypotheses; PAT criticisms and its relevance to IASB/FASB measurement; Williamson’s named market-and-hierarchy formulation in full; Arrow’s General Impossibility Theorem; Agency Conflict Types I and II; Ostrom’s common-pool governance theory; and Coase’s private-negotiation/outside-court mechanism. Search YouTube: `positive accounting theory bonus plan debt covenant political cost hypotheses criticisms efficiency opportunistic IASB FASB lecture`; `Kenneth Arrow general impossibility theorem accounting agency conflict type I type II`; `Ostrom common pool resource governance accounting theory`; `Coase theorem private negotiation outside court Williamson market hierarchy`.

### 7. Sustainable Accounting, Corporate Narratives, and System-Oriented Theories

- [LM3 - Theories of Sustainability Reporting](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oEuHX1AMArI) — **Dr Neale O’Connor: Asia Tech & Supply Chain**. **Covers:** sustainability-reporting theory and system-oriented analysis; the lesson is presented as an overview of theoretical explanations of sustainability reporting, including stakeholder, legitimacy, and institutional perspectives. **Exam value:** the theory-based framing helps develop written answers on why companies report sustainability information.
- **NOT COVERED:** detailed sustainability-accounting regulatory efforts; social-contract mechanisms; legitimacy-gap/legitimation steps; impression-management approaches; managerial versus normative stakeholder branches; media agenda-setting links; Old Institutional Economics versus New Institutional Sociology; isomorphism and decoupling; and integrated reporting/ESG reporting. Search YouTube: `sustainability accounting legitimacy gap legitimation impression management media agenda setting old institutional economics new institutional sociology isomorphism decoupling integrated reporting ESG lecture`.

### 8. Accounting and Capital Markets

- [📈 Market Efficiency & the Efficient Market Hypothesis — CMA Exam Part 2](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TnUtww8gTrU) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. **Covers:** EMH; how information is reflected in security prices; weak, semi-strong, and strong forms; market-efficiency testing concepts. **Exam value:** the exam-oriented explanation organizes EMH forms and provides a basis for classifying information-based tests.
- **NOT COVERED:** accounting-specific earnings-announcement effects on stock-price level/variability; associations between accounting performance and stock returns; implications of relaxing assumptions in capital-markets research; Robert Shiller’s EMH critique/Irrational Exuberance. ### 9. Accounting and Behavioral Theories

- [What is Prospect Theory? (Easiest Explanation)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wYN1bfOSQSY) — **Helpful Professor Explains!**. **Covers:** Kahneman and Tversky’s Prospect Theory as a challenge to expected-utility predictions; reference-dependent evaluation and loss aversion. **Exam value:** a clear conceptual explanation helps distinguish risk choices under Prospect Theory from expected utility.
- [Lecture 43: Richard Thaler – Mental Accounting, Nudges, Anomalies](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FIejjvO1jdA) — **SWAYAM BHU**. **Covers:** Thaler’s mental-accounting topic. **Exam value:** the dedicated lecture title and university channel make it a direct revision pointer for explaining the named theory.
- **NOT COVERED:** full-rationality assumption and its criticism; bounded rationality; a detailed expected-utility critique; Prospect Theory’s diminishing sensitivity and probability-weighting assumptions. Search YouTube: `critical accounting perspectives social practice state society Marx Foucault Bourdieu Gramsci Latour lecture`.

## Coverage audit

**10 numbered detailed-syllabus topics** were audited. **13 unique YouTube videos** are linked; each listed video’s YouTube watch page was opened to verify the page title, channel, and topical relevance. A relevant video is available for topics 1–9, but not for topic 10; **full subtopic coverage is not achieved**. The explicit NOT COVERED items identify gaps within topics 1–9 as well as the topic-10 gap. No Bangladesh-specific statutory, tax, or VAT instruction is recommended here because none is specified in this course’s detailed contents; several educational channels frame lessons for UK/US professional exams, so use them for general concepts, not as Bangladesh-law guidance.

# 4202 Business Analysis and Valuation

**Semester:** Semester 8 — Fourth Year, Second Semester (3 credits)  
**Curriculum basis:** University of Barishal BBA Accounting & Information Systems, effective from 11th batch (session 2022–2023), *Detailed Syllabus*. The semester table and detailed-course heading both identify this course as 4202. The course-text heading itself is consistent; section 5's printed case-analysis quotation is left unclosed in the source. No named Bangladesh law, tax provision, or accounting standard appears in this course's detailed contents.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

The outline below retains all listed concepts, methods and case/practical components from the detailed syllabus.

1. **Introduction to Financial Statements** — Financial statements and their users; objectives of financial statement analysis; from business activities to financial statements; parts of the financial statements; additional information accompanying financial statements; measurement in the financial statements; influences of the accounting system on information quality; financial statement analysis in an efficient capital market; limitations of financial accounting data for financial statement analysis.

2. **Financial Statement Analysis**
   - **2.1 Horizontal and Vertical Analyses** — Comparative financial statement analysis: year-to-year change analysis, index-number trend analysis, and common-size financial statement analysis.
   - **2.2 Ratio Analysis** — Common sets of financial measures and ratios: liquidity, profitability, activity, and solvency measures/ratios; overall measures/ratios: earnings per share (EPS), price-to-earnings (P/E) ratio, price-to-book (P/B) ratio, dividend-payout ratio, and sustainable growth rate.
   - **2.3 Profitability Analysis** — Analysis of return on common equity; first-level breakdown distinguishing financial and operating activities and the effect of leverage; second-level breakdown of drivers of operating profitability; third-level breakdown of profit-margin drivers, turnover drivers, and borrowing-cost drivers.
   - **2.4** Case analysis on “Financial Statement Analysis.”

3. **Use of Financial Statements in Valuation**
   - **3.1 Multiple Analysis** — Method of comparables and screening of multiples.
   - **3.2 Fundamental Analysis** — The process of fundamental analysis, financial statement analysis, and pro-forma analysis.
   - **3.3 Redefining Financial Statements for Valuation** — Defining net operating assets, net financial obligations, and common stock equity; operating income and net financial expenses; return on common equity and its decomposition between profitability of net operating assets and net borrowing costs.
   - **3.4 The Architecture of Fundamental Analysis** — The valuation model; terminal-value calculation; Capital Asset Pricing Model (CAPM); choice of discount rate.
   - **3.5** Case analysis on “Financial Statements Used in Valuation.”

4. **Cash Accounting, Accrual Accounting and Equity Valuation**
   - **4.1** Cash flows, earnings, and accrual accounting; reconciling earnings with free cash flows; operating accruals and financial accruals; accruals and earnings management.
   - **4.2 Valuation Models** — Modigliani–Miller (MM) approach to valuation and the role of leverage; dividend discount model; earnings capitalization model; residual earnings model; free cash flow model.
   - **4.3** Accrual accounting and the price-to-book ratio; relation between P/B ratio and P/E multiple.
   - **4.4 Residual Earnings Model of Equity Valuation** — Concept and features of the residual earnings model; discounting residual earnings; drivers of residual earnings; using hierarchical financial ratios to forecast drivers of residual earnings; drivers of growth in residual earnings.
   - **4.5** Case analysis on “Cash Accounting, Accrual Accounting and Equity Valuation.”

5. **The Analysis of Growth and Sustainable Earnings** — Equity valuation and growth in earnings; concept of sustainable earnings; core operating profitability; core borrowing costs; growth in core profitability; how P/B ratios and trailing P/E ratios articulate; case analysis on growth and sustainable earnings.

6. **The Analysis of Credit Risk, Return and Valuation** — Financial statement analysis for credit evaluation; financing risk; distinguishing financial leverage from operating leverage; relation between return and leverages; leverages and taxes; monetary policies, interest rates, leverages and their effects on residual earnings; leverages and the residual earnings model; liquidity planning and financial strategy; bankruptcy model; case analysis on credit risk, return and valuation.

7. **Corporate Restructuring** — Mergers and acquisitions (M&A) and corporate restructuring; key players in M&A; M&A strategy development; developing takeover strategies and their impact on corporate governance; selecting a potential acquisition target; M&A and financial analysis; alternative approaches to valuation; negotiations, deal structuring, financing, and regulatory considerations.

8. **Comprehensive Case Analysis** — Business valuation; business forecasting; economic analysis of business; industry analysis; market forecast; impact of information technology; impact of the share market on business.

## Topic-to-video map

Each link below is a direct YouTube watch page that was opened for title/channel and lesson-relevance verification. Coverage is limited to what the watch page title, description, chapters, or stated demonstration support; a related-video recommendation is not treated as coverage.

### 1. Introduction to Financial Statements

- [**FINANCIAL STATEMENTS: all the basics in 8 MINS!**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Fi1wkUczuyk) — **Accounting Stuff**. Covers the basic purpose and three primary statements (balance sheet, income statement, cash flow statement). The short chaptered overview gives a quick recall scaffold for identifying statement parts before written analysis questions.

**NOT COVERED:** users and objectives of analysis; tracing business activities into statements; accompanying annual-report information; measurement choices and accounting-system effects on information quality; efficient-capital-market context; limitations of accounting data. Search YouTube: `financial statement analysis users objectives measurement limitations efficient capital markets lecture`.

### 2. Financial Statement Analysis

- [**Horizontal vs. Vertical Analysis: Formulas & Excel Examples**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5ofJX-gvzP4) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers horizontal and vertical analysis, year-over-year changes, common-size statements and trend-chart interpretation. Its worked formulas and Excel calculations are useful for numerical questions and interpreting changes.
- [**FINANCIAL RATIOS: How to Analyze Financial Statements**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3W_LwpeG8c8) — **Accounting Stuff**. Chapters cover profitability, liquidity, efficiency/activity, leverage/solvency and market ratios, including EPS, P/E and dividend-payout ratio. The formula-by-formula walkthrough is useful for calculation practice and quick interpretation.
- [**Return on Equity (ROE) Explained via Dupont Analysis. What is ROA Leverage Interest and Tax Burden**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bs6wIc9fnJA) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Covers ROE decomposition through profit margin, asset turnover, leverage, interest burden and tax burden, plus ROA and leverage risk. It gives an exam-ready explanation of how ratio drivers and leverage affect common-equity return.

**NOT COVERED:** index-number trend analysis as a specifically calculated method; P/B and sustainable growth rate within the ratio set; the syllabus's full first-/second-/third-level operating-profitability breakdown and borrowing-cost analysis beyond the DuPont components above; the 2.4 case itself. Search YouTube: `index number trend analysis financial statements worked example`, `sustainable growth rate ratio calculation`, `Penman profitability analysis net operating assets borrowing costs case`.

### 3. Use of Financial Statements in Valuation

- [**Business Valuation 101: The Comparables Analysis Method**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nDa-hjtXC3M) — **Eric Andrews**. Demonstrates company valuation by comparable-company/multiples analysis with an example. The worked method helps with explaining and applying a comparable valuation in an exam answer.
- [**Financial Modeling Tutorial | Build a Three-Statement Model in 30 Minutes with CFI CEO Tim Vipond**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UMYDxmiVin4) — **Corporate Finance Institute**. Demonstrates an integrated three-statement financial model in Excel. Its hands-on build is useful for practicing linked statement forecasts and pro-forma-style modeling.
- [**Build a Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) Model in Excel | Step-by-Step Valuation Guide**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CfkFFk-r9aA) — **Corporate Finance Institute**. Demonstrates construction of a complete DCF model in Excel. The step-by-step valuation build is useful for practicing forecast-to-value mechanics and model structure.
- [**CAPM - Capital Asset Pricing Model Explained**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_RBevTla7pA) — **Financial Edge Training**. Explains CAPM and its effect on asset pricing and market risk. This supports a concise written explanation of the CAPM's role in selecting a required return/discount rate.

**NOT COVERED:** systematic screening of multiples; a full fundamental-analysis sequence; formal pro-forma analysis assumptions beyond model-building; explicit reclassification/definition of net operating assets, net financial obligations, common stock equity, operating income, and net financial expenses; decomposition of ROCE into profitability of net operating assets and net borrowing costs; a directly verified terminal-value calculation; the specific valuation case in 3.5. Search YouTube: `Penman reformulated financial statements net operating assets net financial obligations valuation lecture`, `terminal value DCF worked example`, `financial statement analysis valuation case study screening multiples`.

### 4. Cash Accounting, Accrual Accounting and Equity Valuation

- [**A Simple Guide to Cash vs Accrual Accounting**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kINTtyXPGwM) — **Accounting Stuff**. Explains cash-basis versus accrual-basis recognition. The clear examples help distinguish cash receipts/payments from earned revenues/incurred expenses in conceptual answers.
- [**Earnings Quality & Ratio Reliability. CMA Exam**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bluA3ADQnVc) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Discusses earnings quality, recurring core operations versus one-time gains/aggressive accounting, and why accounting methods, inflation and seasonality affect ratio reliability. It gives exam-oriented language for assessing sustainable earnings and the risk of earnings management.
- [**The Dividend Discount Model (DDM): The Black Sheep of Valuation?**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2MqVe2Gj3qU) — **Mergers & Inquisitions / Breaking Into Wall Street**. Teaches the dividend discount model. Its model-specific explanation supports written valuation-method comparisons and DDM application.
- [**Lecture 9B - Valuation 2 - Residual Income Model**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IBFKnEPyys4) — **Dr Matthew Grosse**. Covers the residual-income/discounted-abnormal-earnings model and demonstrates it on Gale Pacific. The worked company example helps with residual-earnings calculations and model application.
- [**Price to Book Ratio P/B Explained with Price to Earnings Ratio P/E ratio**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-C5KPuQCmXg) — **Farhat Lectures. The # 1 CPA & Accounting Courses**. Explains P/B and P/E calculations and their relationship through ROE. This directly supports ratio-comparison questions and the accounting logic linking the multiples.

**NOT COVERED:** earnings/free-cash-flow reconciliation; operating versus financial accruals; accrual-based earnings-management tests; MM valuation and leverage proposition; the earnings-capitalization model; a directly stated free-cash-flow valuation-model lesson; residual-income drivers and hierarchical-ratio forecasting beyond model demonstration; full case analysis in 4.5. Search YouTube: `cash flow earnings reconciliation operating financial accruals earnings management worked example`, `Modigliani Miller valuation leverage lecture`, `earnings capitalization model valuation example`, `residual earnings drivers hierarchical ratios forecasting`.

### 5. The Analysis of Growth and Sustainable Earnings

The earnings-quality lesson mapped under section 4 helps frame sustainable earnings as recurring core performance rather than one-off gains, but it does not cover this section's valuation-growth mechanics. The three-statement modeling lesson under section 3 provides forecasting practice, not a direct lesson on sustainable-earnings analysis.

**NOT COVERED:** core operating profitability and core borrowing-cost calculations; growth in core profitability; articulation of P/B with trailing P/E; the section's case analysis. Search YouTube: `sustainable earnings core operating profitability borrowing costs residual income valuation lecture`, `P/B trailing P/E sustainable earnings worked example`.

### 6. The Analysis of Credit Risk, Return and Valuation

- [**Credit Analysis Fundamentals: How Banks & Lenders Assess Creditworthiness Part 1 | CFI Course**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MoZkeN_eQYo) — **Corporate Finance Institute**. Introduces lender creditworthiness assessment as part of a credit-and-risk course. It is useful for framing credit evaluation, though it is not a Bangladesh-specific lending/regulatory lesson and the watch-page evidence does not establish coverage of the syllabus's specific bankruptcy model.

**NOT COVERED:** a direct lesson distinguishing operating from financial leverage and calculating their effects on returns; financing-risk measures beyond general creditworthiness; leverage and taxes; monetary-policy/interest-rate effects on residual earnings; leverage in the residual-earnings model; liquidity planning and financial strategy; bankruptcy prediction/model; the section's case analysis. Search YouTube: `operating leverage financial leverage return taxes worked example`, `monetary policy interest rates leverage residual earnings model lecture`, `credit risk financial statement analysis bankruptcy model Altman Z score`, `liquidity planning financial strategy credit analysis`.

### 7. Corporate Restructuring

- [**Corporate Finance Explained | Mastering Mergers & Acquisitions**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=S29oX348J7o) — **Corporate Finance Institute**. Covers the M&A lifecycle from valuation and financial modeling through financing strategies and post-merger integration. The lifecycle framing helps organize a short written M&A-process answer and connect valuation to deal financing.

**NOT COVERED:** key M&A players; target screening/selection; takeover strategy and corporate-governance effects; detailed negotiations and deal structuring; regulatory considerations. The verified lesson is broad and does not establish Bangladesh-specific corporate/regulatory law coverage. Search YouTube: `Bangladesh mergers acquisitions corporate governance takeover regulations BSEC`, `M&A target selection takeover defense negotiation deal structure regulatory considerations lecture`.

### 8. Comprehensive Case Analysis

**PARTIALLY COVERED:** the comparables, DCF and residual-income examples linked above offer business-valuation methods, while CFI's three-statement model offers a forecasting exercise. No verified video in this set demonstrates a comprehensive case integrating business valuation, business forecasting, economic and industry analysis, market forecasts, information technology, and share-market effects.

**NOT COVERED:** integrated economic/industry/market forecast case; impact of information technology on business valuation; impact of the share market on business; a full cross-topic case. Search YouTube: `business valuation case study economic industry analysis market forecast technology share market`, `integrated financial statement analysis valuation case study forecast industry`.

## Coverage audit

- **Topic count:** 8 numbered syllabus sections (and 14 numbered sub-sections under sections 2–4); detailed items are retained above.
- **Video count:** 15 unique direct-watch links; no duplicate URLs. Each recommended watch page was opened to verify its visible title and creator/channel and to check lesson relevance.
- **Coverage:** 7 of 8 top-level sections have a verified video tied to at least one syllabus component; section 8 has only component-level valuation/forecasting examples, not an integrated comprehensive case. **Not every syllabus subtopic has a verified direct video.** The explicit NOT COVERED lists identify the gaps and provide targeted search terms.
- **Jurisdiction/standards caveat:** this course's detailed syllabus names no jurisdiction-specific law or accounting standard; the credit/M&A videos are general or international, not Bangladesh-law coverage. The video list does not claim local regulatory alignment.

## Topic-to-video map

Only the subtopics explicitly tagged above are mapped to the videos below. Each direct YouTube watch link was opened in the browser and its displayed title and channel checked. The search results/page descriptions were used to confirm relevance; page-player access itself displayed YouTube’s bot/sign-in interstitial in this session.

- **V1 — [The Research Process From Start to End | First Steps Beginner Guide](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8Lzo3nhcTSk)** — **Dr Amina Yonis**. **Covers:** research-process overview and steps. **Exam use:** useful for organizing a written answer around a beginning-to-end research sequence; it is a general beginner guide, not specifically a business-methods lecture.
- **V2 — [The Research Problem & Problem Statement: Plain-Language Explainer (With Examples) + FREE Templates](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFyaJl9T74g)** — **Grad Coach**. **Covers:** identifying/defining a research problem and developing a problem statement. **Exam use:** worked examples help distinguish a broad concern from a clearly framed problem statement.
- **V3 — [How To Write A Literature Review In 3 Simple Steps (FREE Template With Examples)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lw8HPXJP1VA)** — **Grad Coach**. **Covers:** literature-review meaning and stepwise preparation. **Exam use:** provides a memorable sequence and a practical model for written answers about review procedure.
- **V4 — [Develop a Theoretical Framework in 3 Steps | Scribbr 🎓](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4y1BAqOnhMM)** — **Scribbr**. **Covers:** role of theory and development/importance of a theoretical framework. **Exam use:** a three-step explanation supports a structured answer on why and how the framework is built.
- **V5 — [Hypothesis Development: Concept, Characteristics, Null and Alternate Hypotheses with Examples](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Dcrgjt_RH3c)** — **Research With Fawad**. **Covers:** hypothesis types (including null/alternative and directional/non-directional) and hypothesis development. **Exam use:** definitions and examples aid hypothesis classification and formulation questions.
- **V6 — [Qualitative vs Quantitative vs Mixed Methods Research: How To Choose Research Methodology](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hECPeKv5tPM)** — **Grad Coach**. **Covers:** qualitative/quantitative approaches, their differences, and mixed-methods as a comparison. **Exam use:** a clear comparison helps construct short-note or “differentiate between” answers; it does not cover every qualitative design/technique in the syllabus.
- **V7 — [Sampling Methods 101: Probability & Non-Probability Sampling Explained Simply](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fSmedyVv-Us)** — **Grad Coach**. **Covers:** sampling approach, probability versus non-probability methods, and choosing/justifying an approach. **Exam use:** method-selection examples help explain why a sampling technique fits a research question; sample-size calculation is not covered here.
- **V8 — [Research Design: Choosing your Data Collection Methods | Scribbr 🎓](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=q17s84ADGfA)** — **Scribbr**. **Covers:** selecting common data-collection methods, including survey and observation options. **Exam use:** helps frame a comparison of collection choices and their fit to a study; it is not a detailed lesson on survey errors or observation subtypes.
- **V9 — [How To Design A Questionnaire Or Survey | Research Methods Tutorial](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_zoIVE9PRTM)** — **Dr Hayley Stainton | Tourism Teacher**. **Covers:** basic questionnaire/survey design and construction guidance. **Exam use:** practical design tips and examples support questionnaire-design answers; specialized questionnaire types, sequencing, software and pretesting are not claimed.
- **V10 — [Fundamentals of Qualitative Research Methods: Focus Groups (Module 4)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cCAPz14yjd4)** — **Yale University**. **Covers:** focus-group discussion as a qualitative method and its purpose/trade-offs. **Exam use:** an academic lecture gives a credible conceptual foundation for FGD definitions and advantages/limitations; moderator qualities are not claimed.
- **V11 — [measurement scale, measurement in research, Nominal, Ordinal, Interval, Ratio, research methodology](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MWKntm4oVqg)** — **Management Mantra**. **Covers:** nominal, ordinal, interval and ratio levels of measurement. **Exam use:** helps recall scale distinctions and select an appropriate scale-level explanation; attitude scaling and index construction are not covered by this match.
- **V12 — [Secondary Data Sources for Research](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=F4nXaXfbkyc)** — **RD Tutorials**. **Covers:** secondary-data sources and their advantages/disadvantages. **Exam use:** provides a compact source-comparison framework suitable for short notes and advantages/limitations questions.
- **V13 — [Qualitative Data Analysis 101 Tutorial: 6 Analysis Methods + Examples](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=j9A3ceOBihM)** — **Grad Coach**. **Covers:** approaches/types of qualitative analysis methods, with examples. **Exam use:** comparative method examples help distinguish qualitative analysis approaches; CAQDAS and the syllabus’s deductive/inductive procedures are not claimed.
- **V14 — [Quantitative Data Analysis 101 Tutorial: Descriptive vs Inferential Statistics (With Examples)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EUeQRE5UJpg)** — **Grad Coach**. **Covers:** data-analysis overview and descriptive analysis in contrast with inferential analysis. **Exam use:** examples help distinguish summarizing observed data from making inferences; specific tests and regression diagnostics are not covered by this map.
- **V15 — [How to Write a Good Quality Research Report ? | L - 34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dsH2_whhsvI)** — **Professor Rahul Jain**. **Covers:** characteristics/quality of a research report. **Exam use:** a lesson explicitly about report quality supports a written answer on what makes a report effective; it does not verify every report section or graphic-aid detail.

## Coverage audit

**Topic count:** 125 semicolon-separated syllabus subtopics across the detailed contents (including the repeated report-preparation item numbered “12”). **Every syllabus subtopic is accounted for** in the outline with either a verified-video tag or an explicit **NOT COVERED** marker and targeted YouTube search terms. **Not every subtopic has a verified suitable video**: notable gaps include research ethics/rights, sampling-size calculations, detailed questionnaire/survey operations, measurement/attitude scaling, secondary-data analysis, CAQDAS/content analysis, hypothesis-test procedures, correlation/regression and report formatting/referencing. No Bangladesh-specific law, tax, VAT or regulatory lessons were needed for this course.

# 4204 Strategic Management

**Semester:** Semester 8 — Fourth Year, Second Semester (3 credits). This matches the BBA course-list entry for the 11th-batch AIS curriculum; the detailed-syllabus heading also reads **4204: Strategic Management**. No code/title/semester discrepancy affects this course. Source: attached University of Barishal AIS syllabus, 11th-batch curriculum course list (PDF p. 35) and Detailed Syllabus (PDF pp. 124–125).

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Introduction to Strategy and Strategic Management [ILO 1].** Strategic management; stages of strategic management; Ohmae’s strategic thinking; competitive advantage; strategists; vision and mission; external opportunities and threats; internal strengths and weaknesses; long-term objectives; strategies; deliberate and emergent strategies [Mintzberg]; Mintzberg’s five types of strategies; positioning vs. resource-based views of strategic advantage; annual objectives; policies; strategic management model; benefits of strategic management; guidelines for effective strategic management; business ethics and strategic management; role of stakeholders; stakeholder mapping (power and interest); global competition.

2. **The Business Vision and Mission [ILO 2].** Vision and mission; importance of vision and mission statements; elements of a mission; process of developing a mission statement; characteristics of a mission statement; components of a mission statement; writing and evaluating mission statements.

3. **External Analysis [ILO 3, ILO 4].** Business environment and why managers need to understand it; political, economic, social, cultural, demographic, technological, ecological, legal, and competitive forces; Porter’s five forces model; industry life cycles and their strategic implications; industry segments and strategic groups; product life cycle; international business context; Ohmae’s five Cs; Porter’s competitive advantage of nations; international trade life cycle; limits to global business; External Factor Evaluation (EFE) matrix; Competitive Profile Matrix (CPM).

4. **Internal Analysis [ILO 5].** Analysis of a business’s strategic capability; critical success factors (CSFs) and core competencies; CSFs and competitive advantage; internal assessment of management, marketing, finance/accounting, production/operations, research and development, and management information systems; benchmarking competencies; transforming resources through the value chain; outsourcing value activities; supply chain management; Internal Factor Evaluation (IFE) matrix.

5. **Types of Strategies [ILO 6].** Integration strategies; intensive strategies; diversification strategies; defensive strategies; Porter’s generic strategies (cost leadership, differentiation, and focus); Bowman’s strategic clock; means of achieving strategies: joint venture/partnering and merger/acquisition.

6. **Strategic Analysis and Choice [ILO 6].** Strategy-formulation framework (input, matching, and decision stages); matching-stage SWOT, SPACE, BCG, IE, and GSM matrices; decision-stage QSPM; cultural aspects of strategy choice; politics of strategy choice.

7. **Blue Ocean Strategy [ILO 7].** New market space; continuing creation of blue oceans; impact of creating blue oceans; rising imperative of creating blue oceans; value innovation; red ocean vs. blue ocean strategy; formulating and executing blue ocean strategy; eight principles of Blue Ocean Strategy.

8. **Strategy Implementation and Evaluation [ILO 8].** Nature of strategy implementation; resource allocation; managing conflicts; restructuring and reengineering; linking performance and pay to strategies; managing the natural environment; creating a strategy-supportive culture; production/operations and HRM concerns; marketing issues; finance/accounting issues; research and development issues; management information systems issues; strategy-evaluation framework; balanced scorecard; characteristics of an effective evaluation system; contingency planning.

9. **Case Analysis [ILO 9].** Strategic-management case; guidelines for preparing case analysis; written case analysis; oral presentation.

## Topic-to-video map

Each item below was opened as a direct YouTube watch page. Exact titles and channel names are taken from the watch-page metadata. Links are unique; the coverage notes distinguish page-confirmed lesson scope from related syllabus items that remain unverified.

### 1. Introduction to Strategy and Strategic Management

- [Strategic Management Chapter 1: Introduction, Process, Types of Strategies & Benefits](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cEv70vk3F0U) — **Mella Tutorials**. Covers introductory strategic-management concepts, process/stages, strategy types, and benefits. **Exam value:** the chapter-style lecture is useful for defining the process and structuring short answers on why strategic management matters.

**NOT COVERED by a verified direct lesson here:** Ohmae’s strategic thinking; detailed competitive-advantage concepts; strategists; Mintzberg’s deliberate/emergent strategies and five types; positioning vs. resource-based views; annual objectives and policies; a detailed strategic-management model and effectiveness guidelines; business ethics; stakeholder roles and power-interest mapping; global competition. Search: `strategic management Mintzberg five types deliberate emergent positioning resource based Ohmae stakeholder power interest global competition lecture`.

### 2. The Business Vision and Mission

- [Vision, Mission, Goals and Objectives/ ራእይ፤ ተልእኮ፤ ግብ እና አላማ በተቋማት/ Strategic Management Chapter 2](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sn0Gl87ZvB0) — **Mella Tutorials**. Covers the distinction and purpose of organizational vision, mission, goals, and objectives, including their direction-setting role. **Exam value:** supports concise comparisons and purpose-focused written answers.

**NOT COVERED by a verified direct lesson:** mission-statement elements/components, development process, prescribed characteristics, and step-by-step writing/evaluation. Search: `strategic management mission statement components characteristics development process write evaluate example`.

### 3. External Analysis

- [Environmental Analysis: PESTL, Porter’s 5 Forces & Internal Analysis. Strategic Management Chapter 3](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MftCHcXIlGc) — **Mella Tutorials**. A course-style lesson explicitly covering environmental analysis, PESTL, Porter’s five forces, and internal analysis. The channel’s examples are Ethiopian-context examples, not Bangladesh-specific. **Exam value:** offers a connected scan-to-analysis framework that can help organize external-environment answers.
- [PESTEL Analysis EXPLAINED | B2U | Business To You](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bYn4CyL3r5w) — **Business To You**. Covers Political, Economic, Social, Technological, Environmental, and Legal macro-environment factors. **Exam value:** gives a clear framework for classifying macro-environment factors in an exam response.
- [Porter’s 5 Forces EXPLAINED | B2U | Business To You](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Dfp23xSqpdk) — **Business To You**. Explains the five competitive forces and their relationship to industry attractiveness/profitability, with airline-industry examples. **Exam value:** the example-based explanation helps students apply and justify each force rather than merely list it.
- [Strategy Management: External Factor Evaluation (EFE) Matrix (Video #49)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=K-QUImQQapU) — **Ready MBA**. A dedicated lesson on developing the External Factor Evaluation matrix. **Exam value:** its matrix-building focus is useful for practicing an applied EFE question and explaining how external factors are evaluated.

**NOT COVERED by a verified direct lesson:** industry life cycles and implications; segments/strategic groups; product life cycle; international business context; Ohmae’s five Cs; Porter’s competitive advantage of nations; international trade life cycle; limits to global business; Competitive Profile Matrix (CPM). Search: `strategic management industry life cycle strategic groups product life cycle Ohmae five Cs Porter diamond international trade life cycle CPM matrix worked example`.

### 4. Internal Analysis

- [Environmental Analysis: PESTL, Porter’s 5 Forces & Internal Analysis. Strategic Management Chapter 3](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MftCHcXIlGc) — **Mella Tutorials**. The watch-page title and description confirm a lesson section on internal analysis alongside environmental analysis; it does not establish detailed coverage of every functional area or matrix. **Exam value:** useful as a broad conceptual bridge between examining the external environment and examining the organization internally.

**NOT COVERED by a verified direct lesson:** strategic capability; CSFs/core competencies and their relationship to competitive advantage; assessment of management, marketing, finance/accounting, operations, R&D, and MIS; benchmarking; value-chain analysis and outsourcing value activities; supply-chain management; IFE matrix. Search: `strategic management internal functional analysis core competencies value chain outsourcing supply chain IFE matrix worked example`.

### 5. Types of Strategies

- [Strategy Formulation: Corporate Level Strategy (Growth, Stability, and Defensive Strategy). SM CH-4.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GA_cktUKfvM) — **Mella Tutorials**. Covers corporate-level strategy formulation and identifies growth, stability, and defensive strategies. **Exam value:** the lecture format supports classification and comparison answers on broad corporate strategy choices; examples are Ethiopian-context, not Bangladesh-specific.
- [Strategy Formulation: Business Level (Porter Generic Strategies) and Functional Strategies. SM CH-4.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NvadRLUG70M) — **Mella Tutorials**. Covers business-level and Porter generic strategies, with real-world Ethiopian examples. **Exam value:** helps explain how a firm competes and distinguish business-level positioning from functional strategy.
- [Bowman’s Strategic Clock](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hsV1mraa4AI) — **tutor2u**. A revision lesson outlining the options in Bowman’s strategic-positioning model. **Exam value:** its revision framing helps recall and compare the clock’s strategic-positioning choices.

**NOT COVERED by a verified direct lesson:** the syllabus’s detailed subtypes of integration, intensive, diversification, and defensive strategies; joint ventures/partnering; merger/acquisition. Search: `strategic management integration intensive diversification defensive strategies joint venture merger acquisition examples lecture`.

### 6. Strategic Analysis and Choice

- [Strategic Analysis and Choice (SWOT, BCG, SPACE, Grand Strategy & McKinsey 7s Model)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gyAiESRaOTs) — **Mella Tutorials**. The watch page confirms SWOT, BCG, SPACE, and Grand Strategy matrices/models. **Exam value:** a side-by-side framework lesson helps distinguish which matrix diagnoses position and supports strategy choice; examples are Ethiopian-context.
- [Strategy Management - Qualitative Strategic Planning Matrix QSPM & Other Frameworks (Video #78)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MxaiA75NJEo) — **Ready MBA**. Covers QSPM as a strategy-selection/prioritization tool (the description also mentions SAFe and the Industry Life Cycle Matrix). **Exam value:** supports decision-stage answers about prioritizing alternatives using QSPM.

**NOT COVERED by verified direct lessons:** the input–matching–decision-stage framework as an integrated sequence; IE matrix; cultural and political aspects of strategy choice. Search: `strategic management formulation input matching decision stages IE matrix culture politics strategy choice lecture`.

The lecture description confirms Blue Ocean analytical tools/frameworks and the strategy canvas. **Exam value:** its university-lecture treatment can help explain a Blue Ocean diagnostic tool and how strategy-canvas analysis informs formulation.

**NOT COVERED by a verified direct lesson:** continuing creation and impact of new market spaces; rising imperative; value innovation; explicit red-ocean vs. blue-ocean comparison; end-to-end formulation and execution; the eight principles. # 4205 Data Analytics

**Semester:** Semester 8 — Fourth Year, Second Semester  
**Course code/title:** 4205 — Data Analytics

**Syllabus basis and code note.** Extracted from the attached University of Barishal BBA Accounting & Information Systems syllabus, 11th-batch curriculum, *Detailed Syllabus* section (not the earlier Management Studies curriculum). The course-list table names **4205 Data Analytics (2 credits)** and separately **4206 Lab: Application of Data Analytic Tools (2 credits)**. In the detailed section, the heading **“4205: Data Analytics”** occurs twice: the first contains theory, the second contains the hands-on/lab contents and a 75-mark distribution. The second heading appears to be a code/heading duplication, but because its title matches and it supplies the printed practical skills requested here, its lab contents are transcribed below and identified as such—not treated as a separate course recommendation.

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

1. **Analytics overview:** Descriptive analytics; Predictive Analytics; Prescriptive analysis.
2. **Data Mining concepts:** Data Mining definitions; Characteristics and Benefits; How Data Mining works; Data Mining versus Statistics; Data Mining Applications; Data Mining Process Model (CRISP-DM).
3. **Data Mining Methods:** Classification; Accuracy of Classifying Model; Classification Techniques; Decision Tree analysis; Decision Tree algorithm; Cluster analysis; K-MEANS Clustering algorithm; Association rule analysis; Apriori algorithm.
4. **Regression Modelling for Inferential Statistics:** Linear Regression Model; Multiple Regression Model; Logistic Regression Model; Time Series Analysis.
5. **Social Media Analytics:** Social Network analysis; Social Network analysis metrices [sic]; Social Media analytics; Measuring the Social Media Impact.
6. **Text Analytics and Text Mining:** Overview of Text Analytics and Text Mining; Text Mining Terminology; Natural Language Processing (NLP); NLP Applications; Text Mining Applications.
7. **Practical/lab contents printed beneath the repeated 4205 heading:** work through examples and datasets following instructions and class discussion; Association rule analysis in Python Programming; Decision Tree analysis in Python Programming; Cluster analysis in Python Programming; Descriptive Statistics in Python Programming; business applications demonstrated: fraud detection, credit scoring, loan application, and market-basket analysis.

## Topic-to-video map

Each recommended link below was opened as a direct YouTube watch page to check the displayed title, channel, and syllabus relevance. The links are individual `youtube.com/watch?v=...` lessons, not search-result snippets or channel homepages.

1. [**Descriptive vs Diagnostic vs Predictive vs Prescriptive Analytics: What's the Difference?**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QoEpC7jUb9k) — **Maven Analytics**. **Covers:** descriptive, predictive, and prescriptive analytics (the video also explains diagnostic analytics). **Exam value:** Chris Dutton's clear category comparison helps distinguish what each analytics type answers in a short written response.
2. [**Data Mining Explained | Data Engineering | Community Webinar**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9JJIGOiGbAs) — **Data Science Dojo**. **Covers:** data-mining definition and general business applications/use (business intelligence, planning, and operations). **Exam value:** Its concise definition and business framing help form a direct “what is data mining and why is it used?” answer.
3. [**Intro to Data Science: Understanding CRISP DM**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0kEWGCIQSPk) — **Flatiron School**. **Covers:** Data Mining Process Model (CRISP-DM), including the business-problem framing and process through deployment described on the watch page. **Exam value:** The process-oriented explanation is useful for recalling and writing the CRISP-DM stages in order.
4. [**1. Decision Tree | ID3 Algorithm | Solved Numerical Example | by Mahesh Huddar**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=coOTEc-0OGw) — **Mahesh Huddar**. **Covers:** decision-tree analysis and the ID3 decision-tree algorithm, as an example of classification. **Exam value:** The worked numerical example models how to show tree-building steps and the resulting classification in a problem answer.
5. [**Confusion Matrix Solved Example Accuracy Precision Recall F1 Score Prevalence by Mahesh Huddar**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_CGTbkHwUHQ) — **Mahesh Huddar**. **Covers:** evaluating a classifying model, including accuracy calculated from a confusion matrix (plus other metrics). **Exam value:** It walks through classification-performance calculations, making the accuracy formula and numerator/denominator exam-ready.
6. [**K Means Clustering Algorithm | K Means Solved Numerical Example Euclidean Distance by Mahesh Huddar**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KzJORp8bgqs) — **Mahesh Huddar**. **Covers:** cluster analysis through the K-MEANS clustering algorithm and Euclidean-distance assignment. **Exam value:** Its solved calculations show how to assign points and form clusters, which is more useful for numerical questions than a definition-only video.
7. [**#2 Solved Example Apriori Algorithm to find Strong Association Rules Data Mining Machine Learning**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zi_ydmbWfAs) — **Mahesh Huddar**. **Covers:** association-rule analysis and the Apriori algorithm for finding strong rules. **Exam value:** The worked example supports practice in deriving candidate/frequent itemsets and evaluating association rules.
8. [**🛒 Hands-on Market Basket Analysis in Python 📊**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eQr5fu_7UUY) — **Six Sigma Pro SMART**. **Covers:** hands-on Python market-basket analysis of customer transactions to uncover item associations; this also supports the syllabus's market-basket business application. **Exam value:** The implementation gives a practical workflow to relate the association-rule concept to transactional data and a named business use case.
9. [**Regression Analysis | Full Course**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0m-rs2M7K-Y) — **numiqo**. **Covers:** linear and multiple linear regression models, including interpretation and assumptions. **Exam value:** The lecture distinguishes simple from multiple regression and explains interpretation, useful for conceptual answers and model-result questions.
10. [**Logistic Regression: An Easy and Clear Beginner’s Guide**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ax5kqLHls-I) — **numiqo**. **Covers:** logistic regression as a statistical method for a categorical outcome, including calculation and interpretation. **Exam value:** It gives a clean explanation of when logistic rather than linear regression applies and how to describe the result.
11. [**What is Time Series Analysis?**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GE3JOFwTWVM) — **IBM Technology**. **Covers:** time-series analysis, its basic meaning, and the kinds of analytics/use of results applied to a time series. **Exam value:** IBM's introductory framing helps define the method and explain why ordered observations over time are analyzed; it is not a worked time-series calculation.
12. [**An introduction to Social Network Analysis**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IvQDTM82tSY) — **UK DATA SERVICE**. **Covers:** social network analysis as a computational way to study social systems by visualizing and interrogating relationships among people or groups. **Exam value:** The institutional workshop supplies a concise concept-and-purpose framing for a written SNA answer.
13. [**SOCIAL MEDIA ANALYTICS | Complete Guide to Understand Your Metrics**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aRnK_UQc3wY) — **Metricool**. **Covers:** social-media analytics metrics and how to use them to make decisions; relevant to measuring social-media impact. **Exam value:** Explaining metrics as evidence for decisions helps frame a written answer about assessing campaign/content impact rather than merely listing numbers.
14. [**Natural Language Processing (NLP) & Text Mining Tutorial Using NLTK | NLP Training | Edureka**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=05ONoGfmKvA) — **edureka!**. **Covers:** overview of NLP/text mining and core processing terminology such as tokenization, stemming, and lemmatization. **Exam value:** The named processing steps provide concrete terminology and examples for definitions and short notes on text analytics.
15. [**How to Build Your First Decision Tree in Python (scikit-learn)**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkYpGhsCx4c) — **Ryan & Matt Data Science**. **Covers:** implementing a decision-tree classifier in Python with scikit-learn. **Exam value:** The code walkthrough is useful for recalling the practical sequence of preparing data, fitting the classifier, and using it for prediction.

### Not covered by a directly verified suitable video

- **Data-mining concepts:** characteristics; benefits; how data mining works in detail; and data mining versus statistics. Search YouTube for: `data mining characteristics benefits how it works data mining vs statistics lecture`.
- **Classification techniques:** a comparative treatment of classification methods beyond the decision-tree example above. Search: `classification techniques data mining lecture decision tree naive bayes k nearest neighbors`.
- **Social Network analysis metrices [sic]:** specific SNA measures/metrics. The verified SNA introduction establishes the method, but its watch-page description does not establish coverage of individual measures. Search: `social network analysis metrics degree betweenness closeness centrality lecture`.
- **NLP Applications** and **Text Mining Applications:** the verified Edureka lesson covers NLP/text-mining basics and terminology, but its watch-page information does not establish a sufficient applications lesson. Search: `NLP applications text mining applications business examples lecture`.
- **Python lab:** Cluster analysis in Python; Descriptive Statistics in Python. Search: `K means clustering Python worked tutorial dataset`, `descriptive statistics Python pandas practical tutorial`.
- **Named business cases in the printed practical section:** fraud detection; credit scoring; loan application. (Market-basket analysis is covered by item 8.) Search: `Python fraud detection tutorial`, `Python credit scoring loan application machine learning tutorial`, and `credit scoring loan default decision tree case study`.

**Coverage audit:** 40 atomic syllabus topics/skills counted across theory and the practical contents printed under the repeated course heading; 28 have at least one directly verified, relevant video mapped above, and 12 are explicitly marked **NOT COVERED** with targeted YouTube keywords. No Bangladesh-specific law, tax, VAT, or regulatory topics occur in this course's detailed contents, so no jurisdiction-specific substitution is needed. The lab-title/code duplication is the principal syllabus uncertainty.

# 4206 Lab: Application of Data Analytic Tools

**Semester:** Semester 8 — Fourth Year, Second Semester (2 credits)

## Detailed syllabus topic outline

The 11th-batch AIS detailed syllabus lists a practical lab in which students work through examples and datasets according to instructions and class discussion. Its stated contents are:

1. **Lab work with examples and datasets:** hands-on exercises using different examples and datasets, following instructions and class discussion.
2. **Association rule analysis in Python programming.**
3. **Decision Tree analysis in Python programming.**
4. **Cluster analysis in Python programming.**
5. **Descriptive Statistics in Python programming.**
6. **Business applications to be shown:** fraud detection; credit scoring; loan application; market-basket analysis; and other applications (“etc.” in the syllabus).

No named accounting standards, laws, tax/VAT rules, or regulatory requirements appear in this course’s detailed contents.

> **Syllabus heading discrepancy:** The course roster identifies 4206 as “Lab: Application of Data Analytic Tools” in Fourth Year, Second Semester. The practical-lab contents appear in the detailed syllabus under a repeated heading “4205: Data Analytics” (after the regular 4205 Data Analytics block). I matched the lab content by its practical-tool objectives and the roster title/semester, not by the erroneous repeated code/title.

## Topic-to-video map

1. **Lab examples, datasets, instructions, and class discussion** — [Python Project for Data Analysis- Exploratory Data Analysis | Data Analyst Project](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KgCgpCIOkIs) — **Rishabh Mishra**. The watch page identifies a Python exploratory-data-analysis project and links a downloadable data file; following its dataset-to-analysis workflow is useful practice for guided lab tasks.

2. **Association rule analysis in Python** — [Data Science using Python -- Association Rule Mining (Demo using mlxtend)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0yaYwDyBxFA) — **Sucharita Das**. The Python/mlxtend demonstration gives practical implementation to rehearse and a concrete basis for explaining association-rule analysis in a written answer.

3. **Decision Tree analysis in Python** — [Classification Trees in Python from Start to Finish](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=q90UDEgYqeI) — **StatQuest with Josh Starmer**. This start-to-finish Python lesson links the classification-tree method to its implementation, making it useful both for conceptual revision and practical coding questions.

4. **Cluster analysis in Python** — [K-Means Clustering Algorithm with Python Tutorial](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iNlZ3IU5Ffw) — **Andy McDonald**. The lesson walks through running K-means on a dataset, providing a worked method and implementation to revise for a clustering practical.

5. **Descriptive Statistics in Python** — [1 Descriptive Statistics in Python for Data Science](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3Tc63ehFDhE) — **Mathew K Analytics**. It demonstrates descriptive-statistic calculations in Python and supplies a Jupyter notebook, supporting practice with both calculations and code.

6. **Fraud detection (business application)** — [Fraud Detection Using Machine Learning – Full Python Data Science Project (94% Accuracy)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4Od5_z28iIE) — **Onur Baltaci**. This project covers transaction-data preprocessing, feature engineering, model choice, and evaluation, helping frame how analytics is applied to fraud detection in an exam response.

7. **Credit scoring (business application)** — [Build a Credit Scoring Model with Python | FinTech ML Project for Beginners](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pWOoYpJsaDc) — **Epython Lab**. The hands-on model uses Python with loan data and covers preparation, feature engineering, and machine-learning methods, giving concrete steps to recall for a credit-scoring application question.

8. **Loan application (business application)** — [Loan Approval Prediction using Python | Classifications | Machine Learning Projects | #09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EiSzb1oBCvY) — **sn dev**. It builds a loan-approval prediction model in Python, illustrating how application data can support an approval classification decision in a practical or written answer.

9. **Market-basket analysis (business application)** — [Market Basket Analysis Using Apriori in Python](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rI_T8BVMwPI) — **the outlier 73**. The lesson introduces purchase-pattern discovery and Apriori-based market-basket analysis, useful for revising the business purpose and explaining how association patterns inform retail decisions.

## Coverage audit

- **Detailed syllabus topics counted:** 9 (the lab/data-set format, four named Python analyses, and four specifically named business applications).
- **Verified video coverage:** all 9 counted topics have at least one directly opened YouTube watch page with a matching title/channel and relevant lesson description. Association-rule analysis is separately mapped to a Python/mlxtend demo and market-basket analysis to an Apriori lesson; the syllabus’s open-ended “etc.” does not define additional specific applications to map.
- **Gaps/uncertainties:** the lab-format item describes teaching method rather than an analytic method, so the general dataset-based EDA project is a practice analogue, not an exact replication of the course’s classroom instructions/discussion. The Python implementation choices in the videos are learning examples, not stated prescribed libraries or a required course software stack. The business-application videos are general/international examples, not Bangladesh-specific legal or regulatory instruction; the listed applications themselves specify no jurisdiction-specific laws.

## Capstone and examinations

The AIS course matrix names an internship/research/project report after Semester 8. The attached document describes it as a four-month capstone but gives conflicting credit figures in different sections, and the AIS pages do not provide a detailed capstone topic syllabus. For that reason, no video has been presented as comprehensive capstone coverage. The semester viva-voce requirements likewise have no separate topical syllabus in the course matrix.

## How to use the coverage notes

For revision, follow the course topic outline in order. Use each linked lecture to learn the concept or method, then solve the class/textbook questions independently. Recheck statutory, tax, IFRS, and other regulated content against the syllabus version used by the department. Where a topic is marked **NOT COVERED**, the fragment provides targeted YouTube search terms; treat those as unresolved research leads, not as substitutes for a verified lesson.
